“A quaint old custom,” he explained, smiling above the ruins. “When the King receives the representatives of the House of Barker, the hat of the latter is immediately destroyed in this manner. It represents the absolute finality of the act of homage expressed in the removal of it. It declares that never until that hat shall once more appear upon your head (a contingency which I firmly believe to be remote) shall the House of Barker rebel against the Crown of England.”

===Chinese===
他一头闯进去，砰地摔上门，把那顶无可挑剔的丝质礼帽啪地拍在桌上。他张嘴正要说话，国王却先开口了——

"劳驾，你的帽子。"

这位年轻的政治家手指局促地拨弄着，几乎不知道自己在做什么，把帽子递了过去。

国王把它放在自己的椅子上，坐了上去。

"一个古雅的旧俗，"他在一堆残骸之上含笑解释道。"当国王接见巴克家族（House of Barker）的代表时，后者的帽子便须立即以此法销毁。它象征着摘帽这一效忠之举的绝对终结性。它宣告：除非那顶帽子再度戴回你的头上（依我之见，此情形遥不可及），否则巴克家族决不反叛英格兰王冠。"

===Original===
Barker stood with clenched fist, and shaking lip.

“Your jokes,” he began, “and my property—” and then exploded with an oath, and stopped again.

“Continue, continue,” said the King, waving his hands.

“What does it all mean?” cried the other, with a gesture of passionate rationality. “Are you mad?”

“Not in the least,” replied the King, pleasantly. “Madmen are always serious; they go mad from lack of humour. You are looking serious yourself, James.”

===Chinese===
巴克攥紧拳头站着，嘴唇发抖。

"你的笑话，"他开口道，"还有我的财产——"接着一声咒骂爆发出来，又顿住了。

"接着说，接着说，"国王摆着手说。

"这到底是怎么回事？"对方喊道，做一个激烈而理性的手势。"你疯了吗？"

"丝毫没有，"国王愉快地答道。"疯子总是严肃的；他们因为缺少幽默而发疯。你自己看起来就很严肃呢，詹姆斯。"

===Original===
“Why can’t you keep it to your own private life?” expostulated the other. “You’ve got plenty of money, and plenty of houses now to play the fool in, but in the interests of the public—”

“Epigrammatic,” said the King, shaking his finger sadly at him. “None of your daring scintillations here. As to why I don’t do it in private, I rather fail to understand your question. The answer is of comparative limpidity. I don’t do it in private, because it is funnier to do it in public. You appear to think that it would be amusing to be dignified in the banquet hall and in the street, and at my own fireside (I could procure a fireside) to keep the company in a roar. But that is what everyone does. Everyone is grave in public, and funny in private. My sense of humour suggests the reversal of this; it suggests that one should be funny in public, and solemn in private. I desire to make the State functions, parliaments, coronations, and so on, one roaring old-fashioned pantomime. But, on the other hand, I shut myself up alone in a small storeroom for two hours a day, where I am so dignified that I come out quite ill.”

===Chinese===
"你为什么不能把它局限在你自己的私生活里呢？"对方规劝道。"你现在有的是钱，有的是宅子，尽可以在里面装疯卖傻，可为了公众的利益——"

"警句味儿太重，"国王悲伤地冲他摇着手指。"别拿你那些胆大包天的妙语来这儿显摆。至于我为何不在私下里这么做，我倒不大明白你的问题。答案清澈见底。我不在私下做，是因为在公开场合做更有趣。你似乎认为，在宴会厅里、在街头保持庄重是件好玩的事，而在自家的炉边（我弄得到一个炉边）则逗得满座哄堂。但那是人人都做的事。人人都是在公开场合严肃，在私下里滑稽。我的幽默感建议把这件事倒过来；它建议一个人应当在公开场合滑稽，在私下里庄重。我巴望把国家的典礼、议会、加冕礼之类，统统变成一出哄堂大笑的老式滑稽戏。可另一方面，我每天把自己独自关在一间小储藏室里两个钟头，在里面庄重得出来时都要病一场。"

===Original===
By this time Barker was walking up and down the room, his frock coat flapping like the black wings of a bird.

“Well, you will ruin the country, that’s all,” he said shortly.

“It seems to me,” said Auberon, “that the tradition of ten centuries is being broken, and the House of Barker is rebelling against the Crown of England. It would be with regret (for I admire your appearance) that I should be obliged forcibly to decorate your head with the remains of this hat, but—”

“What I can’t understand,” said Barker flinging up his fingers with a feverish American movement, “is why you don’t care about anything else but your games.”

===Chinese===
此时巴克正在屋里来回踱步，他的礼服下摆像鸟的黑色翅膀一样扑闪。

"好啦，你会毁掉这个国家，就这么回事，"他简短地说。

"在我看来，"奥伯伦说，"十个世纪的传统正在被打破，巴克家族正在反叛英格兰王冠。我将十分遗憾（因为我欣赏你的仪表）不得不强行用这顶帽子的残骸装饰你的头，可是——"

"我不明白的，"巴克说着，手指带着一种狂热的美国式动作往上一甩，"是你为什么除了你的游戏以外，什么都不在乎。"

===Original===
The King stopped sharply in the act of lifting the silken remnants, dropped them, and walked up to Barker, looking at him steadily.

“I made a kind of vow,” he said, “that I would not talk seriously, which always means answering silly questions. But the strong man will always be gentle with politicians.

‘The shape my scornful looks deride
						
						Required a God to form;’

if I may so theologically express myself. And for some reason I cannot in the least understand, I feel impelled to answer that question of yours, and to answer it as if there were really such a thing in the world as a serious subject. You ask me why I don’t care for anything else. Can you tell me, in the name of all the gods you don’t believe in, why I should care for anything else?”

===Chinese===
国王在举起那堆丝绸残片的动作中猛地停住，把它们丢下，走到巴克跟前，定定地看着他。

"我曾立下一种誓愿，"他说，"不正经说话，所谓正经说话，向来就是指回答愚蠢的问题。但强者对政客总是宽和的。

'我那鄙夷目光所嘲弄的身形，
						
						须得一位上帝来造就；'

请容我如此作神学式的表达。出于某种我全然不明白的原因，我感到有一股冲动，要回答你那个问题，而且要郑重其事地回答，仿佛世上当真存在着所谓严肃的话题似的。你问我为什么不在乎别的任何事。那么你能不能凭着你所不信的所有神祇之名告诉我，我凭什么要在乎别的任何事？"

===Original===
“Don’t you realise common public necessities?” cried Barker. “Is it possible that a man of your intelligence does not know that it is everyone’s interest—”

“Don’t you believe in Zoroaster? Is it possible that you neglect Mumbo-Jumbo?” returned the King, with startling animation. “Does a man of your intelligence come to me with these damned early Victorian ethics? If, on studying my features and manner, you detect any particular resemblance to the Prince Consort, I assure you you are mistaken. Did Herbert Spencer ever convince you—did he ever convince anybody—did he ever for one mad moment convince himself—that it must be to the interest of the individual to feel a public spirit? Do you believe that, if you rule your department badly, you stand any more chance, or one half of the chance, of being guillotined, that an angler stands of being pulled into the river by a strong pike? Herbert Spencer refrained from theft for the same reason that he refrained from wearing feathers in his hair, because he was an English gentleman with different tastes. I am an English gentleman with different tastes. He liked philosophy. I like art. He liked writing ten books on the nature of human society. I like to see the Lord Chamberlain walking in front of me with a piece of paper pinned to his coattails. It is my humour. Are you answered? At any rate, I have said my last serious word today, and my last serious word I trust for the remainder of my life in this Paradise of Fools. The remainder of my conversation with you today, which I trust will be long and stimulating, I propose to conduct in a new language of my own by means of rapid and symbolic movements of the left leg.” And he began to pirouette slowly round the room with a preoccupied expression.

===Chinese===
"你难道不明白共同的公共需要吗？"巴克喊道。"像你这样有才智的人，难道不知道这符合每个人的利益——"

"你难道不信琐罗亚斯德（Zoroaster）？你竟然怠慢蒙博-琼博（Mumbo-Jumbo）？"国王以令人吃惊的生气反驳道。"像你这样有才智的人，竟拿这些该死的维多利亚时代早期的伦理来见我？倘若你端详我的容貌举止，看出我有什么特别像王夫（the Prince Consort）之处，我向你保证你看错了。赫伯特·斯宾塞（Herbert Spencer）可曾说服过你——可曾说服过任何人——可曾有过哪一个发疯的瞬间说服过他自己——让人觉得胸怀公共精神必定合乎个人的利益？你以为，假如你把自己的部门治理得一塌糊涂，你被送上断头台的几率会比一个钓鱼人被一条壮硕的梭鱼拖下水的几率更大，或者哪怕有它的一半？赫伯特·斯宾塞之不偷窃，与他不在头发上插羽毛是同一个缘故：因为他是一位口味不同的英国绅士。我也是一位口味不同的英国绅士。他好哲学，我好艺术。他喜欢就人类社会的本质写上十本书；我喜欢看见宫务大臣（the Lord Chamberlain）走在我前面，燕尾服上别着一张纸。这就是我的幽默。回答完毕了吧？无论如何，我今日最后一个正经字眼已经说完了，我也信托这是我此后在这座愚人天堂（Paradise of Fools）里余下生涯中最后一个正经字眼。今日余下与你的谈话——我希望它会又长又带劲——我打算用我自己发明的一种新语言来进行，靠左腿的快速而富有象征意味的动作。"说罢他开始带着出神的神情，缓缓地绕着屋子用脚尖旋转。

===Original===
Barker ran round the room after him, bombarding him with demands and entreaties. But he received no response except in the new language. He came out banging the door again, and sick like a man coming on shore. As he strode along the streets he found himself suddenly opposite Cicconani’s restaurant, and for some reason there rose up before him the green fantastic figure of the Spanish General, standing, as he had seen him last, at the door, with the words on his lips, “You cannot argue with the choice of the soul.”

The King came out from his dancing with the air of a man of business legitimately tired. He put on an overcoat, lit a cigar, and went out into the purple night.

“I will go,” he said, “and mingle with the people.”

===Chinese===
巴克绕着屋子追他，用要求与恳求向他轮番轰炸。但他除了那种新语言以外得不到任何回应。他再次砰地摔门出来，像个刚上岸的水手一样犯恶心。他大步走在街上，忽然发现自己正对着奇科卡尼餐厅（Cicconani's），不知怎的，那位西班牙将军那身奇幻的绿衣身影浮现在他眼前——正如他最后一次见到的那样，站在门口，唇边挂着那句话："你无法同灵魂的选择争辩。"

国王跳完舞出来，带着一个公事完毕、理当疲倦的生意人的神气。他穿上大衣，点上一支雪茄，走进了紫色的夜色。

"我要去，"他说，"到民众中间去。"

===Original===
He passed swiftly up a street in the neighbourhood of Notting Hill, when suddenly he felt a hard object driven into his waistcoat. He paused, put up his single eyeglass, and beheld a boy with a wooden sword and a paper cocked hat, wearing that expression of awed satisfaction with which a child contemplates his work when he has hit someone very hard. The King gazed thoughtfully for some time at his assailant, and slowly took a notebook from his breast-pocket.

“I have a few notes,” he said, “for my dying speech;” and he turned over the leaves. “Dying speech for political assassination; ditto, if by former friend—h’m, h’m. Dying speech for death at hands of injured husband (repentant). Dying speech for same (cynical). I am not quite sure which meets the present.…”

“I’m the King of the Castle,” said the boy, truculently, and very pleased with nothing in particular.

The King was a kindhearted man, and very fond of children, like all people who are fond of the ridiculous.

===Chinese===
他快步走过诺丁山（Notting Hill）附近的一条街，忽然感到一个硬邦邦的东西顶进了自己的马甲。他停下，举起单片眼镜，看见一个男孩，手持木剑、头戴纸折的三只角帽，脸上带着那种又敬畏又得意的神情——一个孩子狠狠打中了谁之后，打量自己杰作时正是这种神情。国王若有所思地端详了袭击者好一阵，慢慢从胸前的口袋里掏出一个笔记本。

"我有几份笔记，"他说，"是给我的临终遗言用的；"他翻动着纸页。"政治暗杀用的临终遗言；同上，若凶手是旧友——嗯，嗯。死于被伤害的丈夫之手的临终遗言（忏悔版）。同上（愤世版）。我拿不准哪一篇正合眼前这情形……"

"我是城堡之王，"男孩挑衅地说，没什么特别的由头却十分得意。

国王是个心肠软的人，并且很喜欢小孩——所有喜欢荒唐事物的人都这样。

===Original===
“Infant,” he said, “I’m glad you are so stalwart a defender of your old inviolate Notting Hill. Look up nightly to that peak, my child, where it lifts itself among the stars so ancient, so lonely, so unutterably Notting. So long as you are ready to die for the sacred mountain, even if it were ringed with all the armies of Bayswater—”

The King stopped suddenly, and his eyes shone.

“Perhaps,” he said, “perhaps the noblest of all my conceptions. A revival of the arrogance of the old medieval cities applied to our glorious suburbs. Clapham with a city guard. Wimbledon with a city wall. Surbiton tolling a bell to raise its citizens. West Hampstead going into battle with its own banner. It shall be done. I, the King, have said it.” And, hastily presenting the boy with half a crown, remarking, “For the war-chest of Notting Hill,” he ran violently home at such a rate of speed that crowds followed him for miles. On reaching his study, he ordered a cup of coffee, and plunged into profound meditation upon the project. At length he called his favourite equerry, Captain Bowler, for whom he had a deep affection, founded principally upon the shape of his whiskers.

===Chinese===
"小家伙，"他说，"我很高兴你是你那古老而不可侵犯的诺丁山如此英勇的卫士。夜夜仰望那座山峰吧，我的孩子，它拔地而起，耸立于群星之中，如此古老，如此孤独，如此不可言说地'诺丁'。只要你随时准备为这座圣山赴死，哪怕它被贝斯沃特（Bayswater）的所有大军围困——"

国王戛然而止，双眼放光。

"也许，"他说，"这是我所有构想里最崇高的一项。把旧日中世纪城市的傲气复活过来，移用到我们这些光荣的郊区上。克拉珀姆（Clapham）拥有一支城市卫队。温布尔登（Wimbledon）拥有一道城墙。瑟比顿（Surbiton）敲响召集市民的钟。西汉普斯特德（West Hampstead）打着自己的旗帜上阵。这事一定要办。我，国王，已经说了。"他匆匆赏给男孩半克朗，备注"充作诺丁山的军费"，便发了疯似地往家跑，速度快到人群追着他跑了好多英里。一回到书房，他吩咐一杯咖啡，随即沉入对这个计划的深思。末了他叫来自己最宠幸的侍从武官鲍勒上尉（Captain Bowler）——他对这人怀有深厚的情谊，主要建立在对方连鬓胡子的形状之上。

===Original===
“Bowler,” he said, “isn’t there some society of historical research, or something of which I am an honorary member?”

“Yes, sir,” said Captain Bowler, rubbing his nose, “you are a member of the Encouragers of Egyptian Renaissance, and the Teutonic Tombs Club, and the Society for the Recovery of London Antiquities, and—”

“That is admirable,” said the King. “The London Antiquities does my trick. Go to the Society for the Recovery of London Antiquities and speak to their secretary, and their sub-secretary, and their president, and their vice-president, saying, ‘The King of England is proud, but the honorary member of the Society for the Recovery of London Antiquities is prouder than kings. I should like to tell you of certain discoveries I have made touching the neglected traditions of the London boroughs. The revelations may cause some excitement, stirring burning memories and touching old wounds in Shepherd’s Bush and Bayswater, in Pimlico and South Kensington. The King hesitates, but the honorary member is firm. I approach you invoking the vows of my initiation, the Sacred Seven Cats, the Poker of Perfection, and the Ordeal of the Indescribable Instant (forgive me if I mix you up with the Clan-na-Gael or some other club I belong to), and ask you to permit me to read a paper at your next meeting on the “Wars of the London Boroughs.”’ Say all this to the Society, Bowler. Remember it very carefully, for it is most important, and I have forgotten it altogether, and send me another cup of coffee and some of the cigars that we keep for vulgar and successful people. I am going to write my paper.”

===Chinese===
"鲍勒，"他说，"是不是有那么一个什么历史研究会之类的，我是它的名誉会员？"

"是的，先生，"鲍勒上尉搓着鼻子说，"您是埃及文艺复兴促进会、日耳曼陵墓俱乐部、以及伦敦古物搜求会（Society for the Recovery of London Antiquities）的会员，还有——"

"妙极了，"国王说。"伦敦古物搜求会正合我用。到伦敦古物搜求会去，找他们的秘书、副秘书、会长、副会长，说：'英格兰国王虽感荣耀，但伦敦古物搜求会的这位名誉会员，比国王更感荣耀。我有几项发现，涉及伦敦各区的被忽视的传统，愿向诸位一述。这些揭秘或会引起些许骚动，搅动牧羊人丛（Shepherd's Bush）与贝斯沃特、皮姆利科（Pimlico）与南肯辛顿（South Kensington）那些灼热的记忆，触及旧日的伤疤。国王有所迟疑，但这位名誉会员态度坚决。我以我入会时所立之誓求告诸位——神圣七猫、完美之拨火棍、以及不可名状瞬间之试炼（请原谅我把你们同克兰盖尔会[Clan-na-Gael]或我所属的其他某个俱乐部搞混了）——恳请你们允许我在下次集会上宣读一篇论文，题为《伦敦各区之战》。'把这话全向学会说一遍，鲍勒。务必记牢，因为这至关重要，而我已经把它全忘了。再给我送一杯咖啡来，还有我们专门留给庸俗而得志之人的那种雪茄。我要去写我的论文了。"

===Original===
The Society for the Recovery of London Antiquities met a month after in a corrugated iron hall on the outskirts of one of the southern suburbs of London. A large number of people had collected there under the coarse and flaring gas-jets when the King arrived, perspiring and genial. On taking off his greatcoat, he was perceived to be in evening dress, wearing the Garter. His appearance at the small table, adorned only with a glass of water, was received with respectful cheering.

The chairman (Mr. Huggins) said that he was sure that they had all been pleased to listen to such distinguished lecturers as they had heard for some time past (hear, hear). Mr. Burton (hear, hear), Mr. Cambridge, Professor King (loud and continued cheers), our old friend Peter Jessop, Sir William White (loud laughter), and other eminent men, had done honour to their little venture (cheers). But there were other circumstances which lent a certain unique quality to the present occasion (hear, hear). So far as his recollection went, and in connection with the Society for the Recovery of London Antiquities it went very far (loud cheers), he did not remember that any of their lecturers had borne the title of King. He would therefore call upon King Auberon briefly to address the meeting.

===Chinese===
一个月后，伦敦古物搜求会在伦敦南郊某处的瓦楞铁皮礼堂里开会。国王抵达时，已经有一大群人聚在那粗陋而晃眼的煤气灯下，他满头大汗、和蔼可亲。一脱下大衣，众人便看出他身着晚礼服，佩着嘉德勋章（the Garter）。他在那张只摆着一杯清水的小桌前现身，迎来一片恭敬的欢呼。

主席（哈金斯先生）说，他相信近一段时间以来大家所聆听过的那些杰出讲师，一定都让诸位十分愉快（好哇！好哇！）。伯顿先生（好哇！好哇！）、坎布里奇先生、金教授（经久不息的热烈欢呼）、我们的老朋友彼得·杰索普、威廉·怀特爵士（哄堂大笑），以及其他杰出之士，都为他们这小小的事业增了光（欢呼）。然而今日的场合，另有若干情形赋予它一种独一无二的气质（好哇！好哇！）。就他所忆——而以他与伦敦古物搜求会的渊源，他的记忆伸得极远（热烈欢呼）——他记不起他们的哪位讲师曾享有国王的称号。因此，他恭请奥伯伦国王简短地向大会致辞。

===Original===
The King began by saying that this speech might be regarded as the first declaration of his new policy for the nation. “At this supreme hour of my life I feel that to no one but the members of the Society for the Recovery of London Antiquities can I open my heart (cheers). If the world turns upon my policy, and the storms of popular hostility begin to rise (no, no), I feel that it is here, with my brave Recoverers around me, that I can best meet them, sword in hand” (loud cheers).

His Majesty then went on to explain that, now old age was creeping upon him, he proposed to devote his remaining strength to bringing about a keener sense of local patriotism in the various municipalities of London. How few of them knew the legends of their own boroughs! How many there were who had never heard of the true origin of the Wink of Wandsworth! What a large proportion of the younger generation in Chelsea neglected to perform the old Chelsea Chuff! Pimlico no longer pumped the Pimlies. Battersea had forgotten the name of Blick.

There was a short silence, and then a voice said “Shame!”

===Chinese===
国王开场便说，这篇演说不妨视为他新国策的第一次宣告。"在我一生这至高的时刻，我觉得，除了伦敦古物搜求会的会员们，我无法向任何人敞开心扉（欢呼）。纵使世道反对我的国策，纵使民怨的风暴开始兴起（不，不），我觉得，正是在这里，在我勇敢的搜求会同仁环侍之中，我方能手持长剑，最好地迎击它们"（热烈欢呼）。

接着，国王陛下解释道，如今老之将至，他决意把余力奉献于一件事：在伦敦各自治市镇唤起一种更为敏锐的地方爱国心。他们当中，知道本区传说的何其之少！有多少人从未听说过旺兹沃思之眨眼（the Wink of Wandsworth）的真正由来！切尔西（Chelsea）年轻一代中，有多大比例的人疏于履行古老的切尔西粗汉礼（the Chelsea Chuff）！皮姆利科（Pimlico）再也不抽皮姆利族（the Pimlies）的水了。巴特西（Battersea）已经忘了布利克（Blick）的名字。

片刻沉默之后，一个声音喊道："可耻！"

===Original===
The King continued: “Being called, however unworthily, to this high estate, I have resolved that, so far as possible, this neglect shall cease. I desire no military glory. I lay claim to no constitutional equality with Justinian or Alfred. If I can go down to history as the man who saved from extinction a few old English customs, if our descendants can say it was through this man, humble as he was, that the Ten Turnips are still eaten in Fulham, and the Putney parish councillor still shaves one half of his head, I shall look my great fathers reverently but not fearfully in the face when I go down to the last house of Kings.”

The King paused, visibly affected, but collecting himself, resumed once more.

===Chinese===
国王继续说："蒙召至于此高位，尽管我殊不配，我已下定决心：只要力所能及，必使此种忽视终止。我不求武功之荣。我绝不僭称自己在宪制上可与查士丁尼（Justinian）或阿尔弗雷德（Alfred）比肩。若我能以'从湮没中挽救了几项古老英国风俗之人'的身份载入史册，若我们的后裔能说：正是凭借此人——尽管他卑微——富勒姆（Fulham）至今仍食那十只萝卜（the Ten Turnips），帕特尼（Putney）的教区议员至今仍剃光半个脑袋——那么当我走向诸王的最后一座宅邸时，便能虔诚而非惶恐地直视我那些伟大先辈的面容。"

国王顿住，显然动了情，但定下神，又继续下去。

===Original===
“I trust that to very few of you, at least, I need dwell on the sublime origins of these legends. The very names of your boroughs bear witness to them. So long as Hammersmith is called Hammersmith, its people will live in the shadow of that primal hero, the Blacksmith, who led the democracy of the Broadway into battle till he drove the chivalry of Kensington before him and overthrew them at that place which in honour of the best blood of the defeated aristocracy is still called Kensington Gore. Men of Hammersmith will not fail to remember that the very name of Kensington originated from the lips of their hero. For at the great banquet of reconciliation held after the war, when the disdainful oligarchs declined to join in the songs of the men of the Broadway (which are to this day of a rude and popular character), the great Republican leader, with his rough humour, said the words which are written in gold upon his monument, ‘Little birds that can sing and won’t sing, must be made to sing.’ So that the Eastern Knights were called Cansings or Kensings ever afterwards. But you also have great memories, O men of Kensington! You showed that you could sing, and sing great war-songs. Even after the dark day of Kensington Gore, history will not forget those three Knights who guarded your disordered retreat from Hyde Park (so called from your hiding there), those three Knights after whom Knightsbridge is named. Nor will it forget the day of your re-emergence, purged in the fire of calamity, cleansed of your oligarchic corruptions, when, sword in hand, you drove the Empire of Hammersmith back mile by mile, swept it past its own Broadway, and broke it at last in a battle so long and bloody that the birds of prey have left their name upon it. Men have called it, with austere irony, the Ravenscourt. I shall not, I trust, wound the patriotism of Bayswater, or the lonelier pride of Brompton, or that of any other historic township, by taking these two special examples. I select them, not because they are more glorious than the rest, but partly from personal association (I am myself descended from one of the three heroes of Knightsbridge), and partly from the consciousness that I am an amateur antiquarian, and cannot presume to deal with times and places more remote and more mysterious. It is not for me to settle the question between two such men as Professor Hugg and Sir William Whisky as to whether Notting Hill means Nutting Hill (in allusion to the rich woods which no longer cover it), or whether it is a corruption of Nothing-ill, referring to its reputation among the ancients as an Earthly Paradise. When a Podkins and a Jossy confess themselves doubtful about the boundaries of West Kensington (said to have been traced in the blood of Oxen), I need not be ashamed to confess a similar doubt. I will ask you to excuse me from further history, and to assist me with your encouragement in dealing with the problem which faces us today. Is this ancient spirit of the London townships to die out? Are our omnibus conductors and policemen to lose altogether that light which we see so often in their eyes, the dreamy light of

‘Old unhappy far-off things
					

					And battles long ago’

—to quote the words of a little-known poet who was a friend of my youth? I have resolved, as I have said, so far as possible, to preserve the eyes of policemen and omnibus conductors in their present dreamy state. For what is a state without dreams? And the remedy I propose is as follows:—

===Chinese===
"我相信，至少你们当中很少有人需要我赘述这些传说的崇高起源。你们各区的名字本身就是明证。只要哈默史密斯（Hammersmith）仍叫哈默史密斯，它的人民便活在那位原始英雄——铁匠（the Blacksmith）——的荫庇之下。正是他率领大马路（the Broadway）的平民投入战斗，把肯辛顿的骑士们一路驱赶，终于在那处地方把他们击溃——为了纪念败方贵族所流的最好的血，那地方至今仍叫肯辛顿戈（Kensington Gore）。哈默史密斯的人不会忘记：'肯辛顿'这名字本身，正是出自他们那位英雄之口。因为战后召开了盛大的和解宴会，那些傲慢的寡头不肯与大马路的人们一同唱歌（这些歌至今仍带着粗犷而通俗的风味），伟大的共和派领袖便以其粗豪的幽默说出了那句铭刻在他纪念碑上、以金字书写的话：'会唱歌却不肯唱的小鸟儿，须得逼着它唱。'从此以后，东方的骑士们便被称作'坎辛'（Cansings）或'肯辛'（Kensings）。可你们也有伟大的记忆啊，肯辛顿的男儿们！你们证明了自己会唱，而且唱的是雄壮的战歌。即便在肯辛顿戈那阴沉的一天之后，历史也不会忘记那三位骑士——他们掩护你们从海德公园（Hyde Park，此名正得自你们曾躲藏于此）狼狈撤退，骑士桥（Knightsbridge）正是以这三位骑士命名。历史也不会忘记你们重新崛起的那一天——在灾祸的烈火中淬炼过，洗净了寡头式的腐朽——那一天你们手持长剑，把哈默史密斯帝国一英里一英里地击退，把它扫过它自家的大马路，终于在这一场旷日持久、血流成河的战役中将它击溃，连猛禽都在那里留下了名字。人们以冷峻的反讽，称之为雷文斯考特（the Ravenscourt）。我希望自己并未触伤贝斯沃特的爱国心，也未曾冒犯布朗普顿（Brompton）那更为孤傲的自尊，或任何其他历史古镇的情怀——我所以特举这两个例子，并非因为它们比别人更辉煌，部分是出于个人渊源（我本人便是骑士桥那三位英雄中一位的后裔），部分是因为我自觉不过是个业余的考据家，不敢妄议那些更遥远、更神秘的时空。两位如哈格教授（Professor Hugg）与威廉·威士忌爵士（Sir William Whisky）这般的人物，尚且就'诺丁山究竟是坚果山（Nutting Hill，暗指昔日覆盖其上、今已不存的茂林），还是 Nothing-ill 的讹写（意指古人视之为人间乐园）'争执不下——这种问题，岂是我所能裁断的。当一位波德金斯（Podkins）与一位乔西（Jossy）都坦承自己拿不准西肯辛顿（West Kensington）的疆界（据说以牛血画成）时，我自认怀有同样的疑惑，也不必羞愧。请诸位容我就此打住历史，转而以你们的鼓励助我一臂，共济眼下所面对的难题。难道这伦敦各镇的古风，竟要就此消亡？难道我们的公共马车售票员与警察，要彻底丧失我们时常在他们眼中见到的那种光——那种梦一般的光，那种——

'古老、哀伤、遥远的事物，					
					以及久远的战役'

——用一位我少年时代的朋友、一位鲜为人知的诗人的话来说？我已下定决心，如前所言，尽力把警察与售票员眼里的光维持在眼下这种如梦的状态。因为，一个没有梦的国家，算什么呢？我所提议的对策如下：——"

===Original===
“Tomorrow morning at twenty-five minutes past ten, if Heaven spares my life, I purpose to issue a Proclamation. It has been the work of my life, and is about half finished. With the assistance of a whisky and soda, I shall conclude the other half tonight, and my people will receive it tomorrow. All these boroughs where you were born, and hope to lay your bones, shall be reinstated in their ancient magnificence—Hammersmith, Kensington, Bayswater, Chelsea, Battersea, Clapham, Balham, and a hundred others. Each shall immediately build a city wall with gates to be closed at sunset. Each shall have a city guard, armed to the teeth. Each shall have a banner, a coat-of-arms, and, if convenient, a gathering cry. I will not enter into the details now, my heart is too full. They will be found in the proclamation itself. You will all, however, be subject to enrolment in the local city guards, to be summoned together by a thing called the ‘Tocsin,’ the meaning of which I am studying in my researches into history. Personally, I believe a tocsin to be some kind of highly paid official. If, therefore, any of you happen to have such a thing as a halberd in the house, I should advise you to practise with it in the garden.”

Here the King buried his face in his handkerchief and hurriedly left the platform, overcome by emotions.

The members of the Society for the Recovery of London Antiquities rose in an indescribable state of vagueness. Some were purple with indignation; an intellectual few were purple with laughter; the great majority found their minds a blank. There remains a tradition that one pale face with burning blue eyes remained fixed upon the lecturer, and after the lecture a red-haired boy ran out of the room.

===Chinese===
"明日早晨十点二十五分，倘若上苍留我性命，我拟颁布一道诏书。这是我一生的功课，现已完成约莫一半。今夜借助一杯威士忌苏打（whisky and soda），我将完成另一半，我的人民明日便可收到。你们出生、并希望埋骨于此的这些市镇，都将恢复其古老的辉煌——哈默史密斯、肯辛顿、贝斯沃特、切尔西、巴特西、克拉珀姆、巴拉姆（Balham），以及另外上百个。每一镇须立即建造城墙，设日落即闭的城门。每一镇须有一支武装到牙齿的城市卫队。每一镇须有一面旗帜、一枚纹章，如果方便的话，还要有一声聚众呐喊。详情我现在不赘述，我心潮太满。这一切均见诏书本身。只是你们所有人，都将被编入当地的城卫队，由一种叫做'警钟（Tocsin）'的东西召集到一起——'警钟'究竟何意，我尚在我的历史研究中加以研习。就我个人所信，警钟大概是某种薪酬丰厚的高级官吏。因此，你们当中若有人家中碰巧有这样一件叫做戟（halberd）的器物，我建议你们在花园里多练练手。"

说到这里，国王以手帕掩面，匆匆走下讲台，情难自禁。

伦敦古物搜求会的会员们站起身来，陷入一种难以言状的茫然。有些人因义愤而涨紫了脸；少数有学问的因大笑而涨紫了脸；绝大多数只觉脑中一片空白。流传下来的一种说法是：有一张灼着湛蓝双眼的苍白的脸，始终凝望着讲台者；散场之后，一个红发男孩奔出了大厅。

## II. The Council of the Provosts / 第二章 区督议会

===Original===
The King got up early next morning and came down three steps at a time like a schoolboy. Having eaten his breakfast hurriedly, but with an appetite, he summoned one of the highest officials of the Palace, and presented him with a shilling. “Go and buy me,” he said, “a shilling paintbox, which you will get, unless the mists of time mislead me, in a shop at the corner of the second and dirtier street that leads out of Rochester Row. I have already requested the Master of the Buckhounds to provide me with cardboard. It seemed to me (I know not why) that it fell within his department.”

The King was happy all that morning with his cardboard and his paintbox. He was engaged in designing the uniforms and coats-of-arms for the various municipalities of London. They gave him deep and no inconsiderable thought. He felt the responsibility.

===Chinese===
第二天清晨，国王起个大早，像个小男生似的一次跨三级台阶冲下楼。他匆匆吃完早饭，胃口却极好，随即召来宫里一位最高级别的官员，赏给他一先令。"去给我买，"他说，"一先令一盒的颜料盒，除非岁月的迷雾误导了我，你能在罗切斯特罗（Rochester Row）出来的第二条、也更脏的那条街拐角的铺子里买到。我已经吩咐猎犬长（Master of the Buckhounds）给我备好硬纸板。在我看来（不知何故），这事归他管。"

整整一上午，国王都对着他的硬纸板和颜料盒乐在其中。他正忙着为伦敦各市镇设计制服与纹章。这些设计叫他煞费心思，相当不轻。他感到了肩上的责任。

===Original===
“I cannot think,” he said, “why people should think the names of places in the country more poetical than those in London. Shallow romanticists go away in trains and stop in places called Hugmy-in-the-Hole, or Bumps-on-the-Puddle. And all the time they could, if they liked, go and live at a place with the dim, divine name of St. John’s Wood. I have never been to St. John’s Wood. I dare not. I should be afraid of the innumerable night of fir trees, afraid to come upon a bloodred cup and the beating of the wings of the Eagle. But all these things can be imagined by remaining reverently in the Harrow train.”

And he thoughtfully retouched his design for the headdress of the halberdier of St. John’s Wood, a design in black and red, compounded of a pine tree and the plumage of an eagle. Then he turned to another card. “Let us think of milder matters,” he said. “Lavender Hill! Could any of your glebes and combes and all the rest of it produce so fragrant an idea? Think of a mountain of lavender lifting itself in purple poignancy into the silver skies and filling men’s nostrils with a new breath of life—a purple hill of incense. It is true that upon my few excursions of discovery on a halfpenny tram I have failed to hit the precise spot. But it must be there; some poet called it by its name. There is at least warrant enough for the solemn purple plumes (following the botanical formation of lavender) which I have required people to wear in the neighbourhood of Clapham Junction. It is so everywhere, after all. I have never been actually to Southfields, but I suppose a scheme of lemons and olives represent their austral instincts. I have never visited Parson’s Green, or seen either the Green or the Parson, but surely the pale-green shovel-hats I have designed must be more or less in the spirit. I must work in the dark and let my instincts guide me. The great love I bear to my people will certainly save me from distressing their noble spirit or violating their great traditions.”

===Chinese===
"我想不通，"他说，"人们为什么总觉得乡间的地名比伦敦的更富诗意。浅薄的浪漫主义者坐火车远行，停在一些叫做'坑里的哈格米'（Hugmy-in-the-Hole）或'泥洼上的邦普斯'（Bumps-on-the-Puddle）之类的地方。可他们本可以——只要愿意——去一个有着'圣约翰伍德（St. John's Wood）'这般朦胧而神圣之名的地方栖居。我从未到过圣约翰伍德。我不敢。我会害怕那无数枞树的漫漫长夜，害怕撞见一只血红的杯，听见雄鹰拍击翅膀的声音。然而这一切，只需虔诚地坐在开往哈罗（Harrow）的火车里便可想象得到。"

他若有所思地添上几笔，修饰着圣约翰伍德戟兵的头饰设计——黑红两色，由一棵松树与一片雄鹰的翎羽合成。然后他转向另一张卡片。"咱们想想温和点儿的事，"他说。"薰衣草山（Lavender Hill）！你们那些'格里布'啊'库姆'啊之类的乡野地名，能产出如此芬芳的意象吗？想象一座薰衣草之山，以紫色的尖锐拔入银色的天空，用一缕新的生命之息灌满人的鼻腔——一座紫色的焚香之山。诚然，在我那几次乘半便士电车所作的发现之旅中，我始终未能撞见那个确切的地点。但它一定在那里；某位诗人曾以其名相称。那么，我所要求人们在克拉珀姆交汇站（Clapham Junction）一带佩戴的、那庄严的紫色羽饰（仿薰衣草的植物形态而成），至少是有所本的。说到底处处如此。我从未真到过绍斯菲尔德（Southfields），但我想，柠檬与橄榄的配色方案大概能代表他们南国的气质。我从未造访过帕森格林（Parson's Green），也没见过那'格林'，也没见过那'帕森'（牧师），但想来我所设计的浅绿色铲形帽，或多或少总会合乎其精神。我只能在暗中下笔，任凭我的本能指引。我对人民所怀的伟大热爱，必能保我不致伤了他们高贵的精神，不致亵渎他们伟大的传统。"

===Original===
As he was reflecting in this vein, the door was flung open, and an official announced Mr. Barker and Mr. Lambert.

Mr. Barker and Mr. Lambert were not particularly surprised to find the King sitting on the floor amid a litter of watercolour sketches. They were not particularly surprised because the last time they had called on him they had found him sitting on the floor, surrounded by a litter of children’s bricks, and the time before surrounded by a litter of wholly unsuccessful attempts to make paper darts. But the trend of the royal infant’s remarks, uttered from amid this infantile chaos, was not quite the same affair.

For some time they let him babble on, conscious that his remarks meant nothing. And then a horrible thought began to steal over the mind of James Barker. He began to think that the King’s remarks did not mean nothing.

===Chinese===
他正这般遐想时，门被猛地推开，一名侍从通报巴克先生与兰伯特先生到。

发现国王坐在地板上，被一地水彩草图围着，巴克先生和兰伯特先生并不怎么吃惊。他们之所以不怎么吃惊，是因为上一次来谒，便见他坐在地上，被一地积木包围；再上一次，则被一地全不成功的纸飞镖试制品包围。可这位皇家婴儿从这一片婴儿般的混乱中所发表的言论，其性质就完全是另一回事了。

有一阵子，他们任他咿呀絮叨，心里明白他的话毫无意义。接着，一个可怕的念头开始悄悄爬上詹姆斯·巴克的心头。他开始觉得，国王的话并不是毫无意义。

===Original===
“In God’s name, Auberon,” he suddenly volleyed out, startling the quiet hall, “you don’t mean that you are really going to have these city guards and city walls and things?”

“I am, indeed,” said the infant, in a quiet voice. “Why shouldn’t I have them? I have modelled them precisely on your political principles. Do you know what I’ve done, Barker? I’ve behaved like a true Barkerian. I’ve… but perhaps it won’t interest you, the account of my Barkerian conduct.”

“Oh, go on, go on,” cried Barker.

“The account of my Barkerian conduct,” said Auberon, calmly, “seems not only to interest, but to alarm you. Yet it is very simple. It merely consists in choosing all the provosts under any new scheme by the same principle by which you have caused the central despot to be appointed. Each provost, of each city, under my charter, is to be appointed by rotation. Sleep, therefore, my Barker, a rosy sleep.”

Barker’s wild eyes flared.

===Chinese===
"看在上帝份上，奥伯伦，"他突然像一排子弹似地迸出来，惊破了寂静的大厅，"你不是当真要搞这些城市卫队、城墙之类的东西吧？"

"我正是当真，"那婴儿以平静的声音说道。"我为什么不能搞？我完全是照着你的政治原则如法炮制的。你知道我做了什么吗，巴克？我表现得像个真正的巴克主义者（Barkerian）。我……不过，关于我这番巴克主义行径的陈述，也许你并不感兴趣。"

"哦，说下去，说下去，"巴克喊道。

"关于我这番巴克主义行径的陈述，"奥伯伦平静地说，"似乎不仅让你感兴趣，还让你警觉起来。然而事情再简单不过。它仅仅在于：任何新方案之下的所有区督（provost），都用你们当初指定那位中央独裁者所用的同一原则来挑选。按照我的宪章，每一座城市的每一位区督，都将以轮换制任命。所以，安心睡吧，我的巴克，做个玫瑰色的好梦。"

巴克那双狂乱的眼睛灼灼发亮。

===Original===
“But, in God’s name, don’t you see, Quin, that the thing is quite different? In the centre it doesn’t matter so much, just because the whole object of despotism is to get some sort of unity. But if any damned parish can go to any damned man—”

“I see your difficulty,” said King Auberon, calmly. “You feel that your talents may be neglected. Listen!” And he rose with immense magnificence. “I solemnly give to my liege subject, James Barker, my special and splendid favour, the right to override the obvious text of the Charter of the Cities, and to be, in his own right, Lord High Provost of South Kensington. And now, my dear James, you are all right. Good day.”

“But—” began Barker.

“The audience is at an end, Provost,” said the King, smiling.

===Chinese===
"可是，看在上帝份上，难道你不明白吗，奎因（Quin）？这事完全不一样！在中央，这无所谓，因为独裁的全部目的本就是取得某种统一。可要是任何一个该死的教区都能落到任何一个该死的人头上——"

"我看得出你的难处，"奥伯伦国王平静地说。"你是怕自己的才干受到冷落。听好！"他以极大的气派站起身来。"我郑重地赐予我的臣民詹姆斯·巴克一项特别的、辉煌的恩宠：他有凌驾于城市宪章（the Charter of the Cities）显白条文之上的权利，并以他本人的资格，出任南肯辛顿高级区督（Lord High Provost of South Kensington）。好了，我亲爱的詹姆斯，你妥了。告辞。"

"可是——"巴克开口。

"觐见已毕，区督，"国王微笑着说。

===Original===
How far his confidence was justified, it would require a somewhat complicated description to explain. “The Great Proclamation of the Charter of the Free Cities” appeared in due course that morning, and was posted by bill-stickers all over the front of the Palace, the King assisting them with animated directions, and standing in the middle of the road, with his head on one side, contemplating the result. It was also carried up and down the main thoroughfares by sandwichmen, and the King was, with difficulty, restrained from going out in that capacity himself, being, in fact, found by the Groom of the Stole and Captain Bowler, struggling between two boards. His excitement had positively to be quieted like that of a child.

The reception which the Charter of the Cities met at the hands of the public may mildly be described as mixed. In one sense it was popular enough. In many happy homes that remarkable legal document was read aloud on winter evenings amid uproarious appreciation, when everything had been learnt by heart from that quaint but immortal old classic, Mr. W. W. Jacobs. But when it was discovered that the King had every intention of seriously requiring the provisions to be carried out, of insisting that the grotesque cities, with their tocsins and city guards, should really come into existence, things were thrown into a far angrier confusion. Londoners had no particular objection to the King making a fool of himself, but they became indignant when it became evident that he wished to make fools of them; and protests began to come in.

===Chinese===
他的这份自信究竟有几分站得住脚，要解释起来，须得一段颇为复杂的描述。"自由城市宪章大诏书（the Great Proclamation of the Charter of the Free Cities）"在那天上午依期颁布，由贴招贴的人贴满了王宫正面——国王在一旁兴致勃勃地指挥，还站在路中央，歪着头端详成果。它也由夹板广告人扛着，在主干道上来回游行——国王好不容易才被劝住没亲自去干这一行当，事实上，是御前近侍（Groom of the Stole）与鲍勒上尉发现他正夹在两块板子中间挣扎。他那兴奋劲儿，简直得像哄孩子一样才能平息下来。

城市宪章在公众那里所受到的接待，往轻里说也只能算褒贬不一。从某种意义上说，它够受欢迎的。在许多幸福的家庭里，一到冬夜，大家便把那一份非凡的法律文书高声朗读，笑得前仰后合——其时，那部古怪而不朽的老经典、W.W.雅各布斯先生（Mr. W. W. Jacobs）的作品，他们早已背得滚瓜烂熟。可一旦发现国王竟铁了心要认真推行其中的条款，坚持要让那些滑稽的城市连同其警钟与城市卫队当真现于世间，事情便陷入了一场远为恼怒的混乱。伦敦人并不特别反对国王自取其辱；可一看出他意在让他们集体出丑，便群情激愤起来；抗议书开始纷纷递进。

===Original===
The Lord High Provost of the Good and Valiant City of West Kensington wrote a respectful letter to the King, explaining that upon State occasions it would, of course, be his duty to observe what formalities the King thought proper, but that it was really awkward for a decent householder not to be allowed to go out and put a postcard in a pillar-box without being escorted by five heralds, who announced, with formal cries and blasts of a trumpet, that the Lord High Provost desired to catch the post.

The Lord High Provost of North Kensington, who was a prosperous draper, wrote a curt business note, like a man complaining of a railway company, stating that definite inconvenience had been caused him by the presence of the halberdiers, whom he had to take with him everywhere. When attempting to catch an omnibus to the City, he had found that while room could have been found for himself, the halberdiers had a difficulty in getting in to the vehicle—believe him, theirs faithfully.

The Lord High Provost of Shepherd’s Bush said his wife did not like men hanging round the kitchen.

The King was always delighted to listen to these grievances, delivering lenient and kingly answers, but as he always insisted, as the absolute sine qua non, that verbal complaints should be presented to him with the fullest pomp of trumpets, plumes, and halberds, only a few resolute spirits were prepared to run the gauntlet of the little boys in the street.

===Chinese===
那善良而英勇的西肯辛顿城的高级区督，给国王写了一封恭敬的信，说明在国家大典之际，遵循国王以为妥当的种种礼节，固然是他的本分；可是，一个体面的户主，连出门往邮筒里投张明信片都不成，非得由五名传令官（herald）护送不可——他们还得正式高呼、并吹起号角，宣告"高级区督欲赶投邮"——这委实尴尬。

北肯辛顿高级区督是个发了迹的布商，他写了一张简短的公事便条，像是在向一家铁路公司投诉似的，说自己因戟兵（halberdier）随侍而蒙受了明确的不便，这些戟兵他走到哪儿都得带着。当他想赶一辆公共马车进城时，他发现虽然还能给他自己挤出个位子，戟兵们要挤进车厢却颇费周章——此颂，台端忠仆。

牧羊人丛的高级区督说，他太太不喜欢有男人在厨房附近晃来晃去。

国王一向乐于倾听这些苦情，作出宽厚而富有王者之风的答复；但他总坚持一个绝对不可通融的先决条件：凡口头的陈诉，必须以最隆重的号角、羽饰与戟仗呈上，因此，肯冒街头小男孩夹道起哄之险的，也只有区区几个硬骨头。

===Original===
Among these, however, was prominent the abrupt and businesslike gentleman who ruled North Kensington. And he had before long, occasion to interview the King about a matter wider and even more urgent than the problem of the halberdiers and the omnibus. This was the great question which then and for long afterwards brought a stir to the blood and a flush to the cheek of all the speculative builders and house agents from Shepherd’s Bush to the Marble Arch, and from Westbourne Grove to High Street, Kensington. I refer to the great affair of the improvements in Notting Hill. The scheme was conducted chiefly by Mr. Buck, the abrupt North Kensington magnate, and by Mr. Wilson, the Provost of Bayswater. A great thoroughfare was to be driven through three boroughs, through West Kensington, North Kensington and Notting Hill, opening at one end into Hammersmith Broadway, and at the other into Westbourne Grove. The negotiations, buyings, sellings, bullying and bribing took ten years, and by the end of it Buck, who had conducted them almost single-handed, had proved himself a man of the strongest type of material energy and material diplomacy. And just as his splendid patience and more splendid impatience had finally brought him victory, when workmen were already demolishing houses and walls along the great line from Hammersmith, a sudden obstacle appeared that had neither been reckoned with nor dreamed of, a small and strange obstacle, which, like a speck of grit in a great machine, jarred the whole vast scheme and brought it to a standstill, and Mr. Buck, the draper, getting with great impatience into his robes of office and summoning with indescribable disgust his halberdiers, hurried over to speak to the King.

===Chinese===
然而，在这几个硬骨头当中，那位治理北肯辛顿的、鲁莽而讲求实效的绅士尤为突出。不久之后，他便因一件比戟兵与公共马车之事更为重大、甚至更为紧迫的问题，有了觐见国王的由头。这便是那桩大事业——彼时以及此后很长一段时间里，它令所有从牧羊人丛到大理石拱门（Marble Arch）、从韦斯特伯恩格罗夫（Westbourne Grove）到肯辛顿高街（High Street, Kensington）的投机营造商与房产中介热血沸腾、面颊发烫。我所指的，便是诺丁山改良工程这一桩大事。该方案主要由北肯辛顿那位强势巨头巴克、以及贝斯沃特区督威尔逊先生主持。一条干道将被开凿，贯穿三个市镇——西肯辛顿、北肯辛顿与诺丁山，一端通向哈默史密斯大马路（Hammersmith Broadway），另一端通向韦斯特伯恩格罗夫。谈判、买进、卖出、威吓、利诱，前后历时十年；到末了，几乎独自操持全程的巴克，证明了自己是那种最强的、务实的精力与务实的外交手腕兼具的人。正当他出色的耐心与更出色的不耐烦终于为他赢来胜利——其时工人已在沿线拆毁房屋与院墙——一个始料未及、连做梦也没梦到过的突如其来的障碍出现了，一个古怪的小障碍，像巨型机器里的一粒沙子，硌住了整个庞大的方案，令其戛然停顿；于是布商巴克极不耐烦地套上他的官服，带着难以形容的厌恶召集起他的戟兵，匆匆赶去见国王。

===Original===
Ten years had not tired the King of his joke. There were still new faces to be seen looking out from the symbolic head-gears he had designed, gazing at him from amid the pastoral ribbons of Shepherd’s Bush or from under the sombre hoods of the Blackfriars Road. And the interview which was promised him with the Provost of North Kensington he anticipated with a particular pleasure, for “he never really enjoyed,” he said, “the full richness of the medieval garments unless the people compelled to wear them were very angry and businesslike.”

Mr. Buck was both. At the King’s command the door of the audience-chamber was thrown open and a herald appeared in the purple colours of Mr. Buck’s commonwealth emblazoned with the Great Eagle which the King had attributed to North Kensington, in vague reminiscence of Russia, for he always insisted on regarding North Kensington as some kind of semi-arctic neighbourhood. The herald announced that the Provost of that city desired audience of the King.

===Chinese===
十年并未让国王对他的玩笑生厌。仍能看见新的面孔从他设计的种种象征性头饰里探出来，从牧羊人丛那牧歌风的绶带间，或从布莱克弗赖尔斯路（Blackfriars Road）那阴沉的风帽下凝望着他。而与北肯辛顿区督约定好的这次觐见，他尤为期盼，因为"唯有被迫穿戴的人怒气冲冲、公事公办之时，"他说，"他才算真正领略到那些中世纪衣袍的全部妙处。"

巴克两样都占全了。奉国王之命，觐见厅的大门洞开，一名传令官现身，一身巴克共和国的紫色，上面饰有国王加诸北肯辛顿的大鹰（the Great Eagle）——这是他因隐约联想俄国而设，因为他总坚持把北肯辛顿视作某种半寒带的所在。传令官宣告：该城区督请觐国王。

===Original===
“From North Kensington?” said the King, rising graciously. “What news does he bring from that land of high hills and fair women? He is welcome.”

The herald advanced into the room, and was immediately followed by twelve guards clad in purple, who were followed by an attendant bearing the banner of the Eagle, who was followed by another attendant bearing the keys of the city upon a cushion, who was followed by Mr. Buck in a great hurry. When the King saw his strong animal face and steady eyes, he knew that he was in the presence of a great man of business, and consciously braced himself.

“Well, well,” he said, cheerily coming down two or three steps from a dais, and striking his hands lightly together, “I am glad to see you. Never mind, never mind. Ceremony is not everything.”

“I don’t understand your Majesty,” said the Provost, stolidly.

“Never mind, never mind,” said the King, gaily. “A knowledge of Courts is by no means an unmixed merit; you will do it next time, no doubt.”

The man of business looked at him sulkily from under his black brows and said again without show of civility—

“I don’t follow you.”

“Well, well,” replied the King, good-naturedly, “if you ask me I don’t mind telling you, not because I myself attach any importance to these forms in comparison with the Honest Heart. But it is usual—it is usual—that is all, for a man when entering the presence of Royalty to lie down on his back on the floor and elevating his feet towards heaven (as the source of Royal power) to say three times ‘Monarchical institutions improve the manners.’ But there, there—such pomp is far less truly dignified than your simple kindliness.”

The Provost’s face was red with anger, and he maintained silence.

===Chinese===
"从北肯辛顿来？"国王和蔼可亲地站起身来。"他从那片崇山峻岭、佳丽如云的土地上带来什么消息？欢迎欢迎。"

传令官步入厅内，紧接着是十二名紫衣卫士，其后是一名擎着雄鹰旗的随从，其后是另一名捧着城市钥匙（置于软垫之上）的随从，其后便是在大步流星赶来的巴克。国王一看见他那强健如兽的脸孔与沉稳的双眼，便明白眼前是一位厉害的生意人，不由暗暗绷紧了自己。

"好啦，好啦，"他轻快地从御座台上走下两三级台阶，轻轻拍着手说道，"见到你真高兴。没事，没事。礼数又不是一切。"

"我不明白陛下什么意思，"区督木然说。

"没事，没事，"国王快活地说。"谙熟宫廷礼节，决非纯然的优点；下回你就会了，保准。"

生意人从他的黑眉毛下愠怒地瞪着他，再一次毫无客套地说——

"我听不懂你在说什么。"

"好啦，好啦，"国王好脾气地答道，"你要问，我也不妨告诉你——倒不是我自己把这套礼数看得比那颗'诚实的心'更重。只是通常——通常罢了——一个人觐见王室时，总要以仰面躺在地上、把两脚翘向天空（视为王权之源）、连说三遍'君主制度改善礼仪'为常例。可是得了，得了——这种排场，远不如你那质朴的和气来得真正庄重。"

区督的脸因盛怒而涨红，他保持着沉默。

===Original===
“And now,” said the King, lightly, and with the exasperating air of a man softening a snub; “what delightful weather we are having! You must find your official robes warm, my Lord. I designed them for your own snowbound land.”

“They’re as hot as hell,” said Buck, briefly. “I came here on business.”

“Right,” said the King, nodding a great number of times with quite unmeaning solemnity; “right, right, right. Business, as the sad glad old Persian said, is business. Be punctual. Rise early. Point the pen to the shoulder. Point the pen to the shoulder, for you know not whence you come nor why. Point the pen to the shoulder, for you know not when you go nor where.”

The Provost pulled a number of papers from his pocket and savagely flapped them open.

“Your Majesty may have heard,” he began, sarcastically, “of Hammersmith and a thing called a road. We have been at work ten years buying property and getting compulsory powers and fixing compensation and squaring vested interests, and now at the very end, the thing is stopped by a fool. Old Prout, who was Provost of Notting Hill, was a business man, and we dealt with him quite satisfactorily. But he’s dead, and the cursed lot has fallen on a young man named Wayne, who’s up to some game that’s perfectly incomprehensible to me. We offer him a better price than anyone ever dreamt of, but he won’t let the road go through. And his Council seems to be backing him up. It’s midsummer madness.”

===Chinese===
"那么现在，"国王轻快地说，带着那种叫人窝火的、仿佛在冲淡一记冷落的神情，"天气多好啊！大人您一定觉得这身官服挺暖和吧。我是照着您那片冰天雪地的家乡设计的。"

"热得像地狱，"巴克简短地说。"我是来谈正事的。"

"对，"国王带着毫无意义的庄重一连点了好多次头，"对，对，对。生意就是生意——正如那位又悲又喜的老波斯人所说。要守时。要早起。把笔尖指向肩膀。把笔尖指向肩膀，因为你既不知自己从何而来，亦不知为何而来。把笔尖指向肩膀，因为你既不知自己何时而去，亦不知往何处去。"

区督从口袋里掏出一沓文件，粗暴地把它们哗啦抖开。

"陛下想必听说过哈默史密斯，还有一条叫做'马路'的东西。我们干了十年——置办地产、取得强制征用权、议定补偿、摆平各方既得利益——如今临到末了，这事却被一个傻瓜卡住了。老普劳特（Old Prout）在世时任诺丁山区督，是个生意人，我们跟他打交道十分顺手。可他死了，这该死的签偏偏落到了一个姓韦恩（Wayne）的年轻人头上，他在搞什么名堂，我实在摸不着头脑。我们开的价码比任何人做梦敢想的都高，可他就是不让这条路通过。而且他的区督议会（Council）似乎在给他撑腰。简直是仲夏之狂。"

===Original===
The King, who was rather inattentively engaged in drawing the Provost’s nose with his finger on the windowpane, heard the last two words.

“What a perfect phrase that is!” he said. “‘Midsummer madness’!”

“The chief point is,” continued Buck, doggedly, “that the only part that is really in question is one dirty little street—Pump Street—a street with nothing in it but a public-house and a penny toyshop, and that sort of thing. All the respectable people of Notting Hill have accepted our compensation. But the ineffable Wayne sticks out over Pump Street. Says he’s Provost of Notting Hill. He’s only Provost of Pump Street.”

“A good thought,” replied Auberon. “I like the idea of a Provost of Pump Street. Why not let him alone?”

“And drop the whole scheme!” cried out Buck, with a burst of brutal spirit. “I’ll be damned if we do. No. I’m for sending in workmen to pull down without more ado.”

“Strike for the purple Eagle!” cried the King, hot with historical associations.

“I’ll tell you what it is,” said Buck, losing his temper altogether. “If your Majesty would spend less time in insulting respectable people with your silly coats-of-arms, and more time over the business of the nation—”

The King’s brow wrinkled thoughtfully.

“The situation is not bad,” he said; “the haughty burgher defying the King in his own Palace. The burgher’s head should be thrown back and the right arm extended; the left may be lifted towards Heaven, but that I leave to your private religious sentiment. I have sunk back in this chair, stricken with baffled fury. Now again, please.”

Buck’s mouth opened like a dog’s, but before he could speak another herald appeared at the door.

“The Lord High Provost of Bayswater,” he said, “desires an audience.”

“Admit him,” said Auberon. “This *is* a jolly day.”

===Chinese===
国王这时正漫不经心地用手指在窗玻璃上勾画区督的鼻子，听见最后那几个字。

"这真是个绝妙的说法！"他说。"'仲夏之狂'！"

"要点在于，"巴克固执地接着说，"真正成问题的地段，其实只有一条脏兮兮的小街——泵街（Pump Street）——街上除了酒馆、一便士的玩具店之类之外什么也没有。诺丁山所有体面人都已接受我们的补偿。可那位不可名状的韦恩偏偏在泵街这事上死扛。他说自己是诺丁山区督。他不过是泵街区督罢了。"

"好想法，"奥伯伦答道。"我喜欢'泵街区督'这个点子。何不放着他不管？"

"难道把整个方案都丢了！"巴克发作出一股蛮横的劲头喊道。"我们才不干呢。不。我的意见是直接派工人去拆，不多废话。"

"为紫鹰（the purple Eagle）而战！"国王因历史的联想而激动起来。

"我跟您说，"巴克彻底失了脾气，"要是陛下少花点儿时间拿那些蠢纹章去侮辱体面人，多花点儿时间过问国事——"

国王若有所思地皱起眉头。

"这情形不赖，"他说，"傲慢的市民在国王自家的王宫里顶撞国王。市民的头应当向后仰，右臂前伸；左臂可以抬向天空，不过这一节我留给你私人的宗教情怀。我呢，已颓然瘫倒在这张椅子里，为受阻的怒火所击中。好，请再来一遍。"

巴克的嘴像狗一样张开，可他还没来得及说话，门口又出现了一名传令官。

"贝斯沃特高级区督，"他说，"请求觐见。"

"让他进来，"奥伯伦说。"这下 *真* 是个快活的日子。"

===Original===
The halberdiers of Bayswater wore a prevailing uniform of green, and the banner which was borne after them was emblazoned with a green bay-wreath on a silver ground, which the King, in the course of his researches into a bottle of champagne, had discovered to be the quaint old punning cognisance of the city of Bayswater.

“It is a fit symbol,” said the King, “your immortal bay-wreath. Fulham may seek for wealth, and Kensington for art, but when did the men of Bayswater care for anything but glory?”

Immediately behind the banner, and almost completely hidden by it, came the Provost of the city, clad in splendid robes of green and silver with white fur and crowned with bay. He was an anxious little man with red whiskers, originally the owner of a small sweet-stuff shop.

“Our cousin of Bayswater,” said the King, with delight; “what can we get for you?” The King was heard also distinctly to mutter, “Cold beef, cold ’am, cold chicken,” his voice dying into silence.

“I came to see your Majesty,” said the Provost of Bayswater, whose name was Wilson, “about that Pump Street affair.”

“I have just been explaining the situation to his Majesty,” said Buck, curtly, but recovering his civility. “I am not sure, however, whether his Majesty knows how much the matter affects you also.”

“It affects both of us, yer see, yer Majesty, as this scheme was started for the benefit of the ’ole neighbourhood. So Mr. Buck and me we put our ’eads together—”

The King clasped his hands.

“Perfect!” he cried in ecstasy. “Your heads together! I can see it! Can’t you do it now? Oh, do do it now!”

A smothered sound of amusement appeared to come from the halberdiers, but Mr. Wilson looked merely bewildered, and Mr. Buck merely diabolical.

“I suppose,” he began bitterly, but the King stopped him with a gesture of listening.

“Hush,” he said, “I think I hear someone else coming. I seem to hear another herald, a herald whose boots creak.”

As he spoke another voice cried from the doorway—

“The Lord High Provost of South Kensington desires an audience.”

===Chinese===
贝斯沃特的戟兵身着以绿色为主的制服，紧随其后的旗帜是银底上一只绿色月桂环——这是国王在对一瓶香槟的考据过程中，发现乃是贝斯沃特城那古雅的、双关旧纹章。

"这是个相称的象征，"国王说，"你们那不朽的月桂环（bay-wreath）。富勒姆（Fulham）或可求财，肯辛顿或可求艺，可贝斯沃特的男人何时在乎过荣耀以外的东西？"

紧随旗帜之后、几乎被它完全遮住的，是该城的区督。他身着华美的绿银两色、镶白毛的礼袍，头戴月桂冠。他是个神情焦虑的小个子，留着红鬓角，原先是一家小糖果铺的老板。

"我们贝斯沃特的堂弟，"国王喜形于色，"我们能为您拿点儿什么？"还能清楚地听见国王咕哝了一句："冷牛肉，冷'腿'，冷鸡肉，"声音渐弱至无。

"我是来见陛下的，"贝斯沃特区督说——他名叫威尔逊——"为的就是泵街那档子事。"

"我正向陛下说明情形，"巴克简短地说，却恢复了几分客气。"不过，我不敢说陛下是否清楚这事对您也同样要紧。"

"这事关咱们俩，您瞧，陛下，因为这方案原本是为整个街坊的好处才起的头。所以巴克先生跟我把咱俩的'脑袋'凑到了一块儿——"

国王双手一拍。

"妙极了！"他狂喜地叫道。"你们的脑袋凑到一块儿！我都能看见了！你们现在不能凑一凑吗？哦，现在就凑吧，现在就凑！"

戟兵当中似乎传出一阵压低的、被逗乐的声音，可威尔逊先生只是满脸茫然，而巴克则一脸恶魔般的愠怒。

"我想——"他刻薄地开口，可国王以一个倾听的手势止住了他。

"嘘，"他说，"我好像听见又有人来了。我似乎听见又一名传令官——一名靴子吱嘎作响的传令官。"

正说着，门口又一个声音高喊——

"南肯辛顿高级区督请求觐见。"

===Original===
“The Lord High Provost of South Kensington!” cried the King. “Why, that is my old friend James Barker! What does he want, I wonder? If the tender memories of friendship have not grown misty, I fancy he wants something for himself, probably money. How are you, James?”

Mr. James Barker, whose guard was attired in a splendid blue, and whose blue banner bore three gold birds singing, rushed, in his blue and gold robes, into the room. Despite the absurdity of all the dresses, it was worth noticing that he carried his better than the rest, though he loathed it as much as any of them. He was a gentleman, and a very handsome man, and could not help unconsciously wearing even his preposterous robe as it should be worn. He spoke quickly, but with the slight initial hesitation he always showed in addressing the King, due to suppressing an impulse to address his old acquaintance in the old way.

“Your Majesty—pray forgive my intrusion. It is about this man in Pump Street. I see you have Buck here, so you have probably heard what is necessary. I—”

The King swept his eyes anxiously round the room, which now blazed with the trappings of three cities.

“There is one thing necessary,” he said.

“Yes, your Majesty,” said Mr. Wilson of Bayswater, a little eagerly. “What does yer Majesty think necessary?”

“A little yellow,” said the King, firmly. “Send for the Provost of West Kensington.”

===Chinese===
"南肯辛顿高级区督！"国王叫道。"哎呀，那不正是我的老朋友詹姆斯·巴克！不知道他想干什么？假如友谊的温情记忆尚未模糊，我猜他是想为自己要点什么，多半是钱。你好啊，詹姆斯？"

詹姆斯·巴克先生的卫队身着华丽的蓝衣，他的蓝旗上绘着三只振翅而鸣的金鸟，他便穿着一身蓝金两色的礼袍冲进厅来。尽管所有这些衣袍都荒唐可笑，值得注意的是，他穿起来却比别人都更妥帖，虽然他对它的厌恶不亚于任何人。他是位绅士，又是个极俊美的男子，即便穿着那件荒谬的礼袍，也会不由自主地穿得恰到好处。他说话很快，但开头总带着一丝迟疑——这是他每次向国王陈词时都有的，因为他总要压住一股冲动：按老交情的老方式招呼对方。

"陛下——请恕我擅闯。我是为泵街那个人来的。我看巴克也在这里，所以该说的您大概都听过了。我——"

国王焦切地把目光扫过整间大厅——此刻厅中已是三城仪仗、一片辉煌。

"还有一样是必不可少的，"他说。

"是，陛下，"贝斯沃特的威尔逊先生略带急切地说，"陛下以为必不可少的是什么？"

"一点儿黄色，"国王坚定地说。"去请西肯辛顿区督。"

===Original===
Amid some materialistic protests he was sent for, and arrived with his yellow halberdiers in his saffron robes, wiping his forehead with a handkerchief. After all, placed as he was, he had a good deal to say on the matter.

“Welcome, West Kensington,” said the King. “I have long wished to see you touching that matter of the Hammersmith land to the south of the Rowton House. Will you hold it feudally from the Provost of Hammersmith? You have only to do him homage by putting his left arm in his overcoat and then marching home in state.”

“No, your Majesty; I’d rather not,” said the Provost of West Kensington, who was a pale young man with a fair moustache and whiskers, who kept a successful dairy.

The King struck him heartily on the shoulder.

“The fierce old West Kensington blood,” he said; “they are not wise who ask it to do homage.”

Then he glanced again round the room. It was full of a roaring sunset of colour, and he enjoyed the sight, possible to so few artists—the sight of his own dreams moving and blazing before him. In the foreground the yellow of the West Kensington liveries outlined itself against the dark blue draperies of South Kensington. The crests of these again brightened suddenly into green as the almost woodland colours of Bayswater rose behind them. And over and behind all, the great purple plumes of North Kensington showed almost funereal and black.

“There is something lacking,” said the King—“something lacking. What can—Ah, there it is! there it is!”

In the doorway had appeared a new figure, a herald in flaming red. He cried in a loud but unemotional voice—

“The Lord High Provost of Notting Hill desires an audience.”

===Chinese===
他在一阵偏重实利的抗议声中被请了来，带着他的黄衣戟兵、身着藏红花色的礼袍到达，正用手帕擦额头。说到底，凭他的处境，他对这事也有不少话要说。

"欢迎，西肯辛顿，"国王说。"我早就想见您，谈谈罗顿公寓（Rowton House）以南那块哈默史密斯地皮的事。您是否愿意以封邑的形式从哈默史密斯区督手里领有它？您只需向他行个效忠礼——把他的左臂塞进他的大衣，然后浩浩荡荡地列队回家即可。"

"不，陛下；我宁愿不，"西肯辛顿区督说。他是个脸色苍白的年轻人，留着淡色的八字胡和鬓角，经营着一家生意兴隆的牛奶场。

国王热情地拍拍他的肩。

"西肯辛顿那凶猛的古老血脉，"他说，"求它行效忠礼的人，可不智。"

随后他又环顾大厅。满室色彩，如一轮轰然盛烧的落日，他享受着这副景象——这是极少有艺术家能见到的景象：他自家的梦在他眼前移动、燃烧。近处，西肯辛顿号衣的黄色衬在南肯辛顿深蓝的帷幔之前。这些深蓝之上的纹饰，又随着贝斯沃特那近乎林间色调的绿色在身后升起，蓦地亮成绿色。而在所有这些之上、之后，北肯辛顿那巨大的紫色羽饰，几乎显得如丧服一般，近乎黑色。

"还缺点什么，"国王说——"还缺点什么。能是什么呢——啊，就是这个！就是这个！"

门口已出现了一个新身影，一名一身火红的传令官。他以洪亮却不动声色的声音高呼——

"诺丁山高级区督请求觐见。"

## III. Enter a Lunatic / 第三章 狂人登场

===Original===
The King of the Fairies, who was, it is to be presumed, the godfather of King Auberon, must have been very favourable on this particular day to his fantastic godchild, for with the entrance of the guard of the Provost of Notting Hill there was a certain more or less inexplicable addition to his delight. The wretched navvies and sandwich-men who carried the colours of Bayswater or South Kensington, engaged merely for the day to satisfy the Royal hobby, slouched into the room with a comparatively hangdog air, and a great part of the King’s intellectual pleasure consisted in the contrast between the arrogance of their swords and feathers and the meek misery of their faces. But these Notting Hill halberdiers in their red tunics belted with gold had the air rather of an absurd gravity. They seemed, so to speak, to be taking part in the joke. They marched and wheeled into position with an almost startling dignity and discipline.

They carried a yellow banner with a great red lion, named by the King as the Notting Hill emblem, after a small public-house in the neighbourhood, which he once frequented.

Between the two lines of his followers there advanced towards the King a tall, red-haired young man, with high features and bold blue eyes. He would have been called handsome, but that a certain indefinable air of his nose being too big for his face, and his feet for his legs, gave him a look of awkwardness and extreme youth. His robes were red, according to the King’s heraldry, and, alone among the Provosts, he was girt with a great sword. This was Adam Wayne, the intractable Provost of Notting Hill.

===Chinese===
那位众仙之王——想必是奥伯伦国王的教父——在这一天必是对他这位异想天开的教子格外青眼有加，因为随着诺丁山区督卫队的入厅，他的快乐里又添上了一重多少有些说不清道不明的成分。那些倒霉的掘土工和夹板广告人，不过是为应付国王的癖好而被临时雇来扛贝斯沃特或南肯辛顿旗帜的，走进厅时神色相对委琐；而国王一大部分智识上的乐趣，便来自他们刀剑羽饰的倨傲与脸上那副温顺的苦相之间所形成的反差。可诺丁山这些戟兵，身着红衣、束着金腰带，倒透着一股荒诞的庄重。可以说，他们似乎也加入了这场玩笑。他们迈步、转身、就位，带着一种几乎令人吃惊的尊严与纪律。

他们扛着一面黄旗，上面是一只硕大的红狮——这是国王钦定的诺丁山徽记，得名于附近一家他一度常去的小酒馆。

在两列扈从之间，一个高个子、红头发的年轻人朝国王走来，五官高峻，蓝眼锐利。他本可称作俊美，只是一种难以言说的气质——鼻子对脸而言太大、脚对腿而言太大——给他平添了一层笨拙与极年轻的神气。按照国王的纹章学，他身着红袍，并且在所有区督中独一无二地佩着一柄长剑。这便是亚当·韦恩（Adam Wayne），那位桀骜不驯的诺丁山区督。

===Original===
The King flung himself back in his chair, and rubbed his hands.

“What a day, what a day!” he said to himself. “Now there’ll be a row. I’d no idea it would be such fun as it is. These Provosts are so very indignant, so very reasonable, so very right. This fellow, by the look in his eyes, is even more indignant than the rest. No sign in those large blue eyes, at any rate, of ever having heard of a joke. He’ll remonstrate with the others, and they’ll remonstrate with him, and they’ll all make themselves sumptuously happy remonstrating with me.”

“Welcome, my Lord,” he said aloud. “What news from the Hill of a Hundred Legends? What have you for the ear of your King? I know that troubles have arisen between you and these others, our cousins, but these troubles it shall be our pride to compose. And I doubt not, and cannot doubt, that your love for me is not less tender, no less ardent, than theirs.”

Mr. Buck made a bitter face, and James Barker’s nostrils curled; Wilson began to giggle faintly, and the Provost of West Kensington followed in a smothered way. But the big blue eyes of Adam Wayne never changed, and he called out in an odd, boyish voice down the hall—

“I bring homage to my King. I bring him the only thing I have—my sword.”

And with a great gesture he flung it down on the ground, and knelt on one knee behind it.

There was a dead silence.

“I beg your pardon,” said the King, blankly.

===Chinese===
国王往后一仰，瘫在椅子里，搓着双手。

"多么美好的一天，多么美好的一天！"他自言自语。"这下可有一场好吵了。我没想到会这么好玩。这些区督们是如此愤慨，如此讲理，如此正确。这一位呢，看他的眼神，比其余各位还要愤慨。至少在那双湛蓝的大眼睛里，看不出半点儿听说过'玩笑'的迹象。他会跟其余诸位抗议，其余诸位也会跟他抗议，然后他们通通都会以向我抗议为最大乐事。"

"欢迎，大人，"他出声说道。"百传奇之山（the Hill of a Hundred Legends）有什么消息？您有什么要禀报国王的耳朵？我知道您与这几位——我们的堂弟——之间生了些龃龉，而排解这些龃龉，将是我们的荣光。并且我毫不怀疑、也不能怀疑：您对我的爱，不比他们的更温柔，也不比他们的更炽热。"

巴克苦了一下脸，詹姆斯·巴克的鼻翼翕动；威尔逊开始轻声窃笑，西肯辛顿区督也跟着闷闷地笑。可亚当·韦恩那双蓝色的大眼睛丝毫不变，他以一种古怪的、孩子气的声音朝大厅那一头高喊——

"我来向我的国王献上效忠。我献给他我仅有的一样东西——我的剑。"

说罢，他做一个大开大合的手势，把剑掷在地上，单膝跪在剑后。

一片死寂。

"请您原谅，"国王茫然地说。

===Original===
“You speak well, sire,” said Adam Wayne, “as you ever speak, when you say that my love is not less than the love of these. Small would it be if it were not more. For I am the heir of your scheme—the child of the great Charter. I stand here for the rights the Charter gave me, and I swear, by your sacred crown, that where I stand, I stand fast.”

The eyes of all five men stood out of their heads.

Then Buck said, in his jolly, jarring voice: “Is the whole world mad?”

The King sprang to his feet, and his eyes blazed.

“Yes,” he cried, in a voice of exultation, “the whole world is mad, but Adam Wayne and me. It is true as death what I told you long ago, James Barker, seriousness sends men mad. You are mad, because you care for politics, as mad as a man who collects tram tickets. Buck is mad, because he cares for money, as mad as a man who lives on opium. Wilson is mad, because he thinks himself right, as mad as a man who thinks himself God Almighty. The Provost of West Kensington is mad, because he thinks he is respectable, as mad as a man who thinks he is a chicken. All men are mad but the humorist, who cares for nothing and possesses everything. I thought that there was only one humorist in England. Fools!—dolts!—open your cows’ eyes; there are two! In Notting Hill—in that unpromising elevation—there has been born an artist! You thought to spoil my joke, and bully me out of it, by becoming more and more modern, more and more practical, more and more bustling and rational. Oh, what a feast it was to answer you by becoming more and more august, more and more gracious, more and more ancient and mellow! But this lad has seen how to bowl me out. He has answered me back, vaunt for vaunt, rhetoric for rhetoric. He has lifted the only shield I cannot break, the shield of an impenetrable pomposity. Listen to him. You have come, my Lord, about Pump Street?”

===Chinese===
"陛下说得是，"亚当·韦恩说，"一如您向来所言——您说我的爱不亚于这几位的爱。若不超过他们，那便微不足道了。因为我是您所谋划之事业的继承人——伟大宪章的孩子。我站在这里，为宪章所赋予我的权利而立，我凭您神圣的王冠起誓：我立身之处，决不动摇。"

五个人全都瞪圆了眼。

接着巴克用他快活刺耳的声音说："全天下都疯了吗？"

国王霍地站起，双眼灼灼放光。

"对，"他狂喜地叫道，"全天下都疯了，只除了亚当·韦恩和我。我早就告诉过你的话如今句句应验了，詹姆斯·巴克——较真，会把人逼疯。你疯了，因为你在乎政治，疯得像个收集电车票的人。巴克疯了，因为他在乎钱，疯得像个靠鸦片度日的人。威尔逊疯了，因为他自以为正确，疯得像个自以为是全能上帝的人。西肯辛顿区督疯了，因为他自以为体面，疯得像个自以为是只鸡的人。世人皆疯，唯有那个什么都不在乎、却拥有一切的幽默家是清醒的。我原以为全英国只有这么一位幽默家。蠢材！——笨驴！——睁大你们那双牛眼瞧瞧吧；有两位！就在诺丁山——就在那片毫不起眼的高地上——诞生了一位艺术家！你们原以为越变越现代、越变越实际、越变越忙碌而讲理，就能砸了我的玩笑、把我吓退。哦，用越变越威严、越变越宽厚、越变越古老而醇厚来回应你们，是何等的一场盛宴！可这小子看出了怎么才能把我将死。他针锋相对地回敬了我，夸口对夸口，辞藻对辞藻。他举起了我唯一无法击破的一面盾——一面滴水不漏的装腔作势之盾。听他说。大人，您是为泵街而来的吧？"

===Original===
“About the city of Notting Hill,” answered Wayne, proudly, “of which Pump Street is a living and rejoicing part.”

“Not a very large part,” said Barker, contemptuously.

“That which is large enough for the rich to covet,” said Wayne, drawing up his head, “is large enough for the poor to defend.”

The King slapped both his legs, and waved his feet for a second in the air.

“Every respectable person in Notting Hill,” cut in Buck, with his cold, coarse voice, “is for us and against you. I have plenty of friends in Notting Hill.”

“Your friends are those who have taken your gold for other men’s hearthstones, my Lord Buck,” said Provost Wayne. “I can well believe they are your friends.”

“They’ve never sold dirty toys, anyhow,” said Buck, laughing shortly.

“They’ve sold dirtier things,” said Wayne, calmly: “they have sold themselves.”

“It’s no good, my Buckling,” said the King, rolling about on his chair. “You can’t cope with this chivalrous eloquence. You can’t cope with an artist. You can’t cope with the humorist of Notting Hill. Oh, *Nunc dimittis*—that I have lived to see this day! Provost Wayne, you stand firm?”

“Let them wait and see,” said Wayne. “If I stood firm before, do you think I shall weaken now that I have seen the face of the King? For I fight for something greater, if greater there can be, than the hearthstones of my people and the Lordship of the Lion. I fight for your royal vision, for the great dream you dreamt of the League of the Free Cities. You have given me this liberty. If I had been a beggar and you had flung me a coin, if I had been a peasant in a dance and you had flung me a favour, do you think I would have let it be taken by any ruffians on the road? This leadership and liberty of Notting Hill is a gift from your Majesty, and if it is taken from me, by God! it shall be taken in battle, and the noise of that battle shall be heard in the flats of Chelsea and in the studios of St. John’s Wood.”

===Chinese===
"为的是诺丁山这座城，"韦恩骄傲地答道，"泵街是它鲜活而欢欣的一部分。"

"算不上多大部分，"詹姆斯·巴克轻蔑地说。

"富人既起意来抢，"韦恩昂起头说，"那它对穷人而言，便已大得足堪一守。"

国王拍着双腿，把两脚在空中挥舞了一阵。

"诺丁山每一位体面人，"巴克以他冷硬粗厉的声音插进来，"都站在我们这边，反对你。我在诺丁山有的是朋友。"

"您的朋友，是那些拿了您的金币、出卖别人家炉边石的人，巴克大人，"韦恩区督说。"他们自然是您的朋友，我深信不疑。"

"反正他们没卖过脏玩具，"巴克短促地笑了笑。

"他们卖过更脏的东西，"韦恩平静地说："他们出卖了自己。"

"没用的，我的小巴克，"国王在椅子上滚来滚去地说。"你斗不过这种骑士风度的雄辩。你斗不过一个艺术家。你斗不过诺丁山的这位幽默家。哦，*Nunc dimittis*（如今可请你的仆人安然去世）——我居然活到了看见这一天的份上！韦恩区督，您立场坚定？"

"让他们等着瞧吧，"韦恩说。"若我从前立场坚定，您以为如今我既已见过国王的容颜，反而会软弱下来吗？因为我为之而战的，倘若能有更伟大者的话，是比我人民的炉边石、比雄狮之领有权更伟大的东西。我为您的王者洞见而战，为您所做的那场自由诸城同盟（the League of the Free Cities）的大梦而战。您已赐给我这份自由。倘若我本是个乞丐，而您掷给我一枚硬币；倘若我本是个跳舞的农人，而您抛给我一件信物——您以为我会任由路上的任何恶棍把它夺走吗？这诺丁山的领袖地位与自由，是陛下您所赐的礼物；若有人要从我手中夺走，凭上帝起誓！那必是在战场上夺走，而那一战的厮杀声，必将传到切尔西的公寓，传到圣约翰伍德的画室。"

===Original===
“It is too much—it is too much,” said the King. “Nature is weak. I must speak to you, brother artist, without further disguise. Let me ask you a solemn question. Adam Wayne, Lord High Provost of Notting Hill, don’t you think it splendid?”

“Splendid!” cried Adam Wayne. “It has the splendour of God.”

“Bowled out again,” said the King. “You will keep up the pose. Funnily, of course, it is serious. But seriously, isn’t it funny?”

“What?” asked Wayne, with the eyes of a baby.

“Hang it all, don’t play any more. The whole business—the Charter of the Cities. Isn’t it immense?”

“Immense is no unworthy word for that glorious design.”

“Oh, hang you! But, of course, I see. You want me to clear the room of these reasonable sows. You want the two humorists alone together. Leave us, gentlemen.”

Buck threw a sour look at Barker, and at a sullen signal the whole pageant of blue and green, of red, gold, and purple, rolled out of the room, leaving only two in the great hall, the King sitting in his seat on the dais, and the red-clad figure still kneeling on the floor before his fallen sword.

The King bounded down the steps and smacked Provost Wayne on the back.

“Before the stars were made,” he cried, “we were made for each other. It is too beautiful. Think of the valiant independence of Pump Street. That is the real thing. It is the deification of the ludicrous.”

The kneeling figure sprang to his feet with a fierce stagger.

“Ludicrous!” he cried, with a fiery face.

“Oh, come, come,” said the King, impatiently, “you needn’t keep it up with me. The augurs must wink sometimes from sheer fatigue of the eyelids. Let us enjoy this for half an hour, not as actors, but as dramatic critics. Isn’t it a joke?”

===Chinese===
"太过分了——太过分了，"国王说。"人的天性是软弱的。我必须跟您说话了，艺术家兄弟，不再伪装下去。让我郑重地问您一个问题。亚当·韦恩，诺丁山高级区督（Lord High Provost of Notting Hill），您不觉得它壮丽吗？"

"壮丽！"亚当·韦恩叫道。"它有着上帝的壮丽。"

"又被将死了，"国王说。"您是要把这场戏演到底。当然，有趣的地方在于，它是当真的。可是说正经的，它难道不好笑吗？"

"什么？"韦恩问，睁着一双婴孩般的眼睛。

"嗨，别再演了。整桩事——城市宪章。它岂不宏伟？"

"宏伟一词，配那辉煌的设计，并不辱没。"

"哦，你这可恶的家伙！不过，我当然明白。你是要我把这屋里这些讲理的蠢猪都清出去。你是想让两位幽默家单独待在一起。诸位先生，告退吧。"

巴克朝詹姆斯·巴克投去一道酸溜溜的目光，随着一声闷闷的暗号，蓝与绿、红、金与紫的整支仪仗队滚滚退出大厅，只留下两个人留在空旷的厅里——国王坐在御座台的座上，那身红衣的身影仍跪在地板上，伏在他落下的剑前。

国王蹦下台阶，拍拍韦恩区督的背。

"在群星被造出之前，"他叫道，"我们便是为彼此而造的。这太美了。想想泵街那英勇的独立精神吧。那才是真东西。那是荒诞的神化。"

跪着的人影猛地一晃，跳将起来。

"荒诞！"他叫道，脸涨得通红。

"哦，得了，得了，"国王不耐烦地说，"跟我何必还撑着。占卜官们单单因为眼皮累得慌，也总得有时彼此递个眼色。咱们且以半个钟头享受这件事，不当演员，当戏剧评论家。它难道不好笑吗？"

===Original===
Adam Wayne looked down like a boy, and answered in a constrained voice—

“I do not understand your Majesty. I cannot believe that while I fight for your royal charter your Majesty deserts me for these dogs of the gold hunt.”

“Oh, damn your—But what’s this? What the devil’s this?”

The King stared into the young Provost’s face, and in the twilight of the room began to see that his face was quite white and his lip shaking.

“What in God’s name is the matter?” cried Auberon, holding his wrist.

Wayne flung back his face, and the tears were shining on it.

“I am only a boy,” he said, “but it’s true. I would paint the Red Lion on my shield if I had only my blood.”

King Auberon dropped the hand and stood without stirring, thunderstruck.

“My God in Heaven!” he said; “is it possible that there is within the four seas of Britain a man who takes Notting Hill seriously?”

“And my God in Heaven!” said Wayne passionately; “is it possible that there is within the four seas of Britain a man who does not take it seriously?”

The King said nothing, but merely went back up the steps of the dais, like a man dazed. He fell back in his chair again and kicked his heels.

“If this sort of thing is to go on,” he said weakly, “I shall begin to doubt the superiority of art to life. In Heaven’s name, do not play with me. Do you really mean that you are—God help me!—a Notting Hill patriot; that you are—?”

===Chinese===
亚当·韦恩像孩子一样垂下眼睛，以一种压抑的声音答道——

"我不明白陛下的意思。我无法相信，当我在为您的王者宪章而战时，陛下竟然为这群逐金的走狗而弃我不顾。"

"哦，去你的——可这是什么？这到底是怎么回事？"

国王盯着这位年轻区督的脸，在房间昏暗的光线里，他渐渐看出对方的脸煞白、嘴唇发抖。

"以上帝之名，到底怎么了？"奥伯伦抓住他的手腕叫道。

韦恩仰起脸，脸上泪光闪闪。

"我不过是个孩子，"他说，"可这是真的。倘若只凭我的一腔血，我也会把红狮（the Red Lion）绘上我的盾。"

奥伯伦国王松开手，一动不动地站着，如遭雷击。

"老天爷啊！"他说，"莫非在不列颠四海之内，竟有一个把诺丁山当真的人？"

"而我的老天爷啊！"韦恩激昂地说，"莫非在不列颠四海之内，竟有一个不把它当真的人？"

国王什么也没说，只是像发懵的人一样，走回御座台的台阶上去。他又瘫进椅子，蹬着双脚。

"要是这种事还要继续下去，"他有气无力地说，"我就要开始怀疑艺术比生活优越了。看在老天份上，别耍我。你是当真的吗——你真的是——老天救我！——一个诺丁山爱国者；你真的是——？"

===Original===
Wayne made a violent gesture, and the King soothed him wildly.

“All right—all right—I see you are; but let me take it in. You do really propose to fight these modern improvers with their boards and inspectors and surveyors and all the rest of it?”

“Are they so terrible?” asked Wayne, scornfully.

The King continued to stare at him as if he were a human curiosity.

“And I suppose,” he said, “that you think that the dentists and small tradesmen and maiden ladies who inhabit Notting Hill, will rally with war-hymns to your standard?”

“If they have blood they will,” said the Provost.

“And I suppose,” said the King, with his head back among the cushions, “that it never crossed your mind that”—his voice seemed to lose itself luxuriantly—“never crossed your mind that anyone ever thought that the idea of a Notting Hill idealism was—er—slightly—slightly ridiculous?”

“Of course they think so,” said Wayne. “What was the meaning of mocking the prophets?”

“Where,” asked the King, leaning forward—“where in Heaven’s name did you get this miraculously inane idea?”

“You have been my tutor, Sire,” said the Provost, “in all that is high and honourable.”

“Eh?” said the King.

“It was your Majesty who first stirred my dim patriotism into flame. Ten years ago, when I was a boy (I am only nineteen), I was playing on the slope of Pump Street, with a wooden sword and a paper helmet, dreaming of great wars. In an angry trance I struck out with my sword, and stood petrified, for I saw that I had struck you, Sire, my King, as you wandered in a noble secrecy, watching over your people’s welfare. But I need have had no fear. Then was I taught to understand Kingliness. You neither shrank nor frowned. You summoned no guards. You invoked no punishments. But in august and burning words, which are written in my soul, never to be erased, you told me ever to turn my sword against the enemies of my inviolate city. Like a priest pointing to the altar, you pointed to the hill of Notting. ‘So long,’ you said, ‘as you are ready to die for the sacred mountain, even if it were ringed with all the armies of Bayswater.’ I have not forgotten the words, and I have reason now to remember them, for the hour is come and the crown of your prophecy. The sacred hill is ringed with the armies of Bayswater, and I am ready to die.”

===Chinese===
韦恩做了一个激烈的手势，国王手忙脚乱地安抚他。

"好好——好好——我看出你是了；可让我消化一下。你当真打算同这些现代改良家——连同他们的委员会、督察员、测量员以及诸如此类——干一仗？"

"他们有那么可怕吗？"韦恩轻蔑地问。

国王继续盯着他，仿佛他是一件人类奇观。

"那么我猜，"他说，"你以为，住在诺丁山的那些牙医、小生意人、老小姐们，会唱着战歌聚集到你的旗帜下来？"

"只要他们身上还有血，就会，"区督说。

"我还猜，"国王把头仰进靠垫里说，"你从来没想过"——他的声音似乎沉醉地消散在半途——"从来没想过，竟会有人觉得，所谓'诺丁山理想主义'这个念头——呃——稍微——稍微有点儿可笑？"

"他们当然这么觉得，"韦恩说。"不然，嘲弄先知们，又是何意？"

"看在老天份上，"国王倾身向前问，"你这套荒唐得堪称奇迹的想法，到底从何而来？"

"陛下一直是我的导师，"区督说，"教给我一切崇高而可敬的事物。"

"啊？"国王说。

"正是陛下，最先把我那蒙昧的爱国之心搅成了一团烈火。十年前，我还是个孩子时（我如今才十九岁），我在泵街的坡地上玩耍，手持一把木剑、戴着一顶纸盔，梦着伟大的战争。我在一阵激愤的恍惚里挥剑击出，随即吓得呆立不动——因为我看见自己击中的竟是您，陛下，我的国王，您正以高贵的隐密之姿，踱步巡视您子民的福祉。可我其实无须害怕。就在那一刻，我领教了何谓王者之风。您既未退缩，也未蹙眉。您既未唤卫士，也未施惩罚。您只是以庄严而炽热的言辞——那些话已刻入我的灵魂，永不可磨——叮嘱我，永远把剑指向我那不可侵犯的城池之敌。您像一位祭司指着祭坛那样，指着诺丁山那座山。'只要，'您说，'你随时准备为这座圣山赴死，哪怕它被贝斯沃特的所有大军围困。'我没有忘记这些话，而我现在有理由记起它们，因为时辰已到，您的预言将要加冕。圣山已被贝斯沃特的大军围困，而我已准备好赴死。"

===Original===
The King was lying back in his chair, a kind of wreck.

“Oh, Lord, Lord, Lord,” he murmured, “what a life! what a life! All my work! I seem to have done it all. So you’re the red-haired boy that hit me in the waistcoat. What have I done? God, what have I done? I thought I would have a joke, and I have created a passion. I tried to compose a burlesque, and it seems to be turning halfway through into an epic. What is to be done with such a world? In the Lord’s name, wasn’t the joke broad and bold enough? I abandoned my subtle humour to amuse you, and I seem to have brought tears to your eyes. What’s to be done with people when you write a pantomime for them—call the sausages classic festoons, and the policeman cut in two a tragedy of public duty? But why am I talking? Why am I asking questions of a nice young gentleman who is totally mad? What is the good of it? What is the good of anything? Oh, Lord! Oh, Lord!”

Suddenly he pulled himself upright.

“Don’t you really think the sacred Notting Hill at all absurd?”

“Absurd?” asked Wayne, blankly. “Why should I?”

The King stared back equally blankly.

“I beg your pardon,” he said.

“Notting Hill,” said the Provost, simply, “is a rise or high ground of the common earth, on which men have built houses to live, in which they are born, fall in love, pray, marry, and die. Why should I think it absurd?”

The King smiled.

“Because, my Leonidas—” he began, then suddenly, he knew not how, found his mind was a total blank. After all, why was it absurd? Why was it absurd? He felt as if the floor of his mind had given way. He felt as all men feel when their first principles are hit hard with a question. Barker always felt so when the King said, “Why trouble about politics?”

===Chinese===
国王瘫在椅子里，一副破船似的样儿。

"哦，主啊，主啊，主啊，"他喃喃道，"这是什么样的一生！什么样的一生！我全部的功夫！我似乎全做完了。原来你就是那个用木剑戳中我马甲的红发小子。我都干了些什么？老天，我都干了些什么？我本想开个玩笑，却造出了一段激情。我本想编一出滑稽戏，可它演到一半，竟似乎正变成一部史诗。这样的一个世界，拿它怎么办？看在主的份上，难道这玩笑还不够粗豪、不够响亮吗？我舍弃了我那精微的幽默来逗你，结果似乎倒让你热泪盈眶。给人们写一出哑剧——把香肠称作古典花彩，把警察被劈成两半称作公仆悲剧——拿这样的人怎么办？可我还说什么呢？我干吗去问一个完全疯了的好好年轻绅士这些问题？说它有何用？什么又有何用？哦，主啊！哦，主啊！"

忽然他挺直了身子。

"你真的一点儿都不觉得神圣的诺丁山可笑？"

"可笑？"韦恩茫然问。"我凭什么要觉得？"

国王同样茫然地回望着他。

"请您原谅，"他说。

"诺丁山，"区督朴素地说，"是这平凡大地上的一处高地，人们在上面盖了房子住，他们在那里出生、相恋、祈祷、婚嫁、死去。我凭什么要觉得它可笑？"

国王笑了。

"因为，我的列奥尼达（Leonidas）——"他开口，接着忽然不知怎地，发现自己的脑子一片空白。说到底，它为什么可笑？它为什么可笑？他觉得自己脑中的地板仿佛整个塌了下去。他感受到所有人在自己的第一原理被一个问题重重击中时的那种感受。詹姆斯·巴克总会有这种感受——每当国王说："何必操心政治？"

===Original===
The King’s thoughts were in a kind of rout; he could not collect them.

“It is generally felt to be a little funny,” he said vaguely.

“I suppose,” said Adam, turning on him with a fierce suddenness—“I suppose you fancy crucifixion was a serious affair?”

“Well, I—” began Auberon—“I admit I have generally thought it had its graver side.”

“Then you are wrong,” said Wayne, with incredible violence. “Crucifixion is comic. It is exquisitely diverting. It was an absurd and obscene kind of impaling reserved for people who were made to be laughed at—for slaves and provincials, for dentists and small tradesmen, as you would say. I have seen the grotesque gallows-shape, which the little Roman gutter-boys scribbled on walls as a vulgar joke, blazing on the pinnacles of the temples of the world. And shall I turn back?”

The King made no answer.

Adam went on, his voice ringing in the roof.

“This laughter with which men tyrannise is not the great power you think it. Peter was crucified, and crucified head downwards. What could be funnier than the idea of a respectable old Apostle upside down? What could be more in the style of your modern humour? But what was the good of it? Upside down or right side up, Peter was Peter to mankind. Upside down he still hangs over Europe, and millions move and breathe only in the life of his Church.”

King Auberon got up absently.

“There is something in what you say,” he said. “You seem to have been thinking, young man.”

“Only feeling, sire,” answered the Provost. “I was born, like other men, in a spot of the earth which I loved because I had played boys’ games there, and fallen in love, and talked with my friends through nights that were nights of the gods. And I feel the riddle. These little gardens where we told our loves. These streets where we brought out our dead. Why should they be commonplace? Why should they be absurd? Why should it be grotesque to say that a pillar-box is poetic when for a year I could not see a red pillar-box against the yellow evening in a certain street without being wracked with something of which God keeps the secret, but which is stronger than sorrow or joy? Why should anyone be able to raise a laugh by saying ‘the Cause of Notting Hill’?—Notting Hill where thousands of immortal spirits blaze with alternate hope and fear.”

===Chinese===
国王的思绪一片溃乱；他理不出个头绪来。

"通常都觉得这事儿有点儿滑稽，"他含糊地说。

"我猜，"亚当带着凶猛的突如其来转向他——"我猜你也认为钉十字架是桩严肃的事儿？"

"这个，我嘛——"奥伯伦开口道——"我承认我向来以为它有它严肃的一面。"

"那你就错了，"韦恩以一种难以置信的激烈说。"钉十字架是滑稽的。它精妙得令人发噱。它是一种荒唐而猥亵的穿刺之刑，专为那些生来给人取笑的人而设——为奴隶和乡下人，正如你所言，为牙医和小生意人。我亲眼见过那怪诞的绞架形状——那形状本是罗马街头的小顽童在墙上信手涂鸦当作下流笑话的——如今在全世界庙宇的尖顶上熊熊燃烧。难道我要退缩吗？"

国王没有作答。

亚当继续说，他的声音在屋顶下回响。

"这种人类借以施暴的笑声，并不是你以为的那种大力量。彼得（Peter）被钉了十字架，而且是倒钉的。还有什么比'一位体面的老使徒头朝下'这念头更滑稽的呢？还有什么更合你们现代幽默的口味呢？可这又有什么用？头朝下也好，头朝上也好，对人类而言，彼得终究是彼得。头朝下，他至今仍高悬于欧洲上空，千千万万人只在教会的生命里才动、才呼吸。"

奥伯伦国王心不在焉地站起身。

"你说的话有几分道理，"他说，"你似乎动过脑筋，年轻人。"

"不过是凭感觉罢了，陛下，"区督答道。"我同别人一样，出生在大地上某一个地点，我爱它，因为我在那里玩过孩童的游戏，在那里坠入爱河，在那里与友人彻夜长谈——那样的夜，是诸神之夜。我感受着这个谜。这些小小的花园，我们在其中倾诉过爱情。这些街道，我们从上面抬出过我们的死者。它们为什么就该平庸？为什么就该可笑？凭什么说一根邮筒不富诗意就是荒诞——曾有整整一年，每当我看见一只红色邮筒映着某条街上黄色的傍晚，我便被某种比悲伤或欢乐更有力的东西攫住，那力量，其奥秘只归上帝所知？凭什么有人一说出'诺丁山的事业'，就能博得一阵哄笑？——那诺丁山，有成千上万不灭的灵魂，在希望与恐惧的交替中熊熊燃烧。"

===Original===
Auberon was flicking dust off his sleeve with quite a new seriousness on his face, distinct from the owlish solemnity which was the pose of his humour.

“It is very difficult,” he said at last. “It is a damned difficult thing. I see what you mean; I agree with you even up to a point—or I should like to agree with you, if I were young enough to be a prophet and poet. I feel a truth in everything you say until you come to the words ‘Notting Hill.’ And then I regret to say that the old Adam awakes roaring with laughter and makes short work of the new Adam, whose name is Wayne.”

For the first time Provost Wayne was silent, and stood gazing dreamily at the floor. Evening was closing in, and the room had grown darker.

“I know,” he said, in a strange, almost sleepy voice, “there is truth in what you say, too. It is hard not to laugh at the common names—I only say we should not. I have thought of a remedy; but such thoughts are rather terrible.”

“What thoughts?” asked Auberon.

The Provost of Notting Hill seemed to have fallen into a kind of trance; in his eyes was an elvish light.

“I know of a magic wand, but it is a wand that only one or two may rightly use, and only seldom. It is a fairy wand of great fear, stronger than those who use it—often frightful, often wicked to use. But whatever is touched with it is never again wholly common; whatever is touched with it takes a magic from outside the world. If I touch, with this fairy wand, the railways and the roads of Notting Hill, men will love them, and be afraid of them forever.”

“What the devil are you talking about?” asked the King.

“It has made mean landscapes magnificent, and hovels outlast cathedrals,” went on the madman. “Why should it not make lampposts fairer than Greek lamps; and an omnibus-ride like a painted ship? The touch of it is the finger of a strange perfection.”

“What is your wand?” cried the King, impatiently.

“There it is,” said Wayne; and pointed to the floor, where his sword lay flat and shining.

“The sword!” cried the King; and sprang up straight on the dais.

“Yes, yes,” cried Wayne, hoarsely. “The things touched by that are not vulgar; the things touched by that—”

King Auberon made a gesture of horror.

“You will shed blood for that!” he cried. “For a cursed point of view—”

===Chinese===
奥伯伦正弹着袖上的灰尘，脸上有一种全然新的郑重，与他那种作为幽默姿态的猫头鹰式庄重截然不同。

"这事很难，"他终于说。"这是桩难得要命的难事。我明白你的意思；我甚至在某种程度上赞同你——或者说，倘若我还年轻得足以做个先知和诗人，我会乐意赞同你。你说的一切我都感到其中有个真理，直到你吐出'诺丁山'这几个字。于是我很遗憾地告诉你：那个'旧亚当'（the old Adam）便醒了过来，哄堂大笑，三下两下便收拾掉那个名叫韦恩的'新亚当'（the new Adam）。"

韦恩区督第一次沉默下来，出神地凝望着地板。暮色四合，房间已暗了许多。

"我知道，"他以一种古怪的、近乎梦呓的声音说，"你说的话里也有真理。要不在那些寻常的名字前发笑，的确很难——我只不过说，我们不该笑。我想过一种解药；可这样的念头相当可怕。"

"什么念头？"奥伯伦问。

诺丁山区督似乎陷入了一种恍惚；他眼中有一种精灵般的光。

"我知道一根魔杖，可这根杖，只有一两个人能正当使用，且只偶尔可用。它是一根出于大恐惧的仙杖，比用它的人更强——常常骇人，用起来常常邪恶。但凡被它触过的，便永不再全然寻常；但凡被它触过的，便染上了一种来自这世界之外的魔力。倘若我用这根仙杖，触一触诺丁山的铁路与公路，人们便会爱上它们，并且永远畏惧它们。"

"你到底在胡扯些什么？"国王问。

"它曾使卑微的风景变得壮丽，使茅舍比大教堂更经久，"这疯子继续说。"它为何不能让路灯比希腊的灯盏更美；让一趟公共马车之行像一艘画中的船？它的触碰，是一种古怪完美的手指。"

"你的杖是什么？"国王不耐烦地叫道。

"就在那儿，"韦恩说；指向地板——他的剑平平地躺在那里，寒光闪闪。

"剑！"国王叫道；在御座台上笔直地跳了起来。

"是，是，"韦恩嘶哑地叫道。"被它触过的东西便不俗；被它触过的东西——"

奥伯伦国王做了一个惊骇的手势。

"你要为它流血！"他叫道。"为一种该死的主张——"

===Original===
“Oh, you kings, you kings!” cried out Adam, in a burst of scorn. “How humane you are, how tender, how considerate! You will make war for a frontier, or the imports of a foreign harbour; you will shed blood for the precise duty on lace, or the salute to an admiral. But for the things that make life itself worthy or miserable—how humane you are! I say here, and I know well what I speak of, there were never any necessary wars but the religious wars.
