There were never any just wars but the religious wars. There were never any humane wars but the religious wars. For these men were fighting for something that claimed, at least, to be the happiness of a man, the virtue of a man. A Crusader thought, at least, that Islam hurt the soul of every man, king or tinker, that it could really capture. I think Buck and Barker and these rich vultures hurt the soul of every man, hurt every inch of the ground, hurt every brick of the houses, that they can really capture. Do you think I have no right to fight for Notting Hill, you whose English government has so often fought for tomfooleries? If, as your rich friends say, there are no gods, and the skies are dark above us, what should a man fight for, but the place where he had the Eden of childhood and the short heaven of first love? If no temples and no scriptures are sacred, what is sacred if a man’s own youth is not sacred?”

The King walked a little restlessly up and down the dais.

“It is hard,” he said, biting his lips, “to assent to a view so desperate—so responsible.…”

===Chinese===
"哦，你们这些国王，你们这些国王！"亚当在一阵轻蔑的迸发中高喊。"你们多么人道，多么温柔，多么体恤！你们会为一条边界、为一个外国港口的进口货开战；你们会为精确的蕾丝税、或向一位海军将领鸣放的礼炮而流血。可对于决定生活本身是值得一过还是令人绝望的那些东西——你们多么人道！我在此断言，而且我深知自己说的是什么：除了宗教战争，从来没有过任何必要的战争。除了宗教战争，从来没有过任何正义的战争。除了宗教战争，从来没有过任何人道的战争。因为这些人为之而战的，至少自称是一个人的幸福、一个人的德行。一个十字军（Crusader）至少认为，伊斯兰（Islam）伤害了它所能真正攫获的每一个人的灵魂，无论他是国王还是补锅匠。而我认为，巴克和詹姆斯·巴克以及这些富有的秃鹫，伤害了他们所能真正攫获的每一个人的灵魂，伤害了每一寸土地，伤害了房子的每一块砖。你们英国政府曾常常为蠢事开战——你以为我就没有为诺丁山而战的权利吗？倘若如你的富朋友们所言，并没有什么神，我们头顶的天空一片漆黑，那么一个人除了他曾度过童年伊甸园和初恋那短短天堂的地方，还该为什么而战？倘若没有圣殿、没有经文是神圣的，那么一个人自己的青春若不神圣，还有什么神圣？"

国王在御座台上不安地来回踱了几步。

"很难，"他咬着嘴唇说，"很难赞同一种如此绝望——如此担当重大的观点……"

===Original===
As he spoke, the door of the audience chamber fell ajar, and through the aperture came, like the sudden chatter of a bird, the high, nasal, but well-bred voice of Barker.

“I said to him quite plainly—the public interests—”

Auberon turned on Wayne with violence.

“What the devil is all this? What am I saying? What are you saying? Have you hypnotised me? Curse your uncanny blue eyes! Let me go. Give me back my sense of humour. Give it me back—give it me back, I say!”

“I solemnly assure you,” said Wayne, uneasily, with a gesture, as if feeling all over himself, “that I haven’t got it.”

The King fell back in his chair, and went into a roar of Rabelaisian laughter.

“I don’t think you have,” he cried.

===Chinese===
正说着，觐见厅的门开了一道缝，从门缝里传来詹姆斯·巴克那高亢、带鼻音、却颇有教养的声音，像一只鸟突然的叽喳。

"我跟他讲得明明白白——公众利益——"

奥伯伦猛地转向韦恩。

"这到底都是怎么回事？我在说什么？你在说什么？你把我催眠了吗？诅咒你那双邪门的蓝眼睛！放我走。把我的幽默感还给我。还给我——还给我，我说！"

"我郑重向您保证，"韦恩局促地说，做一个仿佛在全身摸索的手势，"我并没有拿到它。"

国王瘫回椅子里，爆发出一阵拉伯雷式（Rabelaisian）的大笑。

"我看你确实没有，"他叫道。

## Book III / 第三卷

## I. The Mental Condition of Adam Wayne / 第一章 亚当·韦恩的精神状态

===Original===
A little while after the King's accession a small book of poems appeared, called *Hymns on the Hill*. They were not good poems, nor was the book successful, but it attracted a certain amount of attention from one particular school of critics. The King himself, who was a member of the school, reviewed it in his capacity of literary critic to *Straight from the Stables*, a sporting journal. They were known as the Hammock School, because it had been calculated malignantly by an enemy that no less than thirteen of their delicate criticisms had begun with the words, "I read this book in a hammock: half asleep in the sleepy sunlight, I…"; after that there were important differences. Under these conditions they liked everything, but especially everything silly. "Next to authentic goodness in a book," they said—"next to authentic goodness in a book (and that, alas! we never find) we desire a rich badness." Thus it happened that their praise (as indicating the presence of a rich badness) was not universally sought after, and authors became a little disquieted when they found the eye of the Hammock School fixed upon them with peculiar favour.

===Chinese===
国王登基后不久，有一本薄薄的诗集问世，书名《山丘颂歌》（*Hymns on the Hill*）。诗写得并不好，书也不畅销，却引起了某一派批评家特别的关注。国王本人正是这一派的成员，他以《马厩快讯》（*Straight from the Stables*，一份赛马杂志）文学评论员的身份，写了一篇书评。这一派人称"吊床学派"（the Hammock School），因为有个心怀恶意的仇人曾精细地统计过：他们那些纤巧的评论，开篇之语竟有不下十三篇是"我在一张吊床上读到这本书：在令人昏昏欲睡的阳光里半睡着，我……"——此后的内容则有重大的不同。在这种情形下，他们对一切都欣赏，尤其欣赏一切蠢物。"在一本书里，"他们说——"在一本书里，仅次于真正的良善（唉，这良善我们可从找不到！）的，我们渴望一种浓郁的拙劣。"于是他们的赞许（因其意味着浓郁拙劣的存在）并非人尽想望；而作家们一旦发觉吊床学派那双眼睛格外垂青地盯住了自己，多少有些不安。

===Original===
The peculiarity of *Hymns on the Hill* was the celebration of the poetry of London as distinct from the poetry of the country. This sentiment or affectation was, of course, not uncommon in the twentieth century, nor was it, although sometimes exaggerated, and sometimes artificial, by any means without a great truth at its root, for there is one respect in which a town must be more poetical than the country, since it is closer to the spirit of man; for London, if it be not one of the masterpieces of man, is at least one of his sins. A street is really more poetical than a meadow, because a street has a secret. A street is going somewhere, and a meadow nowhere. But, in the case of the book called *Hymns on the Hill*, there was another peculiarity, which the King pointed out with great acumen in his review. He was naturally interested in the matter, for he had himself published a volume of lyrics about London under his pseudonym of "Daisy Daydream."

===Chinese===
《山丘颂歌》的独特之处，在于它所歌咏的是伦敦的诗意，有别于乡野的诗意。这种情怀——或者说这种做作——在二十世纪当然并不罕见；它虽有时被夸大，有时显得矫揉，却绝非没有一条重大的真理作它的根基，因为在某一方面，一座城镇必然比乡野更有诗意，因为它更贴近人的精神；因为伦敦，倘若不算人类的杰作之一，至少也是人类的罪孽之一。一条街其实比一片草地更有诗意，因为街有一个秘密。街要往某处去，而草地哪儿也不去。不过，在《山丘颂歌》这本书上，还有另一种独特之处，是国王在他的书评里以极大的敏锐指出的。他自然对这题目格外上心，因为他自己就曾以"雏菊·白日梦"（Daisy Daydream）的笔名，出版过一本关于伦敦的抒情诗集。

===Original===
This difference, as the King pointed out, consisted in the fact that, while mere artificers like "Daisy Daydream" (on whose elaborate style the King, over his signature of "Thunderbolt," was perhaps somewhat too severe) thought to praise London by comparing it to the country—using nature, that is, as a background from which all poetical images had to be drawn—the more robust author of *Hymns on the Hill* praised the country, or nature, by comparing it to the town, and used the town itself as a background. "Take," said the critic, "the typically feminine lines, 'To the Inventor of The Hansom Cab'—

'Poet, whose cunning carved this amorous shell,
Where twain may dwell.'"

===Chinese===
这种不同，正如国王所指出的，在于：像"雏菊·白日梦"这样的纯然匠人（国王以其"雷霆"（Thunderbolt）的署名，对他那精雕细琢的文风或许略嫌严苛了些）想借比较伦敦与乡野来赞美伦敦——也就是说，把自然当作一切诗意意象必须从中抽取的背景——而《山丘颂歌》那位更粗健的作者，却借比较乡野或自然与城镇来赞美乡野或自然，把城镇本身当作背景。"试看，"批评家说，"那几行典型的女性笔触，《致汉瑟马车发明者》（To the Inventor of The Hansom Cab）——

'诗人啊，你以巧思雕出这多情的贝壳，
恰容两人栖息。'"

===Original===
"Surely," wrote the King, "no one but a woman could have written those lines. A woman has always a weakness for nature; with her art is only beautiful as an echo or shadow of it. She is praising the hansom cab by theme and theory, but her soul is still a child by the sea, picking up shells. She can never be utterly of the town, as a man can; indeed, do we not speak (with sacred propriety) of 'a man about town'? Who ever spoke of a woman about town? However much, physically, 'about town' a woman may be, she still models herself on nature; she tries to carry nature with her; she bids grasses to grow on her head, and furry beasts to bite her about the throat. In the heart of a dim city, she models her hat on a flaring cottage garden of flowers. We, with our nobler civic sentiment, model ours on a chimney pot; the ensign of civilisation. And rather than be without birds, she will commit massacre, that she may turn her head into a tree, with dead birds to sing on it."

===Chinese===
"毫无疑问，"国王写道，"这几行诗除了女人，没人写得出来。女人对自然总有一种偏爱；在她看来，艺术唯有作为自然的回声或影子才美。她在主题和理论上赞美汉瑟马车，可她的灵魂仍是海边拾贝的孩童。她永远不能像男人那样完完全全属于城镇；说真的，我们不是（怀着神圣的得体）说'一个混迹城中的男人'（a man about town）吗？谁又说过'一个混迹城中的女人'呢？一个女人无论在身体上多么'混迹城中'，她仍以自然为楷模；她设法把自然带在身上；她吩咐青草长在她头上，吩咐毛茸茸的兽类在她颈项边咬啮。在一座昏暗城市的中心，她照着一座灿烂村舍花园里的花来给自己的帽子造型。而我们，怀着更高尚的市民情感，照着烟囱管帽来给自己的帽子造型——那才是文明的旗帜。她宁可造下一场屠杀，也不肯没有鸟儿，好把她的头变成一棵树，让死鸟在树上歌唱。"

===Original===
This kind of thing went on for several pages, and then the critic remembered his subject, and returned to it.

"Poet, whose cunning carved this amorous shell,
Where twain may dwell."

===Chinese===
这一类话又接连写了好几页，然后批评家才想起自己的题目，又回到它上来。

"诗人啊，你以巧思雕出这多情的贝壳，
恰容两人栖息。"

===Original===
"The peculiarity of these fine though feminine lines," continued "Thunderbolt," "is, as we have said, that they praise the hansom cab by comparing it to the shell, to a natural thing. Now, hear the author of *Hymns on the Hill*, and how he deals with the same subject. In his fine nocturne, entitled 'The Last Omnibus,' he relieves the rich and poignant melancholy of the theme by a sudden sense of rushing at the end—

'The wind round the old street corner
Swung sudden and quick as a cab.'

"Here the distinction is obvious. 'Daisy Daydream' thinks it a great compliment to a hansom cab to be compared to one of the spiral chambers of the sea. And the author of *Hymns on the Hill* thinks it a great compliment to the immortal whirlwind to be compared to a hackney coach. He surely is the real admirer of London. We have no space to speak of all his perfect applications of the idea; of the poem in which, for instance, a lady's eyes are compared, not to stars, but to two perfect streetlamps guiding the wanderer. We have no space to speak of the fine lyric, recalling the Elizabethan spirit, in which the poet, instead of saying that the rose and the lily contend in her complexion, says, with a purer modernism, that the red omnibus of Hammersmith and the white omnibus of Fulham fight there for the mastery. How perfect the image of two contending omnibuses!"

===Chinese===
"这几行虽属女性笔触却颇为精妙的诗，"雷霆"继续写道，"其独特之处，正如我们所说，在于它们借比较贝壳、比较一件自然之物来赞美汉瑟马车。现在，且听听《山丘颂歌》的作者如何处理同一题目。在他那首优美的夜曲，题为《末班公共马车》（The Last Omnibus）中，他以结尾处一种骤然飞驰之感，缓解了主题那浓郁而锥心的忧郁——

'老街拐角处的风，
像马车一般骤然迅疾地一甩。'

"区别在这里再明显不过。'雏菊·白日梦'认为，把一辆汉瑟马车比作大海某个螺旋形的腔室，是对马车莫大的恭维。而《山丘颂歌》的作者认为，把不灭的旋风比作一辆出租马车，是对旋风莫大的恭维。他才是伦敦真正的爱慕者。我们没有篇幅来谈他把这一意念所作的一切完美运用——比如有一首诗里，一位女士的眼睛不是被比作星星，而是被比作两盏完美的路灯，为迷途者引路。我们也没有篇幅来谈那首优美的抒情诗，它令人想起伊丽莎白时代的精神——诗人不说玫瑰与百合在她的容颜上争辉，而是以一种更纯粹的现代笔调说，哈默史密斯的红色公共马车与富勒姆（Fulham）的白色公共马车在那里争雄。两辆公共马车相争的形象，何其完美！"

===Original===
Here, somewhat abruptly, the review concluded, probably because the King had to send off his copy at that moment, as he was in some want of money. But the King was a very good critic, whatever he may have been as King, and he had, to a considerable extent, hit the right nail on the head. *Hymns on the Hill* was not at all like the poems originally published in praise of the poetry of London. And the reason was that it was really written by a man who had seen nothing else but London, and who regarded it, therefore, as the universe. It was written by a raw, redheaded lad of seventeen, named Adam Wayne, who had been born in Notting Hill. An accident in his seventh year prevented his being taken away to the seaside, and thus his whole life had been passed in his own Pump Street, and in its neighbourhood. And the consequence was, that he saw the streetlamps as things quite as eternal as the stars; the two fires were mingled. He saw the houses as things enduring, like the mountains, and so he wrote about them as one would write about mountains. Nature puts on a disguise when she speaks to every man; to this man she put on the disguise of Notting Hill. Nature would mean to a poet born in the Cumberland hills, a stormy skyline and sudden rocks. Nature would mean to a poet born in the Essex flats, a waste of splendid waters and splendid sunsets. So nature meant to this man Wayne a line of violet roofs and lemon lamps, the chiaroscuro of the town. He did not think it clever or funny to praise the shadows and colours of the town; he had seen no other shadows or colours, and so he praised them—because they were shadows and colours. He saw all this because he was a poet, though in practice a bad poet. It is too often forgotten that just as a bad man is nevertheless a man, so a bad poet is nevertheless a poet.

===Chinese===
书评到此便有些突兀地收了尾，多半是因为国王此刻非得把稿子发出去不可——他正缺着钱花。但不论国王这一国之君当得如何，他确是一名极好的批评家，而且很大程度上是击中了要害的。《山丘颂歌》与最初那些为赞美伦敦诗意而发表的诗，完全不同。原因在于：它的作者其实是一个除了伦敦什么也没见过的人，于是便把伦敦当作了整个宇宙。它的作者是一个十七岁、毛头红发的少年，名叫亚当·韦恩，生于诺丁山。七岁那年的一场意外，使他没能被带去海边，于是他的一生都在自家的泵街（Pump Street）及其附近度过。结果是：他把路灯看作与星星一样永恒的事物；两团火光在他眼里融为了一体。他把房屋看作与群山一样经久的事物，于是他写房屋，就像别人写群山。自然对每一个说话的人都披上一件伪装；对这个人，她披上了诺丁山的伪装。对于一个生于坎伯兰群山（Cumberland hills）的诗人，自然意味着风狂的天际线和突兀的岩石。对于一个生于埃塞克斯滩地（Essex flats）的诗人，自然意味着一片壮阔的水域与壮阔的落日。而对于韦恩这个人，自然意味着一排紫罗兰色的屋顶和柠檬色的灯火，是城镇的明暗光影。他并不觉得赞美城镇的阴影与色彩有什么机巧或可笑之处；他没见过别的阴影与色彩，于是赞美它们——只因为它们是阴影与色彩。他看出这一切，因为他是个诗人，尽管实际上是个蹩脚的诗人。人们太容易忘记：正如一个坏人仍是人，一个坏诗人也仍是诗人。

===Original===
Mr. Wayne's little volume of verse was a complete failure; and he submitted to the decision of fate with a quite rational humility, went back to his work, which was that of a draper's assistant, and wrote no more. He still retained his feeling about the town of Notting Hill, because he could not possibly have any other feeling, because it was the back and base of his brain. But he does not seem to have made any particular attempt to express it or insist upon it.

===Chinese===
韦恩先生这本小诗集一败涂地；他以一种相当理性的谦卑服从了命运的判决，回到他的本行——布店伙计——再也没有写过诗。他对诺丁山这座城的那种感情仍然保留着，因为他不可能有别的感情，因为那是他大脑的脊背与根基。但他似乎并未特别努力去表达它或强调它。

===Original===
He was a genuine natural mystic, one of those who live on the border of fairyland. But he was perhaps the first to realise how often the boundary of fairyland runs through a crowded city. Twenty feet from him (for he was very shortsighted) the red and white and yellow suns of the gaslights thronged and melted into each other like an orchard of fiery trees, the beginning of the woods of elf-land.

===Chinese===
他是一个真正的、天生的神秘主义者，属于那种住在仙境边境上的人。但他或许是头一个认识到仙境的边界有多常穿过一座熙攘城市的人。在他二十英尺开外（因为他极度近视），煤气灯那红、白、黄三色的太阳簇拥着、彼此融汇，像一座火树的果园，那是精灵之林的开端。

===Original===
But, oddly enough, it was because he was a small poet that he came to his strange and isolated triumph. It was because he was a failure in literature that he became a portent in English history. He was one of those to whom nature has given the desire without the power of artistic expression. He had been a dumb poet from his cradle. He might have been so to his grave, and carried unuttered into the darkness a treasure of new and sensational song. But he was born under the lucky star of a single coincidence. He happened to be at the head of his dingy municipality at the time of the King's jest, at the time when all municipalities were suddenly commanded to break out into banners and flowers. Out of the long procession of the silent poets, who have been passing since the beginning of the world, this one man found himself in the midst of an heraldic vision, in which he could act and speak and live lyrically. While the author and the victims alike treated the whole matter as a silly public charade, this one man, by taking it seriously, sprang suddenly into a throne of artistic omnipotence. Armour, music, standards, watch-fires, the noise of drums, all the theatrical properties were thrown before him. This one poor rhymster, having burnt his own rhymes, began to live that life of open air and acted poetry of which all the poets of the Earth have dreamed in vain; the life for which the *Iliad* is only a cheap substitute.

===Chinese===
然而奇怪的是，正因为他是个小诗人，他才赢得了他那奇异而孤绝的胜利。正因为他在文学上一败涂地，他才成了英国历史上一个异数。他属于那种天生被赋予了艺术表达的渴望、却未被赋予表达之力的人。他从摇篮里便是个哑巴诗人。他本可能就这样一直哑到进坟墓，把一宗崭新而骇人的歌吟之宝，未经道出便带入黑暗。但他生在一颗单一巧合的幸运星下。在国王开那个玩笑的当口，在所有市府忽然奉命一举打出旌旗、绽出鲜花的当口，他恰巧坐在这座灰扑扑的市府的首席。自开天辟地以来便络绎行过的那一长列沉默的诗人当中，独独这一个人发觉自己置身于一个纹章的异象之中，在其中他可以行动、说话、如诗一般地生活。当作者和受害者们同样把整桩事当作一出无聊的公开字谜游戏之时，独独这一个人，因为认真以待，一跃而登上了艺术全能的宝座。铠甲、音乐、旌旗、篝火、鼓声，一切戏台上的行头全抛到了他面前。这个可怜的打油诗人，烧掉了自己的打油诗，便开始过起了一种露天的、搬演出来的诗的生活——世上所有诗人都曾徒然梦想过这种生活；而《伊利亚特》（*Iliad*）之于这种生活，不过是一种廉价的替代品。

===Original===
Upwards from his abstracted childhood, Adam Wayne had grown strongly and silently in a certain quality or capacity which is in modern cities almost entirely artificial, but which can be natural, and was primarily almost brutally natural in him, the quality or capacity of patriotism. It exists, like other virtues and vices, in a certain undiluted reality. It is not confused with all kinds of other things. A child speaking of his country or his village may make every mistake in Mandeville or tell every lie in Munchausen, but in his statement there will be no psychological lies any more than there can be in a good song. Adam Wayne, as a boy, had for his dull streets in Notting Hill the ultimate and ancient sentiment that went out to Athens or Jerusalem. He knew the secret of the passion, those secrets which make real old national songs sound so strange to our civilisation. He knew that real patriotism tends to sing about sorrows and forlorn hopes much more than about victory. He knew that in proper names themselves is half the poetry of all national poems. Above all, he knew the supreme psychological fact about patriotism, as certain in connection with it as that a fine shame comes to all lovers, the fact that the patriot never under any circumstances boasts of the largeness of his country, but always, and of necessity, boasts of the smallness of it.

===Chinese===
从他那出神的童年往上长，亚当·韦恩在某种品质或能力里强壮而沉默地成长起来——这种品质或能力在现代城市里几乎完全是人为的，但它可以是天然的，而在他身上起初几乎是蛮横地天然的——那就是爱国这种品质或能力。它和别种美德与恶行一样，以某种未掺水的真实存在着。它不与各种其他事物相混淆。一个孩子说起他的国、他的村，也许会犯尽曼德维尔（Mandeville）所能犯的每一处错误，讲尽明希豪森（Munchausen）所能讲的每一句谎话，但他的话里不会有任何心理上的谎言，一如一首好歌里不可能有。亚当·韦恩幼时，对他诺丁山那些沉闷的街巷怀有一种终极而古老的情感——那种曾涌向雅典或耶路撒冷的情感。他懂得这股激情的秘密，那些使真正的古老民族颂歌在我们的文明听来如此陌生的秘密。他懂得真正的爱国之心，倾向于歌咏苦难和孤注一掷的希望，远甚于歌咏胜利。他懂得在专有名词本身里，就藏着所有民族诗篇一半的诗意。最重要的是，他懂得关于爱国的一个至高的心理事实——这个事实与爱国之心的关联，恰如一种美好的羞怯之与一切恋人那般确凿——那就是：爱国者无论在何种境况下，都绝不夸耀他的国家之大，却总是、并且必然夸耀它的渺小。

===Original===
All this he knew, not because he was a philosopher or a genius, but because he was a child. Anyone who cares to walk up a side slum like Pump Street, can see a little Adam claiming to be king of a paving-stone. And he will always be proudest if the stone is almost too narrow for him to keep his feet inside it.

===Chinese===
这一切他都懂，并非因为他是个哲学家或天才，而是因为他是个孩子。谁只要肯走进泵街那样一条旁街陋巷，就能看见一个小亚当，自称是一块铺路石之王。而那块石头若几乎窄得他双脚踏不进去，他总是最为得意。

===Original===
It was while he was in such a dream of defensive battle, marking out some strip of street or fortress of steps as the limit of his haughty claim, that the King had met him, and, with a few words flung in mockery, ratified forever the strange boundaries of his soul. Thenceforward the fanciful idea of the defence of Notting Hill in war became to him a thing as solid as eating or drinking or lighting a pipe. He disposed his meals for it, altered his plans for it, lay awake in the night and went over it again. Two or three shops were to him an arsenal; an area was to him a moat; corners of balconies and turns of stone steps were points for the location of a culverin or an archer. It is almost impossible to convey to any ordinary imagination the degree to which he had transmitted the leaden London landscape to a romantic gold. The process began almost in babyhood, and became habitual like a literal madness. It was felt most keenly at night, when London is really herself, when her lights shine in the dark like the eyes of innumerable cats, and the outline of the dark houses has the bold simplicity of blue hills. But for him the night revealed instead of concealing, and he read all the blank hours of morning and afternoon, by a contradictory phrase, in the light of that darkness. To this man, at any rate, the inconceivable had happened. The artificial city had become to him nature, and he felt the curbstones and gas-lamps as things as ancient as the sky.

===Chinese===
正当他沉湎于这样一场防御战的梦境、把某一段街或某一座台阶的堡垒划作他那骄矜主张的边界之时，国王遇见了他，并用几句讥嘲的话，永远批准了他灵魂的那些奇异疆界。从此，在战争中保卫诺丁山这个异想天开的念头，对他便成了一件与吃、喝、点烟斗一样实在的事。他为它安排三餐，为它改动计划，夜里睡不着便把它从头到尾再过一遍。两三间铺子在他就是一座军械库；一块下陷的宅前空地在他就是一道护城河；阳台的犄角和石阶的拐弯，是部署一门蛇炮或一名弓箭手的据点。他那灰铅一般的伦敦风景，被他点化为浪漫的黄金——其程度之深，几乎无法传达给任何寻常的想象力。这过程几乎从婴孩时便开始了，业已像一种名副其实的癫狂一般习以为常。夜间感受最深，那时伦敦才真正是她自己，她的灯火在暗中闪烁，像无数只猫的眼睛，黑暗房屋的轮廓有着蓝色山峦般大胆的简朴。但对他而言，黑夜不是遮掩而是揭示，他用一个矛盾的说法——凭那黑暗里的光，去读尽清晨与午后一切空白的时辰。无论如何，对这个人，不可思议的事已然发生。这座人为的城市对他成了自然，他感到那些路沿石和煤气灯，是和天空一样古老的事物。

===Original===
One instance may suffice. Walking along Pump Street with a friend, he said, as he gazed dreamily at the iron fence of a little front garden, "How those railings stir one's blood!"

His friend, who was also a great intellectual admirer, looked at them painfully, but without any particular emotion. He was so troubled about it that he went back quite a large number of times on quiet evenings and stared at the railings, waiting for something to happen to his blood, but without success. At last he took refuge in asking Wayne himself. He discovered that the ecstasy lay in the one point he had never noticed about the railings even after his six visits—the fact that they were, like the great majority of others—in London, shaped at the top after the manner of a spear. As a child, Wayne had half unconsciously compared them with the spears in pictures of Lancelot and St. George, and had grown up under the shadow of the graphic association. Now, whenever he looked at them, they were simply the serried weapons that made a hedge of steel round the sacred homes of Notting Hill. He could not have cleansed his mind of that meaning even if he tried. It was not a fanciful comparison, or anything like it. It would not have been true to say that the familiar railings reminded him of spears; it would have been far truer to say that the familiar spears occasionally reminded him of railings.

===Chinese===
举一个例子便够了。他和一位朋友沿着泵街走，他梦一般地凝望着一座前院小花园的铁栅栏，说："那些栅栏真叫人热血沸腾啊！"

他那位朋友也是个在智识上极崇拜他的人，苦楚地望着那些栅栏，却没有任何特别的情绪。他为此十分苦恼，在安静的晚上折回去看了好多次，盯着栅栏，等着自己热血发生点什么，却毫无结果。最后他只好去问韦恩本人。他发现那阵出神，在于他跑了六趟都未曾注意到的栅栏的一点——那就是：栅栏的顶端，和伦敦绝大多数栅栏一样，被做成了矛头的形状。孩提时，韦恩曾半无意识地把它们与画里兰斯洛特（Lancelot）和圣乔治（St. George）所执的长矛相比，便在这种图形的联想之荫下长大。如今他每逢望去，它们便只是一排密密匝匝的兵器，在诺丁山那些神圣的宅邸四围筑成一道钢铁的篱笆。他就是想洗去这层含义也洗不掉。那不是一种异想天开的比方，或任何类似的东西。若说这些熟悉的栅栏令他想起长矛，那并不确切；远更确切的说法是：那些熟悉的长矛，偶尔才令他想起栅栏。

===Original===
A couple of days after his interview with the King, Adam Wayne was pacing like a caged lion in front of five shops that occupied the upper end of the disputed street. They were a grocer's, a chemist's, a barber's, an old curiosity shop and a toyshop that sold also newspapers. It was these five shops which his childish fastidiousness had first selected as the essentials of the Notting Hill campaign, the citadel of the city. If Notting Hill was the heart of the universe, and Pump Street was the heart of Notting Hill, this was the heart of Pump Street. The fact that they were all small and side by side realised that feeling for a formidable comfort and compactness which, as we have said, was the heart of his patriotism, and of all patriotism. The grocer (who had a wine and spirit licence) was included because he could provision the garrison; the old curiosity shop because it contained enough swords, pistols, partisans, crossbows, and blunderbusses to arm a whole irregular regiment; the toy and paper shop because Wayne thought a free press an essential centre for the soul of Pump Street; the chemist's to cope with outbreaks of disease among the besieged; and the barber's because it was in the middle of all the rest, and the barber's son was an intimate friend and spiritual affinity.

===Chinese===
与国王那次会面之后又过了两三天，亚当·韦恩像一头困在笼中的狮子，在那条有争议的街道上首的五间铺子前来回踱步。这五间铺子是一家杂货铺、一家药铺、一家理发店、一家旧货古玩铺，还有一家兼卖报纸的玩具店。正是这五间铺子，他那孩童式的挑剔头一个挑出来，选作诺丁山一役的要害、这座城的堡垒。如果诺丁山是宇宙的心脏，而泵街是诺丁山的心脏，那么这里便是泵街的心脏。它们全都小小的、紧挨在一起——这一事实正实现了那种令人望而生畏的安逸与紧凑之感，正如我们所说，那是他的爱国之心、也是一切爱国之心的核心。杂货商（领有酒类牌照）被纳入，是因为他能给守军供应粮草；旧货古玩铺被纳入，是因为里面藏有足够的剑、手枪、戟、弩和老式大口径短枪，足以武装一整支非正规军团；玩具兼纸笔铺被纳入，是因为韦恩认为自由的报业是泵街灵魂的一个必要中枢；药铺被纳入，是为应付被围者中间爆发的疾病；而理发店被纳入，是因为它正好位于其余各店的正中，而且理发师的儿子是他一个至交，更是精神上的同道。

===Original===
It was a cloudless October evening settling down through purple into pure silver around the roofs and chimneys of the steep little street, which looked black and sharp and dramatic. In the deep shadows the gas-lit shop fronts gleamed like five fires in a row, and before them, darkly outlined like a ghost against some purgatorial furnaces, passed to and fro the tall birdlike figure and eagle nose of Adam Wayne.

===Chinese===
那是个万里无云的十月黄昏，在陡峭小街的屋顶和烟囱四周，暮色由紫渐沉为纯银，使这条街显得漆黑、锐利、富于戏剧意味。幽深的暗影里，煤气灯照亮的店面像一排五堆篝火那样闪着光；在它们前面，像一个幽灵映在某些炼狱火炉前那样的暗黑剪影，来回踱着的，是亚当·韦恩那高挑的、鸟一般的身影和鹰钩鼻。

===Original===
He swung his stick restlessly, and seemed fitfully talking to himself.

"There are, after all, enigmas," he said, "even to the man who has faith. There are doubts that remain even after the true philosophy is completed in every rung and rivet. And here is one of them. Is the normal human need, the normal human condition, higher or lower than those special states of the soul which call out a doubtful and dangerous glory? those special powers of knowledge or sacrifice which are made possible only by the existence of evil? Which should come first to our affections, the enduring sanities of peace or the half-maniacal virtues of battle? Which should come first, the man great in the daily round or the man great in emergency? Which should come first, to return to the enigma before me, the grocer or the chemist? Which is more certainly the stay of the city, the swift chivalrous chemist or the benignant all-providing grocer? In such ultimate spiritual doubts it is only possible to choose a side by the higher instincts, and to abide the issue. In any case, I have made my choice. May I be pardoned if I choose wrongly, but I choose the grocer."

===Chinese===
他焦躁地挥着手杖，似乎在断断续续地自言自语。

"归根结底，终究是有谜的，"他说，"即便对有信仰的人，也是有谜的。即便真正的哲学已在每一级阶梯、每一颗铆钉上完工，仍有些疑窦挥之不去。眼前便有一个。寻常的人之所需、寻常的人之处境，究竟高于还是低于那些灵魂的特殊状态——那些惟有借恶之存在才成为可能、唤起一种可疑而危险之荣光的知识或牺牲的特殊力量？哪一样更该先得我们钟情——和平那持久的清明理智，还是战斗那半疯半癫的美德？哪一样更该居先——在日常轮回中伟大的人，还是在危难中伟大的人？回到我面前的这个谜——哪一样更该居先，杂货商还是药剂师？谁更确凿地是这座城的支柱——是那迅捷、侠义的药剂师，还是那仁厚、无所不供的杂货商？在这种终极的精神疑虑里，惟有凭更高的本能去择边，并听凭结局的裁判。无论如何，我已作出选择。倘若我选错了，愿我蒙受宽恕，但我选择杂货商。"

===Original===
"Good morning, sir," said the grocer, who was a middle-aged man, partially bald, with harsh red whiskers and beard, and forehead lined with all the cares of the small tradesman. "What can I do for you, sir?"

Wayne removed his hat on entering the shop, with a ceremonious gesture, which, slight as it was, made the tradesman eye him with the beginnings of wonder.

"I come, sir," he said soberly, "to appeal to your patriotism."

"Why, sir," said the grocer, "that sounds like the times when I was a boy and we used to have elections."

"You will have them again," said Wayne, firmly, "and far greater things. Listen, Mr. Mead. I know the temptations which a grocer has to a too cosmopolitan philosophy. I can imagine what it must be to sit all day as you do surrounded with wares from all the ends of the earth, from strange seas that we have never sailed and strange forests that we could not even picture. No Eastern king ever had such argosies or such cargoes coming from the sunrise and the sunset, and Solomon in all his glory was not enriched like one of you. India is at your elbow," he cried, lifting his voice and pointing his stick at a drawer of rice, the grocer making a movement of some alarm, "China is before you, Demerara is behind you, America is above your head, and at this very moment, like some old Spanish admiral, you hold Tunis in your hands."

===Chinese===
"早上好，先生，"杂货商说。他是中年人，有些秃顶，生着粗糙的红络腮胡和红胡须，额上刻满小生意人一切操劳的皱纹。"我能为您效劳吗，先生？"

韦恩进店时摘下帽子，动作一丝不苟；这动作虽轻，却使那生意人以一种初萌的惊奇打量起他来。

"我前来，先生，"他郑重地说，"是要唤起您的爱国之心。"

"嗳，先生，"杂货商说，"这听起来倒像我小时候那些日子，我们那时还搞选举呢。"

"你们会再搞的，"韦恩坚定地说，"而且会搞远更重大的事。听着，米德先生。我懂得一个杂货商所面临的诱惑——一种过于世界主义的哲学。我能想象得到，整日坐在那儿，像您这样被天涯海角来的货物所环绕——那些货物来自我们从未航行过的陌生海洋、来自我们连想都想象不出的陌生森林，那是怎样一种情形。任何一位东方的国王都不曾拥有过这样的船队、这样的船货，从日出之地和日落之地源源而来；盛极一时的所罗门（Solomon），其富庶也比不上你们当中随便哪一位。印度就在您的肘边，"他提高嗓门，用手杖直指一抽屉大米——杂货商惊得动了动——"中国在您面前，德梅拉拉（Demerara）在您身后，美利坚在您头顶，而此时此刻，您像一个老西班牙的海军上将一般，把突尼斯（Tunis）攥在手里。"

===Original===
Mr. Mead dropped the box of dates which he was just lifting, and then picked it up again vaguely.

Wayne went on with a heightened colour, but a lowered voice,

"I know, I say, the temptations of so international, so universal a vision of wealth. I know that it must be your danger not to fall like many tradesmen into too dusty and mechanical a narrowness, but rather to be too broad, to be too general, too liberal. If a narrow nationalism be the danger of the pastrycook, who makes his own wares under his own heavens, no less is cosmopolitanism the danger of the grocer. But I come to you in the name of that patriotism which no wanderings or enlightenments should ever wholly extinguish, and I ask you to remember Notting Hill. For, after all, in this cosmopolitan magnificence, she has played no small part. Your dates may come from the tall palms of Barbary, your sugar from the strange islands of the tropics, your tea from the secret villages of the Empire of the Dragon. That this room might be furnished, forests may have been spoiled under the Southern Cross, and leviathans speared under the Polar Star. But you yourself—surely no inconsiderable treasure—you yourself, the brain that wields these vast interests—you yourself, at least, have grown to strength and wisdom between these grey houses and under this rainy sky. This city which made you, and thus made your fortunes, is threatened with war. Come forth and tell to the ends of the earth this lesson. Oil is from the North and fruits from the South; rices are from India and spices from Ceylon; sheep are from New Zealand and men from Notting Hill."

The grocer sat for some little while, with dim eyes and his mouth open, looking rather like a fish. Then he scratched the back of his head, and said nothing. Then he said—

"Anything out of the shop, sir?"

Wayne looked round in a dazed way. Seeing a pile of tins of pineapple chunks, he waved his stick generally towards them.

"Yes," he said; "I'll take those."

"All those, sir?" said the grocer, with greatly increased interest.

"Yes, yes; all those," replied Wayne, still a little bewildered, like a man splashed with cold water.

"Very good, sir; thank you, sir," said the grocer with animation. "You may count upon my patriotism, sir."

"I count upon it already," said Wayne, and passed out into the gathering night.

===Chinese===
米德先生正搬起一箱椰枣，手一松，掉了下去，然后又茫茫然把它捡起来。

韦恩脸上红晕更深，嗓门却压低了，继续说道：

"我知道，我说，如此国际、如此普世的一种财富之观，其所带来的诱惑。我知道您的危险，不在于像许多手艺人那样陷入一种太尘封、太机械的狭隘，而在于太宽、太泛、太博爱。如果说，对一位在自己这片天底下亲手自制糕点的糕点师而言，危险是狭隘的民族主义，那么对一位杂货商而言，危险便是世界主义。但我以那种爱国之心的名义来到您面前——任何漂泊、任何开明都不该把它完全扑灭——我请您记起诺丁山。因为，说到底，在这一派世界主义的辉煌里，她并非无足轻重。您的椰枣也许来自巴巴里（Barbary）的高大棕榈，您的糖来自热带的陌生岛屿，您的茶来自龙之帝国隐秘的村落。为了陈设这一间屋子，森林也许已在南十字星下被砍伐净尽，巨兽也许已在北极星下被矛刺捕杀。但您本人——无疑不是一笔微不足道的财宝——您本人，那个驾驭着这些庞大利害的头脑——至少您本人，是在这些灰房子之间、在这片多雨的天空之下，长成了力量与智慧的。这座造就了您、从而也造就了您家业的城，正受到战争的威胁。出来吧，向天涯海角宣告这一教训：油来自北方，果来自南方；米来自印度，香料来自锡兰（Ceylon）；羊来自新西兰，而人来自诺丁山。"

杂货商呆坐了好一会儿，两眼发直，嘴半张着，活像一条鱼。然后他挠了挠后脑勺，什么也没说。然后他说——

"先生，铺子里要点儿什么吗？"

韦恩茫然地四下张望。看见一堆菠萝罐头，他便拿手杖朝那堆东西泛泛地一挥。

"好，"他说，"那些我都要了。"

"全都要，先生？"杂货商说，兴趣顿时大增。

"是，是，全都要，"韦恩答道，仍有些发懵，像一个被泼了一脸冷水的人。

"好极了，先生；多谢您，先生，"杂货商眉飞色舞地说，"我的爱国之心，您尽可仰仗，先生。"

"我早已仰仗着它了，"韦恩说，便走入渐浓的夜色中。

===Original===
The grocer put the box of dates back in its place.

"What a nice fellow he is!" he said. "It's odd how often they are nice. Much nicer than those as are all right."

===Chinese===
杂货商把那箱椰枣放回原处。

"他这人可真好！"他说，"也怪，这种人往往都挺好。比那些个正经人可爱多了。"

===Original===
Meanwhile Adam Wayne stood outside the glowing chemist's shop, unmistakably wavering.

"What a weakness it is!" he muttered. "I have never got rid of it from childhood—the fear of this magic shop. The grocer is rich, he is romantic, he is poetical in the truest sense, but he is not—no, he is not supernatural. But the chemist! All the other shops stand in Notting Hill, but this stands in Elf-land. Look at those great burning bowls of colour. It must be from them that God paints the sunsets. It is superhuman, and the superhuman is all the more uncanny when it is beneficent. That is the root of the fear of God. I am afraid. But I must be a man and enter."

===Chinese===
与此同时，亚当·韦恩站在灯火通明的药铺门外，分明在踌躇。

"这是何等的软弱！"他喃喃道，"我从童年起就没摆脱过——对这间魔法铺子的恐惧。杂货商是富有的，他是浪漫的，他以最真切的意义而言是诗意的，但他不是——不，他不是超自然的。可药剂师呢！所有别的铺子都坐落在诺丁山，唯独这一间坐落在精灵之国。瞧那些燃烧着的、巨大的色彩之钵。上帝必定是从它们里面蘸取颜料，画下落日的。它是超乎人的，而超乎人的东西一旦仁慈，便愈发可怖。这正是畏惧上帝的根源。我害怕。但我必须做个男子汉，走进去。"

===Original===
He was a man, and entered. A short, dark young man was behind the counter with spectacles, and greeted him with a bright but entirely businesslike smile.

"A fine evening, sir," he said.

"Fine indeed, strange Father," said Adam, stretching his hands somewhat forward. "It is on such clear and mellow nights that your shop is most itself. Then they appear most perfect, those moons of green and gold and crimson, which from afar oft guide the pilgrim of pain and sickness to this house of merciful witchcraft."

===Chinese===
他是个男子汉，便走了进去。柜台后面是个戴眼镜的矮个黑发年轻人，以一个明朗却全然公事化的微笑迎接他。

"今儿晚上天真好，先生，"他说。

"的确好，奇异的父啊，"亚当说，把双手微微向前伸。"正是在这样澄澈温煦的夜里，您的铺子才最是它自己。那时它们显得最完美——那些绿色、金色、深红的月亮，从远处时常指引着痛苦与病患的朝圣者，走向这座慈悲的巫术之屋。"

===Original===
"Can I get you anything?" asked the chemist.

"Let me see," said Wayne, in a friendly but vague manner. "Let me have some sal-volatile."

"Eightpence, tenpence, or one and sixpence a bottle?" said the young man, genially.

"One and six—one and six," replied Wayne, with a wild submissiveness. "I come to ask you, Mr. Bowles, a terrible question."

He paused and collected himself.

"It is necessary," he muttered—"it is necessary to be tactful, and to suit the appeal to each profession in turn."

===Chinese===
"我能给您拿点什么吗？"药剂师问。

"让我瞧瞧，"韦恩说，态度友善却茫茫然。"给我来点儿嗅盐吧（sal-volatile）。"

"八便士、十便士，还是一先令六便士一瓶的？"年轻人和气地说。

"一先令六便士——一先令六便士，"韦恩答道，带着一种近乎狂热的顺从。"我来是要问您一个问题，鲍尔斯先生，一个可怕的问题。"

他顿了顿，定下神来。

"非得如此不可，"他喃喃道——"非得有手腕不可，得把这一番呼吁挨个儿地、按每种职业各各量身定做。"

===Original===
"I come," he resumed aloud, "to ask you a question which goes to the roots of your miraculous toils. Mr. Bowles, shall all this witchery cease?" And he waved his stick around the shop.

Meeting with no answer, he continued with animation—

"In Notting Hill we have felt to its core the elfish mystery of your profession. And now Notting Hill itself is threatened."

"Anything more, sir?" asked the chemist.

"Oh," said Wayne, somewhat disturbed—"oh, what is it chemists sell? Quinine, I think. Thank you. Shall it be destroyed? I have met these men of Bayswater and North Kensington—Mr. Bowles, they are materialists. They see no witchery in your work, even when it is wrought within their own borders. They think the chemist is commonplace. They think him human."

===Chinese===
"我来，"他提高嗓门继续说，"是要问您一个问题，一个直抵您那奇迹般劳作之根本的问题。鲍尔斯先生，这一切巫术，难道要让它终止吗？"他一边说，一边拿手杖朝铺子里四下一挥。

不见回答，他又兴致勃勃地接着说——

"在诺丁山，我们触及了您这一行那精灵般的神秘之核。而如今，诺丁山本身正受到威胁。"

"还要别的吗，先生？"药剂师问。

"哦，"韦恩说，有些不安——"哦，药剂师卖的是什么来着？奎宁，我想是。谢谢。难道要让这一切被毁掉吗？我见过贝斯沃特和北肯辛顿那些人——鲍尔斯先生，他们是唯物主义者。他们看不见您这行当里的巫术，哪怕这巫术就在他们自家的地界里施展。他们认为药剂师是寻常的。他们认为他是人。"

===Original===
The chemist appeared to pause, only a moment, to take in the insult, and immediately said—

"And the next article, please?"

"Alum," said the Provost, wildly. "I resume. It is in this sacred town alone that your priesthood is reverenced. Therefore, when you fight for us you fight not only for yourself, but for everything you typify. You fight not only for Notting Hill, but for Fairyland, for as surely as Buck and Barker and such men hold sway, the sense of Fairyland in some strange manner diminishes."

===Chinese===
药剂师似乎只顿了片刻，好把这桩侮辱领受进去，随即说道——

"请问下一件要什么？"

"明矾，"区督狂乱地说，"我接着说。唯有在这座圣城里，您的祭司之职才受人敬重。因此，您为我们而战时，不单是为您自己而战，更是为您所象征的一切而战。您不单为诺丁山而战，更为仙境而战；因为巴克和詹姆斯·巴克以及这等人一旦得势，仙境之感便必以某种奇异的方式衰减。"

===Original===
"Anything more, sir?" asked Mr. Bowles, with unbroken cheerfulness.

"Oh yes, jujubes—Gregory powder—magnesia. The danger is imminent. In all this matter I have felt that I fought not merely for my own city (though to that I owe all my blood), but for all places in which these great ideas could prevail. I am fighting not merely for Notting Hill, but for Bayswater itself; for North Kensington itself. For if the gold-hunters prevail, these also will lose all their ancient sentiments and all the mystery of their national soul. I know I can count upon you."

"Oh yes, sir," said the chemist, with great animation; "we are always glad to oblige a good customer."

Adam Wayne went out of the shop with a deep sense of fulfilment of soul.

"It is so fortunate," he said, "to have tact, to be able to play upon the peculiar talents and specialities, the cosmopolitanism of the grocer and the world-old necromancy of the chemist. Where should I be without tact?"

===Chinese===
"还要别的吗，先生？"鲍尔斯先生问，欢快一如既往。

"哦，要，止咳糖——格雷戈里散——镁乳。危险迫在眉睫。在这整桩事里，我一直感到自己不单是为我自己的城而战（虽然我一身热血皆亏了它），更是为这些伟大观念所能盛行的所有地方而战。我不单为诺丁山而战，也为贝斯沃特本身而战；为北肯辛顿本身而战。因为倘若淘金者得势，这些地方也将尽失其古老的情怀、尽失其民族灵魂的全部神秘。我知道我可以仰仗您。"

"哦，那是，先生，"药剂师说，极为殷勤；"我们一向乐于为好主顾效劳。"

亚当·韦恩走出铺子，怀着一种灵魂深处的圆满之感。

"真幸运，"他说，"真幸运有这手腕，能拨动各人独特的才具与专长——杂货商的世界主义，药剂师那与世同古的巫术。没有这手腕，我还能成什么事呢？"

## II. The Remarkable Mr. Turnbull / 第二章 非凡的特恩布尔先生

===Original===
After two more interviews with shopmen, however, the patriot's confidence in his own psychological diplomacy began vaguely to wane. Despite the care with which he considered the peculiar rationale and the peculiar glory of each separate shop, there seemed to be something unresponsive about the shopmen. Whether it was a dark resentment against the uninitiate for peeping into their masonic magnificence, he could not quite conjecture.

===Chinese===
然而，与店主人又谈了两回之后，这位爱国者对自己那套心理外交的信心，便开始隐隐地衰退。尽管他煞费苦心地琢磨每一间铺子各自独特的道理与独特的荣光，店主人们似乎总有些油盐不进。这是否是一种阴暗的愤懑——愤懑于外行人窥探了他们那共济会般的堂皇——他也揣度不透。

===Original===
His conversation with the man who kept the shop of curiosities had begun encouragingly. The man who kept the shop of curiosities had, indeed, enchanted him with a phrase. He was standing drearily at the door of his shop, a wrinkled man with a grey pointed beard, evidently a gentleman who had come down in the world.

===Chinese===
他与那间古玩铺老板的攀谈，起初倒是颇有几分鼓舞。古玩铺的老板，确切地说，曾用一句话把他说得着了迷。那是个愁眉苦脸地站在店门口的人，满脸皱纹，一把灰色的尖须，分明是个家道中落了的体面人。

===Original===
"And how does your commerce go, you strange guardian of the past?" said Wayne, affably.

"Well, sir, not very well," replied the man, with that patient voice of his class which is one of the most heartbreaking things in the world. "Things are terribly quiet."

===Chinese===
"你这买卖可还好吗，你这往昔岁月的奇异守护者？"韦恩和气地说。

"唉，先生，不太好，"那人答道，带着他那阶层特有的耐烦声气——那是世上最令人心碎的事物之一。"生意冷清得可怕。"

===Original===
Wayne's eyes shone suddenly.

"A great saying," he said, "worthy of a man whose merchandise is human history. Terribly quiet; that is in two words the spirit of this age, as I have felt it from my cradle. I sometimes wondered how many other people felt the oppression of this union between quietude and terror. I see blank well-ordered streets and men in black moving about inoffensively, sullenly. It goes on day after day, day after day, and nothing happens; but to me it is like a dream from which I might wake screaming. To me the straightness of our life is the straightness of a thin cord stretched tight. Its stillness is terrible. It might snap with a noise like thunder. And you who sit, amid the debris of the great wars, you who sit, as it were, upon a battlefield, you know that war was less terrible than this evil peace; you know that the idle lads who carried those swords under Francis or Elizabeth, the rude Squire or Baron who swung that mace about in Picardy or Northumberland battles, may have been terribly noisy, but were not like us, terribly quiet."

===Chinese===
韦恩的眼里忽然亮了起来。

"一句了不起的话，"他说，"配得上一个以人类历史为货品的人。冷清得可怕——这几个字便是我们这个时代的精神，我从摇篮里便感到了它。我有时纳闷，还有多少人感到过这静寂与恐怖联姻所带来的压迫。我看见空空荡荡、井然有序的街道，看见黑衣人不动声色地、闷闷地走动。日复一日，日复一日，什么也不发生；可对我而言，这像是一场我会从中惊醒尖叫的梦。对我而言，我们生活的笔直，是一根绷紧的细绳的笔直。它的安静可怕得很。它也许会发出一声雷鸣般的响动而崩断。而你，坐在这历次大战的残骸之间，你，好比坐在一片战场上，你知道战争并不像这邪恶的和平这般可怕；你知道那些懒散的小伙子，曾在弗朗西斯（Francis）或伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）麾下执那些剑，那个曾在皮卡第（Picardy）或诺森伯兰（Northumberland）的战阵里抡起那根权杖的粗野乡绅或男爵，也许吵闹得可怕，却不像我们这般——冷清得可怕。"

===Original===
Whether it was a faint embarrassment of conscience as to the original source and date of the weapons referred to, or merely an engrained depression, the guardian of the past looked, if anything, a little more worried.

===Chinese===
不知是因为良心上对那些兵器的来历和年代有一丝尴尬，还是纯粹出于一种根深蒂固的消沉，这位往昔岁月的守护者，若说有什么变化的话，倒显得更愁了几分。

===Original===
"But I do not think," continued Wayne, "that this horrible silence of modernity will last, though I think for the present it will increase. What a farce is this modern liberality! Freedom of speech means practically, in our modern civilisation, that we must only talk about unimportant things. We must not talk about religion, for that is illiberal; we must not talk about bread and cheese, for that is talking shop; we must not talk about death, for that is depressing; we must not talk about birth, for that is indelicate. It cannot last. Something must break this strange indifference, this strange dreamy egoism, this strange loneliness of millions in a crowd. Something must break it. Why should it not be you and I? Can you do nothing else but guard relics?"

===Chinese===
"不过我倒不认为，"韦恩接着说，"现代这种骇人的沉默能持久，虽然我认为眼下它还会加重。所谓的现代自由，是何等的一出闹剧！言论自由，在我们现代文明里实际上意味着：我们只许谈些无关紧要的事。不许谈宗教，因为那不够宽容；不许谈面包和奶酪，因为那是三句不离本行；不许谈死亡，因为那令人沮丧；不许谈出生，因为那有伤风化。这局面长不了。总得有什么来打破这古怪的冷漠、这古怪的梦呓般的自我中心、这古怪的人潮之中千百万人的孤独。总得有什么来打破它。为什么这不能是你和我呢？难道你除了守着些遗物，就什么也干不了吗？"

===Original===
The shopman wore a gradually clearing expression, which would have led those unsympathetic with the cause of the Red Lion to think that the last sentence was the only one to which he had attached any meaning.

===Chinese===
店主脸上的神情渐渐开朗起来——这份开朗，落在那些对红狮旗事业不表同情的人眼里，定会以为：刚才那一长篇话里，他听出意思来的，唯有最后一句。

===Original===
"I am rather old to go into a new business," he said, "and I don't quite know what to be, either."

===Chinese===
"我上年纪了，再干一行新营生嫌晚，"他说，"再说，我也不知道该改做什么才好。"

===Original===
"Why not," said Wayne, gently having reached the crisis of his delicate persuasion—"why not be a colonel?"

===Chinese===
"为什么不，"韦恩说，柔声到了他那番细腻游说的紧要关头——"为什么不当个上校呢？"

===Original===
It was at this point, in all probability, that the interview began to yield more disappointing results. The man appeared inclined at first to regard the suggestion of becoming a colonel as outside the sphere of immediate and relevant discussion. A long exposition of the inevitable war of independence, coupled with the purchase of a doubtful sixteenth-century sword for an exaggerated price, seemed to resettle matters. Wayne left the shop, however, somewhat infected with the melancholy of its owner.

===Chinese===
多半正是在这一刻，这场会面开始结出更叫人失望的果子。那人起初似乎倾向于把"当个上校"这个建议，视作不在眼下相关讨论范围之内的事。一通关于那场不可避免的独立战争的长篇大论，再加上以离谱的价钱买下一把来路可疑的十六世纪佩剑，似乎又把事情摆平了。然而韦恩走出店门时，多少也染上了店主人那股郁色。

===Original===
That melancholy was completed at the barber's.

===Chinese===
那股郁色，到了理发店便算补满了。

===Original===
"Shaving, sir?" inquired that artist from inside his shop.

"War!" replied Wayne, standing on the threshold.

===Chinese===
"刮脸吗，先生？"那位艺术家从店里探问。

"战争！"韦恩站在门槛上答道。

===Original===
"I beg your pardon," said the other, sharply.

"War!" said Wayne, warmly. "But not for anything inconsistent with the beautiful and the civilised arts. War for beauty. War for society. War for peace. A great chance is offered you of repelling that slander which, in defiance of the lives of so many artists, attributes poltroonery to those who beautify and polish the surface of our lives. Why should not hairdressers be heroes? Why should not—"

===Chinese===
"您说什么？"对方尖刻地说。

"战争！"韦恩热烈地说，"但不是为了任何与美和文明之艺相抵触的事。为美而战。为社交而战。为和平而战。有人诽谤那些美化、打磨我们生活表面的人，说他们是懦夫——这诽谤全然无视那么多艺术家的一生。眼下正有一个大好机会摆在你面前，叫你驳斥这诽谤。理发师为什么不能当英雄呢？为什么不能——"

===Original===
"Now, you get out," said the barber, irascibly. "We don't want any of your sort here. You get out."

===Chinese===
"去，你给我出去，"理发师勃然大怒地说，"我们这儿不要你这种人。你出去。"

===Original===
And he came forward with the desperate annoyance of a mild person when enraged.

===Chinese===
他走上前来，带着一个温吞人被惹恼时那种豁出去的恼怒。

===Original===
Adam Wayne laid his hand for a moment on the sword, then dropped it.

"Notting Hill," he said, "will need her bolder sons;" and he turned gloomily to the toyshop.

===Chinese===
亚当·韦恩的手在剑柄上放了一放，又松开了。

"诺丁山，"他说，"需要她更大胆的儿子们。"他闷闷地转身，朝那间玩具店走去。

===Original===
It was one of those queer little shops so constantly seen in the side streets of London, which must be called toyshops only because toys upon the whole predominate; for the remainder of goods seem to consist of almost everything else in the world—tobacco, exercise-books, sweet-stuff, novelettes, halfpenny paper clips, halfpenny pencil sharpeners, bootlaces, and cheap fireworks. It also sold newspapers, and a row of dirty-looking posters hung along the front of it.

===Chinese===
那是伦敦旁街里屡见不鲜的一种古怪小店，称之为玩具店，不过是因为玩具总算占了多数；至于其余的货色，似乎包罗了世上几乎一切别的东西——烟草、练习簿、糖果、廉价小说、半便士一只的回形针、半便士一只的削铅笔刀、鞋带，还有廉价的烟花。它也卖报纸，店门前挂着一排脏兮兮的招贴。

===Original===
"I am afraid," said Wayne, as he entered, "that I am not getting on with these tradesmen as I should. Is it that I have neglected to rise to the full meaning of their work? Is there some secret buried in each of these shops which no mere poet can discover?"

===Chinese===
"我怕，"韦恩走进店时说，"我跟这些生意人打交道，并不如我所愿的那样顺遂。是不是我没能领会他们营生的全部深意？是不是这些铺子里头，各各埋着一个纯粹的诗人发现不了的奥秘？"

===Original===
He stepped to the counter with a depression which he rapidly conquered as he addressed the man on the other side of it—a man of short stature, and hair prematurely white, and the look of a large baby.

"Sir," said Wayne, "I am going from house to house in this street of ours, seeking to stir up some sense of the danger which now threatens our city. Nowhere have I felt my duty so difficult as here. For the toyshop keeper has to do with all that remains to us of Eden before the first wars began. You sit here meditating continually upon the wants of that wonderful time when every staircase leads to the stars, and every garden-path to the other end of nowhere. Is it thoughtlessly, do you think, that I strike the dark old drum of peril in the paradise of children? But consider a moment; do not condemn me hastily. Even that paradise itself contains the rumour or beginning of that danger, just as the Eden that was made for perfection contained the terrible tree. For judge childhood, even by your own arsenal of its pleasures. You keep bricks; you make yourself thus, doubtless, the witness of the constructive instinct older than the destructive. You keep dolls; you make yourself the priest of that divine idolatry. You keep Noah's Arks; you perpetuate the memory of the salvation of all life as a precious, an irreplaceable thing. But do you keep only, sir, the symbols of this prehistoric sanity, this childish rationality of the earth? Do you not keep more terrible things? What are those boxes, seemingly of lead soldiers, that I see in that glass case? Are they not witnesses to that terror and beauty, that desire for a lovely death, which could not be excluded even from the immortality of Eden? Do not despise the lead soldiers, Mr. Turnbull."

===Chinese===
他快步走到柜台前，带着一股他迅速压下去的消沉，向柜台对面那人搭话——那人身材矮小，头发早早地白了，一副大婴孩的长相。

"先生，"韦恩说，"我正在我们这条街上一户一户地走，想激起人们对眼下威胁着我们这座城的危险之感。我哪儿也没觉得职责如此艰难过，唯独在这里。因为玩具店主所打交道的，是最初那几场战争开打之前、伊甸园留给我们的一切残余。你坐在这里，不住地思量着那段奇妙时光里的种种所需——那时每一段楼梯都通向群星，每一条花园小径都通向乌有的彼端。你以为，我在孩童的乐园里擂响那面古老而阴暗的危险之鼓，是漫不经心的吗？但请你略作思量，别忙着怪罪。即便是那个乐园本身，也包含着那危险的流言或开端，一如那为圆满而造的伊甸园里也曾有一棵可怖的树。试以你自己所备的、那座童年乐趣的兵器库来评判童年吧。你备着积木；于是你无疑便成了那种比破坏更古老的建造本能的见证人。你备着洋娃娃；于是你便成了那种神圣偶像崇拜的祭司。你备着诺亚方舟；于是你便传下了那样一种记忆——一切生灵的获救，乃是一件珍贵、不可替代之物。可是先生，你难道只备着这些史前清明、这大地童年般理性的象征吗？你难道不也备着更可怕的东西？我在那玻璃柜里看到的、那一盒盒像是铅制小兵的东西，是什么？它们岂不是那种恐怖与美、那种对一种壮丽之死的渴望的见证吗？这种渴望，就连伊甸园的不朽也无法把它排斥在外。不要轻看那些铅制小兵啊，特恩布尔先生。"

===Original===
"I don't," said Mr. Turnbull, of the toyshop, shortly, but with great emphasis.

===Chinese===
"我不轻看，"玩具店的特恩布尔先生简短地说，语气却极重。

===Original===
"I am glad to hear it," replied Wayne. "I confess that I feared for my military schemes the awful innocence of your profession. How, I thought to myself, will this man, used only to the wooden swords that give pleasure, think of the steel swords that give pain? But I am at least partly reassured. Your tone suggests to me that I have at least the entry of a gate of your fairyland—the gate through which the soldiers enter, for it cannot be denied—I ought, sir, no longer to deny, that it is of soldiers that I come to speak. Let your gentle employment make you merciful towards the troubles of the world. Let your own silvery experience tone down our sanguine sorrows. For there is war in Notting Hill."

===Chinese===
"听你这么说，我就放心了，"韦恩答道，"我承认，我曾为我那些军事方案担心过，怕的就是你这一行那种骇人的天真。我暗自思忖：这个人，一向只习惯给人快乐的木剑，会怎样看待那给人痛苦的钢剑呢？可我如今至少略略宽心。你这话的口气告诉我，我至少摸到了你那仙乡的一道门——士兵们从那道门进来，因为无可否认——先生，我也不该再否认——我来正是要谈谈士兵的事。愿你这温柔的营生，使你对世上的苦难心怀怜悯。愿你那段银铃般的经历，冲淡我们这些血色斑斓的悲哀。因为诺丁山要打仗了。"

===Original===
The little toyshop keeper sprang up suddenly, slapping his fat hands like two fans on the counter.

"War?" he cried. "Not really, sir? Is it true? Oh, what a joke! Oh, what a sight for sore eyes!"

===Chinese===
那矮小的玩具店主猛地跳起来，两只胖手像两把扇子似的拍在柜台上。

"打仗？"他叫道，"当真的，先生？这是真的？噢，妙极了！噢，这可叫我开了眼了！"

===Original===
Wayne was almost taken aback by this outburst.

"I am delighted," he stammered. "I had no notion—"

===Chinese===
韦恩几乎被这一阵迸发弄得一怔。

"我太高兴了，"他结结巴巴地说，"我没想到——"

===Original===
He sprang out of the way just in time to avoid Mr. Turnbull, who took a flying leap over the counter and dashed to the front of the shop.

===Chinese===
他赶紧闪开，恰好避开特恩布尔先生——后者一个飞跃跳过柜台，冲到店前头去了。

===Original===
"You look here, sir," he said; "you just look here."

He came back with two of the torn posters in his hand which were flapping outside his shop.

"Look at those, sir," he said, and flung them down on the counter.

===Chinese===
"你瞧这儿，先生，"他说，"你这就瞧这儿。"

他手里拿着两张破招贴走回来——那是挂在他店门外迎风翻飞的两张。

"瞧瞧这两张，先生，"他说着，把它们往柜台上一摔。

===Original===
Wayne bent over them, and read on one—

"Last fighting.

Reduction of the central Dervish city.

Remarkable, etc."

On the other he read—

"Last small republic annexed.

Nicaraguan capital surrenders after a month's fighting.

Great slaughter."

===Chinese===
韦恩俯下身，读到其中一张上写着——

"最后的战事。

攻陷中部德尔维希（Dervish）之城。

详情可观，等等。"

另一张上他读到——

"最后一个小共和国被吞并。

尼加拉瓜首都苦战一月后投降。

大肆杀戮。"

===Original===
Wayne bent over them again, evidently puzzled; then he looked at the dates. They were both dated in August fifteen years before.

"Why do you keep these old things?" he said, startled entirely out of his absurd tact of mysticism. "Why do you hang them outside your shop?"

"Because," said the other, simply, "they are the records of the last war. You mentioned war just now. It happens to be my hobby."

===Chinese===
韦恩又俯身看了看，分明是糊涂了；接着他去瞧那日期。两张都是十五年前八月的。

"你留着这些旧东西做什么？"他说，那副做作的神秘手腕全吓跑了。"你干吗把它们挂在你店门外？"

"因为，"那人简单地答道，"它们是上一场战争的记录。你刚才提到打仗。这恰好是我的爱好。"

===Original===
Wayne lifted his large blue eyes with an infantile wonder.

===Chinese===
韦恩抬起他那双蓝色的大眼睛，满是婴孩般的惊奇。

===Original===
"Come with me," said Turnbull, shortly, and led him into a parlour at the back of the shop.

===Chinese===
"跟我来，"特恩布尔简短地说，把他领进店后头一间起居室。

===Original===
In the centre of the parlour stood a large deal table. On it were set rows and rows of the tin and lead soldiers which were part of the shopkeeper's stock. The visitor would have thought nothing of it if it had not been for a certain odd grouping of them, which did not seem either entirely commercial or entirely haphazard.

"You are acquainted, no doubt," said Turnbull, turning his big eyes upon Wayne—"you are acquainted, no doubt, with the arrangement of the American and Nicaraguan troops in the last battle;" and he waved his hand towards the table.

===Chinese===
起居室正中摆着一张杉木大桌。桌上一排排立着锡制和铅制的小兵，那是店主存货的一部分。要不是这些小兵有一种古怪的布阵——既不全然像是在卖货，也不全然像是随手乱摆——客人本不会把它放在心上。

"想必你是熟悉的，"特恩布尔把那双大眼睛转向韦恩——"想必你熟悉上次那一仗里美国军队和尼加拉瓜军队的布阵吧。"他说着朝桌子一挥手。

===Original===
"I am afraid not," said Wayne. "I—"

"Ah! you were at that time occupied too much, perhaps, with the Dervish affair. You will find it in this corner." And he pointed to a part of the floor where there was another arrangement of children's soldiers grouped here and there.

===Chinese===
"怕是不熟悉，"韦恩说，"我——"

"啊！那时候你也许太忙于德尔维希那桩事了。你在这个角落里找得到。"他指了指地板上一处，那里另有一群群小兵东一堆西一堆地布着阵。

===Original===
"You seem," said Wayne, "to be interested in military matters."

"I am interested in nothing else," answered the toyshop keeper, simply.

===Chinese===
"看来，"韦恩说，"你对军事挺感兴趣。"

"我对别的一概不感兴趣，"玩具店主简单地答道。

===Original===
Wayne appeared convulsed with a singular, suppressed excitement.

"In that case," he said, "I may approach you with an unusual degree of confidence. Touching the matter of the defence of Notting Hill, I—"

===Chinese===
韦恩似乎被一种奇异的、压抑着的兴奋攫住了。

"既是如此，"他说，"我便可带着一种非同寻常的信心来接近你了。关于保卫诺丁山这件事，我——"

===Original===
"Defence of Notting Hill? Yes, sir. This way, sir," said Turnbull, with great perturbation. "Just step into this side room;" and he led Wayne into another apartment, in which the table was entirely covered with an arrangement of children's bricks. A second glance at it told Wayne that the bricks were arranged in the form of a precise and perfect plan of Notting Hill. "Sir," said Turnbull, impressively, "you have, by a kind of accident, hit upon the whole secret of my life. As a boy, I grew up among the last wars of the world, when Nicaragua was taken and the dervishes wiped out. And I adopted it as a hobby, sir, as you might adopt astronomy or bird-stuffing. I had no ill-will to anyone, but I was interested in war as a science, as a game. And suddenly I was bowled out. The big Powers of the world, having swallowed up all the small ones, came to that confounded agreement, and there was no more war. There was nothing more for me to do but to do what I do now—to read the old campaigns in dirty old newspapers, and to work them out with tin soldiers. One other thing had occurred to me. I thought it an amusing fancy to make a plan of how this district or ours ought to be defended if it were ever attacked. It seems to interest you too."

===Chinese===
"保卫诺丁山？是，先生。这边请，先生，"特恩布尔说，大为动容。"请到这间侧屋里来。"他把韦恩领进另一间屋子，屋里的桌上整个盖着一堆搭成阵势的儿童积木。再瞧一眼，韦恩便看出那些积木搭成的，是一幅精确而完美的诺丁山地图。"先生，"特恩布尔郑重地说，"你这是出于一种偶然，撞见了我一辈子的全部秘密。少年时，我在世上最后那几场战争中长大，那时尼加拉瓜被攻陷、德尔维希人被荡平。我便把打仗当作一种爱好，先生，就像你可能把天文学或鸟类标本制作当作爱好一样。我对谁都没有恶意，我只是把战争当作一门科学、一场游戏来喜爱。忽然间，我被淘汰了。世上几个大国把小国全吞了之后，订下了那个要命的协议，于是再也没有仗打了。我再没别的可做，只好做我现在做的这些——在脏兮兮的旧报纸里读那些旧战役，再用锡兵把它们推演出来。还有一件事，是我一直在做的。我把它当作一个有趣的念头：画出一张图，标明咱们这片地界倘若遭到攻击，本该怎样防守。看来这事你也感兴趣。"

===Original===
"If it were ever attacked," repeated Wayne, awed into an almost mechanical enunciation. "Mr. Turnbull, it is attacked. Thank Heaven, I am bringing to at least one human being the news that is at bottom the only good news to any son of Adam. Your life has not been useless. Your work has not been play. Now, when the hair is already grey on your head, Turnbull, you shall have your youth. God has not destroyed, He has only deferred it. Let us sit down here, and you shall explain to me this military map of Notting Hill. For you and I have to defend Notting Hill together."

===Chinese===
"倘若遭到攻击，"韦恩重复道，敬畏得几乎像机械般一字一顿。"特恩布尔先生，它遭到了攻击。感谢上苍，我总算带给至少一个人类这条消息——归根结底，这是亚当的任何儿子所能听到的唯一好消息。你这一生并没有白活。你这些活儿并不只是儿戏。如今，你头上的头发已花白了，特恩布尔，你却将重获你的青春。上帝没有毁灭它，只是把它推迟了。我们就在这儿坐下，你来给我讲解讲解这幅诺丁山的军事地图吧。因为你我得一道来保卫诺丁山。"

===Original===
Mr. Turnbull looked at the other for a moment, then hesitated, and then sat down beside the bricks and the stranger. He did not rise again for seven hours, when the dawn broke.

===Chinese===
特恩布尔先生看了对方一会儿，犹豫了一下，然后在积木旁、在那陌生人身边坐下。他这一坐便是七个钟头没有起来——直到天亮。

---

===Original===
The headquarters of Provost Adam Wayne and his Commander-in-Chief consisted of a small and somewhat unsuccessful milk-shop at the corner of Pump Street. The blank white morning had only just begun to break over the blank London buildings when Wayne and Turnbull were to be found seated in the cheerless and unswept shop. Wayne had something feminine in his character; he belonged to that class of persons who forget their meals when anything interesting is in hand. He had had nothing for sixteen hours but hurried glasses of milk, and, with a glass standing empty beside him, he was writing and sketching and dotting and crossing out with inconceivable rapidity with a pencil and a piece of paper. Turnbull was of that more masculine type in which a sense of responsibility increases the appetite, and with his sketch-map beside him he was dealing strenuously with a pile of sandwiches in a paper packet, and a tankard of ale from the tavern opposite, whose shutters had just been taken down. Neither of them spoke, and there was no sound in the living stillness except the scratching of Wayne's pencil and the squealing of an aimless-looking cat. At length Wayne broke the silence by saying—

"Seventeen pounds eight shillings and ninepence."

===Chinese===
亚当·韦恩区督和他的总司令的司令部，是泵街拐角上一家不大景气的牛奶小铺。苍白的清晨才刚刚开始在那一片苍白的伦敦楼群之上破晓，韦恩和特恩布尔已坐在那间既无暖意又未打扫的铺子里了。韦恩的性格里有些近乎女性的东西；他属于那种一遇上有趣的事便忘了吃饭的人。整整十六个钟头，他除了匆忙灌下几杯牛奶什么也没吃；此刻一只空杯搁在手边，他正用一支铅笔一张纸，以不可思议的速度写着、画着、点点戳戳、涂涂改改。特恩布尔则属于那种更男性化的类型——责任感一上来，胃口便大增——他手边摊着草图，正卖力地对付着一纸包三明治，还有一大杯从对面酒馆打来的麦芽酒——那酒馆的铺板刚刚卸下。两人都不说话，这片活生生的寂静里，惟有韦恩铅笔的沙沙声和一只漫无目的的猫的尖叫。终于，韦恩打破沉默，说道——

"十七镑八先令九便士。"

===Original===
Turnbull nodded and put his head in the tankard.

"That," said Wayne, "is not counting the five pounds you took yesterday. What did you do with it?"

"Ah, that is rather interesting!" replied Turnbull, with his mouth full. "I used that five pounds in a kindly and philanthropic act."

===Chinese===
特恩布尔点点头，把头埋进了酒杯里。

"那，"韦恩说，"还没算你昨天拿走的五镑呢。你拿它做什么了？"

"哈，这倒挺有意思！"特恩布尔嘴里塞得满满的答道，"我把那五镑用在一桩仁慈而乐善好施的义举上了。"

===Original===
Wayne was gazing with mystification in his queer and innocent eyes.

"I used that five pounds," continued the other, "in giving no less than forty little London boys rides in hansom cabs."

===Chinese===
韦恩正用他那双古怪而天真的眼睛，一脸困惑地望着他。

"我把那五镑，"对方继续说，"拿来给不下四十个伦敦小男孩，一人坐了一趟双轮马车（hansom cab）。"

===Original===
"Are you insane?" asked the Provost.

"It is only my light touch," returned Turnbull. "These hansom-cab rides will raise the tone—raise the tone, my dear fellow—of our London youths, widen their horizon, brace their nervous system, make them acquainted with the various public monuments of our great city. Education, Wayne, education. How many excellent thinkers have pointed out that political reform is useless until we produce a cultured populace. So that twenty years hence, when these boys are grown up—"

===Chinese===
"你疯了不成？"区督问。

"这不过是我那举重若轻的手法罢了，"特恩布尔回答，"坐这些双轮马车，会提高——提高我们伦敦少年的格调，亲爱的老兄——会开阔他们的眼界，强健他们的神经系统，使他们结识咱们这座伟大城市里种种公共纪念碑。教育啊，韦恩，教育。有多少卓越的思想家指出过：在培养出有教养的民众之前，政治改革是没用的。是以二十年之后，等这些孩子长大了——"

===Original===
"Mad!" said Wayne, laying down his pencil; "and five pounds gone!"

"You are in error," explained Turnbull. "You grave creatures can never be brought to understand how much quicker work really goes with the assistance of nonsense and good meals. Stripped of its decorative beauties, my statement was strictly accurate. Last night I gave forty half-crowns to forty little boys, and sent them all over London to take hansom cabs. I told them in every case to tell the cabman to bring them to this spot. In half an hour from now the declaration of war will be posted up. At the same time the cabs will have begun to come in, you will have ordered out the guard, the little boys will drive up in state, we shall commandeer the horses for cavalry, use the cabs for barricade, and give the men the choice between serving in our ranks and detention in our basements and cellars. The little boys we can use as scouts. The main thing is that we start the war with an advantage unknown in all the other armies—horses. And now," he said, finishing his beer, "I will go and drill the troops."

===Chinese===
"疯了！"韦恩放下铅笔说，"五镑就这么没了！"

"你可想错了，"特恩布尔解释道，"你们这些一本正经的人，永远也没法明白：有了胡闹和美餐的襄助，事情办起来其实要快多少。剥去它那些华丽的修饰，我那番话可是字字确凿的。昨晚我给了四十个小男孩每人一枚半克朗，打发他们到伦敦各处去搭双轮马车。我嘱咐他们每一回都叫车夫把他们拉到这个地方来。再过半个钟头，宣战的布告就要贴出来了。与此同时，那些马车也将陆续赶到，你便会下令把卫队调出来，小男孩们排场十足地乘车赶到，我们便征用那些马来当骑兵，把马车拿来当街垒，并给那些车夫一个选择：要么入我们的伍，要么被关进咱们的地下室和地窖里。那些小男孩，我们可以派去当斥候。要紧的是：我们这场仗一开打，便拥有了一支别国军队全都不曾拥有的优势——马。"他说着，喝完了啤酒，"我现在去操练队伍。"

===Original===
And he walked out of the milk-shop, leaving the Provost staring.

===Chinese===
他走出牛奶铺，留下区督在那儿干瞪眼。

===Original===
A minute or two afterwards, the Provost laughed. He only laughed once or twice in his life, and then he did it in a queer way as if it were an art he had not mastered. Even he saw something funny in the preposterous coup of the half-crowns and the little boys. He did not see the monstrous absurdity of the whole policy and the whole war. He enjoyed it seriously as a crusade, that is, he enjoyed it far more than any joke can be enjoyed. Turnbull enjoyed it partly as a joke, even more perhaps as a reversion from the things he hated—modernity and monotony and civilisation. To break up the vast machinery of modern life and use the fragments as engines of war, to make the barricade of omnibuses and points of vantage of chimney-pots, was to him a game worth infinite risk and trouble. He had that rational and deliberate preference which will always to the end trouble the peace of the world, the rational and deliberate preference for a short life and a merry one.

===Chinese===
一两分钟之后，区督笑了。他这辈子统共只笑过一两次，而且笑得古怪，仿佛那是他尚未掌握的一门技艺。即便是他，也看出了半克朗和小男孩那桩荒唐妙计里好笑的地方。可他没看出整套方略、整场战争那骇人的荒谬。他一本正经地以一场十字军东征的心情享受着它，也就是说，他享受得远胜过任何笑话所能带来的享受。特恩布尔则部分地把它当作一个笑话来享受，也许更多地是当作一种对那些他所憎恨之物——现代性、单调、文明——的反拨来享受。把现代生活那庞大的机器拆散，拿碎片来当战争器械，用公共马车搭起街垒，把烟囱顶当作有利据点——在他看来，这是一场值得冒无穷风险、下无穷功夫的游戏。他怀着那种理性的、深思熟虑的偏好——这种偏好将永远搅扰这世界的太平，直到末日——那种理性而深思熟虑的偏好：宁可短命，也要快活。

## III. The Experiment of Mr. Buck / 第三章 巴克先生的实验

===Original===
An earnest and eloquent petition was sent up to the King signed with the names of Wilson, Barker, Buck, Swindon, and others. It urged that at the forthcoming conference to be held in his Majesty's presence touching the final disposition of the property in Pump Street, it might be held not inconsistent with political decorum and with the unutterable respect they entertained for his Majesty if they appeared in ordinary morning dress, without the costume decreed for them as Provosts. So it happened that the company appeared at that council in frock-coats and that the King himself limited his love of ceremony to appearing (after his not unusual manner), in evening dress with one order—in this case not the Garter, but the button of the Club of Old Clipper's Best Pals, a decoration obtained (with difficulty) from a halfpenny boy's paper. Thus also it happened that the only spot of colour in the room was Adam Wayne, who entered in great dignity with the great red robes and the great sword.

===Chinese===
一封恳切而雄辩的请愿书递到了国王面前，上面署着威尔逊、詹姆斯·巴克、巴克、斯温顿（Swindon）等人的名字。请愿书恳请：在即将于国王陛下御前召开、关乎泵街那处产业最终归属的会议上，倘若他们以寻常晨礼服现身、不穿身为区督所规定的那身行头，望陛下不致认为这有失政界礼数、也不致辜负他们对陛下那难以言宣的敬意。于是便有了这样的场面：与会诸公皆着双排扣长礼服出席此次会议；而国王本人也把他对仪典的偏爱，收敛为（照他并非罕见的做派）一身晚礼服加一枚勋章——此番不是嘉德勋章，而是"老克利珀铁哥们俱乐部"（the Club of Old Clipper's Best Pals）的徽章，那是一件（费了些劲才）从一份半便士男孩画报上弄来的装饰。也于是便有了这样一幕：满屋子里唯一的一抹亮色，便是亚当·韦恩——他仪态庄严地走了进来，披着大红的袍子，佩着那柄大剑。

===Original===
"We have met," said Auberon, "to decide the most arduous of modern problems. May we be successful." And he sat down gravely.

===Chinese===
"我们此番聚首，"奥伯伦说，"是要裁断现代最棘手的问题之一。愿我们马到成功。"说罢，他庄重地坐下。

===Original===
Buck turned his chair a little, and flung one leg over the other.

"Your Majesty," he said, quite good-humouredly, "there is only one thing I can't understand, and that is why this affair is not settled in five minutes. Here's a small property which is worth a thousand to us and is not worth a hundred to anyone else. We offer the thousand. It's not businesslike, I know, for we ought to get it for less, and it's not reasonable and it's not fair on us, but I'm damned if I can see why it's difficult."

===Chinese===
巴克把椅子转了转，翘起二郎腿。

"陛下，"他说，全无半点恶意，"只有一件事我闹不明白，那就是这桩事为何不五分钟便了结。这么一小处产业，对我们值一千镑，对旁人连一百镑都不值。我们出一千。我知道这不像做生意——本该花更少的钱拿到手；这既不合理，对我们也不公道；可我要是瞧得出这有什么难的，那才见鬼了。"

===Original===
"The difficulty may be very simply stated," said Wayne. "You may offer a million and it will be very difficult for you to get Pump Street."

===Chinese===
"这难处可以一句话说清，"韦恩说，"你们就是出一百万，要拿到泵街也照样难如登天。"

===Original===
"But look here, Mr. Wayne," cried Barker, striking in with a kind of cold excitement. "Just look here. You've no right to take up a position like that. You've a right to stand out for a bigger price, but you aren't doing that. You're refusing what you and every sane man knows to be a splendid offer simply from malice or spite—it must be malice or spite. And that kind of thing is really criminal; it's against the public good. The King's government would be justified in forcing you."

===Chinese===
"可是你瞧，韦恩先生，"詹姆斯·巴克嚷道，插了进来，带着一种冷冷的激动。"你就瞧这儿吧。你没权利摆出这么一副姿态。你有权为更高的价钱坚持，可你现在干的不是这个。你拒绝的，是你和每一个明白人都知道是个极优厚的条件——这纯粹是出于恶意或怨恨——准是恶意或怨恨。这种做法实属犯罪；它违背公共利益。国王的政府哪怕强行逼你就范，也是名正言顺的。"

===Original===
With his lean fingers spread on the table, he stared anxiously at Wayne's face, which did not move.

"In forcing you… it would," he repeated.

===Chinese===
他把瘦骨嶙峋的手指摊在桌上，焦灼地盯着韦恩的脸——那张脸纹丝不动。

"逼你就范……是名正言顺的。"他又重复了一遍。

===Original===
"It shall," said Buck, shortly, turning to the table with a jerk. "We have done our best to be decent."

===Chinese===
"一定得逼，"巴克简短地说，猛地转向桌子。"我们已经尽了力，把事做得体面了。"

===Original===
Wayne lifted his large eyes slowly.

"Was it my Lord Buck," he inquired, "who said that the King of England 'shall' do something?"

===Chinese===
韦恩缓缓抬起那双大眼睛。

"方才说英格兰国王'一定得'如何如何的，"他问，"可是巴克大人？"

===Original===
Buck flushed and said testily—

"I mean it must—it ought to. As I say, we've done our best to be generous; I defy anyone to deny it. As it is, Mr. Wayne, I don't want to say a word that's uncivil. I hope it's not uncivil to say that you can be, and ought to be, in gaol. It is criminal to stop public works for a whim. A man might as well burn ten thousand onions in his front garden or bring up his children to run naked in the street, as do what you say you have a right to do. People have been compelled to sell before now. The King could compel you, and I hope he will."

===Chinese===
巴克涨红了脸，没好气地说——

"我是说，必须——理当如此。我再说一遍，我们已经尽了力宽大为怀；谁要是否认，我倒要请教。如今这个地步，韦恩先生，我不想说一句不客气的话。但我说你这个人可以、也理应进监狱，希望这话不算不客气。为一时心血来潮便阻挠公共工程，这是犯罪。一个人若是做着你声称你有权去做的事，那他倒不如在自家前院烧掉一万颗洋葱，或者把孩子养成在街上赤身裸体地跑。从前也有过强令人变卖的先例。国王可以强逼你，我也盼望他会这么做。"

===Original===
"Until he does," said Wayne, calmly, "the power and government of this great nation is on my side and not yours, and I defy you to defy it."

===Chinese===
"在他这么做之前，"韦恩平静地说，"这个伟大国度的权柄与政府，是站在我这边、不站在你们那边的，我倒要看看你们敢不敢与它作对。"

===Original===
"In what sense," cried Barker, with his feverish eyes and hands, "is the government on your side?"

===Chinese===
"在哪一层意义上，"詹姆斯·巴克嚷道，那一双眼睛和一双手都发热似的，"政府是站在你那边的？"

===Original===
With one ringing movement Wayne unrolled a great parchment on the table. It was decorated down the sides with wild watercolour sketches of vestrymen in crowns and wreaths.

"The Charter of the Cities," he began.

===Chinese===
韦恩一个响亮的动作，在桌上展开了一大张羊皮纸。纸的两边饰有一气呵成的水彩画：戴着冠冕、戴着花环的教区委员们。

"城市宪章，"他开腔道。

===Original===
Buck exploded in a brutal oath and laughed.

"That tomfool's joke. Haven't we had enough—"

===Chinese===
巴克爆出一声粗野的咒骂，笑了起来。

"那套蠢人的玩笑。我们还没受够——"

===Original===
"And there you sit," cried Wayne, springing erect and with a voice like a trumpet, "with no argument but to insult the King before his face."

===Chinese===
"而你们就这么坐着，"韦恩一跃而起，声如号角，"除了当着国王的面侮辱国王，再拿不出半句道理来。"

===Original===
Buck rose also with blazing eyes.

"I am hard to bully," he began—and the slow tones of the King struck in with incomparable gravity—

===Chinese===
巴克也站了起来，双眼冒火。

"我可不是好欺负的，"他开口——国王那慢悠悠的声调，以无与伦比的庄重插了进来——

===Original===
"My Lord Buck, I must ask you to remember that your King is present. It is not often that he needs to protect himself among his subjects."

===Chinese===
"巴克大人，我不得不请你记着，你的国王在场。他难得需要在臣民中间卫护自己。"

===Original===
Barker turned to him with frantic gestures.

"For God's sake don't back up the madman now," he implored. "Have your joke another time. Oh, for Heaven's sake—"

===Chinese===
詹姆斯·巴克手舞足蹈地转向他。

"看在上帝份上，这会儿可别给这疯子撑腰啦，"他恳求道，"你的玩笑改日再开吧。哦，老天爷——"

===Original===
"My Lord Provost of South Kensington," said King Auberon, steadily, "I do not follow your remarks, which are uttered with a rapidity unusual at Court. Nor do your well-meant efforts to convey the rest with your fingers materially assist me. I say that my Lord Provost of North Kensington, to whom I spoke, ought not in the presence of his Sovereign to speak disrespectfully of his Sovereign's ordinances. Do you disagree?"

===Chinese===
"南肯辛顿区督大人，"奥伯伦国王不慌不忙地说，"你这一番话我有点跟不上——你说得这么快，在宫廷里可不常见。你那一番好意、想用手指头把剩下的话比划给我看的努力，于我也无甚帮助。我说的是：我所对话的这位北肯辛顿区督大人，不当在君王驾前、对君王的诏令出言不逊。你可有异议？"

===Original===
Barker turned restlessly in his chair, and Buck cursed without speaking. The King went on in a comfortable voice—

"My Lord Provost of Notting Hill, proceed."

===Chinese===
詹姆斯·巴克在椅子里不安地扭动，巴克不发一言地暗暗咒骂。国王用一种舒舒服服的嗓音接着说——

"诺丁山区督大人，请接着说。"

===Original===
Wayne turned his blue eyes on the King, and to everyone's surprise there was a look in them not of triumph, but of a certain childish distress.

"I am sorry, your Majesty," he said; "I fear I was more than equally to blame with the Lord Provost of North Kensington. We were debating somewhat eagerly, and we both rose to our feet. I did so first, I am ashamed to say. The Provost of North Kensington is, therefore, comparatively innocent. I beseech your Majesty to address your rebuke chiefly, at least, to me. Mr. Buck is not innocent, for he did no doubt, in the heat of the moment, speak disrespectfully. But the rest of the discussion he seems to me to have conducted with great good temper."

===Chinese===
韦恩把那双蓝眼睛转向国王，出乎众人意料，他眼中流露的不是得意，而是一种近乎孩童的为难。

"我很抱歉，陛下，"他说，"只怕我比起北肯辛顿区督大人来，更是难辞其咎。我们方才辩论得有些激烈，双双站了起来。说来惭愧，我先站起来的。因此北肯辛顿区督大人算是相对无辜的。我恳请陛下把申斥，至少主要地，落在我头上。巴克先生并非无辜，因为他确实在一时意气之下，出言不逊。但在我看来，这场讨论的余下部分，他的态度倒是极佳的。"

===Original===
Buck looked genuinely pleased, for business men are all simple-minded, and have therefore that degree of communion with fanatics. The King, for some reason, looked, for the first time in his life, ashamed.

===Chinese===
巴克显得由衷地高兴，因为生意人都是心思单纯的人，因此与狂徒之间倒有那么几分相通。而国王，不知怎的，生平头一遭露出了羞愧之色。

===Original===
"This very kind speech of the Provost of Notting Hill," began Buck, pleasantly, "seems to me to show that we have at least got on to a friendly footing. Now come, Mr. Wayne. Five hundred pounds have been offered to you for a property you admit not to be worth a hundred. Well, I am a rich man and I won't be outdone in generosity. Let us say fifteen hundred pounds, and have done with it. And let us shake hands;" and he rose, glowing and laughing.

===Chinese===
"诺丁山区督这番极其厚道的话，"巴克愉快地开口，"在我看来，至少说明我们已踏上友好的地步了。那么来吧，韦恩先生。你已承认不值一百镑的产业，人家出了五百镑。好，我是个有钱人，慷慨上头不甘落后。就算一千五百镑，就此了结吧。咱们握个手。"他说着站起身来，满面红光，笑着。

===Original===
"Fifteen hundred pounds," whispered Mr. Wilson of Bayswater; "can we do fifteen hundred pounds?"

"I'll stand the racket," said Buck, heartily. "Mr. Wayne is a gentleman and has spoken up for me. So I suppose the negotiations are at an end."

===Chinese===
"一千五百镑，"贝斯沃特的威尔逊先生悄声说，"咱们出得起一千五百镑吗？"

"这笔账我来扛，"巴克爽朗地说，"韦恩先生是个君子，又替我说了话。这么说来，谈判该收场了吧。"

===Original===
Wayne bowed.

"They are indeed at an end. I am sorry I cannot sell you the property."

===Chinese===
韦恩一鞠躬。

"的确是收场了。很抱歉，我不能把这处产业卖给你们。"

===Original===
"What?" cried Mr. Barker, starting to his feet.

===Chinese===
"什么？"詹姆斯·巴克先生叫着，跳了起来。

===Original===
"Mr. Buck has spoken correctly," said the King.

"I have, I have," cried Buck, springing up also; "I said—"

"Mr. Buck has spoken correctly," said the King; "the negotiations are at an end."

===Chinese===
"巴克先生说得不错。"国王说。

"我说得不错，我说得不错，"巴克也跳起来叫道，"我说——"

"巴克先生说得不错，"国王说，"谈判已经收场了。"

===Original===
All the men at the table rose to their feet; Wayne alone rose without excitement.

"Have I, then," he said, "your Majesty's permission to depart? I have given my last answer."

"You have it," said Auberon, smiling, but not lifting his eyes from the table. And amid a dead silence the Provost of Notting Hill passed out of the room.

===Chinese===
桌旁诸人都站了起来；唯独韦恩站起时不带一丝激动。

"那么，"他说，"我可获陛下恩准，告退了？我已给出了最后的答复。"

"恩准了，"奥伯伦说着，微微一笑，目光却未从桌上抬起。于是一片死寂之中，诺丁山区督走出了房间。

===Original===
"Well?" said Wilson, turning round to Barker—"well?"

Barker shook his head desperately.

===Chinese===
"怎么样？"威尔逊转身问詹姆斯·巴克——"怎么样？"

詹姆斯·巴克绝望地摇了摇头。

===Original===
"The man ought to be in an asylum," he said. "But one thing is clear—we need not bother further about him. The man can be treated as mad."

===Chinese===
"这个人该进疯人院，"他说，"但有一件事是清楚的——我们犯不着再为他费神了。这人可以当作疯子来处理。"

===Original===
"Of course," said Buck, turning to him with sombre decisiveness. "You're perfectly right, Barker. He is a good enough fellow, but he can be treated as mad. Let's put it in simple form. Go and tell any twelve men in any town, go and tell any doctor in any town, that there is a man offered fifteen hundred pounds for a thing he could sell commonly for four hundred, and that when asked for a reason for not accepting it he pleads the inviolate sanctity of Notting Hill and calls it the Holy Mountain. What would they say? What more can we have on our side than the common sense of everybody? On what else do all laws rest? I'll tell you, Barker, what's better than any further discussion. Let's send in workmen on the spot to pull down Pump Street. And if old Wayne says a word, arrest him as a lunatic. That's all."

===Chinese===
"自然，"巴克说，转身向着他，带着一种阴沉的果决。"你说得完全对，詹姆斯·巴克。他这人不算坏，但可以当作疯子处理。咱们把这事儿说简单些。你去随便哪个城里，跟随便十二个人说；去跟随便哪个城里的随便哪个医生说：有这么一个人，人家为一件通常只值四百镑的东西出一千五百镑给他，问他不肯接受的理由时，他却搬出诺丁山不可侵犯的神圣，称它为圣山。他们会怎么说？咱们这边还能有什么，比得上人人都有的常识呢？一切法律，除了常识之外，还建立在什么别的东西之上？我告诉你，詹姆斯·巴克，什么也比不上这么办——不必再多费口舌了。咱们就派工人到现场，把泵街拆了。老韦恩要是哼一声，就以疯子罪抓起来。就这样。"

===Original===
Barker's eyes kindled.

"I always regarded you, Buck, if you don't mind my saying so, as a very strong man. I'll follow you."

===Chinese===
詹姆斯·巴克的眼睛亮了起来。

"我一向把你看作一个很强的人，巴克，恕我直言。我跟你走。"

===Original===
"So, of course, will I," said Wilson.

===Chinese===
"我当然也跟你走。"威尔逊说。

===Original===
Buck rose again impulsively.

"Your Majesty," he said, glowing with popularity, "I beseech your Majesty to consider favourably the proposal to which we have committed ourselves. Your Majesty's leniency, our own offers, have fallen in vain on that extraordinary man. He may be right. He may be God. He may be the devil. But we think it, for practical purposes, more probable that he is off his head. Unless that assumption were acted on, all human affairs would go to pieces. We act on it, and we propose to start operations in Notting Hill at once."

===Chinese===
巴克又按捺不住地站了起来。

"陛下，"他说，因深得人心而满面红光，"恳请陛下恩准我们所已承担的这项提议。陛下的宽厚，我们开出的条件，落在那位异人身上都是枉然。他也许是对的。他也许是神。他也许是魔鬼。但出于务实起见，我们认为更可能的是——他疯了。若不照此假设行事，人间一切事务都将散架。我们便照此行事，并提议立即在诺丁山开工。"

===Original===
The King leaned back in his chair.

"The Charter of the Cities…," he said with a rich intonation.

But Buck, being finally serious, was also cautious, and did not again make the mistake of disrespect.

===Chinese===
国王往椅背上一靠。

"城市宪章……"他用一种韵味悠长的语调说。

但巴克既已认真起来，也就谨慎了，不再重蹈失敬的覆辙。

===Original===
"Your Majesty," he said, bowing, "I am not here to say a word against anything your Majesty has said or done. You are a far better educated man than I, and no doubt there were reasons, upon intellectual grounds, for those proceedings. But may I ask you and appeal to your common good-nature for a sincere answer? When you drew up the Charter of the Cities, did you contemplate the rise of a man like Adam Wayne? Did you expect that the Charter—whether it was an experiment, or a scheme of decoration, or a joke—could ever really come to this—to stopping a vast scheme of ordinary business, to shutting up a road, to spoiling the chances of cabs, omnibuses, railway stations, to disorganising half a city, to risking a kind of civil war? Whatever were your objects, were they that?"

===Chinese===
"陛下，"他一鞠躬说，"我在此并非要说一句有损陛下所言所行的任何话。陛下受过的教育远胜于我，那些举措，无疑是有智识上的道理的。但我能否请问陛下、并诉诸陛下一贯的好心，求一个恳切的回答？当年陛下起草城市宪章时，可曾料到会出现一个像亚当·韦恩这样的人？你可曾想到，这宪章——无论它是一项实验、一套装饰方案、还是一个玩笑——竟能当真走到这一步：叫停一桩庞大的日常商务，封死一条路，断送马车、公共马车、火车站的生意，搅乱半座城的秩序，冒上一场内战的风险？无论你的本意为何，你的本意可是这个？"

===Original===
Barker and Wilson looked at him admiringly; the King more admiringly still.

===Chinese===
詹姆斯·巴克和威尔逊钦佩地望着他；国王更是钦佩得无以复加。

===Original===
"Provost Buck," said Auberon, "you speak in public uncommonly well. I give you your point with the magnanimity of an artist. My scheme did not include the appearance of Mr. Wayne. Alas! would that my poetic power had been great enough."

===Chinese===
"巴克区督，"奥伯伦说，"你当众讲话，讲得着实出色。我以一个艺术家的胸襟，把这一分让给你。我的方案，确乎不曾包括韦恩先生这个人物的出现。唉！但愿我的诗才够大就好了。"

===Original===
"I thank your Majesty," said Buck, courteously, but quickly. "Your Majesty's statements are always clear and studied; therefore I may draw a deduction. As the scheme, whatever it was, on which you set your heart did not include the appearance of Mr. Wayne, it will survive his removal. Why not let us clear away this particular Pump Street, which does interfere with our plans, and which does not, by your Majesty's own statement, interfere with yours."

===Chinese===
"谢陛下，"巴克客气而敏捷地说。"陛下的话向来清楚而审慎；因此我大可作一推论。既然陛下所倾心的那套方案——无论它是什么——并不包括韦恩先生的出现，那么它便能经受住把他搬开这一步。既然这一段泵街确实妨碍我们的计划，而据陛下自己说，并不妨碍陛下的计划，那何不让我们把这一段泵街清掉呢？"

===Original===
"Caught out!" said the King, enthusiastically and quite impersonally, as if he were watching a cricket match.

===Chinese===
"抓到了！"国王热情而全然置身事外地说，仿佛在看一场板球赛。

===Original===
"This man Wayne," continued Buck, "would be shut up by any doctors in England. But we only ask to have it put before them. Meanwhile no one's interests, not even in all probability his own, can be really damaged by going on with the improvements in Notting Hill. Not our interests, of course, for it has been the hard and quiet work of ten years. Not the interests of Notting Hill, for nearly all its educated inhabitants desire the change. Not the interests of your Majesty, for you say, with characteristic sense, that you never contemplated the rise of the lunatic at all. Not, as I say, his own interests, for the man has a kind heart and many talents, and a couple of good doctors would probably put him righter than all the free cities and sacred mountains in creation. I therefore assume, if I may use so bold a word, that your Majesty will not offer any obstacle to our proceeding with the improvements."

===Chinese===
"韦恩这个人，"巴克接着说，"英格兰随便哪个医生都会把他关起来。我们不过请事交他们裁定罢了。与此同时，继续推进诺丁山的改良工程，不会真正损害任何人的利益——多半连他本人的利益也不会损害。当然不会损害我们的利益，因为这是我们十年辛苦而沉默的劳作。不会损害诺丁山的利益，因为它几乎所有有教养的居民都盼着这一改变。不会损害陛下的利益，因为陛下以惯有的见识说，你压根儿没料到这个疯子的出现。也不会损害——如我所说——他本人的利益，因为这人心地不坏，又颇有才干，一两位好医生多半能把他治得，比创世以来所有的自由城和圣山都来得灵光。因此我假定——倘若我可用如此大胆的字眼——陛下将不会在我们推进改良工程一事上设置任何障碍。"

===Original===
And Mr. Buck sat down amid subdued but excited applause among the allies.

===Chinese===
于是巴克先生在盟友们低抑却兴奋的掌声中坐了下来。

===Original===
"Mr. Buck," said the King, "I beg your pardon, for a number of beautiful and sacred thoughts, in which you were generally classified as a fool. But there is another thing to be considered. Suppose you send in your workmen, and Mr. Wayne does a thing regrettable indeed, but of which, I am sorry to say, I think him quite capable—knocks their teeth out?"

===Chinese===
"巴克先生，"国王说，"我求你宽恕——为从前那许多美好而神圣的念头，在那些念头里，你通常被归入蠢人之列。但还有一件事要考虑。假定你把工人派了进去，而韦恩先生做出一桩事来——诚然令人遗憾，但我不得不说，我认为他完全干得出来——把他们的牙敲掉？"

===Original===
"I have thought of that, your Majesty," said Mr. Buck, easily, "and I think it can simply be guarded against. Let us send in a strong guard of, say, a hundred men—a hundred of the North Kensington Halberdiers" (he smiled grimly), "of whom your Majesty is so fond. Or say a hundred and fifty. The whole population of Pump Street, I fancy, is only about a hundred."

===Chinese===
"这一点我想过了，陛下，"巴克先生轻描淡写地说，"我看只需简单地防一防就是。咱们派一支强卫队去，比方说，一百人——一百名北肯辛顿戟兵"（他冷酷地笑了笑），"就是陛下那么钟爱的那些。或者说一百五十名。泵街的全部人口，我料想也不过百来号。"

===Original===
"Still they might stand together and lick you," said the King, dubiously.

===Chinese===
"可他们要是抱成一团，倒过来把你们揍一顿呢。"国王将信将疑地说。

===Original===
"Then say two hundred," said Buck, gaily.

===Chinese===
"那就两百。"巴克乐呵呵地说。

===Original===
"It might happen," said the King, restlessly, "that one Notting Hiller fought better than two North Kensingtons."

===Chinese===
"也说不定会出这种事，"国王不安地说，"一个诺丁山人打得过两个北肯辛顿人。"

===Original===
"It might," said Buck, coolly; "then say two hundred and fifty."

===Chinese===
"也说不定，"巴克冷冷地说，"那就两百五。"

===Original===
The King bit his lip.

"And if they are beaten too?" he said viciously.

===Chinese===
国王咬了咬嘴唇。

"要是他们也被打败了呢？"他悻悻地说。

===Original===
"Your Majesty," said Buck, and leaned back easily in his chair, "suppose they are. If anything be clear, it is clear that all fighting matters are mere matters of arithmetic.
