Here we have a hundred and fifty, say, of Notting Hill soldiers. Or say two hundred. If one of them can fight two of us—we can send in, not four hundred, but six hundred, and smash him. That is all. It is out of all immediate probability that one of them could fight four of us. So what I say is this. Run no risks. Finish it at once. Send in eight hundred men and smash him—smash him almost without seeing him. And go on with the improvements."

===Chinese===
"陛下，"巴克说着，往椅背上惬意一靠，"就算他们被打败了，又怎样。要是有一件事清楚，那就是清楚这一点：一切打仗的事，不过是算术的事。咱们这里有，比方说，一百五十名诺丁山士兵。或者说两百名。要是他们一个能打我们两个——我们便可以派进去，不是四百，而是六百，把他碾碎。如此而已。他们一个能打我们四个，这种可能眼下几乎不存在。所以我的话是：别冒险。立即了结。派八百人进去，把他碾碎——碾碎他，几乎连看都不必看见他。然后继续改良工程。"

===Original===
And Mr. Buck pulled out a bandana and blew his nose.

===Chinese===
于是巴克先生掏出一方印花大帕，擤了擤鼻子。

===Original===
"Do you know, Mr. Buck," said the King, staring gloomily at the table, "the admirable clearness of your reason produces in my mind a sentiment which I trust I shall not offend you by describing as an aspiration to punch your head. You irritate me sublimely. What can it be in me? Is it the relic of a moral sense?"

===Chinese===
"你可知道，巴克先生，"国王闷闷地盯着桌子说，"你那令人钦佩的思路之清晰，在我心里激起了一种情绪——我形容它为一种想揍你脑袋的冲动，希望你不会见怪。你把我激怒到了一种崇高的地步。这会是我身上的什么呢？莫非是某种道德感的遗存？"

===Original===
"But your Majesty," said Barker, eagerly and suavely, "does not refuse our proposals?"

===Chinese===
"可是陛下，"詹姆斯·巴克热切而圆滑地说，"并没有拒绝我们的提议吧？"

===Original===
"My dear Barker, your proposals are as damnable as your manners. I want to have nothing to do with them. Suppose I stopped them altogether. What would happen?"

Barker answered in a very low voice—

"Revolution."

===Chinese===
"亲爱的詹姆斯·巴克，你的提议和你的举止一样该死。我才不想沾边。假定我把它整个儿拦下来。会怎样？"

詹姆斯·巴克压低嗓门答道——

"革命。"

===Original===
The King glanced quickly at the men round the table. They were all looking down silently: their brows were red.

===Chinese===
国王飞快地扫了一眼围坐桌旁的人。他们都默默地低着头：他们的额头涨得通红。

===Original===
He rose with a startling suddenness, and an unusual pallor.

===Chinese===
他猛地站起身来——猛得叫人一惊，脸色也不同寻常地苍白。

===Original===
"Gentlemen," he said, "you have overruled me. Therefore I can speak plainly. I think Adam Wayne, who is as mad as a hatter, worth more than a million of you. But you have the force, and, I admit, the common sense, and he is lost. Take your eight hundred halberdiers and smash him. It would be more sportsmanlike to take two hundred."

===Chinese===
"诸位，"他说，"你们推翻了我的意见。所以我大可把话说白。我以为亚当·韦恩——他疯得像帽匠（mad as a hatter）——抵得过你们一百万人。可你们有武力，我也承认，有常识，他完了。带上你们那八百名戟兵，去碾碎他吧。带两百人去，倒还更像个运动家的派头。"

===Original===
"More sportsmanlike," said Buck, grimly, "but a great deal less humane. We are not artists, and streets purple with gore do not catch our eye in the right way."

===Chinese===
"更像个运动家的派头，"巴克冷冷地说，"可人道少了去。我们不是艺术家，紫血流满街这种景象，落在我们眼里，味道可不对。"

===Original===
"It is pitiful," said Auberon. "With five or six times their number, there will be no fight at all."

===Chinese===
"可悲啊，"奥伯伦说，"以五倍六倍于他们的人数，根本就不会有什么仗打了。"

===Original===
"I hope not," said Buck, rising and adjusting his gloves. "We desire no fight, your Majesty. We are peaceable business men."

===Chinese===
"我也希望没有，"巴克说着站起来，理了理手套。"我们并不想打仗，陛下。我们是爱好和平的生意人。"

===Original===
"Well," said the King, wearily, "the conference is at an end at last."

And he went out of the room before anyone else could stir.

===Chinese===
"好吧，"国王疲惫地说，"会议总算到头了。"

他趁着别人还没来得及动弹，便走出了房间。

---

===Original===
Forty workmen, a hundred Bayswater Halberdiers, two hundred from South, and three from North Kensington, assembled at the foot of Holland Walk and marched up it, under the general direction of Barker, who looked flushed and happy in full dress. At the end of the procession a small and sulky figure lingered like an urchin. It was the King.

===Chinese===
四十名工人、一百名贝斯沃特戟兵、两百名南肯辛顿戟兵，外加三名北肯辛顿戟兵，集结在荷兰道（Holland Walk）脚下，沿着它向上开拔，由詹姆斯·巴克统一指挥——他一身盛装，容光焕发，一副快活模样。队伍末尾，一个小小的、悻悻的身影磨磨蹭蹭地跟着，活像个顽童。那是国王。

===Original===
"Barker," he said at length, appealingly, "you are an old friend of mine—you understand my hobbies as I understand yours. Why can't you let it alone? I hoped that such fun might come out of this Wayne business. Why can't you let it alone? It doesn't really so much matter to you—what's a road or so? For me it's the one joke that may save me from pessimism. Take fewer men and give me an hour's fun. Really and truly, James, if you collected coins or hummingbirds, and I could buy one with the price of your road, I would buy it. I collect incidents—those rare, those precious things. Let me have one. Pay a few pounds for it. Give these Notting Hillers a chance. Let them alone."

===Chinese===
"詹姆斯·巴克，"他终于开口，带着恳求，"你是我多年的老友——你懂我的癖好，正如我懂你的。你为什么不能放过它呢？我原本盼着，这桩韦恩的事能闹出点乐子来。你为什么不能放过它呢？这于你其实没多大要紧——一两条路算什么？于我，这却是唯一一个也许能把我从悲观主义里救出来的玩笑。少带些人，给我一个钟头的乐子吧。说真的，詹姆斯，倘若你收藏的是钱币或蜂鸟，而我可以用你那条路的代价来买上一只，我会买的。我收藏的是事件——那些罕见的、珍贵的东西。让我得着一个吧。花几个镑买它。给这些诺丁山人一个机会吧。放过他们吧。"

===Original===
"Auberon," said Barker, kindly, forgetting all royal titles in a rare moment of sincerity, "I do feel what you mean. I have had moments when these hobbies have hit me. I have had moments when I have sympathised with your humours. I have had moments, though you may not easily believe it, when I have sympathised with the madness of Adam Wayne. But the world, Auberon, the real world, is not run on these hobbies. It goes on great brutal wheels of facts—wheels on which you are the butterfly; and Wayne is the fly on the wheel."

===Chinese===
"奥伯伦，"詹姆斯·巴克和气地说，在一个难得的真诚时刻忘却了一切王室尊称，"我确乎明白你的意思。这种癖好袭上我心头的时候，我也是有的。你的种种幽默，我也有过感同身受的时刻。亚当·韦恩的疯狂，我也有过感同身受的时刻——尽管你未必肯信。可是这世界，奥伯伦，这个真实的世界，不是靠这些癖好运转的。它靠的是事实那些巨大而粗暴的车轮——那车轮之上，你是蝴蝶；而韦恩，是轮子上的苍蝇。"

===Original===
Auberon's eyes looked frankly at the other's.

"Thank you, James; what you say is true. It is only a parenthetical consolation to me to compare the intelligence of flies somewhat favourably with the intelligence of wheels. But it is the nature of flies to die soon, and the nature of wheels to go on forever. Go on with the wheel. Goodbye, old man."

===Chinese===
奥伯伦的眼睛坦率地望着对方的眼睛。

"谢谢你，詹姆斯；你说的是实情。把苍蝇的智力拿来、略胜一筹地比一比车轮的智力，于我也不过是一句题外的安慰罢了。但苍蝇的本性是早早便死，车轮的本性是永远转下去。接着转你的车轮吧。再会了，老伙计。"

===Original===
And James Barker went on, laughing, with a high colour, slapping his bamboo on his leg.

===Chinese===
于是詹姆斯·巴克笑着往前走去，满脸红光，拿他的竹手杖一下一下地拍着腿。

===Original===
The King watched the tail of the retreating regiment with a look of genuine depression, which made him seem more like a baby than ever. Then he swung round and struck his hands together.

"In a world without humour," he said, "the only thing to do is to eat. And how perfect an exception! How can these people strike dignified attitudes, and pretend that things matter, when the total ludicrousness of life is proved by the very method by which it is supported? A man strikes the lyre, and says, 'Life is real, life is earnest,' and then goes into a room and stuffs alien substances into a hole in his head. I think Nature was indeed a little broad in her humour in these matters. But we all fall back on the pantomime, as I have in this municipal affair. Nature has her farces, like the act of eating or the shape of the kangaroo, for the more brutal appetite. She keeps her stars and mountains for those who can appreciate something more subtly ridiculous." He turned to his equerry. "But, as I said 'eating,' let us have a picnic like two nice little children. Just run and bring me a table and a dozen courses or so, and plenty of champagne, and under these swinging boughs, Bowler, we will return to Nature."

===Chinese===
国王望着那支远去队伍的尾巴，一脸由衷的颓丧，那副神情使他比从前任何时候都更像一个婴孩。接着他猛地转过身，拍了拍手。

"在一个没有幽默的世界里，"他说，"唯一可做的事便是吃。而这是何等完美的一个例外！这些人怎能摆出庄严的姿态、装出事情很要紧的模样，既然生活全然的荒谬，恰恰由它赖以维系的方式所证明了？一个人弹起里拉琴，说道'生活是真实的，生活是认真的'，然后走进一个房间，把一些异物塞进头上的一个洞里。我觉得在这些事上，大自然的幽默确实粗放了些。可我们终究都落回到哑剧上来，正如我在这桩市政公事上所做的那样。大自然自有她的闹剧，比如吃这个动作、比如袋鼠的形状，是为那更粗暴的胃口准备的。她把她的星星和群山，留给那些懂得欣赏更精微之荒谬的人。"他转向他的副官。"不过，既然我说了'吃'，那咱们就像两个乖小孩一样野餐一顿吧。快跑去给我弄一张桌子来，外加十来道菜，还有充足的香槟，鲍勒，就在这些摇曳的枝叶下，咱们回归自然。"

===Original===
It took about an hour to erect in Holland Lane the monarch's simple repast, during which time he walked up and down and whistled, but still with an unaffected air of gloom. He had really been done out of a pleasure he had promised himself, and had that empty and sickened feeling which a child has when disappointed of a pantomime. When he and the equerry had sat down, however, and consumed a fair amount of dry champagne, his spirits began mildly to revive.

===Chinese===
在荷兰巷（Holland Lane）支起这位君主那顿简朴的便餐，前后花了大约一个钟头；这期间他来回踱着、吹着口哨，却仍带着一种不假掩饰的郁色。他确乎被夺去了一桩他指望着的乐事，心里头有一种空落落的、败兴的感觉，正如一个孩子因一场哑剧落空而失望时的那种感觉。然而，等他和副官坐了下来，灌下相当份量的纯香槟之后，他的兴致便微微地回升了。

===Original===
"Things take too long in this world," he said. "I detest all this Barkerian business about evolution and the gradual modification of things. I wish the world had been made in six days, and knocked to pieces again in six more. And I wish I had done it. The joke's good enough in a broad way, sun and moon and the image of God, and all that, but they keep it up so damnably long. Did you ever long for a miracle, Bowler?"

===Chinese===
"这世上什么事都拖得太久，"他说。"我讨厌詹姆斯·巴克那一套关于进化、关于事物渐变的玩意儿。我巴不得这世界六天里造出来，再六天里砸个粉碎。我还巴不得那是我干的。这玩笑大而化之地讲倒也够精彩——太阳、月亮、上帝的形象，等等——可他们把它拖得实在该死地长。你可曾渴望过一桩奇迹，鲍勒？"

===Original===
"No, sir," said Bowler, who was an evolutionist, and had been carefully brought up.

===Chinese===
"没有，先生，"鲍勒说——他是个进化论者，从小被精心教养大。

===Original===
"Then I have," answered the King. "I have walked along a street with the best cigar in the cosmos in my mouth, and more Burgundy inside me than you ever saw in your life, and longed that the lamppost would turn into an elephant to save me from the hell of blank existence. Take my word for it, my evolutionary Bowler, don't you believe people when they tell you that people sought for a sign, and believed in miracles because they were ignorant. They did it because they were wise, filthily, vilely wise—too wise to eat or sleep or put on their boots with patience. This seems delightfully like a new theory of the origin of Christianity, which would itself be a thing of no mean absurdity. Take some more wine."

===Chinese===
"我可曾渴望过。"国王答道。"我曾嘴里叼着全宇宙最好的雪茄，肚里灌的勃艮第酒比你一辈子见过的都多，沿着一条街走过，巴不得那根灯柱变成一头大象，好把我从空白存在的地狱里救出来。相信我，我那进化论的鲍勒，人家告诉你人们寻求神迹、信奉奇迹是因为无知，你别信。他们那么做，是因为他们有智慧——污浊地、卑劣地有智慧——智慧到无法耐着性子吃饭、睡觉、穿靴子。这话听来妙极，像一套关于基督教起源的新学说，而那本身便是一件不小的荒谬。再来点酒。"

===Original===
The wind blew round them as they sat at their little table, with its white cloth and bright wine-cups, and flung the treetops of Holland Park against each other, but the sun was in that strong temper which turns green into gold. The King pushed away his plate, lit a cigar slowly, and went on—

===Chinese===
风围着他们坐的那张小桌吹——桌上铺着白布，摆着亮晶晶的酒杯——把荷兰园（Holland Park）的树梢刮得彼此撞击；可阳光正是一种强烈的脾性，把绿色都点成了金色。国王推开盘子，慢慢地点燃一支雪茄，接着说——

===Original===
"Yesterday I thought that something next door to a really entertaining miracle might happen to me before I went to amuse the worms. To see that red-haired maniac waving a great sword, and making speeches to his incomparable followers, would have been a glimpse of that Land of Youth from which the Fates shut us out. I had planned some quite delightful things. A Congress of Knightsbridge with a treaty, and myself in the chair, and perhaps a Roman triumph, with jolly old Barker led in chains. And now these wretched prigs have gone and stamped out the exquisite Mr. Wayne altogether, and I suppose they will put him in a private asylum somewhere in their damned humane way. Think of the treasures daily poured out to his unappreciative keeper! I wonder whether they would let me be his keeper. But life is a vale. Never forget at any moment of your existence to regard it in the light of a vale. This graceful habit, if not acquired in youth—"

===Chinese===
"昨天我还以为，在我去给虫子逗乐之前，说不定会有一桩近乎真正有趣的神迹落到我头上。眼看那个红发疯子挥舞一柄大剑，向他那群举世无双的追随者发表演说——那该是青春之国的一瞥，而命运之神偏把我们关在青春之国之外。我盘算过一些相当赏心乐事。一场骑士桥的会议，外加一项条约，本人坐在主席位上，也许再来一场罗马式的凯旋式，快活的詹姆斯·巴克戴着锁链被牵进来。可如今这帮可恶的道学先生跑去把那位精致的韦恩先生整个儿踏灭了，我想他们会以他们那该死的人道方式，把他塞进某个私家疯人院去。想想那些每日倾注在他那不懂领情的看护面前的珍宝吧！不知他们肯不肯让我当他的看护。可人生是一道山谷。你此生任何一刻，都别忘记把它视作一道山谷。这一优雅的习惯若非在少年时养成——"

===Original===
The King stopped, with his cigar lifted, for there had slid into his eyes the startled look of a man listening. He did not move for a few moments; then he turned his head sharply towards the high, thin, and lath-like paling which fenced certain long gardens and similar spaces from the lane. From behind it there was coming a curious scrambling and scraping noise, as of a desperate thing imprisoned in this box of thin wood. The King threw away his cigar, and jumped on to the table. From this position he saw a pair of hands hanging with a hungry clutch on the top of the fence. Then the hands quivered with a convulsive effort, and a head shot up between them—the head of one of the Bayswater Town Council, his eyes and whiskers wild with fear. He swung himself over, and fell on the other side on his face, and groaned openly and without ceasing. The next moment the thin, taut wood of the fence was struck as by a bullet, so that it reverberated like a drum, and over it came tearing and cursing, with torn clothes and broken nails and bleeding faces, twenty men at one rush. The King sprang five feet clear off the table on to the ground. The moment after the table was flung over, sending bottles and glasses flying, and the debris was literally swept along the ground by that stream of men pouring past, and Bowler was borne along with them, as the King said in his famous newspaper article, "like a captured bride." The great fence swung and split under the load of climbers that still scaled and cleared it. Tremendous gaps were torn in it by this living artillery; and through them the King could see more and more frantic faces, as in a dream, and more and more men running. They were as miscellaneous as if someone had taken the lid off a human dustbin. Some were untouched, some were slashed and battered and bloody, some were splendidly dressed, some tattered and half naked, some were in the fantastic garb of the burlesque cities, some in the dullest modern dress. The King stared at all of them, but none of them looked at the King. Suddenly he stepped forward.

"Barker," he said, "what is all this?"

"Beaten," said the politician—"beaten all to hell!" And he plunged past with nostrils shaking like a horse's, and more and more men plunged after him.

===Chinese===
国王住了口，雪茄擎在半空——他眼里掠过一种倾听者受惊的神情。他僵了几息没动；接着猛地把头转向那道高高的、薄薄的、板条似的栅栏——那栅栏把一块块狭长的花园和类似的空地与巷子隔开。栅栏后头传来一阵古怪的攀爬抓挠之声，像是一件绝望的东西被关在这个薄板箱子里挣命。国王扔掉雪茄，跳上桌子。从这上面他看见一双手，饥饿地抠住栅栏的顶端。接着那双手一阵痉挛似的使力一颤，一颗脑袋从两手之间蹿了上来——那是贝斯沃特市议会一位议员的脑袋，他的眼睛和络腮胡因恐惧而散乱。他翻身而过，摔到另一侧，脸着地，毫不掩饰地、不住地呻吟。紧接着，栅栏那绷紧的薄板被像子弹似的一击，嗡嗡地震响如鼓，二十个人一涌而过，撕着衣裳、断着指甲、流着血脸，破口大骂地翻了过来。国王从桌上一跃五英尺，跳到地上。紧接着桌子被掀翻，酒瓶酒杯乱飞，那群人涌过，把残骸简直是从地面上一路扫走，鲍勒也被他们裹挟着带走了——正如国王在他那篇著名的报纸文章里所说，"像一个被掳的新娘"。那道大栅栏在还在攀越的攀登者的重压下摇晃、开裂。这一门门活的炮弹在它上面撕开一个个巨大的口子；透过口子，国王像在梦里一样，看见一张张愈发惊狂的脸，看见越来越多的人奔逃着。他们五花八门，活像有人掀开了一口人类的垃圾箱。有的毫发无损，有的被砍被砸、血肉模糊，有的盛装华服，有的衣衫褴褛、半身赤裸，有的穿着那些滑稽城邦的古怪装束，有的穿着最乏味不过的现代衣装。国王瞪着他们全体，却没有一个人看国王一眼。忽然他跨上一步。

"詹姆斯·巴克，"他说，"这都是怎么回事？"

"败了，"这位政治家说——"败得一塌糊涂！"他鼻翼像马一样翕动着冲了过去，越来越多的人跟着他涌过。

===Original===
Almost as he spoke, the last standing strip of fence bowed and snapped, flinging, as from a catapult, a new figure upon the road. He wore the flaming red of the halberdiers of Notting Hill, and on his weapon there was blood, and in his face victory. In another moment masses of red glowed through the gaps of the fence, and the pursuers, with their halberds, came pouring down the lane. Pursued and pursuers alike swept by the little figure with the owlish eyes, who had not taken his hands out of his pockets.

===Chinese===
话音未落，栅栏最后一段还立着的板条弯折、崩断，像从一架投石机里似的，把一个崭新的身影甩到了路上。他一身诺丁山戟兵那火一般的红，他的兵器上沾着血，他的脸上是胜利。转眼间，一团团红色透过栅栏的缺口透出来，追兵们挺着戟，沿着巷子倾泻而下。被追的和追人的，一律从那个猫头鹰眼睛的小小身影旁边扫过——他始终没把手从口袋里抽出来。

===Original===
The King had still little beyond the confused sense of a man caught in a torrent—the feeling of men eddying by. Then something happened which he was never able afterwards to describe, and which we cannot describe for him. Suddenly in the dark entrance, between the broken gates of a garden, there appeared framed a flaming figure.

===Chinese===
国王所有的，仍不过是一个被卷入洪流的人那种混乱之感——人在他身旁打着旋涌过的感觉。接着发生了一件事——这件事他事后始终形容不出，我们也替他形容不出。忽然间，在一座花园那破损的门之间那幽暗的入口，赫然框出了一个火焰般的身影。

===Original===
Adam Wayne, the conqueror, with his face flung back, and his mane like a lion's, stood with his great sword point upwards, the red raiment of his office flapping round him like the red wings of an archangel. And the King saw, he knew not how, something new and overwhelming. The great green trees and the great red robes swung together in the wind. The sword seemed made for the sunlight. The preposterous masquerade, born of his own mockery, towered over him and embraced the world. This was the normal, this was sanity, this was nature; and he himself, with his rationality and his detachment and his black frock-coat, he was the exception and the accident—a blot of black upon a world of crimson and gold.

===Chinese===
亚当·韦恩，这位征服者，脸向后仰起，鬃毛似的头发如狮鬃，立在那里，大剑剑尖朝上，他那身官职的大红袍在他四围猎猎翻飞，宛如一位天使长的红色双翼。而国王看见了——他不知是怎样看见的——某种崭新而压倒一切的东西。巨大的绿树与巨大的红袍一同在风中摇摆。那柄剑仿佛就是为这阳光而铸。这场荒唐透顶的化装游行，原由他自己的讥嘲所生，此刻高耸于他之上，拥抱了整个世界。这才是正常的，这才是健全，这才是自然；而他自己，连同他的理性、他的超然、他那件黑色双排扣长礼服，他才是例外、才是意外——一抹黑，落在一片猩红与金黄的世界之上。

## Book IV / 第四卷

## I. The Battle of the Lamps / 第一章 灯之战

===Original===
Mr. Buck, who, though retired, frequently went down to his big drapery stores in Kensington High Street, was locking up those premises, being the last to leave. It was a wonderful evening of green and gold, but that did not trouble him very much. If you had pointed it out, he would have agreed seriously, for the rich always desire to be artistic.

He stepped out into the cool air, buttoning up his light yellow coat, and blowing great clouds from his cigar, when a figure dashed up to him in another yellow overcoat, but unbuttoned and flying behind him.

“Hullo, Barker!” said the draper. “Any of our summer articles? You’re too late. Factory Acts, Barker. Humanity and progress, my boy.”

“Oh, don’t chatter,” cried Barker, stamping. “We’ve been beaten.”

“Beaten—by what?” asked Buck, mystified.

“By Wayne.”

Buck looked at Barker’s fierce white face for the first time, as it gleamed in the lamplight.

“Come and have a drink,” he said.

===Chinese===
巴克先生虽已半退休，却仍常去肯辛顿高街（Kensington High Street）他那爿大布店里看看。此刻他正在锁店门——他是最后一个离开的。这是一个绿金交辉的美妙黄昏，可这并未怎样触动他。倘若你指给他看，他会一本正经地附和，因为有钱人总巴望自己能有些艺术眼光。

他迈入清凉的空气里，扣上那件浅黄色外衣，雪茄吐出一团团浓烟。就在这时，一个身影朝他直冲过来，身上也披着一件黄外衣，却敞着襟，在身后猎猎飞扬。

"喂，詹姆斯·巴克（James Barker）！"这位布商说，"来找我们的夏季货品？你太迟了。工厂法（Factory Acts，限制作业时间的劳工法）哪，巴克先生。人道与进步啊，老弟。"

"噢，别扯淡了，"巴克先生顿着脚嚷道，"我们被打败了。"

"打败了——被什么打败的？"巴克满头雾水地问。

"被韦恩。"

巴克这才头一回打量起巴克先生那张铁青惨白的脸——那张脸在灯影里幽幽发亮。

"走，喝一杯去，"他说。

===Original===
They adjourned to a cushioned and glaring buffet, and Buck established himself slowly and lazily in a seat, and pulled out his cigar-case.

“Have a smoke,” he said.

Barker was still standing, and on the fret, but after a moment’s hesitation, he sat down as if he might spring up again the next minute. They ordered drinks in silence.

“How did it happen?” asked Buck, turning his big bold eyes on him.

“How the devil do I know?” cried Barker. “It happened like—like a dream. How can two hundred men beat six hundred? How can they?”

“Well,” said Buck, coolly, “how did they? You ought to know.”

===Chinese===
他们转进一家椅垫柔软、灯火耀眼的餐饮部。巴克慢条斯理、懒洋洋地在座位上安顿下来，掏出雪茄烟匣。

"来一根，"他说。

巴克先生仍站着，焦躁不安；迟疑了片刻，他才坐下，那姿态仿佛下一分钟就要重新弹起来。两人默默要了酒。

"到底怎么搞的？"巴克问，把那双又大又锋利的眼睛转向他。

"我怎么知道？"巴克先生叫道，"那场面像——像一场梦。两百人怎么能打败六百人？怎么可能？"

"嗯，"巴克冷冷地说，"那他们怎么打败的？你总该知道吧。"

===Original===
“I don’t know; I can’t describe,” said the other, drumming on the table. “It seemed like this. We were six hundred, and marched with those damned poleaxes of Auberon’s—the only weapons we’ve got. We marched two abreast. We went up Holland Walk, between the high palings which seemed to me to go straight as an arrow for Pump Street. I was near the tail of the line, and it was a long one. When the end of it was still between the high palings, the head of the line was already crossing Holland Park Avenue. Then the head plunged into the network of narrow streets on the other side, and the tail and myself came out on the great crossing. When we also had reached the northern side and turned up a small street that points, crookedly as it were, towards Pump Street, the whole thing felt different. The streets dodged and bent so much that the head of our line seemed lost altogether: it might as well have been in North America. And all this time we hadn’t seen a soul.”

===Chinese===
"我不知道；我说不上来，"对方说着，手指笃笃地敲着桌面，"情形好像是这样的。我们六百人，手里拿着奥伯伦那种该死的长戟——那是我们仅有的家伙。我们两人一排，顺着荷兰步道（Holland Walk）开上去，走在两道高高的栅板之间，我觉得那栅板直得像一支箭，直指泵街（Pump Street）。我在队伍靠尾巴的地方，队伍拖得很长。队伍的尾巴还夹在那两道高栅板之间时，队伍的脑袋已经穿过荷兰公园大道（Holland Park Avenue）了。随后那脑袋便扎进对面那片蛛网似的小街里去，我和尾巴这才走到了那个大十字路口。等我们也到了路北，拐进一条仿佛歪歪斜斜指向泵街的小街，整件事的感觉就全变了。那些街七拐八弯，把我们队伍的脑袋像是整个儿拐没了：简直大可以当作它已经到了北美洲。而这一路，我们连一个人影也没见着。"

===Original===
Buck, who was idly dabbing the ash of his cigar on the ashtray, began to move it deliberately over the table, making feathery grey lines, a kind of map.

“But though the little streets were all deserted (which got a trifle on my nerves), as we got deeper and deeper into them, a thing began to happen that I couldn’t understand. Sometimes a long way ahead—three turns or corners ahead, as it were—there broke suddenly a sort of noise, clattering, and confused cries, and then stopped. Then, when it happened, something, I can’t describe it—a kind of shake or stagger went down the line, as if the line were a live thing, whose head had been struck, or had been an electric cord. None of us knew why we were moving, but we moved and jostled. Then we recovered, and went on through the little dirty streets, round corners, and up twisted ways. The little crooked streets began to give me a feeling I can’t explain—as if it were a dream. I felt as if things had lost their reason, and we should never get out of the maze. Odd to hear me talk like that, isn’t it? The streets were quite well-known streets, all down on the map. But the fact remains. I wasn’t afraid of something happening. I was afraid of nothing ever happening—nothing ever happening for all God’s eternity.”

===Chinese===
巴克原本在漫不经心地把雪茄灰往烟灰缸里掸，这会儿却开始有意无意地把烟灰在桌面上挪来挪去，画出些羽毛似的灰线，像是一幅地图。

"可那些小街虽然全都空空荡荡（这叫我神经有点发毛），我们愈往里钻，就有一桩我说不明白的事发生了。有时候在前方很远的地方——比方说隔着三个转弯或街角的地方——会突然炸开一阵响动，叮叮当当、喊声嘈杂，随即又停了。每逢这种响动一上来，就有一种我形容不出的东西——一种震颤或是踉跄顺着队伍传下来，仿佛整支队伍是个活物，脑袋挨了一下，又仿佛是一根通了电的线。我们谁也不知道自己为什么在往前挪，可我们就是在挪，在挤。然后定下神来，继续穿过那些肮脏的小街，绕过街角，爬上曲曲折折的坡道。那些歪歪扭扭的小街开始给我一种我说不出的感觉——像是在做梦。我觉得一切都丧失了理性，我们再也走不出这座迷宫了。听我这么说挺古怪，是不是？那些都是熟街熟巷，地图上一条条都标着。可事实就是这样。我怕的不是要出什么事。我怕的是永远什么也不会发生——在上帝的整个永恒里，永远什么也不发生。"

===Original===
He drained his glass and called for more whisky. He drank it, and went on.

“And then something did happen. Buck, it’s the solemn truth, that nothing has ever happened to you in your life. Nothing had ever happened to me in my life.”

“Nothing ever happened!” said Buck, staring. “What do you mean?”

“Nothing has ever happened,” repeated Barker, with a morbid obstinacy. “You don’t know what a thing happening means? You sit in your office expecting customers, and customers come; you walk in the street expecting friends, and friends meet you; you want a drink, and get it; you feel inclined for a bet, and make it. You expect either to win or lose, and you do either one or the other. But things happening!” and he shuddered ungovernably.

“Go on,” said Buck, shortly. “Get on.”

===Chinese===
他一饮而尽，又添了威士忌。喝下之后，接着说：

"后来，事就出了。巴克，这是千真万确的：你这一辈子里，从来没有过什么事发生在你身上。我这一辈子里，也从来没有过什么事发生在我身上。"

"从来没有过什么事发生！"巴克瞪着眼，"你这话什么意思？"

"从来没有过什么事发生，"巴克先生执拗地重复，那股劲近乎病态，"你不懂'有事发生'是什么意思吧？你坐在办公室里等主顾，主顾就来了；你在街上走，盼着朋友，朋友就碰上了；你想喝一杯，就喝上了；你手痒想赌一把，就赌了。你不是赢就是输，你也确确实实非赢即输。可'有事发生'！"他猛地打了个控制不住的寒噤。

"说下去，"巴克简短地说，"接着说。"

===Original===
“As we walked wearily round the corners, something happened. When something happens, it happens first, and you see it afterwards. It happens of itself, and you have nothing to do with it. It proves a dreadful thing—that there are other things besides one’s self. I can only put it in this way. We went round one turning, two turnings, three turnings, four turnings, five. Then I lifted myself slowly up from the gutter where I had been shot half senseless, and was beaten down again by living men crashing on top of me, and the world was full of roaring, and big men rolling about like ninepins.”

Buck looked at his map with knitted brows.

“Was that Portobello Road?” he asked.

“Yes,” said Barker—“yes; Portobello Road. I saw it afterwards; but, my God, what a place it was! Buck, have you ever stood and let a six foot of man lash and lash at your head with six feet of pole with six pounds of steel at the end? Because, when you have had that experience, as Walt Whitman says, ‘you reexamine philosophies and religions.’”

===Chinese===
"我们疲惫地绕过一个又一个街角，事，就出了。一件事要发生，总是它先发生，你随后才看见。它自顾自地发生，与你毫不相干。它证明了一桩可怕的事——在你自己之外，还有别的东西。我只能这么说。我们拐过一个弯，两个弯，三个弯，四个弯，五个。然后我才慢慢从阴沟里撑起身子——我早被打得半昏迷，栽在那儿——可活人又劈头盖脸地砸在我身上，把我压了下去，满世界都是吼叫，大汉们像九柱戏的木桩似的滚作一团。"

巴克皱着眉，看着自己那幅地图。

"那是波多贝罗路（Portobello Road）？"他问。

"对，"巴克先生说——"对，是波多贝罗路。我事后才看清；可是老天，那是怎样一个地方啊！巴克，你可曾站着，任凭一个六英尺高的汉子拿一根六英尺长的杆子——杆头还带着六磅重的钢——一下一下朝你脑袋抽过来？因为等你尝过这滋味，就会像惠特曼（Walt Whitman）所说的那样：'你重新审视种种哲学与宗教。'"

===Original===
“I have no doubt,” said Buck. “If that was Portobello Road, don’t you see what happened?”

“I know what happened exceedingly well. I was knocked down four times; an experience which, as I say, has an effect on the mental attitude. And another thing happened, too. I knocked down two men. After the fourth fall (there was not much bloodshed—more brutal rushing and throwing—for nobody could use their weapons), after the fourth fall, I say, I got up like a devil, and I tore a poleaxe out of a man’s hand and struck where I saw the scarlet of Wayne’s fellows, struck again and again. Two of them went over, bleeding on the stones, thank God; and I laughed and found myself sprawling in the gutter again, and got up again, and struck again, and broke my halberd to pieces. I hurt a man’s head, though.”

Buck set down his glass with a bang, and spat out curses through his thick moustache.

“What is the matter?” asked Barker, stopping, for the man had been calm up to now, and now his agitation was far more violent than his own.

===Chinese===
"这我相信，"巴克说，"如果那是波多贝罗路，你难道看不出事情是怎样发生的？"

"事情怎样发生，我清楚得很。我被砸倒四次；这种经历，我说过，会影响一个人的心态。另外还发生了一件事：我也砸倒了两个人。第四次倒地之后（其实没怎么流血——多半是野蛮的冲撞和摔扑——因为谁都使不上手里的家伙），我说，第四次倒地之后，我像恶鬼似的跳起来，从一个汉子手里硬夺过一柄长戟，朝着韦恩那伙人穿猩红衣服的地方就劈，一劈再劈。他们有两个栽倒了，在石头上淌血，谢天谢地；我大笑一声，却发现自己又摊手摊脚地栽进了阴沟，再爬起来，再劈，把那柄戟劈成了碎片。不过我劈伤了一个人的脑袋。"

巴克砰地放下酒杯，从浓髭后面啐出一串咒骂。

"怎么了？"巴克先生停下来问——这人方才一直很镇定，此刻那激动反倒远甚于他自己。

===Original===
“The matter?” said Buck, bitterly; “don’t you see how these maniacs have got us? Why should two idiots, one a clown and the other a screaming lunatic, make sane men so different from themselves? Look here, Barker; I will give you a picture. A very well-bred young man of this century is dancing about in a frock-coat. He has in his hands a nonsensical seventeenth-century halberd, with which he is trying to kill men in a street in Notting Hill. Damn it! don’t you see how they’ve got us? Never mind how you felt—that is how you looked. The King would put his cursed head on one side and call it exquisite. The Provost of Notting Hill would put his cursed nose in the air and call it heroic. But in Heaven’s name what would you have called it—two days before?”

Barker bit his lip.

“You haven’t been through it, Buck,” he said. “You don’t understand fighting—the atmosphere.”

“I don’t deny the atmosphere,” said Buck, striking the table. “I only say it’s their atmosphere. It’s Adam Wayne’s atmosphere. It’s the atmosphere which you and I thought had vanished from an educated world forever.”

===Chinese===
"怎么了？"巴克酸涩地说，"你没看出这帮疯子是怎样拿住我们的吗？凭什么两个白痴——一个小丑，另一个是个尖叫的疯子——竟能叫神智正常的人变得跟他们自己判若两人？听着，巴克先生；我给你画一幅像。本世纪一个教养极好的青年，穿着燕尾服在那儿又蹦又跳。他手里握着一把荒唐的十七世纪长戟，正想用它在一截诺丁山的街上杀人。他妈的！你难道看不出他们怎样拿住了我们？别管你当时是什么感觉——你那副模样就是这样。国王准会把他那颗该死的脑袋一歪，称之为'妙不可言'。诺丁山区督准会把他那只该死的鼻子朝天一翘，称之为'英勇'。可凭老天爷说，要是搁在两天以前，你自己会管这叫什么？"

巴克先生咬着嘴唇。

"你没亲身经过，巴克，"他说，"你不懂打仗——那种气氛。"

"我不否认那气氛，"巴克敲着桌子说，"我只是说，那是他们的气氛。是亚当·韦恩（Adam Wayne）的气氛。是你我原以为早已从这个文明世界里永远消失的那种气氛。"

===Original===
“Well, it hasn’t,” said Barker; “and if you have any lingering doubts, lend me a poleaxe, and I’ll show you.”

There was a long silence, and then Buck turned to his neighbour and spoke in that good-tempered tone that comes of a power of looking facts in the face—the tone in which he concluded great bargains.

“Barker,” he said, “you are right. This old thing—this fighting, has come back. It has come back suddenly and taken us by surprise. So it is first blood to Adam Wayne. But, unless reason and arithmetic and everything else have gone crazy, it must be next and last blood to us. But when an issue has really arisen, there is only one thing to do—to study that issue as such and win in it. Barker, since it is fighting, we must understand fighting. I must understand fighting as coolly and completely as I understand drapery; you must understand fighting as coolly and completely as you understand politics. Now, look at the facts. I stick without hesitation to my original formula. Fighting, when we have the stronger force, is only a matter of arithmetic. It must be. You asked me just now how two hundred men could defeat six hundred. I can tell you. Two hundred men can defeat six hundred when the six hundred behave like fools. When they forget the very conditions they are fighting in; when they fight in a swamp as if it were a mountain; when they fight in a forest as if it were a plain; when they fight in streets without remembering the object of streets.”

“What is the object of streets?” asked Barker.

“What is the object of supper?” cried Buck, furiously. “Isn’t it obvious? This military science is mere common sense. The object of a street is to lead from one place to another; therefore all streets join; therefore street fighting is quite a peculiar thing. You advanced into that hive of streets as if you were advancing into an open plain where you could see everything. Instead of that, you were advancing into the bowels of a fortress, with streets pointing at you, streets turning on you, streets jumping out at you, and all in the hands of the enemy. Do you know what Portobello Road is? It is the only point on your journey where two side streets run up opposite each other. Wayne massed his men on the two sides, and when he had let enough of your line go past, cut it in two like a worm. Don’t you see what would have saved you?”

===Chinese===
"哼，它没有消失，"巴克先生说，"你要是还有半分疑心，借我一柄长戟，我做给你看。"

一阵长久的沉默之后，巴克转向他的同伴，用那种由直面事实的本领所生出的好脾气口吻说起话来——他谈成大生意时，用的就是这种口吻。

"巴克先生，"他说，"你是对的。这件老古董——打仗这件事，又回来了。它来得猝不及防，杀我们一个措手不及。所以第一阵血，让亚当·韦恩赢了去。可除非理性、算术以及其他一切全都发了疯，下一阵血、也是最后那一阵血，必是我们的。不过，一旦当真冒出了一个问题，那就只有一件事好做——把它当作一个问题来研究，并在其中取胜。巴克先生，既然是打仗，我们就必须懂打仗。我必须像懂得布料那样冷静而透彻地懂得打仗；你必须像懂得政治那样冷静而透彻地懂得打仗。现在，看看事实。我毫不犹豫地坚持我最初的公式：当我们兵力更强时，打仗不过是算术。它必定如此。你刚才问我两百人怎么能打败六百人。我告诉你：当那六百人蠢得像白痴的时候，两百人就能打败六百人。当他们忘了自己作战所处的环境；当他们在沼泽里打，却当作是在山上打；当他们在森林里打，却当作是在平原上打；当他们在街上打，却忘了街道是做什么用的。"

"街道是做什么用的？"巴克先生问。

"晚饭是做什么用的？"巴克怒吼道，"这不是明摆着的吗？所谓兵法，不过是常识。一条街的用处，是从一个地方通到另一个地方；因此所有的街都是相通的；因此在街上打仗，是件颇为特别的事。你开进那一窝小街，像是开进一片一切都尽收眼底的开阔平原。其实呢，你是开进了一座堡垒的五脏六腑，一条条街指着你，一条条街朝你转身，一条条街朝你扑出来，而且全在敌人手里。你可知道波多贝罗路是什么地方？那是你那一路上唯一一个两条横街正面相对的地方。韦恩把人马埋伏在这两侧，等你的队伍过去够多了，就像切一条虫子似的，把它一刀两段。你难道看不出，本该怎样做才能救你？"

===Original===
Barker shook his head.

“Can’t your ‘atmosphere’ help you?” asked Buck, bitterly. “Must I attempt explanations in the romantic manner? Suppose that, as you were fighting blindly with the red Notting Hillers who imprisoned you on both sides, you had heard a shout from behind them. Suppose, oh, romantic Barker! that behind the red tunics you had seen the blue and gold of South Kensington taking them in the rear, surrounding them in their turn and hurling them on to your halberds.”

“If the thing had been possible,” began Barker, cursing.

“The thing would have been as possible,” said Buck, simply, “as simple as arithmetic. There are a certain number of street entries that lead to Pump Street. There are not nine hundred; there are not nine million. They do not grow in the night. They do not increase like mushrooms. It must be possible, with such an overwhelming force as we have, to advance by all of them at once. In every one of the arteries, or approaches, we can put almost as many men as Wayne can put into the field altogether. Once do that, and we have him to demonstration. It is like a proposition of Euclid.”

“You think that is certain?” said Barker, anxious, but dominated delightfully.

“I’ll tell you what I think,” said Buck, getting up jovially. “I think Adam Wayne made an uncommonly spirited little fight; and I think I am confoundedly sorry for him.”

“Buck, you are a great man!” cried Barker, rising also. “You’ve knocked me sensible again. I am ashamed to say it, but I was getting romantic. Of course, what you say is adamantine sense. Fighting, being physical, must be mathematical. We were beaten because we were neither mathematical nor physical nor anything else—because we deserved to be beaten. Hold all the approaches, and with our force we must have him. When shall we open the next campaign?”

“Now,” said Buck, and walked out of the bar.

“Now!” cried Barker, following him eagerly. “Do you mean now? It is so late.”

Buck turned on him, stamping.

“Do you think fighting is under the Factory Acts?” he said; and he called a cab. “Notting Hill Gate Station,” he said; and the two drove off.

===Chinese===
巴克先生摇摇头。

"你那'气氛'帮不了你么？"巴克酸酸地问，"难道非要我用浪漫派的路子给你解说一番不成？假设，你正与那群从两面夹住你的红衣诺丁山人盲目厮打时，忽然听见他们身后响起一声呐喊。假设——噢，浪漫的巴克先生！——在那一片红衫背后，你看见了南肯辛顿的蓝底金光，从他们屁股后面杀上来，反过来把他们包围，朝你们的戟尖上摔过去。"

"这事要是办得到——"巴克先生开口骂道。

"这事当然办得到，"巴克平淡地说，"和算术一样简单。通到泵街去的街口一共就那么多。没有九百个；没有九百万个。它们夜里不会长出来。它们不会像蘑菇那样冒。凭我们这样压倒性的兵力，要同时从所有的街口一齐推进，必然是办得到的。每一条通道、每一个入口，我们都能塞进几乎和韦恩全军一样多的人。一旦做到这一点，他便铁定落网。这就像欧几里得（Euclid）的一条定理。"

"你觉得这是板上钉钉的？"巴克先生说，心里发急，却又心悦诚服地被他镇住。

"我告诉你我怎么想，"巴克乐呵呵地站起身来，"我想，亚当·韦恩打了一场极其漂亮的小仗；我还想，我真是该死地替他可惜。"

"巴克，你是个伟人！"巴克先生也站起来喊道，"你一拳把我打回了神智。说来惭愧，我方才竟浪漫起来了。当然，你说的都是铁一般的道理。打仗既是件体力活，就必然是数学。我们之所以吃败仗，是因为我们既不数学，又不体力，别的什么都不沾——因为我们活该吃败仗。守住所有的入口，凭我们的兵力，必定拿下他。下一场仗什么时候打？"

"现在，"巴克说着，走出了酒吧。

"现在！"巴克先生急切地追着他喊，"你说现在？天都这么晚了。"

巴克转过身来，顿着脚。

"你以为打仗也归工厂法管？"他说；随即叫了一辆马车。"诺丁山门站（Notting Hill Gate Station），"他说；两人便驱车而去。

===Original===
A genuine reputation can sometimes be made in an hour. Buck, in the next sixty or eighty minutes, showed himself a really great man of action. His cab carried him like a thunderbolt from the King to Wilson, from Wilson to Swindon, from Swindon to Barker again; if his course was jagged, it had the jaggedness of the lightning. Only two things he carried with him—his inevitable cigar and the map of North Kensington and Notting Hill. There were, as he again and again pointed out, with every variety of persuasion and violence, only nine possible ways of approaching Pump Street within a quarter of a mile round it; three out of Westbourne Grove, two out of Ladbroke Grove, and four out of Notting Hill High Street. And he had detachments of two hundred each, stationed at every one of the entrances before the last green of that strange sunset had sunk out of the black sky.

The sky was particularly black, and on this alone was one false protest raised against the triumphant optimism of the Provost of North Kensington. He overruled it with his infectious common sense.

“There is no such thing,” he said, “as night in London. You have only to follow the line of street lamps. Look, here is the map. Two hundred purple North Kensington soldiers under myself march up Ossington Street, two hundred more under Captain Bruce, of the North Kensington Guard, up Clanricarde Gardens.1 Two hundred yellow West Kensingtons under Provost Swindon attack from Pembridge Road. Two hundred more of my men from the eastern streets, leading away from Queen’s Road. Two detachments of yellows enter by two roads from Westbourne Grove. Lastly, two hundred green Bayswaters come down from the North through Chepstow Place, and two hundred more under Provost Wilson himself, through the upper part of Pembridge Road. Gentlemen, it is mate in two moves. The enemy must either mass in Pump Street and be cut to pieces; or they must retreat past the Gaslight & Coke Co., and rush on my four hundred; or they must retreat past St. Luke’s Church, and rush on the six hundred from the West. Unless we are all mad, it’s plain. Come on. To your quarters and await Captain Brace’s signal to advance. Then you have only to walk up a line of gas-lamps and smash this nonsense by pure mathematics. Tomorrow we shall all be civilians again.”

===Chinese===
真正的声名，有时一个钟头便能挣来。在接下来的六七十分钟里，巴克显出他是一个真正了不起的实干家。他的马车像雷霆似的载着他，从国王那儿奔到威尔逊那儿，从威尔逊那儿奔到斯温登那儿，又从斯温登那儿奔回巴克先生这儿；他的行迹纵然折来折去，却有着闪电的那种折法。他随身只带两样东西——那根永不离口的雪茄，和一张北肯辛顿与诺丁山的地图。他不厌其烦地、一会儿软劝一会儿硬压地反复指出：在泵街周围四分之一英里之内，可能逼近泵街的入口一共只有九条——三条出自韦斯特伯恩格罗夫（Westbourne Grove），两条出自拉德布罗克格罗夫（Ladbroke Grove），四条出自诺丁山高街（Notting Hill High Street）。在那场怪异夕阳的最后一点残绿沉入漆黑天幕之前，他已经把每路两百人的分队，派到了每一个入口。

天色黑得出奇，唯独这一点，引来了一句唱反调的话，反对北肯辛顿区督那压倒一切的乐观。他用他那富于感染力的常识把它压了下去。

"在伦敦，"他说，"压根儿没有'夜晚'这种东西。你们只消顺着那一溜路灯走。瞧，这是地图。两百名北肯辛顿紫衣兵，由我亲自带领，从奥辛顿街（Ossington Street）开上；另有两百名，由北肯辛顿卫队的布鲁斯上尉（Captain Bruce）带领，从克兰里卡德花园（Clanricarde Gardens）开上。两百名西肯辛顿黄衣兵，在斯温登区督带领下，从彭布里奇路（Pembridge Road）发起进攻。再有两百名我的人，从王后街（Queen's Road）向东延伸出去的那几条街出动。两支黄衣分队，从韦斯特伯恩格罗夫的两条路进入。最后，两百名贝斯沃特绿衣兵从北面经切普斯托广场（Chepstow Place）下来，另有两百名，在威尔逊区督亲自带领之下，经彭布里奇路的上段进入。诸位，这是两步将杀。敌人要么聚在泵街里被剁成肉酱；要么从煤气灯与焦炭公司（Gaslight & Coke Co.）旁边退过去，撞上我这四百人；要么从圣路加教堂（St. Luke's Church）旁边退过去，撞上西边来的那六百人。除非我们全都疯了，否则这是明摆着的事。出发。各回各的岗位，等候布鲁斯上尉的出击信号。到时候，你们只需沿着一路煤气灯走上前去，凭纯粹的数学，把这桩荒唐事砸个粉碎。到明天，我们又全是平民了。"

===Original===
His optimism glowed like a great fire in the night, and ran round the terrible ring in which Wayne was now held helpless. The fight was already over. One man’s energy for one hour had saved the city from war.

For the next ten minutes Buck walked up and down silently beside the motionless clump of his two hundred. He had not changed his appearance in any way, except to sling across his yellow overcoat a case with a revolver in it. So that his light-clad modern figure showed up oddly beside the pompous purple uniforms of his halberdiers, which darkly but richly coloured the black night.

At length a shrill trumpet rang from some way up the street; it was the signal of advance. Buck briefly gave the word, and the whole purple line, with its dimly shining steel, moved up the side alley. Before it was a slope of street, long, straight, and shining in the dark. It was a sword pointed at Pump Street, the heart at which nine other swords were pointed that night.

===Chinese===
他的乐观像黑夜中的一团大火，熊熊发光，沿着那道把韦恩困得动弹不得的可怕的铁环奔流。胜负已定。一个人的精力，用一个钟头，便使这座城免于一场战争。

接下来的十分钟里，巴克默默地在他那两百人凝然不动的人群旁来回踱步。他的装束毫无改变，只是在黄外衣外面斜挎了一只装左轮手枪的枪套。于是，他这身轻装的、现代派的人影，在他那些戟兵的华丽紫制服旁边显得颇为古怪——那些紫制服沉沉地、却又华美地点染着黑夜。

终于，街头远处响起一声尖厉的号角；那是出击的信号。巴克简短地下达了命令，整条紫色的队伍连同那隐隐发亮的钢尖，便沿着侧巷开拔。在他们面前是一道长长、笔直、在暗夜里闪闪发亮的坡形街道。那是一柄直指泵街的剑——今夜，另有九柄剑也指着同一颗心脏。

===Original===
A quarter of an hour’s silent marching brought them almost within earshot of any tumult in the doomed citadel. But still there was no sound and no sign of the enemy. This time, at any rate, they knew that they were closing in on it mechanically, and they marched on under the lamplight and the dark without any of that eerie sense of ignorance which Barker had felt when entering the hostile country by one avenue alone.

“Halt—point arms!” cried Buck, suddenly, and as he spoke there came a clatter of feet tumbling along the stones. But the halberds were levelled in vain. The figure that rushed up was a messenger from the contingent of the North.

“Victory, Mr. Buck!” he cried, panting; “they are ousted. Provost Wilson of Bayswater has taken Pump Street.”

Buck ran forward in his excitement.

“Then, which way are they retreating? It must be either by St. Luke’s to meet Swindon, or by the Gas Company to meet us. Run like mad to Swindon, and see that the yellows are holding the St. Luke’s Road. We will hold this, never fear. We have them in an iron trap. Run!”

===Chinese===
一刻钟默不作声的行军，已把他们带到几乎听得见那座注定要陷落的堡垒里任何骚动的距离之内。可仍然没有一点声响，也没有敌人的一丝影子。这一回，无论如何，他们知道自己正机械地收紧包围，他们在灯光与黑暗之下向前推进，再没有那种巴克先生单从一条路进入敌境时所感到的、叫人毛骨悚然的无知之感。

"立定——举戟！"巴克忽然喊道。话音未落，便传来一阵脚步踉跄踩过石板的声响。可那一排戟却是白举了。冲上来的那个人影，是从北面分队来的传令兵。

"胜利了，巴克先生！"他喘着气喊道，"他们被赶出来了。贝斯沃特的威尔逊区督已经拿下了泵街。"

巴克激动地冲上前去。

"那他们朝哪个方向退？要么是走圣路加那条路去撞斯温登，要么是走煤气公司那条路来撞我们。你拼着命跑去告诉斯温登，看住那些黄衣兵，把圣路加路守住。这一头我们来把，放心。我们已经把他们关进了铁笼子。快去！"

===Original===
As the messenger dashed away into the darkness, the great guard of North Kensington swung on with the certainty of a machine. Yet scarcely a hundred yards further their halberd-points again fell in line gleaming in the gaslight; for again a clatter of feet was heard on the stones, and again it proved to be only the messenger.

“Mr. Provost,” he said, “the yellow West Kensingtons have been holding the road by St. Luke’s for twenty minutes since the capture of Pump Street. Pump Street is not two hundred yards away; they cannot be retreating down that road.”

“Then they are retreating down this,” said Provost Buck, with a final cheerfulness, “and by good fortune down a well-lighted road, though it twists about. Forward!”

As they moved along the last three hundred yards of their journey, Buck fell, for the first time in his life, perhaps, into a kind of philosophical reverie, for men of his type are always made kindly, and as it were melancholy, by success.

===Chinese===
传令兵冲进黑暗里去了，北肯辛顿这支大军便像机器一般笃定地继续推进。可还没走出一百码，他们的戟尖又在煤气灯光里排成了一线闪光——因为石板路上又一次响起了脚步声，又一次，来人只是那个传令兵。

"区督大人，"他说，"自从拿下泵街之后，西肯辛顿的黄衣兵已在圣路加那条路上守了二十分钟。泵街离这儿不到两百码；他们不可能从那条路上退下去。"

"那他们就是从这一条退下去，"巴克区督带着一种终结的快活说，"而且幸亏退的是一条灯光明亮的路，虽说它拐来拐去。前进！"

当他们走上旅程的最后三百码时，巴克平生第一次——也许是头一遭——陷入了一种哲思般的遐想，因为他这种类型的人，一经成功，总会变得和善，近乎忧郁。

===Original===
“I am sorry for poor old Wayne, I really am,” he thought. “He spoke up splendidly for me at that Council. And he blacked old Barker’s eye with considerable spirit. But I don’t see what a man can expect when he fights against arithmetic, to say nothing of civilisation. And what a wonderful hoax all this military genius is! I suspect I’ve just discovered what Cromwell discovered, that a sensible tradesman is the best general, and that a man who can buy men and sell men can lead and kill them. The thing’s simply like adding up a column in a ledger. If Wayne has two hundred men, he can’t put two hundred men in nine places at once. If they’re ousted from Pump Street they’re flying somewhere. If they’re not flying past the church they’re flying past the Works. And so we have them. We business men should have no chance at all except that cleverer people than we get bees in their bonnets that prevent them from reasoning properly—so we reason alone. And so I, who am comparatively stupid, see things as God sees them, as a vast machine. My God, what’s this?” and he clapped his hands to his eyes and staggered back.

Then through the darkness he cried in a dreadful voice—

“Did I blaspheme God? I am struck blind.”

“What?” wailed another voice behind him, the voice of a certain Wilfred Jarvis of North Kensington.

“Blind!” cried Buck; “blind!”

“I’m blind too!” cried Jarvis, in an agony.

“Fools, all of you,” said a gross voice behind them; “we’re all blind. The lamps have gone out.”

“The lamps! But why? where?” cried Buck, turning furiously in the darkness. “How are we to get on? How are we to chase the enemy? Where have they gone?”

“The enemy went—” said the rough voice behind, and then stopped doubtfully.

“Where?” shouted Buck, stamping like a madman.

“They went,” said the gruff voice, “past the Gas Works, and they’ve used their chance.”

“Great God!” thundered Buck, and snatched at his revolver; “do you mean they’ve turned out—”

But almost before he had spoken the words, he was hurled like a stone from catapult into the midst of his own men.

“Notting Hill! Notting Hill!” cried frightful voices out of the darkness, and they seemed to come from all sides, for the men of North Kensington, unacquainted with the road, had lost all their bearings in the black world of blindness.

“Notting Hill! Notting Hill!” cried the invisible people, and the invaders were hewn down horribly with black steel, with steel that gave no glint against any light.

===Chinese===
"我真替可怜的老韦恩惋惜，真是惋惜，"他想，"在那次区督议会（Council）上，他替我说过极漂亮的话。他还挺有气势地一拳把老巴克先生的眼睛打乌了。可我看不出，一个人跟算术作对——更不必说跟整个文明作对——还能指望什么。再说，所谓军事天才，原来是个多么妙的骗局！我疑心我刚刚发现了克伦威尔（Cromwell）当年发现的东西：一个精明的生意人才是最好的将军；一个能把人买进卖出的人，就能率领人、杀死人。这事儿简直就像把账簿上一栏数字加起来。韦恩若只有两百人，就不能同时把两百人放到九个地方。他们既被赶出泵街，就一定正在朝某处逃。他们若没从教堂那儿跑，就是从煤气厂那儿跑。于是我们就拿住了他们。我们这些生意人本不会有半点机会，要不是那些比我们聪明的人脑壳里钻进了蜜蜂，弄得他们不能好好推理——于是只剩我们来推理。于是，我这个相对愚笨的人，倒能像上帝那样看事物，看成一部庞大的机器。我的天，这是什么？"他一把捂住眼睛，踉跄着倒退。

随即，他在黑暗里用一个骇人的声音喊道——

"我亵渎了上帝吗？我瞎了。"

"什么？"身后另一个声音哀号道，那是北肯辛顿一个叫威尔弗雷德·贾维斯（Wilfred Jarvis）的人的声音。

"瞎了！"巴克喊道，"瞎了！"

"我也瞎了！"贾维斯痛苦地喊。

"你们这帮蠢货，"他们身后一个粗哑的声音说，"我们全瞎了。灯，灭了。"

"灯！可为什么？在哪儿？"巴克在黑暗里暴怒地转着，"我们还怎么走？还怎么追敌人？他们上哪儿去了？"

"敌人走了——"身后那个粗哑的声音说，随即又迟疑地停住。

"哪儿？"巴克疯子似的顿着脚喊。

"他们走了，"那粗哑的声音说，"从煤气厂旁边过去，而且他们抓住了这个机会。"

"老天！"巴克一声怒吼，伸手去拔左轮手枪，"你是说他们把——"

可几乎还没等他说完这句话，他就像弹弓发出的一块石头，被抛进了自己队伍的人堆里。

"诺丁山！诺丁山！"黑暗中响起可怖的喊声，而且仿佛从四面八方涌来，因为北肯辛顿的兵不熟这条路，在那盲目的黑色世界里已完全辨不清方向。

"诺丁山！诺丁山！"看不见的人群喊着，入侵者被黑色的钢刀可怖地砍翻——那钢刀黑得不反一点光，任何灯光都照不出一丝寒芒。

===Original===
Buck, though badly maimed with the blow of a halberd, kept an angry but splendid sanity. He groped madly for the wall and found it. Struggling with crawling fingers along it, he found a side opening and retreated into it with the remnants of his men. Their adventures during that prodigious night are not to be described. They did not know whether they were going towards or away from the enemy. Not knowing where they themselves were, or where their opponents were, it was mere irony to ask where was the rest of their army. For a thing had descended upon them which London does not know—darkness, which was before the stars were made, and they were as much lost in it as if they had been made before the stars. Every now and then, as those frightful hours wore on, they buffeted in the darkness against living men, who struck at them and at whom they struck, with an idiot fury. When at last the grey dawn came, they found they had wandered back to the edge of the Uxbridge Road. They found that in those horrible eyeless encounters, the North Kensingtons and the Bayswaters and the West Kensingtons had again and again met and butchered each other, and they heard that Adam Wayne was barricaded in Pump Street.

===Chinese===
巴克虽被一戟砍成重伤，却保持着一种愤怒而辉煌的神智。他发狂似的摸向墙壁，摸到了。他用爬动的手指沿着墙一点点挪，摸到了一个岔口，便带着残兵退了进去。他们那个惊天地的一夜里的种种遭遇，难以尽述。他们不知道自己是在朝敌人走，还是远离敌人。连自己在哪儿都不知道，连对手在哪儿都不知道，再问大军其余部分在哪儿，便纯属讽刺了。因为一桩东西降临到了他们头上——一桩伦敦所不知道的东西：那是在群星未被造出之前就存在着的黑暗；他们迷失其中，仿佛自己也是群星诞生之前所造之物。在那几个可怖的钟头里，他们不时在黑暗中撞上活人，对方砍他们，他们也砍对方，带着一种痴狂的怒火。等到灰白的黎明终于来临，他们发觉自己竟已漂泊回了乌克斯桥路（Uxbridge Road）边上。他们这才得知，在那几次可怖的盲目相撞之中，北肯辛顿人、贝斯沃特人和西肯辛顿人一次又一次地相遇，互相屠戮；而他们又听说，亚当·韦恩已设下街垒，据守在泵街。

## II. The Correspondent of the Court Journal / 第二章 《宫廷杂志》的通讯员

===Original===
Journalism had become, like most other such things in England under the cautious government and philosophy represented by James Barker, somewhat sleepy and much diminished in importance. This was partly due to the disappearance of party government and public speaking, partly to the compromise or deadlock which had made foreign wars impossible, but mostly, of course, to the temper of the whole nation which was that of a people in a kind of backwater. Perhaps the most well known of the remaining newspapers was the Court Journal, which was published in a dusty but genteel-looking office just out of Kensington High Street. For when all the papers of a people have been for years growing more and more dim and decorous and optimistic, the dimmest and most decorous and most optimistic is very likely to win. In the journalistic competition which was still going on at the beginning of the twentieth century, the final victor was the Court Journal.

===Chinese===
在詹姆斯·巴克所代表的那种小心翼翼的政务与哲学之下，英格兰的新闻业——正如大多数别的事物一样——变得有些昏昏欲睡，重要性也大为缩减。这一半是因为党派政治与公开演说的消失，一半是因为那种使对外战争沦为不可能的妥协或僵局，但最主要的，当然还是由于整个民族的心境——那是一种身处一潭死水之中的国民心境。在残存的几份报纸里，最出名的或许要数《宫廷杂志》（Court Journal），它编辑部设在肯辛顿高街外头一间灰尘扑扑、却看着颇为体面的办公室里。因为，当一个民族的全部报纸多年来都愈发黯淡、愈发端庄、愈发乐观之时，那最黯淡、最端庄、最乐观的一份，往往最有可能胜出。在那场一直延续到二十世纪初的新闻业角逐中，最终的赢家便是《宫廷杂志》。

===Original===
For some mysterious reason the King had a great affection for hanging about in the Court Journal office, smoking a morning cigarette and looking over files. Like all ingrainedly idle men, he was very fond of lounging and chatting in places where other people were doing work. But one would have thought that, even in the prosaic England of his day, he might have found a more bustling centre.

On this particular morning, however, he came out of Kensington Palace with a more alert step and a busier air than usual. He wore an extravagantly long frock-coat, a pale-green waistcoat, a very full and dégagé black tie, and curious yellow gloves. This was his uniform as Colonel of a regiment of his own creation, the 1st Decadents Green. It was a beautiful sight to see him drilling them. He walked quickly across the Park and the High Street, lighting his cigarette as he went, and flung open the door of the Court Journal office.

“You’ve heard the news, Pally—you’ve heard the news?” he said.

===Chinese===
出于某种神秘的原因，国王极爱在《宫廷杂志》编辑部里消磨时光，抽一根晨间香烟，翻翻过期的合订本。像一切积重难返的懒人一样，他格外喜欢在别人正干活的地方闲荡聊天。不过照理说，即便在他那个乏味的英格兰，他本也该找得到一处更热闹的中心。

然而就在这个特别的早晨，他从肯辛顿宫（Kensington Palace）出来时，步子比平常更轻快，神情比平常更忙碌。他穿一件长得夸张的燕尾服，一件淡绿色马甲，一条系得十分松散随意（dégagé）的黑领结，戴着一双古怪的黄手套。这是他自创一团、自任上校的"第一颓废绿团"（1st Decadents Green）的军服。看他操练那个团，是一桩赏心乐事。他快步穿过御苑和高街，边走边点着香烟，一把推开了《宫廷杂志》编辑部的门。

"你听到消息没有，帕利——你听到消息没有？"他说。

===Original===
The Editor’s name was Hoskins, but the King called him Pally, which was an abbreviation of Paladium of our Liberties.

“Well, your Majesty,” said Hoskins, slowly (he was a worried, gentlemanly looking person, with a wandering brown beard)—“well, your Majesty, I have heard rather curious things, but I—”

“You’ll hear more of them,” said the King, dancing a few steps of a kind of negro shuffle. “You’ll hear more of them, my blood-and-thunder tribune. Do you know what I am going to do for you?”

“No, your Majesty,” replied the Paladium, vaguely.

“I’m going to put your paper on strong, dashing, enterprising lines,” said the King. “Now, where are your posters of last night’s defeat?”

“I did not propose, your Majesty,” said the Editor, “to have any posters exactly—”

“Paper, paper!” cried the King, wildly; “bring me paper as big as a house. I’ll do you posters. Stop, I must take my coat off.” He began removing that garment with an air of set intensity, flung it playfully at Mr. Hoskins’ head, entirely enveloping him, and looked at himself in the glass. “The coat off,” he said, “and the hat on. That looks like a subeditor. It is indeed the very essence of subediting. Well,” he continued, turning round abruptly, “come along with that paper.”

===Chinese===
主编名叫霍斯金斯（Hoskins），可国王偏叫他"帕利"（Pally），那是"我们自由的帕拉第昂"（Paladium of our Liberties，帕拉第昂原指特洛伊护城神像，引申为邦国命脉所系的守护物）的缩写。

"这个，陛下，"霍斯金斯慢吞吞地说（他是个满面愁容、看着颇有绅士派头的人，生着一把乱蓬蓬的棕胡子）——"这个，陛下，我确是听到了些颇为古怪的事，可是我——"

"你还会听到更多，"国王说，跳了几步一种黑人曳步舞。'你还会听到更多，我那位血肉横飞的保民官。你可知道我要为你做什么？"

"不知道，陛下，"那位"帕拉第昂"茫然答道。

"我要把你这份报纸办得雄健、泼辣、有闯劲，"国王说，"那么，你昨晚那场败仗的招贴画在哪儿？"

"我并不打算，陛下，"主编说，"出什么确切的招贴——"

"纸，纸！"国王狂野地喊，"给我拿纸来，要像房子那么大的。我来给你画招贴。等等，我得把外衣脱了。"他一脸决绝地开始脱那件衣服，嬉皮笑脸地朝霍斯金斯先生头上掷去，把他整个儿罩住，然后照了照镜子。"外衣脱了，"他说，"帽子戴上。这看着就像个副主编。这的确正是副主编的精髓所在。好啦，"他猛地转过身，接着说，"把纸拿来。"

===Original===
The Paladium had only just extricated himself reverently from the folds of the King’s frock-coat, and said bewildered—

“I am afraid, your Majesty—”

“Oh, you’ve got no enterprise,” said Auberon. “What’s that roll in the corner? Wallpaper? Decorations for your private residence? Art in the home, Pally? Fling it over here, and I’ll paint such posters on the back of it that when you put it up in your drawing-room you’ll paste the original pattern against the wall.” And the King unrolled the wallpaper, spreading it over the whole floor. “Now give me the scissors,” he cried, and took them himself before the other could stir.

He slit the paper into about five pieces, each nearly as big as a door. Then he took a big blue pencil, and went down on his knees on the dusty oilcloth and began to write on them, in huge letters—

“From the front.

					General Buck defeated.

					Darkness, danger, and death.

					Wayne said to be in Pump Street.

					Feeling in the city.”

===Chinese===
那位"帕拉第昂"才恭恭敬敬地从国王燕尾服的褶皱里把自己解救出来，迷迷糊糊地说——

"我怕是，陛下——"

"噢，你这个人一点闯劲都没有，"奥伯伦说，"角落里那一卷是什么？糊墙纸？给你私宅添置的装饰？家中的艺术，帕利？把它扔过来，我在它背面画上那样一些招贴，等你把它贴进客厅，你准会把它原先的花纹朝墙贴。"国王把那卷糊墙纸展开，铺满了整整一地。"现在把剪刀给我，"他喊，还没等对方动弹，便自己一把抢了过去。

他把那卷纸裁成大约五张，每张都差不多有一扇门那么大。然后他拿起一支粗蓝铅笔，在落满灰尘的油毡上跪了下来，开始用斗大的字在上面写——

"来自前线。

					巴克将军败北。

					黑暗、危险与死亡。

					传闻韦恩据守泵街。

					本城群情。"

===Original===
He contemplated it for some time, with his head on one side, and got up, with a sigh.

“Not quite intense enough,” he said—“not alarming. I want the Court Journal to be feared as well as loved. Let’s try something more hard-hitting.” And he went down on his knees again. After sucking the blue pencil for some time, he began writing again busily. “How will this do?” he said—

“Wayne’s wonderful victory.”

“I suppose,” he said, looking up appealingly, and sucking the pencil—“I suppose we couldn’t say ‘wictory’—‘Wayne’s wonderful wictory’? No, no. Refinement, Pally, refinement. I have it.”

“Wayne wins.

					Astounding fight in the dark.

					The gas-lamps in their courses fought against Buck.”

“(Nothing like our fine old English translation.) What else can we say? Well, anything to annoy old Buck;” and he added, thoughtfully, in smaller letters—

“Rumoured Court-martial on General Buck.”

“Those will do for the present,” he said, and turned them both face downwards. “Paste, please.”

===Chinese===
他歪着头端详了一会儿，叹了口气，站起身来。

"火候还不够，"他说——"不够骇人。我要《宫廷杂志》既为人所爱，又为人所惧。来点更猛的试试。"于是他又跪了下去。把那支蓝铅笔吮了半天之后，又忙碌地写起来。"这个怎么样？"他说——

"韦恩的辉煌大捷。"

"我看，"他抬起头，央求似的望着，一边吮着铅笔——"我看我们能不能写成'辉皇大捷'——'韦恩的辉皇大捷'（原文明为 alliteration 头韵 'Wayne's wonderful wictory'，故意把 victory 儿化作 wictory）？不，不成。要雅致，帕利，要雅致。有了。"

"韦恩获胜。

					暗夜中的惊天鏖战。

					路灯循其轨道，与巴克争战。"

（这出自《旧约·士师记》"星宿从其轨道，与西西拉争战"的戏仿——到底还是我们那部优美的古英文《圣经》译本好啊。）我们还能说点什么？嗯，凡是叫老巴克窝火的都行；"他一边若有所思地用更小的字添道——

"传闻将对巴克将军进行军法审判。"

"这两张眼下够用了，"他说着，把两张都翻过去反扣着。"浆糊，劳驾。"

===Original===
The Paladium, with an air of great terror, brought the paste out of an inner room.

The King slabbed it on with the enjoyment of a child messing with treacle. Then taking one of his huge compositions fluttering in each hand, he ran outside, and began pasting them up in prominent positions over the front of the office.

“And now,” said Auberon, entering again with undiminished vivacity—“now for the leading article.”

He picked up another of the large strips of wallpaper, and, laying it across a desk, pulled out a fountain-pen and began writing with feverish intensity, reading clauses and fragments aloud to himself, and rolling them on his tongue like wine, to see if they had the pure journalistic flavour.

===Chinese===
那位"帕拉第昂"一脸惊恐，从里间端出浆糊来。

国王像个拿糖浆胡闹的小孩一样，乐在其中地把浆糊厚厚地糊上去。然后两手各拎着一张他那巨作，迎风招展地跑到外头，把它们张贴在编辑部门面最显眼的位置上。

"现在，"奥伯伦又走进来，兴头丝毫不减——"现在来写社论。"

他又抄起一大张糊墙纸条，横摊在一张书桌上，拔出自来水笔，狂热地写了起来，时不时把一些半句、残句大声念给自己听，像品酒一样把它们在舌尖上滚来滚去，看看是否具有那纯粹的新闻风味。

===Original===
“The news of the disaster to our forces in Notting Hill, awful as it is—awful as it is—(no, distressing as it is), may do some good if it draws attention to the what’s-his-name inefficiency (scandalous inefficiency, of course) of the government’s preparations. In our present state of information, it would be premature (what a jolly word!)—it would be premature to cast any reflections upon the conduct of General Buck, whose services upon so many stricken fields (ha, ha!), and whose honourable scars and laurels, give him a right to have judgment upon him at least suspended. But there is one matter on which we must speak plainly. We have been silent on it too long, from feelings, perhaps of mistaken caution, perhaps of mistaken loyalty. This situation would never have arisen but for what we can only call the indefensible conduct of the King. It pains us to say such things, but, speaking as we do in the public interests (I plagiarise from Barker’s famous epigram), we shall not shrink because of the distress we may cause to any individual, even the most exalted. At this crucial moment of our country, the voice of the People demands with a single tongue, ‘Where is the King?’ What is he doing while his subjects tear each other in pieces in the streets of a great city? Are his amusements and his dissipations (of which we cannot pretend to be ignorant) so engrossing that he can spare no thought for a perishing nation? It is with a deep sense of our responsibility that we warn that exalted person that neither his great position nor his incomparable talents will save him in the hour of delirium from the fate of all those who, in the madness of luxury or tyranny, have met the English people in the rare day of its wrath.”

===Chinese===
"我军此次在诺丁山所遭之惨败，诚然可怖——诚然可怖——（不，应当说令人痛心），但若能由此唤起各方对当局那个、那个什么来着——无能（当然是骇人听闻的无能）之防务准备的关注，亦属一桩好事。以我等目前所掌握之消息而论，遽下定论未免为时过早（多么漂亮的一个词！）——遽下任何不利于巴克将军之结论，未免为时过早；盖将军曾效力疆场、屡建奇功（哈哈！），其光荣之伤痕与桂冠，至少使其有权获得一桩暂缓判决。然有一事，我等不得不直言。此事我等缄默已久，或出于误置之审慎，或出于误置之忠忱。当前之危局，倘非因国王那——我等只能称之为——无可辩解之行径，本不至于发生。此言出诸我等之口，实所痛心；然我等既以公众利益为念而行文（我这句是剽窃巴克先生那句名言隽语的），便不致因可能令某一位个人——哪怕是最尊贵之个人——难堪，而有所退缩。值此国运攸关之时刻，人民异口同声发问：'国王在哪里？'当其子民正于一座大城之街巷相互撕扯之际，他究竟在做什么？他的种种游乐与放荡（对此我等岂能佯作不知）竟如此缠身，竟使他无暇分神顾及一个垂死之国家么？我等怀着深切之责任感警告那位至尊之人：纵有其崇高之地位、其无匹之才华，在那谵妄之时辰，亦不能救他免于一切奢靡或暴政之狂徒在英格兰人民罕见的震怒之日所遭之命运。"

===Original===
“I am now,” said the King, “going to write an account of the battle by an eyewitness.” And he picked up a fourth sheet of wallpaper. Almost at the same moment Buck strode quickly into the office. He had a bandage round his head.

“I was told,” he said, with his usual gruff civility, “that your Majesty was here.”

“And of all things on earth,” cried the King, with delight, “here is an eyewitness! An eyewitness who, I regret to observe, has at present only one eye to witness with. Can you write us the special article, Buck? Have you a rich style?”

Buck, with a self-restraint which almost approached politeness, took no notice whatever of the King’s maddening geniality.

“I took the liberty, your Majesty,” he said shortly, “of asking Mr. Barker to come here also.”

As he spoke, indeed, Barker came swinging into the office, with his usual air of hurry.

“What is happening now?” asked Buck, turning to him with a kind of relief.

“Fighting still going on,” said Barker. “The four hundred from West Kensington were hardly touched last night. They hardly got near the place. Poor Wilson’s Bayswater men got cut about, though. They fought confoundedly well. They took Pump Street once. What mad things do happen in the world. To think that of all of us it should be little Wilson with the red whiskers who came out best.”

The King made a note on his paper—

“Romantic Conduct of Mr. Wilson.”

“Yes,” said Buck; “it makes one a bit less proud of one’s h’s.”

===Chinese===
"我现在，"国王说，"要写一篇由目击者执笔的战事报道。"他抄起第四张糊墙纸。几乎就在同一刻，巴克大步流星地走进编辑部，头上缠着绷带。

"我听说，"他用他素常那种粗声粗气的客气说，"陛下在这儿。"

"而世上万千事物之中，"国王喜不自胜地嚷道，"这里竟有一位目击者！一位目击者——很遗憾地注意到——目前只有一只眼睛可供目击。巴克，你能给我们写篇特稿吗？你的文笔华丽不华丽？"

巴克以一种近乎客气的自制力，对国王那叫人发狂的殷勤完全不闻不问。

"我冒昧地，陛下，"他简短地说，"也请了巴克先生到这儿来。"

果不其然，他正说着，巴克先生便风风火火地闯进了编辑部，带着他素常那副匆忙的神气。

"现在情形怎样？"巴克问他，带着一种如释重负的口气。

"仗还在打，"巴克先生说，"西肯辛顿那四百人昨夜几乎没动一根毫毛。他们压根儿没靠近那地方。不过可怜的威尔逊那帮贝斯沃特人倒挨了不少刀。他们打得该死的好。他们一度拿下过泵街。世上竟会发生这样疯狂的事。想想看，我们这帮人里头，到头来打得最漂亮的，竟会是那个红络腮胡的小个子威尔逊。"

国王在他的纸上记下一笔——

"威尔逊先生之浪漫行径。"

"可不是，"巴克说，"这叫人对自己说话咬字那点体面，多少没那么得意了。"（原文明为"对自己的 h 音（表示教养的吐字）没那么得意"，因威尔逊操伦敦土腔，把 h 音略去。）

===Original===
The King suddenly folded or crumpled up the paper, and put it in his pocket.

“I have an idea,” he said. “I will be an eyewitness. I will write you such letters from the Front as will be more gorgeous than the real thing. Give me my coat, Paladium. I entered this room a mere King of England. I leave it, Special War Correspondent of the Court Journal. It is useless to stop me, Pally; it is vain to cling to my knees, Buck; it is hopeless, Barker, to weep upon my neck. ‘When duty calls’—the remainder of the sentiment escapes me. You will receive my first article this evening by the eight-o’clock post.”

And, running out of the office, he jumped upon a blue Bayswater omnibus that went swinging by.

“Well,” said Barker, gloomily, “well.”

“Barker,” said Buck, “business may be lower than politics, but war is, as I discovered last night, a long sight more like business. You politicians are such ingrained demagogues that even when you have a despotism you think of nothing but public opinion. So you learn to tack and run, and are afraid of the first breeze. Now we stick to a thing and get it. And our mistakes help us. Look here! at this moment we’ve beaten Wayne.”

“Beaten Wayne,” repeated Barker.

===Chinese===
国王忽然把那张纸折叠——揉皱，塞进衣袋。

"我有个主意，"他说，"我要亲自去做一个目击者。我要给你们写一批来自前线的信，比真事还要绚烂。把外衣给我，帕拉第昂。我进这间屋子时，只不过是英格兰国王。我离开它时，是《宫廷杂志》的特派战地通讯员。拦我是没用的，帕利；抱我的膝盖是徒劳的，巴克；趴在我脖子上哭也是无望的，巴克先生。'当责任在召唤'——后半句想不起来了。你们今晚八点钟的邮班，会收到我的第一篇稿子。"

说完跑出编辑部，纵身跳上一辆正晃悠着驶过的蓝色贝斯沃特公共马车。

"唉，"巴克先生郁郁地说，"唉。"

"巴克先生，"巴克说，"做生意也许比搞政治低一等，可打仗这回事，我昨夜才发现，比做生意还要像做生意得多。你们这些政客是天生的煽动家，即便手里握着的是专制权力，满脑子想的仍不外乎舆论。所以你们学会的是见风使舵、掉头就跑，一阵微风就把你们吓住。而我们，认准一件事就死抓到底，直到把它弄到手。我们的错误也帮我们的忙。瞧！这会儿我们已经把韦恩打败了。"

"把韦恩打败了，"巴克先生重复道。

===Original===
“Why the dickens not?” cried the other, flinging out his hands. “Look here. I said last night that we had them by holding the nine entrances. Well, I was wrong. We should have had them but for a singular event—the lamps went out. But for that it was certain. Has it occurred to you, my brilliant Barker, that another singular event has happened since that singular event of the lamps going out?”

“What event?” asked Barker.

“By an astounding coincidence, the sun has risen,” cried out Buck, with a savage air of patience. “Why the hell aren’t we holding all those approaches now, and passing in on them again? It should have been done at sunrise. The confounded doctor wouldn’t let me go out. You were in command.”

Barker smiled grimly.

“It is a gratification to me, my dear Buck, to be able to say that we anticipated your suggestions precisely. We went as early as possible to reconnoitre the nine entrances. Unfortunately, while we were fighting each other in the dark, like a lot of drunken navvies, Mr. Wayne’s friends were working very hard indeed. Three hundred yards from Pump Street, at every one of those entrances, there is a barricade nearly as high as the houses. They were finishing the last, in Pembridge Road, when we arrived. Our mistakes,” he cried bitterly, and flung his cigarette on the ground. “It is not we who learn from them.”

There was a silence for a few moments, and Barker lay back wearily in a chair. The office clock ticked exactly in the stillness.

===Chinese===
"怎么他妈的不行？"对方双手一摊嚷道，"你听着。我昨夜说过，守住那九个入口就能拿住他们。嗯，我说错了。要不是出了那一桩意外——灯灭了——我们就拿住他们了。要没有那桩意外，这是板上钉钉的。我可否问一句，我英明的巴克先生，自从灯灭那一桩意外之后，可又出了另一桩意外，这你可曾想到？"

"什么意外？"巴克先生问。

"碰巧得很，太阳升起来了，"巴克喊道，带着一种凶狠的耐心，"那我们这会儿为什么没有重新守住所有的入口，重新朝他们合围过去？这本该在日出时就做的。那个该死的大夫不让我出门。当时是你指挥。"

巴克先生冷酷地一笑。

"我亲爱的巴克，能告诉你这一点，颇令我快慰：我们已经丝毫不差地料到了你的建议。我们尽可能早地出动，去侦察那九个入口。不幸的是，当我们像一帮喝醉的苦力似的在黑暗里自相残杀之时，韦恩先生的朋友们却干得极卖力。离泵街三百码处，每一个入口都筑起了一道几乎与房屋齐高的街垒。我们赶到时，他们正在彭布里奇路那一段修最后的一道路障。"我们的错误，"他痛心地喊，把香烟摔在地上，"学到东西的，并不是我们。"

沉默了一会儿，巴克先生疲惫地向后靠在椅子里。编辑部那只钟在寂静中嘀嗒作响，走得一丝不苟。

===Original===
At length Barker said suddenly—

“Buck, does it ever cross your mind what this is all about? The Hammersmith to Maida Vale thoroughfare was an uncommonly good speculation. You and I hoped a great deal from it. But is it worth it? It will cost us thousands to crush this ridiculous riot. Suppose we let it alone?”

“And be thrashed in public by a red-haired madman whom any two doctors would lock up?” cried out Buck, starting to his feet. “What do you propose to do, Mr. Barker? To apologise to the admirable Mr. Wayne? To kneel to the Charter of the Cities? To clasp to your bosom the flag of the Red Lion? To kiss in succession every sacred lamppost that saved Notting Hill? No, by God! My men fought jolly well—they were beaten by a trick. And they’ll fight again.”

“Buck,” said Barker, “I always admired you. And you were quite right in what you said the other day.”

“In what?”

“In saying,” said Barker, rising quietly, “that we had all got into Adam Wayne’s atmosphere and out of our own. My friend, the whole territorial kingdom of Adam Wayne extends to about nine streets, with barricades at the end of them. But the spiritual kingdom of Adam Wayne extends, God knows where—it extends to this office, at any rate. The red-haired madman whom any two doctors would lock up is filling this room with his roaring, unreasonable soul. And it was the red-haired madman who said the last word you spoke.”

Buck walked to the window without replying. “You understand, of course,” he said at last, “I do not dream of giving in.”

===Chinese===
终于，巴克先生突然说——

"巴克，你可曾想过，这一切究竟所为何来？从哈默史密斯（Hammersmith）到梅达谷（Maida Vale）的那条干道，本是极好的一笔投资。你我原本对它寄望甚深。可这值得么？要扑灭这场荒唐的骚乱，得花我们好几千镑。要是索性撒手不管呢？"

"任由一个随便两个大夫都会把他关进疯人院的红头发疯子，当众把我们揍一顿？"巴克霍地站起来喊道，"巴克先生，你打算怎么办？去向那位可敬的韦恩先生道歉？去给城市宪章（the Charter of the Cities）下跪？去把红狮旗（the Red Lion）紧紧搂在怀里？去挨个亲吻每一根救了诺丁山的神圣路灯柱？不，休想！我的人打得很好——他们是被一个诡计打败的。他们还要再打。"

"巴克，"巴克先生说，"我一向佩服你。你那天说的话，也一点不错。"

"哪句话？"

"你说，"巴克先生静静地站起来说，"我们都落进了亚当·韦恩的气氛里，跑出了自己的气氛。我的朋友，亚当·韦恩那个属地的王国，统共不过九条街，街尽头各筑一道街垒。可亚当·韦恩那个属灵的王国，天知道延伸到哪里——至少延伸到了这间编辑部。那个随便两个大夫都会把他关进疯人院的红头发疯子，正用他那咆哮的、蛮不讲理的灵魂，灌满了这间屋子。而你方才说的最后那一句话，也正是那个红头发疯子说的。"

巴克没有回答，走到窗前。"你当然明白，"他终于说，"我做梦也没想过要认输。"

===Original===
The King, meanwhile, was rattling along on the top of his blue omnibus. The traffic of London as a whole had not, of course, been greatly disturbed by these events, for the affair was treated as a Notting Hill riot, and that area was marked off as if it had been in the hands of a gang of recognised rioters. The blue omnibuses simply went round as they would have done if a road were being mended, and the omnibus on which the correspondent of the Court Journal was sitting swept round the corner of Queen’s Road, Bayswater.

The King was alone on the top of the vehicle, and was enjoying the speed at which it was going.

“Forward, my beauty, my Arab,” he said, patting the omnibus encouragingly, “fleetest of all thy bounding tribe. Are thy relations with thy driver, I wonder, those of the Bedouin and his steed? Does he sleep side by side with thee—”

His meditations were broken by a sudden and jarring stoppage. Looking over the edge, he saw that the heads of the horses were being held by men in the uniform of Wayne’s army, and heard the voice of an officer calling out orders.

===Chinese===
与此同时，国王正坐在他那辆蓝色公共马车的车顶上，一路颠个不停。伦敦整体而言的交通，当然没有被这些事怎样惊扰，因为这事被当作一场诺丁山骚乱来处理，那片区域被划了出来，仿佛落入了一伙公认为骚乱者之手。那些蓝色公共马车不过是绕道而行，就像哪条路在修时那样绕一绕；而这位《宫廷杂志》通讯员所坐的那一辆，便绕过贝斯沃特的王后街拐角，飞驰而去。

国王独自一人坐在车顶，正享受着车速带来的快意。

"前进，我的美人，我的阿拉伯骏马，"他说着，鼓励地拍拍那辆公共马车，"你是你那奔腾一族中最迅捷的一匹。我且问你，你和你那位车夫的关系，可是贝都因人与其坐骑那样的关系？他可与你并肩而眠——"

他那冥想被一阵突如其来的、刺耳的急刹车打断了。他往车沿下一看，只见马头正被几个身穿韦恩军队制服的人攥住，又听得一个军官在发号施令。

===Original===
King Auberon descended from the omnibus with dignity. The guard or picket of red halberdiers who had stopped the vehicle did not number more than twenty, and they were under the command of a short, dark, clever-looking young man, conspicuous among the rest as being clad in an ordinary frock-coat, but girt round the waist with a red sash and a long seventeenth-century sword. A shiny silk hat and spectacles completed the outfit in a pleasing manner.

“To whom have I the honour of speaking?” said the King, endeavouring to look like Charles I, in spite of personal difficulties.

The dark man in spectacles lifted his hat with equal gravity.

“My name is Bowles,” he said. “I am a chemist. I am also a captain of O company of the army of Notting Hill. I am distressed at having to incommode you by stopping the omnibus, but this area is covered by our proclamation, and we intercept all traffic. May I ask to whom I have the honour—Why, good gracious, I beg your Majesty’s pardon. I am quite overwhelmed at finding myself concerned with the King.”

===Chinese===
奥伯伦国王庄重地从公共马车上下来。截住车辆的那队红衣戟兵哨队不过二十来人，由一个矮小、黝黑、模样精明的青年统率。他在众人中间格外显眼，因为他穿一件寻常的燕尾服，腰间却束一条红绶带，佩着一柄长长的十七世纪式样剑。一顶亮闪闪的大礼帽和一副眼镜，恰到好处地点缀了这一身装束。

"我有幸在和谁说话？"国王说，尽管处境颇为尴尬，仍竭力要摆出一副查理一世（Charles I）的神气。

戴眼镜的黝黑的人同样庄重地举了举帽。

"敝姓鲍尔斯（Bowles），"他说，"我是药剂师。我同时也是诺丁山军队 O 连的上尉。不得不停下车来给您添麻烦，我深为不安；但这一带已在我们公告的范围之内，一切车辆我们都得拦下。敢问我有幸在和——哎呀，我的天，乞陛下恕罪。我万没想到自己竟和陛下打起了交道，真是不胜惶恐。"

===Original===
Auberon put up his hand with indescribable grandeur.

“Not with the King,” he said; “with the special war correspondent of the Court Journal.”

“I beg your Majesty’s pardon,” began Mr. Bowles, doubtfully.

“Do you call me Majesty? I repeat,” said Auberon, firmly, “I am a representative of the press. I have chosen, with a deep sense of responsibility, the name of Pinker. I should desire a veil to be drawn over the past.”

“Very well, sir,” said Mr. Bowles, with an air of submission, “in our eyes the sanctity of the press is at least as great as that of the throne. We desire nothing better than that our wrongs and our glories should be widely known. May I ask, Mr. Pinker, if you have any objection to being presented to the Provost and to General Turnbull?”

“The Provost I have had the honour of meeting,” said Auberon, easily. “We old journalists, you know, meet everybody. I should be most delighted to have the same honour again. General Turnbull, also, it would be a gratification to know. The younger men are so interesting. We of the old Fleet Street gang lose touch with them.”

“Will you be so good as to step this way?” said the leader of O company.

“I am always good,” said Mr. Pinker. “Lead on.”

===Chinese===
奥伯伦举起一只手，那气度妙不可言。

"不是和一位国王打交道，"他说，"而是和《宫廷杂志》的特派战地通讯员打交道。"

"乞陛下恕罪，"鲍尔斯先生迟疑地开口。

"你还叫我'陛下'？我再说一遍，"奥伯伦坚定地说，"我是报界代表。我怀着深重的责任感，给自己选定了'平克'（Pinker）这个名字。过去的事，我但求一笔勾销。"

"很好，先生，"鲍尔斯先生一副顺从的神气说，"在我们眼里，新闻界的神圣，至少与王座同等。我们的冤屈与我们的荣光能广为人知，正是我们求之不得的。平克先生，敢问您可愿意见一见区督和特恩布尔（Turnbull）将军？"

"区督嘛，我有幸已经见过了，"奥伯伦随口道，"我们这些老记者，你知道，什么人都见得到。能再得此荣幸，我是再乐意不过。特恩布尔将军嘛，能结识一下也是一桩快事。年轻一辈真是有趣得很。我们这些舰队街（Fleet Street）老帮伙，已和他们脱了节。"

"那么，请您这边走，好吗？"O 连的连长说。

"我一向很好，"平克先生说，"带路吧。

## III. The Great Army of South Kensington / 第三章 南肯辛顿的大军

===Original===
The article from the special correspondent of the Court Journal arrived in due course, written on very coarse copy-paper in the King’s arabesque of handwriting, in which three words filled a page, and yet were illegible. Moreover, the contribution was the more perplexing at first, as it opened with a succession of erased paragraphs. The writer appeared to have attempted the article once or twice in several journalistic styles. At the side of one experiment was written, “Try American style,” and the fragment began—

“The King must go. We want gritty men. Flapdoodle is all very…” and then broke off, followed by the note, “Good sound journalism safer. Try it.”

The experiment in good sound journalism appeared to begin—

“The greatest of English poets has said that a rose by any…”

This also stopped abruptly. The next annotation at the side was almost undecipherable, but seemed to be something like—

“How about old Steevens and the mot juste? E.g.…”

===Chinese===
《宫廷杂志》特派通讯员的那篇稿件如期送到了，写在极粗糙的稿纸上，用的是国王那一手阿拉伯式花体字——三个字占满一整页，却仍叫人无法辨认。况且，这篇投稿起初更令人困惑，因为它开头是一连串涂掉的段落。作者显然是用好几种新闻笔法，把这篇文章试写了一两遍。在一篇试笔之旁，写着"试试美国式笔法"，那残段开头是——

"国王非走不可。我们要的是有骨气的人。空话倒也还算……"接着便断了，下面附一句："还是稳妥的、像样的新闻笔法靠得住。试试这个。"

那一篇像样的新闻笔法的试笔，似乎是这样开头的——

"英国最伟大的诗人说过，玫瑰任凭怎样……"

这一篇也是戛然而止。旁边下一行批注几乎无法辨认，似乎大致是——

"老斯蒂文斯（Steevens）和'恰当的词'（mot juste，法语）那一套怎么样？比如……"

===Original===
“Morning winked a little wearily at me over the curt edge of Campden Hill and its houses with their sharp shadows. Under the abrupt black cardboard of the outline, it took some little time to detect colours; but at length I saw a brownish yellow shifting in the obscurity, and I knew that it was the guard of Swindon’s West Kensington army. They are being held as a reserve, and lining the whole ridge above the Bayswater Road. Their camp and their main force is under the great Waterworks Tower on Campden Hill. I forgot to say that the Waterworks Tower looked swart.

“As I passed them and came over the curve of Silver Street, I saw the blue cloudy masses of Barker’s men blocking the entrance to the high road like a sapphire smoke (good). The disposition of the allied troops, under the general management of Mr. Wilson, appears to be as follows: The Yellow army (if I may so describe the West Kensingtonians) lies, as I have said, in a strip along the ridge, its furthest point westward being the west side of Campden Hill Road, its furthest point eastward the beginning of Kensington Gardens. The Green army of Wilson lines the Notting Hill High Road itself from Queen’s Road to the corner of Pembridge Road, curving round the latter, and extending some three hundred yards up towards Westbourne Grove. Westbourne Grove itself is occupied by Barker of South Kensington. The fourth side of this rough square, the Queen’s Road side, is held by some of Buck’s Purple warriors.

===Chinese===
"晨光在坎普登山（Campden Hill）那截峭立的边缘及其房屋锐利的阴影之上，略带倦意地向我眨了眨眼。在那一片突兀的黑纸板似的轮廓之下，要辨出颜色颇费了些工夫；但终于，我看见一抹褐黄色在昏暗里移动，我便知道，那是斯温登的西肯辛顿军的哨兵。他们被留作后备，沿着贝斯沃特路（Bayswater Road）上方那整道山脊排开。他们的营地与主力，驻在坎普登山那座大水塔（Waterworks Tower）之下。忘了说一句，那座水塔瞧着黑黝黝的。

"我从他们身边经过，越过银街（Silver Street）的弯道，便看见巴克先生那些蓝莹莹的人马，像一团蓝宝石色的烟雾似的堵住了大路的入口（好）。盟军的部署，据我看是在威尔逊先生总揽之下，情形如下：黄军（姑且这样称呼西肯辛顿人罢）如我方才所言，呈一条带状沿山脊铺开，其最西端抵坎普登山公路（Campden Hill Road）西侧，最东端抵肯辛顿花园（Kensington Gardens）入口。威尔逊的绿军，则沿诺丁山高街本身一字排开，从王后街直到彭布里奇路拐角，再绕过后者，朝韦斯特伯恩格罗夫方向延伸约三百码。韦斯特伯恩格罗夫那一道，由南肯辛顿的巴克先生占据。这个大致方阵的第四边，即王后街那一边，由巴克的一部分紫衣武士把守。

===Original===
“The whole resembles some ancient and dainty Dutch flowerbed. Along the crest of Campden Hill lie the golden crocuses of West Kensington. They are, as it were, the first fiery fringe of the whole. Northward lies our hyacinth Barker, with all his blue hyacinths. Round to the southwest run the green rushes of Wilson of Bayswater, and a line of violet irises (aptly symbolised by Mr. Buck) complete the whole. The argent exterior… (I am losing the style. I should have said ‘Curving with a whisk’ instead of merely ‘Curving.’ Also I should have called the hyacinths ‘sudden.’ I cannot keep this up. War is too rapid for this style of writing. Please ask office-boy to insert mots justes.)

===Chinese===
"整个布阵，恰似一方古雅而精致的荷兰花圃。沿坎普登山山脊一字排开的，是西肯辛顿那些金黄色的番红花。它们可以说是全阵最外缘那一圈火热的镶边。北面是我们那位风信子巴克先生，带着他全部蓝色的风信子。向西南逶迤而去的，是贝斯沃特的威尔逊那一丛绿色的灯芯草，而一排紫罗兰色的鸢尾花（恰恰由巴克先生来象征，再贴切不过）为全阵收了尾。那银白的外缘……（我这股笔劲撑不住了。我方才应当写'嗖地一弯'，而不光是'一弯'。还有，我本该把那些风信子称作'猝然绽放的'。我撑不下去了。这种笔法跟不上打仗的速度。请叫茶房把'恰当的词'替我填上。）

===Original===
“The truth is that there is nothing to report. That commonplace element which is always ready to devour all beautiful things (as the Black Pig in the Irish Mythology will finally devour the stars and gods); that commonplace element, as I say, has in its Black Piggish way devoured finally the chances of any romance in this affair; that which once consisted of absurd but thrilling combats in the streets, has degenerated into something which is the very prose of warfare—it has degenerated into a siege. A siege may be defined as a peace plus the inconvenience of war. Of course Wayne cannot hold out. There is no more chance of help from anywhere else than of ships from the moon. And if old Wayne had stocked his street with tinned meats till all his garrison had to sit on them, he couldn’t hold out for more than a month or two. As a matter of melancholy fact, he has done something rather like this. He has stocked his street with food until there must be uncommonly little room to turn round. But what is the good? To hold out for all that time and then to give in of necessity, what does it mean? It means waiting until your victories are forgotten, and then taking the trouble to be defeated. I cannot understand how Wayne can be so inartistic.

===Chinese===
"实情是：根本没什么可报道的。那种随时准备吞噬一切美物的平庸之物（一如爱尔兰神话里的黑猪，终将吞噬众星与众神）；那平庸之物，我说，已按它那'黑猪式'的做派，彻底吞噬掉了这场纷争中一切浪漫的可能；那原本由荒唐却惊心动魄的巷战所构成的东西，已退化为某种可称战争之散文的东西——它退化成了一场围攻。围攻一词，可以定义为'和平'加上'战争的不便'。韦恩当然撑不下去。再没有从任何地方盼来援军的可能，正如同没有从月亮上盼来船队的可能。即便老韦恩把他那条街塞满罐头肉，塞到他全部守军只能坐在罐头上，他也撑不过一两个月。而说来可悲，他还当真做了些差不多的事。他把那条街塞满了粮食，塞到里面的人恐怕连转个身的地方都没有了。可这又有什么用？苦撑那么久，到头来仍不得不投降，这意味着什么？这意味着，等到你那些胜绩都被人遗忘，你才费心去吃一场败仗。我不懂韦恩怎能如此不懂艺术。

===Original===
“And how odd it is that one views a thing quite differently when one knows it is defeated! I always thought Wayne was rather fine. But now, when I know that he is done for, there seem to be nothing else but Wayne. All the streets seem to point at him, all the chimneys seem to lean towards him. I suppose it is a morbid feeling; but Pump Street seems to be the only part of London that I feel physically. I suppose, I say, that it is morbid. I suppose it is exactly how a man feels about his heart when his heart is weak. ‘Pump Street’—the heart is a pump. And I am drivelling.

===Chinese===
"而一旦知道一件事已经败定，人看它的眼光竟会这般不同，这又是何等古怪！我一向觉得韦恩颇有几分了不起。可如今，一旦知道他完了，世上便仿佛只剩下韦恩一个人了。所有的街都像是指向他，所有的烟囱都像是朝他倾斜。我想这是一种病态的感觉；可泵街似乎是我整个伦敦城里唯一能凭肉身感觉得到的一块地方。我想，我说，这是病态。我想，这正像一个人心脏衰弱时，对他那颗心脏的感觉。'泵街'——心脏本就是一只泵。而我在胡言乱语了。

===Original===
“Our finest leader at the front is, beyond all question, General Wilson. He has adopted alone among the other Provosts the uniform of his own halberdiers, although that fine old sixteenth-century garb was not originally intended to go with red side-whiskers. It was he who, against a most admirable and desperate defence, broke last night into Pump Street and held it for at least half an hour. He was afterwards expelled from it by General Turnbull, of Notting Hill, but only after desperate fighting and the sudden descent of that terrible darkness which proved so much more fatal to the forces of General Buck and General Swindon.

“Provost Wayne himself, with whom I had, with great good fortune, a most interesting interview, bore the most eloquent testimony to the conduct of General Wilson and his men. His precise words are as follows: ‘I have bought sweets at his funny little shop when I was four years old, and ever since. I never noticed anything, I am ashamed to say, except that he talked through his nose, and didn’t wash himself particularly. And he came over our barricade like a devil from hell.’ I repeated this speech to General Wilson himself, with some delicate improvements, and he seemed pleased with it. He does not, however, seem pleased with anything so much just now as he is with the wearing of a sword. I have it from the front on the best authority that General Wilson was not completely shaved yesterday. It is believed in military circles that he is growing a moustache.…

===Chinese===
"前线我们最出色的将领，毫无疑问，是威尔逊将军。在各位区督之中，唯独他换上了他那队戟兵的制服，虽说那身漂亮的十六世纪古式军装，原本并不是配着红鬓脚设计的。昨夜正是他，在一场极为可嘉、极为绝望的防守面前，杀进了泵街，并至少守住了半个钟头。后来他被诺丁山的特恩布尔将军赶了出来——但那是在一场殊死搏斗之后，是在那场可怖的黑暗猝然降临之后；那场黑暗，对巴克将军和斯温登将军的部队而言，证明是致命得多。

"韦恩区督本人——我侥幸得以与他作了一次极有趣的访谈——对威尔逊将军及其部下的表现，作了最为雄辩的证词。他的原话如下：'我四岁起就在他那爿古怪的小铺子里买糖吃，一直买到现在。说来惭愧，我从前从没留意过他有什么特别，只除了他说话带鼻音，澡也不大洗。可他翻过我们的街垒时，简直像是从地狱里钻出来的魔鬼。'我把这番话转述给威尔逊将军本人听，作了若干雅致的润色，他听了似乎颇为受用。不过，眼下最叫他受用的，似乎还是佩剑这件事。我据前方最可靠的消息得知：威尔逊将军昨天并没有把胡子刮干净。军界相信，他正在蓄八字胡。

===Original===
“As I have said, there is nothing to report. I walk wearily to the pillar-box at the corner of Pembridge Road to post my copy. Nothing whatever has happened, except the preparations for a particularly long and feeble siege, during which I trust I shall not be required to be at the Front. As I glance up Pembridge Road in the growing dusk, the aspect of that road reminds me that there is one note worth adding. General Buck has suggested, with characteristic acumen, to General Wilson that, in order to obviate the possibility of such a catastrophe as overwhelmed the allied forces in the last advance on Notting Hill (the catastrophe, I mean, of the extinguished lamps), each soldier should have a lighted lantern round his neck. This is one of the things which I really admire about General Buck. He possesses what people used to mean by ‘the humility of the man of science,’ that is, he learns steadily from his mistakes. Wayne may score off him in some other way, but not in that way. The lanterns look like fairy lights as they curve round the end of Pembridge Road.

===Chinese===
