"正如我方才所说，没什么可报道的。我疲惫地走到彭布里奇路拐角那只邮筒前，去投我的稿子。什么事也没有发生，除了正在筹备一场格外漫长而疲软的围攻——我但愿这场围攻期间不必待在前线。在愈来愈浓的暮色里，我朝彭布里奇路那一头望了一眼，那条路的样子提醒我，还有一笔值得一提。巴克将军以其素有的精明，向威尔逊将军建议：为了杜绝盟军上次进攻诺丁山时所遭遇的那场灾祸（我指的是灯火熄灭那场灾祸）重演，每个兵脖子上都该挂一盏点亮的提灯。这是巴克将军身上我真心钦佩的一点。他具有人们过去所说'科学家的谦逊'那种品质，也就是说，他能从自己的错误里稳步地学东西。韦恩也许能在别的什么地方占他的便宜，但在这上头占不了。那些提灯绕着彭布里奇路的尽头弯过去，看上去像一串仙灯。

===Original===
“Later.—I write with some difficulty, because the blood will run down my face and make patterns on the paper. Blood is a very beautiful thing; that is why it is concealed. If you ask why blood runs down my face, I can only reply that I was kicked by a horse. If you ask me what horse, I can reply with some pride that it was a warhorse. If you ask me how a warhorse came on the scene in our simple pedestrian warfare, I am reduced to the necessity, so painful to a special correspondent, of recounting my experiences.

“I was, as I have said, in the very act of posting my copy at the pillar-box, and of glancing as I did so up the glittering curve of Pembridge Road, studded with the lights of Wilson’s men. I don’t know what made me pause to examine the matter, but I had a fancy that the line of lights, where it melted into the indistinct brown twilight, was more indistinct than usual. I was almost certain that in a certain stretch of the road where there had been five lights there were now only four. I strained my eyes; I counted them again, and there were only three. A moment after there were only two; an instant after only one; and an instant after that the lanterns near to me swung like jangled bells, as if struck suddenly. They flared and fell; and for the moment the fall of them was like the fall of the sun and stars out of heaven. It left everything in a primal blindness. As a matter of fact, the road was not yet legitimately dark. There were still red rays of a sunset in the sky, and the brown gloaming was still warmed, as it were, with a feeling as of firelight. But for three seconds after the lanterns swung and sank, I saw in front of me a blackness blocking the sky. And with the fourth second I knew that this blackness which blocked the sky was a man on a great horse; and I was trampled and tossed aside as a swirl of horsemen swept round the corner. As they turned I saw that they were not black, but scarlet; they were a sortie of the besieged, Wayne riding ahead.

===Chinese===
"稍后。——我写字有些困难，因为血会顺着我的脸往下淌，在纸上洇成花纹。血是一件极美的东西；正因如此，它才被藏了起来。你若问血为何顺着我的脸往下淌，我只能答道：我被马踢了。你若问我被什么马踢了，我可以带着几分自豪地答道：是一匹战马。你若问，在我们这场简朴的步战里，一匹战马是怎么冒出来的，我便不得不——这对一个特派通讯员是件痛苦的事——讲述我的亲身经历了。

"我方才说了，我正把稿子投进邮筒，一边投，一边朝彭布里奇路那一段闪闪发亮的弯道瞥了一眼——路上缀满了威尔逊手下人的灯火。我不知道是什么叫我停下来细看，可我隐约觉得，那一溜灯火融进那朦朦胧胧的褐色暮色之处，比平常更朦胧了。我几乎可以肯定，在某一段原先有五盏灯的路上，眼下只剩了四盏。我眯起眼细数；再数一遍，只剩三盏了。片刻之后只剩两盏；一瞬之后只剩一盏；再过一瞬，我近旁那些提灯就像被撞响的钟似的，猛地晃了起来——像是猝然挨了一下。它们一明一灭，跌落下去；刹那间，它们的坠落，仿佛是太阳和众星自天坠落。它把一切留在一种原始的盲目里。其实，那条路这时还不到该黑的时候。天上尚存一抹落日的红光，那褐色的暮霭，仿佛仍带着炉火般的暖意。可在那些提灯晃动、沉落之后的整整三秒钟里，我看见面前堵着一团挡住天空的黑。到第四秒，我才知道，这团挡住天空的黑，是一个骑在一匹大马上的人；我被践踏、被抛到一边，一队骑兵旋风似的绕过街角。他们转弯时，我才看清他们并非黑色，而是猩红色；他们是围城守军的一支突击队，韦恩骑在最前。

===Original===
“I lifted myself from the gutter, blinded with blood from a very slight skin-wound, and, queerly enough, not caring either for the blindness or for the slightness of the wound. For one mortal minute after that amazing cavalcade had spun past, there was dead stillness on the empty road. And then came Barker and all his halberdiers running like devils in the track of them. It had been their business to guard the gate by which the sortie had broken out; but they had not reckoned, and small blame to them, on cavalry. As it was, Barker and his men made a perfectly splendid run after them, almost catching Wayne’s horses by the tails.

“Nobody can understand the sortie. It consists only of a small number of Wayne’s garrison. Turnbull himself, with the vast mass of it, is undoubtedly still barricaded in Pump Street. Sorties of this kind are natural enough in the majority of historical sieges, such as the siege of Paris in 1870, because in such cases the besieged are certain of some support outside. But what can be the object of it in this case? Wayne knows (or if he is too mad to know anything, at least Turnbull knows) that there is not, and never has been, the smallest chance of support for him outside; that the mass of the sane modern inhabitants of London regard his farcical patriotism with as much contempt as they do the original idiocy that gave it birth—the folly of our miserable King. What Wayne and his horsemen are doing nobody can even conjecture. The general theory round here is that he is simply a traitor, and has abandoned the besieged. But all such larger but yet more soluble riddles are as nothing compared to the one small but unanswerable riddle: Where did they get the horses?

===Chinese===
"我从阴沟里撑起身子，一道很浅的皮外伤出的血流得我睁不开眼，说来也怪，我既不在意这看不见，也不在意这伤口之轻。在那一队惊人的骑兵旋风般卷过之后，整整一分钟，空荡荡的路上死一般寂静。随后，巴克先生带着他全部的戟兵，像恶鬼似的顺着他们的蹄印追了上来。守卫突击队冲出那道大门的，本是他们这一路；可他们没有料到——这也怪不得他们——对方会有骑兵。即便如此，巴克先生和他的人仍追得极漂亮，几乎要揪住韦恩那些马的尾巴。

"没有人能看懂这次突击。它只由韦恩守军中的一小部分人组成。特恩布尔本人带着大队人马，无疑仍据守在泵街的街垒之后。在大多数历史上的围城战里，比如1870年巴黎之围，这种突击是极为自然的，因为在那些战例里，被围的一方在外头必能得到某些接应。可这一次，它的目的能是什么呢？韦恩知道（就算他疯得什么都不懂，至少特恩布尔是懂的），外头不曾有过、也绝无半点可能给他任何支援；伦敦那些神智正常的现代居民，对他的这场滑稽爱国之举，正与对那个孕育了它的原始蠢行——我们那位可悲国王的蠢行——一样鄙夷。韦恩和他的骑兵在做什么，谁也猜不透。这一带普遍的看法是：他干脆是个叛徒，丢下被围的人跑了。可这一切虽说更大、却也更易解的谜，比起那一个小而无法回答的谜来，都不算什么：他们的马是从哪儿弄来的？

===Original===
“Later.—I have heard a most extraordinary account of the origin of the appearance of the horses. It appears that that amazing person, General Turnbull, who is now ruling Pump Street in the absence of Wayne, sent out, on the morning of the declaration of war, a vast number of little boys (or cherubs of the gutter, as we pressmen say), with half-crowns in their pockets, to take cabs all over London. No less than a hundred and sixty cabs met at Pump Street; were commandeered by the garrison. The men were set free, the cabs used to make barricades, and the horses kept in Pump Street, where they were fed and exercised for several days, until they were sufficiently rapid and efficient to be used for this wild ride out of the town. If this is so, and I have it on the best possible authority, the method of the sortie is explained. But we have no explanation of its object. Just as Barker’s Blues were swinging round the corner after them, they were stopped, but not by an enemy; only by the voice of one man, and he a friend. Red Wilson of Bayswater ran alone along the main road like a madman, waving them back with a halberd snatched from a sentinel. He was in supreme command, and Barker stopped at the corner, staring and bewildered. We could hear Wilson’s voice loud and distinct out of the dusk, so that it seemed strange that the great voice should come out of the little body. ‘Halt, South Kensington! Guard this entry, and prevent them returning. I will pursue. Forward, the Green Guards!’

“A wall of dark blue uniforms and a wood of poleaxes was between me and Wilson, for Barker’s men blocked the mouth of the road in two rigid lines. But through them and through the dusk I could hear the clear orders and the clank of arms, and see the green army of Wilson marching by towards the west. They were our great fighting-men. Wilson had filled them with his own fire; in a few days they had become veterans. Each of them wore a silver medal of a pump, to boast that they alone of all the allied armies had stood victorious in Pump Street.

===Chinese===
"稍后。——关于那群马从何而来，我听到了一个最离奇的说法。据说，韦恩不在时正代管泵街的那位奇人特恩布尔将军，在宣战那天早晨，派出了一大群小男孩（用我们报界中人的话说，就是阴沟里的小天使），每个人兜里揣着半克朗，到伦敦各处去叫马车。足有一百六十辆马车齐集泵街，被守军征用。车夫被放走，马车用来筑街垒，马则留在泵街，在那儿喂了几天、溜了几天，直到它们足够迅捷、足够使得上手，可用来作这一次冲出重围的狂奔。若果真如此——而我据最可靠的消息得知如此——那么这次突击的手段便有了答案。可我们仍无从解释它的目的。就在巴克先生的蓝衣兵正要跟着他们拐过街角追去之时，他们被叫停了——叫停他们的不是敌人，而是一个人的声音，一个朋友的声音。贝斯沃特的红发威尔逊（Red Wilson），独自一人沿大路像个疯子似的跑来，手里挥着一柄从一个哨兵手里夺来的戟，把他们喝退。他此刻是最高统帅，巴克先生便停在街角，目瞪口呆。我们能从暮色里听见威尔逊那洪亮而清晰的声音，奇怪如此巨大的嗓门竟出自那样一个小身板。'南肯辛顿，立定！守住这个入口，别让他们折回来。我去追。绿衣卫队，前进！'

"一堵深蓝色制服的墙、一林子的长戟，隔在我和威尔逊之间，因为巴克先生的人排成两道僵硬的横线堵住了路口。但透过他们，透过暮色，我听得见清脆的口令和兵器的铿锵，看得见威尔逊的绿军朝西边开拔而过。他们是我们的头等勇士。威尔逊把自己的火灌输给了他们；短短几日，他们便成了老兵。他们每人胸前都挂着一枚银质的小泵形奖章，炫耀他们在所有盟军之中，唯独在泵街上胜利地站住过脚。

===Original===
“I managed to slip past the detachment of Barker’s Blues, who are guarding the end of Pembridge Road, and a sharp spell of running brought me to the tail of Wilson’s green army as it swung down the road in pursuit of the flying Wayne. The dusk had deepened into almost total darkness; for some time I only heard the throb of the marching pace. Then suddenly there was a cry, and the tall fighting men were flung back on me, almost crushing me, and again the lanterns swung and jingled, and the cold nozzles of great horses pushed into the press of us. They had turned and charged us.

“‘You fools!’ came the voice of Wilson, cleaving our panic with a splendid cold anger. ‘Don’t you see? the horses have no riders!’

“It was true. We were being plunged at by a stampede of horses with empty saddles. What could it mean? Had Wayne met some of our men and been defeated? Or had he flung these horses at us as some kind of ruse or mad new mode of warfare, such as he seemed bent on inventing? Or did he and his men want to get away in disguise? Or did they want to hide in houses somewhere?

===Chinese===
"我设法溜过了把守彭布里奇路尽头的那一支巴克先生的蓝衣兵，一阵疾跑，赶上了威尔逊绿军的尾巴——他们正顺着大路挥军追击奔逃的韦恩。暮色已深作近乎彻底的黑暗；有一阵子，我只听见行军步伐的鼓动。忽然一声喊，那些高大的勇士被朝我这边摔了回来，几乎把我挤扁，那些提灯又一次晃动作响，大马冰冷的鼻口朝我们人堆里拱过来。他们掉过头，朝我们冲来了。

"'你们这群笨蛋！'威尔逊的声音传来，以一种绝妙的、冷冷的怒气，劈开我们的惊慌。'你们没看见？这些马上没有人！'

"果然。朝我们冲过来的是一群空鞍的马，发着狂奔。这意味着什么？是韦恩撞上了我们的某些人，被打败了？还是他把这些马朝我们甩过来，当作某种诡计，或某种他似乎铁了心要发明的、疯狂的新型战法？又或者，他和他的部下想乔装逃走？又或者，他们想在什么地方躲进屋子？

===Original===
“Never did I admire any man’s intellect (even my own) so much as I did Wilson’s at that moment. Without a word, he simply pointed the halberd (which he still grasped) to the southern side of the road. As you know, the streets running up to the ridge of Campden Hill from the main road are peculiarly steep, they are more like sudden flights of stairs. We were just opposite Aubrey Road, the steepest of all; up that it would have been far more difficult to urge half-trained horses than to run up on one’s feet.

“‘Left wheel!’ hallooed Wilson. ‘They have gone up here,’ he added to me, who happened to be at his elbow.

“‘Why?’ I ventured to ask.

“‘Can’t say for certain,’ replied the Bayswater General. ‘They’ve gone up here in a great hurry, anyhow. They’ve simply turned their horses loose, because they couldn’t take them up. I fancy I know. I fancy they’re trying to get over the ridge to Kensingston or Hammersmith, or somewhere, and are striking up here because it’s just beyond the end of our line. Damned fools, not to have gone further along the road, though. They’ve only just shaved our last outpost. Lambert is hardly four hundred yards from here. And I’ve sent him word.’

===Chinese===
"我从来没有像那一刻那样佩服过任何人的头脑（连我自己的都不佩服）。威尔逊一言不发，只是把手里仍握着的那柄戟，朝路的南边一指。你们知道的，从大路通往坎普登山山脊的那几条街，陡得非同寻常，简直像是一段段陡立的阶梯。我们正对着其中最陡的奥布里路（Aubrey Road）；要驱赶半驯的马上去，可比自己用脚跑上去难得多了。

"'左转！'威尔逊高喊。'他们就是从这儿上去的，'他对我补充道——我恰巧在他肘边。

"'为什么？'我斗胆一问。

"'说不准，'这位贝斯沃特将军答道，'无论如何，他们是从这儿匆匆上去的。他们干脆把马放掉了，因为带不上去。我大概猜得到。我猜他们想翻过山脊，到肯辛顿、或哈默史密斯、或者别的什么地方去，所以从这里往上插，因为这里恰在我们防线尽头之外一点。这帮该死的笨蛋，本该再沿路往前走一点。他们只差一点儿就刮着了我们最末一个前哨。兰伯特（Lambert）离这儿不到四百码。我已经派人给他送了信。'

===Original===
“‘Lambert!’ I said. ‘Not young Wilfrid Lambert—my old friend.’

“‘Wilfrid Lambert’s his name,’ said the General; ‘used to be a “man about town;” silly fellow with a big nose. That kind of man always volunteers for some war or other; and what’s funnier, he generally isn’t half bad at it. Lambert is distinctly good. The yellow West Kensingtons I always reckoned the weakest part of the army; but he has pulled them together uncommonly well, though he’s subordinate to Swindon, who’s a donkey. In the attack from Pembridge Road the other night he showed great pluck.’

“‘He has shown greater pluck than that,’ I said. ‘He has criticised my sense of humour. That was his first engagement.’

“This remark was, I am sorry to say, lost on the admirable commander of the allied forces. We were in the act of climbing the last half of Aubrey Road, which is so abrupt a slope that it looks like an old-fashioned map leaning up against the wall. There are lines of little trees, one above the other, as in the old-fashioned map.

===Chinese===
"'兰伯特！'我说，'莫不是年轻的威尔弗里德·兰伯特（Wilfrid Lambert）——我的老朋友。'

"'威尔弗里德·兰伯特正是他，'将军说，'从前是个"花花公子"；大鼻子、傻里傻气的家伙。这种人总会自告奋勇去参加这个那个的战争；而更妙的是，他往往干得还不赖。兰伯特就干得相当漂亮。西肯辛顿那帮黄衣兵，我一向看作全军最弱的一环；可他把他们出奇地拧成了一股绳，尽管他得听命于斯温登——那是一头驴。前两天夜里从彭布里奇路发起进攻时，他表现得极有胆量。'

"'他表现的胆量比这还大，'我说，'他批评过我的幽默感。那才是他第一次上阵。'

"我很遗憾地说，盟军这位出色统帅没听出这话的味道。我们这时正在爬奥布里路最后那一半——这坡陡得，看上去像一幅斜靠在墙上的老式地图。坡上一行行小树层层叠叠，正像那老式地图上画的那样。

===Original===
“We reached the top of it, panting somewhat, and were just about to turn the corner by a place called (in chivalrous anticipation of our wars of sword and axe) Tower Creçy, when we were suddenly knocked in the stomach (I can use no other term) by a horde of men hurled back upon us. They wore the red uniform of Wayne; their halberds were broken; their foreheads bleeding; but the mere impetus of their retreat staggered us as we stood at the last ridge of the slope.

“‘Good old Lambert!’ yelled out suddenly the stolid Mr. Wilson of Bayswater, in an uncontrollable excitement. ‘Damned jolly old Lambert! He’s got there already! He’s driving them back on us! Hurrah! hurrah! Forward, the Green Guards!’

“We swung round the corner eastwards, Wilson running first, brandishing the halberd—

===Chinese===
"我们爬上坡顶，多少有些气喘，正要拐过一个名叫"克雷西塔"（Tower Creçy，这名字是以骑士之风，预先为我们日后刀斧之战起的）的地方转弯，忽被一大堆倒摔过来的人马迎面撞在肚子上（我找不到别的说法）。他们穿着韦恩一伙的红制服；戟断了；额头在流血；可单是他们溃退下来的那股冲劲，就撞得站在坡顶最后一道脊上的我们踉踉跄跄。

"'好老兰伯特！'木讷的贝斯沃特的威尔逊先生忽然按捺不住地大喊。'该死的好老兰伯特！他已经先到了！他正把他们朝我们这边赶！好哇！好哇！绿衣卫队，前进！'

"我们朝东拐过弯去，威尔逊跑在最前，挥舞着那柄戟——

===Original===
“Will you pardon a little egotism? Everyone likes a little egotism, when it takes the form, as mine does in this case, of a disgraceful confession. The thing is really a little interesting, because it shows how the merely artistic habit has bitten into men like me. It was the most intensely exciting occurrence that had ever come to me in my life; and I was really intensely excited about it. And yet, as we turned that corner, the first impression I had was of something that had nothing to do with the fight at all. I was stricken from the sky as by a thunderbolt, by the height of the Waterworks Tower on Campden Hill. I don’t know whether Londoners generally realise how high it looks when one comes out, in this way, almost immediately under it. For the second it seemed to me that at the foot of it even human war was a triviality. For the second I felt as if I had been drunk with some trivial orgy, and that I had been sobered by the shock of that shadow. A moment afterwards, I realised that under it was going on something more enduring than stone, and something wilder than the dizziest height—the agony of man. And I knew that, compared to that, this overwhelming tower was itself a triviality; it was a mere stalk of stone which humanity could snap like a stick.

“I don’t know why I have talked so much about this silly old Waterworks Tower, which at the very best was only a tremendous background. It was that, certainly, a sombre and awful landscape, against which our figures were relieved. But I think the real reason was, that there was in my own mind so sharp a transition from the tower of stone to the man of flesh. For what I saw first when I had shaken off, as it were, the shadow of the tower, was a man, and a man I knew.

===Chinese===
"请恕我略带一点自吹自擂，可好？人人都喜欢一点自吹自擂——尤其当它像我这回这样，以一种丢脸的忏悔形式出现。这件事还真有一点意思，因为它显出：纯粹的艺术习惯，已怎样咬进了像我这样的人骨里。这是我有生以来最最叫人血脉偾张的一桩遭遇；我也确乎为它血脉偾张。然而，就在我们拐过那个弯的当口，我头一个得到的印象，竟是一桩与这场厮杀毫不相干的事。我像被雷霆当头劈下似的，被坎普登山上那座水塔的高度震慑住了。我不知道伦敦人通常是否意识到，当一个人这样几乎是径直走到它的脚下时，它看上去有多高。有那么一秒钟，我觉得在它的脚下，连人类的战争都成了一桩微不足道的小事。有那么一秒钟，我觉得自己仿佛刚为某场无谓的狂欢醉倒，而那道阴影的冲击把我泼醒了。片刻之后，我才意识到，在它脚下正进行着的，是某种比石头更持久的东西，某种比最高的高处更狂放的东西——人的 agony、人的剧痛。而我知道，与之相比，这座压顶之塔本身也不过是一件微不足道的小事；它不过是一根石头的茎梗，人类大可像折一根棍子似的把它折断。

"我不知道自己为什么对这座傻乎乎的旧水塔唠叨了这么久——它充其量不过是一块惊人的背景罢了。它确是一块背景，一幅阴沉而骇人的风景，我们的人影就映衬在它之上。可我想，真正的原因是：在我自己心里，从那座石塔到那个血肉之躯的人，转换得太突兀了。因为，当我把那塔的阴影——可以这样讲——从心头抖落之后，我头一眼看见的，是一个人，一个我认识的人。

===Original===
“Lambert stood at the further corner of the street that curved round the tower, his figure outlined in some degree by the beginning of moonrise. He looked magnificent, a hero; but he looked something much more interesting than that. He was, as it happened, in almost precisely the same swaggering attitude in which he had stood nearly fifteen years ago, when he swung his walking-stick and struck it into the ground, and told me that all my subtlety was drivel. And, upon my soul, I think he required more courage to say that than to fight as he does now. For then he was fighting against something that was in the ascendant, fashionable, and victorious. And now he is fighting (at the risk of his life, no doubt) merely against something which is already dead, which is impossible, futile; of which nothing has been more impossible and futile than this very sortie which has brought him into contact with it. People nowadays allow infinitely too little for the psychological sense of victory as a factor in affairs. Then he was attacking the degraded but undoubtedly victorious Quin; now he is attacking the interesting but totally extinguished Wayne.

===Chinese===
"兰伯特站在那条绕塔而过的街道更远的那个拐角上，月色初升，把他的身形勾出了个大致轮廓。他看来气宇轩昂，是个英雄；可他看来还要比这有趣得多。巧得很，他此刻那副神气活现的架势，几乎与近十五年前他站在我面前时一模一样——当年他甩着手杖，把它往地上一顿，对我说我那点子机锋全是废话。而凭良心说，我认为他当年说那句话，比他眼下这样打仗，所需要的勇气还要大。因为当年他所对抗的，是一种正在得势、时髦、节节胜利的东西。而如今他所对抗的（无疑是在拿性命冒险），却只是一种已经死了的东西，一种不可能、徒劳的东西；而再没有比这一次把他和它牵到一起的突击，更不可能、更徒劳的了。如今的人，把'胜利在心理上给人的感觉'在世事中所占的分量，估得太低了。当年，他攻击的是那个堕落的、却无疑胜券在握的奎因（Quin）；如今，他攻击的却是那个颇有趣味、却已彻底覆灭的韦恩。

===Original===
“His name recalls me to the details of the scene. The facts were these. A line of red halberdiers, headed by Wayne, were marching up the street, close under the northern wall, which is, in fact, the bottom of a sort of dyke or fortification of the Waterworks. Lambert and his yellow West Kensingtons had that instant swept round the corner and had shaken the Waynites heavily, hurling back a few of the more timid, as I have just described, into our very arms. When our force struck the tail of Wayne’s, everyone knew that all was up with him. His favourite military barber was struck down. His grocer was stunned. He himself was hurt in the thigh, and reeled back against the wall. We had him in a trap with two jaws. ‘Is that you?’ shouted Lambert, genially, to Wilson, across the hemmed-in host of Notting Hill. ‘That’s about the ticket,’ replied General Wilson; ‘keep them under the wall.’

“The men of Notting Hill were falling fast. Adam Wayne threw up his long arms to the wall above him, and with a spring stood upon it; a gigantic figure against the moon. He tore the banner out of the hands of the standard-bearer below him, and shook it out suddenly above our heads, so that it was like thunder in the heavens.

===Chinese===
"他的名字把我唤回到了这场景的细节上来。事实是这样的。一队红衣戟兵，由韦恩领头，正沿着街道、紧贴着北边那堵墙推进——那堵墙实际上是水塔一道壕沟或工事的底缘。兰伯特和他的西肯辛顿黄衣兵刚刚扫过街角，把韦恩一伙打得大震，把几个胆小的，正如我方才所说，摔进了我们怀里。等到我军撞上韦恩队伍的尾巴，人人都知道他完了。他最宠爱的随军理发师被打翻在地。他的杂货商被打晕。他自己大腿上挂了彩，踉跄着靠到墙上。我们已把他夹进了一张两颌的罗网。'是你吗？'兰伯特隔着被围困的诺丁山那群人，亲切地朝威尔逊喊道。'差不离，'威尔逊将军答道，'把他们压在墙根下。'

"诺丁山的人倒得飞快。亚当·韦恩把他那双长臂朝头顶的墙上一搭，纵身一跃，站到了墙头——映着月亮，一个硕大的身影。他从脚下擎旗手手里一把夺过旗帜，猛地在我们头顶抖开，那声响如同天上的雷霆。

===Original===
“‘Round the Red Lion!’ he cried. ‘Swords round the Red Lion! Halberds round the Red Lion! They are the thorns round rose.’

“His voice and the crack of the banner made a momentary rally, and Lambert, whose idiotic face was almost beautiful with battle, felt it as by an instinct, and cried—

“‘Drop your public-house flag, you footler! Drop it!’

“‘The banner of the Red Lion seldom stoops,’ said Wayne, proudly, letting it out luxuriantly on the night wind.

“The next moment I knew that poor Adam’s sentimental theatricality had cost him much. Lambert was on the wall at a bound, his sword in his teeth, and had slashed at Wayne’s head before he had time to draw his sword, his hands being busy with the enormous flag. He stepped back only just in time to avoid the first cut, and let the flagstaff fall, so that the spear-blade at the end of it pointed to Lambert.

“‘The banner stoops,’ cried Wayne, in a voice that must have startled streets. ‘The banner of Notting Hill stoops to a hero.’ And with the words he drove the spear-point and half the flagstaff through Lambert’s body and dropped him dead upon the road below, a stone upon the stones of the street.

===Chinese===
"'围拢红狮旗！'他喊道，'剑围拢红狮旗！戟围拢红狮旗！它们是玫瑰周围的刺。'

"他的声音和旗帜的爆响唤起了一阵短暂的回勇，而兰伯特——他那张蠢脸因厮杀而近乎变得美丽——凭本能察觉到了这股劲，便喊——

"'放下你那酒馆子旗，你这蠢货！放下！'

"'红狮旗（the Red Lion）罕有低垂之时，'韦恩傲然说，任那旗帜在夜风里奢侈地舒展开来。

"下一刻我便明白，可怜的亚当那感伤的戏瘾，让他付出了沉重的代价。兰伯特一跃上了墙头，嘴里咬着剑，趁韦恩来不及拔剑——他双手正忙于擎那面巨旗——便朝他脑袋劈去。韦恩堪堪退后避过第一劈，让旗杆倒下，使杆头的矛刃指向兰伯特。

"'旗帜低垂了，'韦恩喊道，那嗓门想必惊动了数条街。'诺丁山的旗帜向一位英雄低垂。'话音落处，他把矛尖连着半根旗杆捅穿了兰伯特的身体，把他一具尸体似的扔到下头的路上——一块石头，落在街道的石子之上。

===Original===
“‘Notting Hill! Notting Hill!’ cried Wayne, in a sort of divine rage. ‘Her banner is all the holier for the blood of a brave enemy! Up on the wall, patriots! Up on the wall! Notting Hill!’

“With his long strong arm he actually dragged a man up on to the wall to be silhouetted against the moon, and more and more men climbed up there, pulled themselves and were pulled, till clusters and crowds of the half-massacred men of Pump Street massed upon the wall above us.

“‘Notting Hill! Notting Hill!’ cried Wayne, unceasingly.

“‘Well, what about Bayswater?’ said a worthy workingman in Wilson’s army, irritably. ‘Bayswater forever!’

“‘We have won!’ cried Wayne, striking his flagstaff in the ground. ‘Bayswater forever! We have taught our enemies patriotism!’

“‘Oh, cut these fellows up and have done with it!’ cried one of Lambert’s lieutenants, who was reduced to something bordering on madness by the responsibility of succeeding to the command.

“‘Let us by all means try,’ said Wilson, grimly; and the two armies closed round the third.

===Chinese===
"'诺丁山！诺丁山！'韦恩以一种近乎神授的狂怒喊道，'它的旗帜因一个勇敢敌人的鲜血而更加神圣！上墙来，爱国者们！上墙来！诺丁山！'

"凭他那条又长又有力的胳膊，他竟硬生生把一个人拽上了墙头，映在月亮里；接着又有越来越多的人爬了上去，有自己爬的，也有被人拉的，直到泵街那些被屠杀过半的人，一簇簇、一群群地聚集在我们头顶那道墙上。

"'诺丁山！诺丁山！'韦恩不住地喊。

"'嗳，贝斯沃特呢？'威尔逊军中一个本分的工匠气鼓鼓地说，'贝斯沃特万岁！'

"'我们胜了！'韦恩喊道，把旗杆往地上一顿，'贝斯沃特万岁！我们教会了我们的敌人什么叫爱国！'

"'噢，把这帮家伙剁了算啦！'兰伯特手下一个副官喊道——接掌指挥权的责任，已把他逼到了近乎发疯的边缘。

"'我们不妨一试，'威尔逊冷冷地说；于是两支军队朝第三支合拢过去。

===Original===
“I simply cannot describe what followed. I am sorry, but there is such a thing as physical fatigue, as physical nausea, and, I may add, as physical terror. Suffice it to say that the above paragraph was written about 11 p.m., and that it is now about 2 a.m., and that the battle is not finished, and is not likely to be. Suffice it further to say that down the steep streets which lead from the Waterworks Tower to the Notting Hill High Road, blood has been running, and is running, in great red serpents, that curl out into the main thoroughfare and shine in the moon.

===Chinese===
"接下去所发生的，我简直无法描述。很抱歉，可世上毕竟有体力枯竭这种东西，有生理上的反胃这种东西，我还得加上一句——有生理上的恐惧这种东西。只需说，上面这一段是大约夜里11点写的，而此刻大约是凌晨2点，仗还没有打完，也不像会打完。再只需说：从水塔通往诺丁山高街的那些陡街之上，鲜血一直在流，此刻仍在流，流成一条条巨大的红蛇，蜿蜒着漫到大街上，在月光下闪闪发亮。

===Original===
“Later.—The final touch has been given to all this terrible futility. Hours have passed; morning has broken; men are still swaying and fighting at the foot of the tower and round the corner of Aubrey Road; the fight has not finished. But I know it is a farce.

“News has just come to show that Wayne’s amazing sortie, followed by the amazing resistance through a whole night on the wall of the Waterworks, is as if it had not been. What was the object of that strange exodus we shall probably never know, for the simple reason that everyone who knew will probably be cut to pieces in the course of the next two or three hours.

===Chinese===
"稍后。——这一切可悲的徒劳，已被添上了最后一笔。几个钟头过去了；天已破晓；人们仍在那塔脚下、在奥布里路拐角一带摇来摆去地厮杀；这一仗还没有打完。可我知道，这是一场闹剧。

"刚刚传来的消息表明：韦恩那一次惊人的突击，以及随之而来、在水塔墙上一整夜惊人的抵抗，都等于没有发生过。那次奇异的出走究竟所为何来，我们大概永远不会知道了，原因很简单：凡是知情的人，多半在接下来的两三个钟头里都会被剁成肉酱。

===Original===
“I have heard, about three minutes ago, that Buck and Buck’s methods have won after all. He was perfectly right, of course, when one comes to think of it, in holding that it was physically impossible for a street to defeat a city. While we thought he was patrolling the eastern gates with his Purple army; while we were rushing about the streets and waving halberds and lanterns; while poor old Wilson was scheming like Moltke and fighting like Achilles to entrap the wild Provost of Notting Hill—Mr. Buck, retired draper, has simply driven down in a hansom cab and done something about as plain as butter and about as useful and nasty. He has gone down to South Kensington, Brompton, and Fulham, and by spending about four thousand pounds of his private means, has raised an army of nearly as many men; that is to say, an army big enough to beat, not only Wayne, but Wayne and all his present enemies put together. The army, I understand, is encamped along High Street, Kensington, and fills it from the Church to Addison Road Bridge. It is to advance by ten different roads uphill to the north.

===Chinese===
"大约三分钟前我听说：到头来，还是巴克，还是巴克的法子，赢了。当然，细想想，他完全对——一条街要在物质上打败一座城，根本不可能。当我们以为他在用紫衣军巡守东边各门之时；当我们还在街上乱冲乱撞、挥舞长戟和提灯之时；当可怜的老威尔逊正像毛奇（Moltke）那样运筹、像阿喀琉斯（Achilles）那样厮杀、想要困住那个狂野的诺丁山区督之时——巴克先生，这位退休布商，干脆坐着双轮马车（hansom cab）驰下去，做了一件像黄油一样平淡、像黄油一样管用而讨厌的事。他跑到南肯辛顿、布朗普顿（Brompton）和富勒姆（Fulham），花了大约四千镑私财，招募了一支人数与之相仿的大军；也就是说，这支大军不仅足以击败韦恩，而且足以击败韦恩和他此刻所有敌人加在一起。据我所知，这支大军正沿肯辛顿高街（High Street, Kensington）扎营，从教堂一直填到艾迪森路桥（Addison Road Bridge）。它将分作十路，由南向北，沿坡而上。

===Original===
“I cannot endure to remain here. Everything makes it worse than it need be. The dawn, for instance, has broken round Campden Hill; splendid spaces of silver, edged with gold, are torn out of the sky. Worse still, Wayne and his men feel the dawn; their faces, though bloody and pale, are strangely hopeful… insupportably pathetic. Worst of all, for the moment they are winning. If it were not for Buck and the new army they might just, and only just, win.

“I repeat, I cannot stand it. It is like watching that wonderful play of old Maeterlinck’s (you know my partiality for the healthy, jolly old authors of the nineteenth century), in which one has to watch the quiet conduct of people inside a parlour, while knowing that the very men are outside the door whose word can blast it all with tragedy. And this is worse, for the men are not talking, but writhing and bleeding and dropping dead for a thing that is already settled—and settled against them. The great grey masses of men still toil and tug and sway hither and thither around the great grey tower; and the tower is still motionless, as it will always be motionless. These men will be crushed before the sun is set; and new men will arise and be crushed, and new wrongs done, and tyranny will always rise again like the sun, and injustice will always be as fresh as the flowers of spring. And the stone tower will always look down on it. Matter, in its brutal beauty, will always look down on those who are mad enough to consent to die, and yet more mad, since they consent to live.”

===Chinese===
"我待不下去了。这里的一切，都把这出戏弄得比它本来的更难堪。比方说，黎明已经在坎普登山四周破开；一片片辉煌的银光，镶着金边，从天上撕扯出来。更糟的是，韦恩和他的人感觉到了黎明；他们的脸虽浴着血、虽惨白，却奇异地满含希望……令人难以忍受地令人心酸。最糟的是，眼下他们正在赢。要不是有巴克和那支新军，他们说不定，勉强地，刚好能赢。

"我再说一遍，我受不了。这就像看老梅特林克（Maeterlinck）那出绝妙的戏（你知道我对十九世纪那些健康、快活的老作家是有偏爱的）——在那戏里，你不得不看着客厅里几个人安安静静地举止谈笑，而心里却明白：门外站着的，正是那开口一句便能以悲剧毁灭一切的人。而眼前这出更糟，因为这些人不是在谈笑，而是在扭动、在流血、在一具具倒下死去——为了一桩早已定局的事，而且定局对她们不利。一大片灰色的人群，仍在那座灰色的大塔四周劳苦、拉扯、摇来摆去；而那座塔仍纹丝不动，正如它将永远纹丝不动。这些人，不等日落就要被碾碎；而新的人又会起来，又被碾碎，新的冤屈又会发生，暴政总会像太阳一样重新升起，不义总会像春天的花朵一样新鲜。而那座石塔，将永远俯瞰着这一切。物质，以它那蛮荒之美，将永远俯瞰着那些疯狂到甘愿赴死的人——更疯狂，因为他们还甘愿活着。"

===Original===
Thus ended abruptly the first and last contribution of the Special Correspondent of the Court Journal to that valued periodical.

The Correspondent himself, as has been said, was simply sick and gloomy at the last news of the triumph of Buck. He slouched sadly down the steep Aubrey Road, up which he had the night before run in so unusual an excitement, and strolled out into the empty dawn-lit main road, looking vaguely for a cab. He saw nothing in the vacant space except a blue-and-gold glittering thing, running very fast, which looked at first like a very tall beetle, but turned out, to his great astonishment, to be Barker.

“Have you heard the good news?” asked that gentleman.

“Yes,” said Quin, with a measured voice. “I have heard the glad tidings of great joy. Shall we take a hansom down to Kensington? I see one over there.”

===Chinese===
《宫廷杂志》那位特派通讯员对这份珍贵刊物的头一篇、也是末一篇投稿，就这样戛然而止了。

至于这位通讯员本人——如前所述——一听到巴克最终胜利的消息，便只觉一阵作呕和郁抑。他闷闷不乐地拖着脚步，顺那条陡峭的奥布里路走下去——昨夜他正是顺着这条路、以一种平素罕见的激动跑上去的——他踱到空荡荡、被晨光照亮的大路上，茫然地想找一辆马车。他在那片空地上什么也没看见，只看见一样蓝金交织、闪闪发亮的东西飞快地跑过来，起初像一只很高的甲虫，待到看清竟是他大吃一惊——原来是巴克先生。

"你听到好消息了么？"那位绅士问。

"听到了，"奎因用一种有分寸的声音说，"我听到了那大喜的信息。我们坐双轮马车下肯辛顿去，可好？我瞧见那边有一辆。"

===Original===
They took the cab, and were, in four minutes, fronting the ranks of the multitudinous and invincible army. Quin had not spoken a word all the way, and something about him had prevented the essentially impressionable Barker from speaking either.

The great army, as it moved up Kensington High Street, calling many heads to the numberless windows, for it was long indeed—longer than the lives of most of the tolerably young—since such an army had been seen in London. Compared with the vast organisation which was now swallowing up the miles, with Buck at its head as leader, and the King hanging at its tail as journalist, the whole story of our problem was insignificant. In the presence of that army the red Notting Hills and the green Bayswaters were alike tiny and straggling groups. In its presence the whole struggle round Pump Street was like an anthill under the hoof of an ox. Every man who felt or looked at that infinity of men knew that it was the triumph of Buck’s brutal arithmetic. Whether Wayne was right or wrong, wise or foolish, was quite a fair matter for discussion. But it was a matter of history. At the foot of Church Street, opposite Kensington Church, they paused in their glowing good humour.

===Chinese===
两人坐上马车，四分钟后，便来到了那支人多势众、所向无敌的大军的队列前。奎因这一路上一句话也没说，而他身上有某种东西，令本质易受感染的巴克先生也没敢开口。

大军沿着肯辛顿高街开上去，把许多脑袋唤到数不清的窗前——伦敦见到这样一支军队，确乎是好久好久以前的事了，久得超过了大多数还算年轻的人一辈子的寿命。与这口吞没一英里又一英里的庞大组织相比——巴克作为统帅昂首走在队前，国王作为记者坠在队尾——我们这桩公案的始末，便显得微不足道。在这支大军面前，红衣的诺丁山人也好，绿衣的贝斯沃特人也好，都同样是无足轻重、零零落落的小群。在它面前，泵街周围那场整场鏖战，就像牛蹄底下的一座蚁冢。每一个感觉到、或望见那一望无际的人海的人，都明白：这是巴克那蛮荒算术的胜利。韦恩究竟是对是错、是智是愚，倒大可一辩。但那已是历史的事了。在教堂街（Church Street）口，肯辛顿教堂（Kensington Church）对面，他们在一片光彩的好心情里停了下来。

===Original===
“Let us send some kind of messenger or herald up to them,” said Buck, turning to Barker and the King. “Let us send and ask them to cave in without more muddle.”

“What shall we say to them?” said Barker, doubtfully.

“The facts of the case are quite sufficient,” rejoined Buck. “It is the facts of the case that make an army surrender. Let us simply say that our army that is fighting their army, and their army that is fighting our army, amount altogether to about a thousand men. Say that we have four thousand. It is very simple. Of the thousand fighting, they have at the very most, three hundred, so that, with those three hundred, they have now to fight four thousand seven hundred men. Let them do it if it amuses them.”

And the Provost of North Kensington laughed.

===Chinese===
"我们派个什么传令的、或是传令官（herald）上去，"巴克转向巴克先生和国王说，"我们派人去叫他们别再瞎折腾，干脆认输算了。"

"我们怎么说呢？"巴克先生迟疑地说。

"把事实摆给他们就够了，"巴克答道，"使一支军队投降的，正是事实。我们只消说：我们的军队加他们的军队，两边打仗的加起来，总共约一千人。再说我们有四千。这很简单。这一千个打仗的里边，他们至多不过三百，也就是说，凭那三百人，他们此刻要对付的是四千七百人。要是他们觉得有趣，就让他们对付好了。"

北肯辛顿区督说罢，大笑。

===Original===
The herald who was despatched up Church Street in all the pomp of the South Kensington blue and gold, with the Three Birds on his tabard, was attended by two trumpeters.

“What will they do when they consent?” asked Barker, for the sake of saying something in the sudden stillness of that immense army.

“I know my Wayne very well,” said Buck, laughing. “When he submits he will send a red herald flaming with the Lion of Notting Hill. Even defeat will be delightful to him, since it is formal and romantic.”

The King, who had strolled up to the head of the line, broke silence for the first time.

“I shouldn’t wonder,” he said, “if he defied you, and didn’t send the herald after all. I don’t think you do know your Wayne quite so well as you think.”

“All right, your Majesty,” said Buck, easily; “if it isn’t disrespectful, I’ll put my political calculations in a very simple form. I’ll lay you ten pounds to a shilling the herald comes with the surrender.”

“All right,” said Auberon. “I may be wrong, but it’s my notion of Adam Wayne that he’ll die in his city, and that, till he is dead, it will not be a safe property.”

“The bet’s made, your Majesty,” said Buck.

===Chinese===
被派上教堂街的那位传令官，全副南肯辛顿蓝金排场，号衣上绣着三只鸟（the Three Birds，即金鸟旗 the Golden Birds 纹章），由两名号手随行。

"他们要是认了输，会怎么做？"巴克先生问，只为在这支庞大军队突如其来的寂静里说点什么。

"我的韦恩，我太了解了，"巴克笑着说，"他投降时，一定会派一个红衣传令官来，通体烈焰般绣着诺丁山的狮。连失败，对他都是一桩赏心乐事，因为失败也是讲礼数的、浪漫的。"

国王原本踱到了队首，这时第一次开了口。

"我也不会奇怪，"他说，"如果他干脆抗命，压根儿不派传令官来。我看你并不像你自以为的那样了解你的韦恩。"

"好罢，陛下，"巴克随口说，"要是不算冒犯的话，我愿把我的政治盘算，用一个极简单的形式来表达。我跟你赌十镑对一先令：传令官会带着降书来。"

"好，"奥伯伦说，"我也可能错，不过依我对亚当·韦恩的看法，他会死在他的城里；在他死之前，这地方不会是一份安稳的产业。"

"赌定了，陛下，"巴克说。

===Original===
Another long silence ensued, in the course of which Barker alone, amid the motionless army, strolled and stamped in his restless way.

Then Buck suddenly leant forward.

“It’s taking your money, your Majesty,” he said. “I knew it was. There comes the herald from Adam Wayne.”

“It’s not,” cried the King, peering forward also. “You brute, it’s a red omnibus.”

“It’s not,” said Buck, calmly; and the King did not answer, for down the centre of the spacious and silent Church Street was walking, beyond question, the herald of the Red Lion, with two trumpeters.

===Chinese===
又一阵长久的沉默随之而来，其间唯有一人——巴克先生——在这支一动不动的军队里踱来踱去、顿来顿去，按他那停不下来的性子。

忽然，巴克把身子朝前一探。

"这是在白拿你的钱，陛下，"他说，"我早知道是。亚当·韦恩的传令官来了。"

"才不是，"国王也向前探着身子喊道，"你这坏东西，那是一辆红色公共马车。"

"不是的，"巴克平静地说；国王没有答话——因为，沿着那条宽阔而寂静的教堂街正中走过来的，毫无疑问，是红狮旗的传令官，身后跟着两名号手。

===Original===
Buck had something in him which taught him how to be magnanimous. In his hour of success he felt magnanimous towards Wayne, whom he really admired; magnanimous towards the King, off whom he had scored so publicly; and, above all, magnanimous towards Barker, who was the titular leader of this vast South Kensington army, which his own talent had evoked.

“General Barker,” he said, bowing, “do you propose now to receive the message from the besieged?”

Barker bowed also, and advanced towards the herald.

“Has your master, Mr. Adam Wayne, received our request for surrender?” he asked.

The herald conveyed a solemn and respectful affirmative.

Barker resumed, coughing slightly, but encouraged.

“What answer does your master send?”

The herald again inclined himself submissively, and answered in a kind of monotone.

“My message is this. Adam Wayne, Lord High Provost of Notting Hill, under the charter of King Auberon and the laws of God and all mankind, free and of a free city, greets James Barker, Lord High Provost of South Kensington, by the same rights free and honourable, leader of the army of the South. With all friendly reverence, and with all constitutional consideration, he desires James Barker to lay down his arms, and the whole army under his command to lay down their arms also.”

===Chinese===
巴克骨子里有某种东西，教他懂得怎样宽宏大量。在他得胜的时辰，他对韦恩——他真心佩服的人——感到宽宏；他对国王——他刚当众赢了对方一筹——感到宽宏；而最要紧的，他对巴克先生——这位名义上统率着巴克一己之才召唤出来的南肯辛顿庞大军队的人——感到宽宏。

"巴克将军，"他鞠了一躬说，"您现在可愿接收被围者送来的口信？"

巴克先生也鞠了一躬，朝传令官迎上前去。

"你的主人，亚当·韦恩先生，可曾收到我们要他投降的要求？"他问。

传令官庄严而恭敬地传达了一个肯定的表示。

巴克先生清了清嗓子，又受了点鼓舞，接着说——

"你的主人作何答复？"

传令官又恭顺地躬了躬身，用一种近乎单调的声调答道——

"我的口信如下：亚当·韦恩，诺丁山高级区督（Lord High Provost），秉奥伯伦国王宪章（the charter of King Auberon）及上帝与全人类的律法，为一座自由城市的自由之人，问候詹姆斯·巴克，南肯辛顿高级区督——以同等权利，自由而可敬——南方军队的统帅。怀着一切友善的敬意，怀着一切合乎宪法的体谅，他请詹姆斯·巴克放下武器，并请他统率下的全部军队，一并放下武器。"

===Original===
Before the words were ended the King had run forward into the open space with shining eyes. The rest of the staff and the forefront of the army were literally struck breathless. When they recovered they began to laugh beyond restraint; the revulsion was too sudden.

“The Lord High Provost of Notting Hill,” continued the herald, “does not propose, in the event of your surrender, to use his victory for any of those repressive purposes which others have entertained against him. He will leave you your free laws and your free cities, your flags and your governments. He will not destroy the religion of South Kensington, or crush the old customs of Bayswater.”

An irrepressible explosion of laughter went up from the forefront of the great army.

“The King must have had something to do with this humour,” said Buck, slapping his thigh. “It’s too deliciously insolent. Barker, have a glass of wine.”

And in his conviviality he actually sent a soldier across to the restaurant opposite the church and brought out two glasses for a toast.

===Chinese===
话音未落，国王已眼中闪光，奔到那片空地上来。其余的幕僚和大军的最前列，简直都被惊得喘不上气。等他们定下神来，便忍不住放声大笑；这反差来得太突然了。

"诺丁山高级区督，"传令官接着说，"在你们投降之后，并不打算把他这场胜利，用于任何别人曾想加之于他的那些压制手段。他将保留你们自由的法律、自由的城市，保留你们的旗帜和政府。他不会毁掉南肯辛顿的宗教，也不会压垮贝斯沃特古老的风俗。"

大军最前列爆发出一阵压抑不住的大笑。

"这股幽默劲儿，准是国王掺和的，"巴克拍着大腿说，"傲慢得未免太好玩了。巴克先生，来杯酒吧。"

他一时高兴，竟当真派了一个兵，跑到教堂对面那家餐馆去，端出两杯酒来干杯。

===Original===
When the laughter had died down, the herald continued quite monotonously—

“In the event of your surrendering your arms and dispersing under the superintendence of our forces, these local rights of yours shall be carefully observed. In the event of your not doing so, the Lord High Provost of Notting Hill desires to announce that he has just captured the Waterworks Tower, just above you, on Campden Hill, and that within ten minutes from now, that is, on the reception through me of your refusal, he will open the great reservoir and flood the whole valley where you stand in thirty feet of water. God save King Auberon!”

Buck had dropped his glass and sent a great splash of wine over the road.

“But—but—” he said; and then by a last and splendid effort of his great sanity, looked the facts in the face.

“We must surrender,” he said. “You could do nothing against fifty thousand tons of water coming down a steep hill, ten minutes hence. We must surrender. Our four thousand men might as well be four. Vicisti Galilaee! Perkins, you may as well get me another glass of wine.”

In this way the vast army of South Kensington surrendered and the Empire of Notting Hill began. One further fact in this connection is perhaps worth mentioning—the fact that, after his victory, Adam Wayne caused the great tower on Campden Hill to be plated with gold and inscribed with a great epitaph, saying that it was the monument of Wilfrid Lambert, the heroic defender of the place, and surmounted with a statue, in which his large nose was done something less than justice to.

===Chinese===
笑声平息之后，传令官又用那一成不变的声调接着说——

"在你们交出武器、并由我军监督解散的情况下，你们这些地方性权利将得到悉心尊重。在你们不肯照办的情况下，诺丁山高级区督愿在此宣告：他刚刚夺取了水塔——就在你们头顶，坎普登山上的那座——而自现在起十分钟之内，也就是说，一经我收到你们的拒绝，他便要打开那座大水库，放三十英尺深的水，淹掉你们所站的这一整片谷地。上帝保佑奥伯伦国王！"

巴克失手落了酒杯，把一大摊酒泼在了路上。

"可是——可是——"他说；随即，凭着一种最后的、辉煌的、由他那份伟大的神智所生的努力，他直面了事实。

"我们只能投降，"他说，"十分钟后，五万吨水顺着陡坡冲下来，谁也挡不住。我们只能投降。我们这四千人，跟四个也差不多。*Vicisti Galilaee!*（加利利人啊，你胜了！）珀金斯（Perkins），你也替我再弄一杯酒来吧。"

南肯辛顿这支庞大的军队，就这样投降了；诺丁山的帝国，就此肇始。关于这一节，还有一桩事或许值得一提——那就是：亚当·韦恩得胜之后，下令把坎普登山上的那座大塔镀了金，又刻上一段长长的墓志铭，说它是威尔弗里德·兰伯特——此地的英勇保卫者——的纪念碑；塔顶还安了一座雕像，只是雕像上那只大鼻子，对他未免有失公道。"

## Book V / 第五卷

## I. The Empire of Notting Hill / 第一章 诺丁山帝国

===Original===
On the evening of the third of October, twenty years after the great victory of Notting Hill, which gave it the dominion of London, King Auberon came, as of old, out of Kensington Palace.

He had changed little, save for a streak or two of grey in his hair, for his face had always been old, and his step slow, and, as it were, decrepit.

===Chinese===
十月三日傍晚，诺丁山那场使其夺得伦敦统治权的伟大胜利已过去了二十年，奥伯伦国王一如往日，从肯辛顿宫（Kensington Palace）里走了出来。

他几乎没什么变化，只是头发里多了那么一两缕灰白，因为他的脸本来就一向显老，脚步本来就一向迟缓，甚至可以说，本来就带着几分老朽。

===Original===
If he looked old, it was not because of anything physical or mental. It was because he still wore, with a quaint conservatism, the frock-coat and high hat of the days before the great war. "I have survived the Deluge," he said. "I am a pyramid, and must behave as such."

===Chinese===
他若显得老，那并非因为身心上有什么衰颓。那是因为他仍以一种古怪的保守，穿着大战之前那种礼服和高筒帽。"我熬过了大洪水，"他说，"我是一座金字塔，行事就该像座金字塔。"

===Original===
As he passed up the street the Kensingtonians, in their picturesque blue smocks, saluted him as a King, and then looked after him as a curiosity. It seemed odd to them that men had once worn so elvish an attire.

The King, cultivating the walk attributed to the oldest inhabitant ("Gaffer Auberon" his friends were now confidentially desired to call him), went toddling northward. He paused, with reminiscence in his eye, at the Southern Gate of Notting Hill, one of those nine great gates of bronze and steel, wrought with reliefs of the old battles, by the hand of Chiffy himself.

===Chinese===
他沿街走去，肯辛顿人穿着他们那好看的蓝色罩衫，先以国王之礼向他致敬，随即又把他当作一桩奇观目送他远去。在他们看来颇为古怪：人类竟曾一度穿过这般精灵似的装束。

国王刻意学着那位据说最年高的老住户的步态（他如今私下里请朋友们叫他"老奥伯伦"〔Gaffer Auberon〕），一摇一晃地向北走去。他在诺丁山的南门前驻足，眼中浮起追忆——那是九座青铜与钢铁铸成的大门之一，门上饰有往昔诸役的浮雕，皆出奇菲（Chiffy）亲手所制。

===Original===
"Ah!" he said, shaking his head and assuming an unnecessary air of age, and a provincialism of accent—"Ah! I mind when there warn't none of this here."

He passed through the Ossington Gate, surmounted by a great lion, wrought in red copper on yellow brass, with the motto, "Nothing Ill." The guard in red and gold saluted him with his halberd.

===Chinese===
"啊！"他说，摇着头，摆出一副多余的龙钟之态，又操起一口多余的乡音——"啊！俺还记得这地界啥都没有的那会儿呢。"

他穿过奥辛顿门（Ossington Gate），门顶上有一头巨狮，以红铜铸于黄铜之上，旁刻铭文"Nothing Ill"（百无邪厄——与 Notting Hill 谐音的双关）。那红金制服的卫士以戟向他致敬。

===Original===
It was about sunset, and the lamps were being lit. Auberon paused to look at them, for they were Chiffy's finest work, and his artistic eye never failed to feast on them. In memory of the Great Battle of the Lamps, each great iron lamp was surmounted by a veiled figure, sword in hand, holding over the flame an iron hood or extinguisher, as if ready to let it fall if the armies of the South and West should again show their flags in the city. Thus no child in Notting Hill could play about the streets without the very lampposts reminding him of the salvation of his country in the dreadful year.

===Chinese===
其时约莫日落，街灯正一盏盏点亮。奥伯伦驻足凝望，因为这是奇菲最精妙的作品，他这双艺术的眼睛从不曾放过对它们的饱览。为纪念伟大的灯之战（the Great Battle of the Lamps），每一盏巨大的铁灯顶端都立着一个戴面纱的人像，手持长剑，在火焰上方举着一只铁罩——也可说是灭火帽——仿佛随时准备将它罩落，以防南面和西面的大军再次在城中打出他们的旗帜。于是，诺丁山没有一个孩童能在街巷间嬉戏而不被那一根根灯柱提醒：在那可怕的年头，他的祖国是如何得救的。

===Original===
"Old Wayne was right in a way," commented the King. "The sword does make things beautiful. It has made the whole world romantic by now. And to think people once thought me a buffoon for suggesting a romantic Notting Hill. Deary me, deary me! (I think that is the expression)—it seems like a previous existence."

===Chinese===
"老韦恩在某层意义上倒是对的，"国王评道，"剑确实使事物变得美丽。到如今，它已使整个世界都浪漫起来了。想当年，人们还因为我提议一个浪漫的诺丁山而把我当作小丑呢。天哪，天哪！（我想这个说法是这么讲的吧）——恍若隔世啊。"

===Original===
Turning a corner, he found himself in Pump Street, opposite the four shops which Adam Wayne had studied twenty years before. He entered idly the shop of Mr. Mead, the grocer. Mr. Mead was somewhat older, like the rest of the world, and his red beard, which he now wore with a moustache, and long and full, was partly blanched and discoloured. He was dressed in a long and richly embroidered robe of blue, brown, and crimson, interwoven with an Eastern complexity of pattern, and covered with obscure symbols and pictures, representing his wares passing from hand to hand and from nation to nation. Round his neck was the chain with the Blue Argosy cut in turquoise, which he wore as Grand Master of the Grocers. The whole shop had the sombre and sumptuous look of its owner. The wares were displayed as prominently as in the old days, but they were now blended and arranged with a sense of tint and grouping, too often neglected by the dim grocers of those forgotten days. The wares were shown plainly, but shown not so much as an old grocer would have shown his stock, but rather as an educated virtuoso would have shown his treasures. The tea was stored in great blue and green vases, inscribed with the nine indispensable sayings of the wise men of China. Other vases of a confused orange and purple, less rigid and dominant, more humble and dreamy, stored symbolically the tea of India. A row of caskets of a simple silvery metal contained tinned meats. Each was wrought with some rude but rhythmic form, as a shell, a horn, a fish, or an apple, to indicate what material had been canned in it.

===Chinese===
拐过一个街角，他便置身于泵街（Pump Street），正对着二十年前亚当·韦恩曾细细考察过的那四间铺子。他信步走进了杂货商米德先生（Mr. Mead）的铺子。米德先生也与世上其余的人一样，略见老态，他那把红胡子——如今配上髭须，蓄得又长又密——已部分褪色泛白。他身穿一袭长袍，蓝、褐、绯红三色相间，绣工繁丽，纹样交织得有东方的错综之妙，袍上满布晦涩的符号与图画，描摹着他的货物如何一手一手、一国一国地流转。他颈间挂着一条链子，上面以绿松石雕着"蓝色商船队"（the Blue Argosy）的记号——他身为杂货商行会总长而佩戴此物。整间铺子都带着它主人那种沉郁而华贵的气派。货物一如既往地陈列得醒目，但如今它们的色调与搭配都经过了精心的调和与布置——这在那些已被遗忘的年代里昏暗的杂货商们是太常疏忽的。货物摆得清清楚楚，但与其说是一个老杂货商在陈列他的存货，不如说是一个受过教养的鉴赏家在展示他的珍藏。茶叶贮存在蓝绿两色的大瓷瓮里，瓮上题着中国智者的九句不可不备的格言。另一些瓷瓮呈混沌的橙紫之色，不那么凌厉逼人，更谦卑、更如梦，象征性地贮存着印度茶。一排素银金属的小匣子装着罐头肉。每一只都饰有某种粗犷而有韵律的造型——或贝壳、或号角、或鱼、或苹果——以表明其中所装何物。

===Original===
"Your Majesty," said Mr. Mead, sweeping an Oriental reverence. "This is an honour to me, but yet more an honour to the city."

Auberon took off his hat.

"Mr. Mead," he said, "Notting Hill, whether in giving or taking, can deal in nothing but honour. Do you happen to sell liquorice?"

===Chinese===
"陛下，"米德先生说着，行了一个东方式的深深躬身礼。"这于我是荣幸，于本城却更是荣幸。"

奥伯伦脱了脱帽。

"米德先生，"他说，"诺丁山无论是予是取，所经手的无非荣耀二字。你这儿可有甘草卖？"

===Original===
"Liquorice, sire," said Mr. Mead, "is not the least important of our benefits out of the dark heart of Arabia."

And going reverently towards a green and silver canister, made in the form of an Arabian mosque, he proceeded to serve his customer.

===Chinese===
"甘草吗，陛下，"米德先生说，"实乃我们从阿拉伯那颗幽暗心脏中所得的恩赐之一，绝非最微不足道的一种。"

说罢他恭恭敬敬地走向一只绿银两色的茶叶罐——那罐子做成阿拉伯清真寺的形状——开始招呼起他的顾客来。

===Original===
"I was just thinking, Mr. Mead," said the King, reflectively, "I don't know why I should think about it just now, but I was just thinking of twenty years ago. Do you remember the times before the war?"

The grocer, having wrapped up the liquorice sticks in a piece of paper (inscribed with some appropriate sentiment), lifted his large grey eyes dreamily, and looked at the darkening sky outside.

===Chinese===
"我方才正想着呢，米德先生，"国王若有所思地说，"我也不知道为什么偏偏这会儿想起这个，但我方才正想着二十年前的事。你可还记得战前的那些日子？"

杂货商已把甘草棒用一张纸包好（纸上印着某句应景的吉语），他抬起那双灰色的大眼，出神地望向外头渐暗的天色。

===Original===
"Oh yes, your Majesty," he said. "I remember these streets before the Lord Provost began to rule us. I can't remember how we felt very well. All the great songs and the fighting change one so; and I don't think we can really estimate all we owe to the Provost; but I can remember his coming into this very shop twenty-two years ago, and I remember the things he said. The singular thing is that, as far as I remember, I thought the things he said odd at that time. Now it's the things that I said, as far as I can recall them, that seem to me odd—as odd as a madman's antics."

===Chinese===
"哦，记得，陛下，"他说，"我记得高级区督尚未开始治理我们时的那些街巷。我已经不太能记得清我们当时是怎样一种心境了。那些伟大的歌和那些战斗，把一个人改变得太厉害；我想我们也实在估量不出我们欠区督多少；但我记得二十二年前他走进这间铺子，我记得他说过的那些话。奇异的是，就我记忆所及，当时我还觉得他说的话挺古怪。可如今，是我自己说过的话——就我所能回忆起来的——倒显得古怪了，古怪得像一个疯子的做派。"

===Original===
"Ah!" said the King; and looked at him with an unfathomable quietness.

"I thought nothing of being a grocer then," he said. "Isn't that odd enough for anybody? I thought nothing of all the wonderful places that my goods come from, and wonderful ways that they are made. I did not know that I was for all practical purposes a king with slaves spearing fishes near the secret pool, and gathering fruits in the islands under the world. My mind was a blank on the thing. I was as mad as a hatter."

===Chinese===
"啊！"国王说，以一种深不可测的安静望着他。

"那时候我一点也不把当个杂货商当回事，"他说，"这岂不是够古怪的了吗？我一点也不把我的货物来自的那些奇妙的地方、它们被制成的那些奇妙的方式当回事。我不知道，就一切实际的效用而言，我乃是一位国王——有奴隶在隐秘的水潭边以鱼叉刺鱼，在世界尽头的岛屿上采集果实。我在这件事上心如死水，浑然无觉。我疯得像帽匠（mad as a hatter）一样。"

===Original===
The King turned also, and stared out into the dark, where the great lamps that commemorated the battle were already flaming.

"And is this the end of poor old Wayne?" he said, half to himself. "To inflame everyone so much that he is lost himself in the blaze. Is this his victory that he, my incomparable Wayne, is now only one in a world of Waynes? Has he conquered and become by conquest commonplace? Must Mr. Mead, the grocer, talk as high as he? Lord! what a strange world in which a man cannot remain unique even by taking the trouble to go mad!"

And he went dreamily out of the shop.

===Chinese===
国王也转过身，凝望向外面的夜色，那儿，纪念那场战役的一盏盏大灯已然熊熊燃起。

"这难道就是可怜的老韦恩的结局吗？"他半是自语地说，"把每一个人都点燃到这般地步，以至于他自己反而湮没在了那片烈焰之中。难道他的胜利就是——他，我这个无与伦比的韦恩，如今只不过是韦恩的世界里的一个韦恩罢了？他征服了，却因征服而变得平庸了吗？难道连米德先生这样一个杂货商，也得像他那样高谈阔论吗？主啊！这是怎样一个古怪的世界——一个人竟无法哪怕费尽心机去发一场疯，来保住自己的独一无二！"

他恍恍惚惚地走出了铺子。

===Original===
He paused outside the next one almost precisely as the Provost had done two decades before.

"How uncommonly creepy this shop looks!" he said. "But yet somehow encouragingly creepy, invitingly creepy. It looks like something in a jolly old nursery story in which you are frightened out of your skin, and yet know that things always end well. The way those low sharp gables are carved like great black bat's wings folded down, and the way those queer-coloured bowls underneath are made to shine like giant's eyeballs. It looks like a benevolent warlock's hut. It is apparently a chemist's."

===Chinese===
他在隔壁那间铺子门前停下，几乎与二十年前区督所做的一模一样。

"这间铺子看上去多么不同寻常地阴森可怖啊！"他说，"可这种阴森里，又莫名带着几分叫人安心的意味，几分引人入内的意味。它像是某个欢快的古老摇篮故事里的什么物事——你被吓得魂飞魄散，却心知事情总归会有个好收场。瞧那几道低矮尖削的山墙，被雕成巨大的黑色蝙蝠翅膀收拢下来的模样；瞧底下那些颜色诡异的钵子，被擦得像巨人的眼珠子一般锃亮。它像是一个善意的巫师的小屋。这显然是一间药铺。"

===Original===
Almost as he spoke, Mr. Bowles, the chemist, came to his shop door in a long black velvet gown and hood, monastic as it were, but yet with a touch of the diabolic. His hair was still quite black, and his face even paler than of old. The only spot of colour he carried was a red star cut in some precious stone of strong tint, hung on his breast. He belonged to the Society of the Red Star of Charity, founded on the lamps displayed by doctors and chemists.

"A fine evening, sir," said the chemist. "Why, I can scarcely be mistaken in supposing it to be your Majesty. Pray step inside and share a bottle of sal-volatile, or anything that may take your fancy. As it happens, there is an old acquaintance of your Majesty's in my shop carousing (if I may be permitted the term) upon that beverage at this moment."

===Chinese===
话音未落，药剂师鲍尔斯先生（Mr. Bowles）便来到了他的店门口，身穿一袭黑色天鹅绒长袍、戴一顶风帽，修士似的，却又带着一丝邪魔的气息。他的头发依旧乌黑，脸色却比从前更白了。他身上唯一的色彩，是胸前挂着的一颗红星，以某种色泽浓烈的宝石雕成。他隶属于红星慈善会（Society of the Red Star of Charity）——此会之设立，乃是以医生与药剂师所悬的灯为蓝本。

"好一个宜人的夜晚，先生，"药剂师说，"哎呀，要我说来者便是陛下，想必不致有错。请移步入内，与在下共饮一瓶嗅盐酒（sal-volatile），或别的什么合您心意之物吧。碰巧得很，此刻在下铺子里正有一位陛下的故交，在那饮品上头（容我如此措辞）纵情痛饮着呢。"

===Original===
The King entered the shop, which was an Aladdin's garden of shades and hues, for as the chemist's scheme of colour was more brilliant than the grocer's scheme, so it was arranged with even more delicacy and fancy. Never, if the phrase may be employed, had such a nosegay of medicines been presented to the artistic eye.

But even the solemn rainbow of that evening interior was rivalled or even eclipsed by the figure standing in the centre of the shop. His form, which was a large and stately one, was clad in a brilliant blue velvet, cut in the richest Renaissance fashion, and slashed so as to show gleams and gaps of a wonderful lemon or pale yellow. He had several chains round his neck, and his plumes, which were of several tints of bronze and gold, hung down to the great gold hilt of his long sword. He was drinking a dose of sal-volatile, and admiring its opal tint. The King advanced with a slight mystification towards the tall figure, whose face was in shadow; then he said—

"By the Great Lord of Luck, Barker!"

===Chinese===
国王走进店堂，但见其间光影色调斑斓，有如阿拉丁的花园——因为药剂师的配色比杂货商的配色更为绚烂，其布置也就愈发精巧而富于巧思。倘若可以这么说的话，这般赏心悦目的一束"药草花束"，献给艺术的慧眼，尚属前所未有。

然而，即便是这傍晚店堂里那一派肃穆的彩虹，也敌不过——甚至被盖过了——立在铺子正中的那个人影。他身形高大而气宇轩昂，穿一身灿亮的蓝色天鹅绒，裁剪是极尽华美的文艺复兴样式，衣身的割口处露出美妙的柠檬黄、淡黄的闪光与缝隙。他颈间挂着数条链子，他的羽饰由青铜与黄金的数种色调构成，一直垂到他那柄长剑硕大的金剑柄上。他正在饮一剂嗅盐酒，欣赏着它那蛋白石般的色泽。国王带着一丝困惑，向那高大的身影走去——那人的脸笼在阴影里；随即他说——

"凭万能的好运之神，巴克先生（Barker）！"

===Original===
The figure removed his plumed cap, showing the same dark head and long, almost equine face which the King had so often seen rising out of the high collar of Bond Street. Except for a grey patch on each temple, it was totally unchanged.

"Your Majesty," said Barker, "this is a meeting nobly retrospective, a meeting that has about it a certain October gold. I drink to old days;" and he finished his sal-volatile with simple feeling.

===Chinese===
那身影摘下饰羽的帽子，露出那一颗乌发的头和那张长长的、近乎马脸的面孔——国王曾无数次看见它从邦德街（Bond Street）的高领子里拔起来。除了两鬓各有一块灰白之外，全然未变。

"陛下，"巴克先生说，"这是一次格调高贵的怀旧之聚，一次带着几分十月金色的重逢。我为旧日干杯；"说罢他满怀朴素的真情，饮尽了那杯嗅盐酒。

===Original===
"I am delighted to see you again, Barker," said the King. "It is indeed long since we met. What with my travels in Asia Minor, and my book having to be written (you have read my Life of Prince Albert for Children, of course?), we have scarcely met twice since the Great War. That is twenty years ago."

"I wonder," said Barker, thoughtfully, "if I might speak freely to your Majesty?"

"Well," said Auberon, "it's rather late in the day to start speaking respectfully. Flap away, my bird of freedom."

===Chinese===
"又见到你真是太高兴了，巴克先生，"国王说，"我们确实久未谋面了。一来我在小亚细亚游历，二来我又得写书（你当然读过我的《写给儿童的阿尔伯特亲王传》〔Life of Prince Albert for Children〕咯？），自大战以来我们几乎只见过两回。那可是二十年前的事了。"

"不知，"巴克先生若有所思地说，"我能否在陛下面前畅所欲言？"

"这个嘛，"奥伯伦说，"到了这把岁数才开始讲究恭顺，未免太晚了些。尽情地扑腾吧，我那自由的鸟儿。"

===Original===
"Well, your Majesty," replied Barker, lowering his voice, "I don't think it will be so long to the next war."

"What do you mean?" asked Auberon.

"We will stand this insolence no longer," burst out Barker, fiercely. "We are not slaves because Adam Wayne twenty years ago cheated us with a water-pipe. Notting Hill is Notting Hill; it is not the world. We in South Kensington, we also have memories—ay, and hopes. If they fought for these trumpery shops and a few lampposts, shall we not fight for the great High Street and the sacred Natural History Museum?"

===Chinese===
"那么，陛下，"巴克先生压低了声音答道，"我倒是觉得，离下一场战争，怕是不会再有那样久了。"

"你这话什么意思？"奥伯伦问。

"我们再也受不了这种跋扈了，"巴克先生激烈地爆发道，"我们并不是奴隶，并不因为二十年前亚当·韦恩用一根水管骗了我们一次，便世世代代做奴隶。诺丁山就是诺丁山；它不是全世界。我们南肯辛顿人，我们也有记忆——是啊，也有希望。倘若他们能为这些不值一提的铺子和几根灯柱而战，我们难道不能为伟大的高街和神圣的自然历史博物馆（Natural History Museum）而战吗？"

===Original===
"Great Heavens!" said the astounded Auberon. "Will wonders never cease? Have the two greatest marvels been achieved? Have you turned altruistic, and has Wayne turned selfish? Are you the patriot, and he the tyrant?"

"It is not from Wayne himself altogether that the evil comes," answered Barker. "He, indeed, is now mostly wrapped in dreams, and sits with his old sword beside the fire. But Notting Hill is the tyrant, your Majesty. Its Council and its crowds have been so intoxicated by the spreading over the whole city of Wayne's old ways and visions, that they try to meddle with everyone, and rule everyone, and civilise everyone, and tell everyone what is good for him. I do not deny the great impulse which his old war, wild as it seemed, gave to the civic life of our time. It came when I was still a young man, and I admit it enlarged my career. But we are not going to see our own cities flouted and thwarted from day to day because of something Wayne did for us all nearly a quarter of a century ago. I am just waiting here for news upon this very matter. It is rumoured that Notting Hill has vetoed the statue of General Wilson they are putting up opposite Chepstow Place. If that is so, it is a black and white shameless breach of the terms on which we surrendered to Turnbull after the battle of the Tower. We were to keep our own customs and self-government. If that is so—"

===Chinese===
"老天爷！"惊愕的奥伯伦说，"奇迹难道永无休止吗？最了不起的那两大奇迹可都达成了吗？莫非你变成了利他主义者，而韦恩变成了自私鬼？莫非你成了爱国者，而他却成了暴君？"

"祸患并非全然来自韦恩本人，"巴克先生答道，"他嘛，如今多半沉溺在梦里，抱着他那柄旧剑坐在炉火旁。可暴君是诺丁山啊，陛下。它的区督议会和它的人群，已经被韦恩旧日的做派与幻象在全城蔓延开来这件事灌得酩酊大醉，竟想要插手每一个人、统治每一个人、开化每一个人，告诉每一个人什么才是对他好的。我并不否认他那场旧日的战争——尽管看起来狂妄——给予我们这个时代的市政生活的那一伟大推动。它来时我还是个年轻人，我承认它拓展了我的前程。可我们绝不打算眼睁睁看着自己的城邦日复一日地被轻慢、被阻挠，全因为韦恩在将近四分之一世纪之前替我们大家做了那么一件事。我正是在这里等候关于此事的消息。有谣传说，诺丁山已经否决了他们正竖在切普斯托广场（Chepstow Place）对面的那座威尔逊将军（General Wilson）像。若果真如此，那便是公然的、赤裸裸的背信——是对水塔之战（the battle of the Tower）后我们向特恩布尔（Turnbull）投降时所订条件的公然背弃。我们本应保有我们自己的习俗与自治。若果真如此——"

===Original===
"It is so," said a deep voice; and both men turned round.

A burly figure in purple robes, with a silver eagle hung round his neck and moustaches almost as florid as his plumes, stood in the doorway.

"Yes," he said, acknowledging the King's start, "I am Provost Buck, and the news is true. These men of the Hill have forgotten that we fought round the Tower as well as they, and that it is sometimes foolish, as well as base, to despise the conquered."

===Chinese===
"果真如此，"一个低沉的声音说；两人都转过身来。

一个魁梧的身影立在门口，身着紫袍，颈间悬着一只银鹰，两撇髭须几乎和他的羽饰一样火红。

"不错，"他说，应住了国王那一惊，"我是区督巴克（Buck），消息属实。这些山城的人忘了，当年围塔而战的不止他们，我们也是一样；他们也忘了，鄙夷被征服者，有时既属卑劣，亦属愚蠢。"

===Original===
"Let us step outside," said Barker, with a grim composure.

Buck did so, and stood rolling his eyes up and down the lamp-lit street.

"I would like to have a go at smashing all this," he muttered, "though I am over sixty. I would like—"

His voice ended in a cry, and he reeled back a step, with his hands to his eyes, as he had done in those streets twenty years before.

"Darkness!" he cried—"darkness again! What does it mean?"

===Chinese===
"我们到外头去吧，"巴克先生阴沉而镇定地说。

巴克照办了，站在灯烛通明的街上来回扫视，眼珠子上下滚动。

"我真想动手把这统统砸个稀烂，"他嘟囔道，"哪怕我已年过六十。我真想——"

他的声音以一声惊叫收尾，他踉跄着倒退了一步，双手捂住了眼睛——一如二十年前他在这些街上所做的那样。

"黑暗！"他叫道——"又是黑暗！这是什么意思？"

===Original===
For in truth every lamp in the street had gone out, so that they could not see even each other's outline, except faintly. The voice of the chemist came with startling cheerfulness out of the density.

"Oh, don't you know?" he said. "Did they never tell you this is the Feast of the Lamps, the anniversary of the great battle that almost lost and just saved Notting Hill? Don't you know, your Majesty, that on this night twenty-one years ago we saw Wilson's green uniforms charging down this street, and driving Wayne and Turnbull back upon the gasworks, fighting with their handful like fiends from hell? And that then, in that great hour, Wayne sprang through a window of the gasworks, with one blow of his hand brought darkness on the whole city, and then with a cry like a lion's, that was heard through four streets, flew at Wilson's men, sword in hand, and swept them, bewildered as they were, and ignorant of the map, clear out of the sacred street again? And don't you know that upon that night every year all lights are turned out for half an hour while we sing the Notting Hill anthem in the darkness? Hark! there it begins."

===Chinese===
原来这条街上每一盏灯确确实实都熄灭了，他们甚至连彼此的轮廓也看不清了，只能隐隐约约地辨出一点。药剂师的声音从那浓密的黑暗中传来，欢快得令人吃惊。

"哦，你们不知道吗？"他说，"难道从来没人告诉过你们，这是灯节（the Feast of the Lamps），是那场几乎断送、又恰好拯救了诺丁山的大战的周年纪念？陛下难道不知道，二十一年前的这个夜晚，我们亲眼看着威尔逊的绿军服沿着这条街冲锋，把韦恩和特恩布尔一路逼退到煤气厂，他们那寥寥数人如地狱的恶鬼般死战？而就在那伟大的时刻，韦恩从煤气厂的一扇窗子纵身跃入，只一掌便打得全城陷入黑暗，随即发出一声怒狮般的吼叫——那吼声穿透了四条街——他便手持长剑扑向威尔逊的人，把那些在黑暗中晕头转向、又不识路况的人，从这条神圣的街上扫地出门？难道你们不知道，每年这一夜，全城的灯都要熄灭半个时辰，让我们在黑暗中齐唱诺丁山颂歌吗？听！开始了。"

===Original===
Through the night came a crash of drums, and then a strong swell of human voices—

"When the world was in the balance, there was night on Notting Hill,

(There was night on Notting Hill): it was nobler than the day;

On the cities where the lights are and the firesides glow,
From the seas and from the deserts came the thing we did not know,
Came the darkness, came the darkness, came the darkness on the foe,
And the old guard of God turned to bay.

For the old guard of God turns to bay, turns to bay,
And the stars fall down before it ere its banners fall today:
For when armies were around us as a howling and a horde,
When falling was the citadel and broken was the sword,
The darkness came upon them like the Dragon of the Lord,
When the old guard of God turned to bay."

===Chinese===
夜色中传来一阵鼓声如潮，紧接着人声大作——

"当世界悬于一线，诺丁山有的是黑夜，

（诺丁山有的是黑夜）：那黑夜比白昼更高贵；
在那些灯火通明、炉火融融的城邦之上，
从海洋、从荒漠，来了我们所未知的事物，
来了黑暗，来了黑暗，黑暗降临到敌人头上，
上帝的老近卫军，背水一战。

因为上帝的老近卫军，背水一战，背水一战，
星辰在它的旗帜今日倒下之前，先自它面前坠落：
因为当大军如嚎叫的蛮族，把我们层层围困，
当城堡将倾、长剑已折，
黑暗便如主的巨龙般降临于他们，
当上帝的老近卫军，背水一战。"

===Original===
The voices were just uplifting themselves in a second verse when they were stopped by a scurry and a yell. Barker had bounded into the street with a cry of "South Kensington!" and a drawn dagger. In less time than a man could blink, the whole packed street was full of curses and struggling. Barker was flung back against the shopfront, but used the second only to draw his sword as well as his dagger, and calling out, "This is not the first time I've come through the thick of you," flung himself again into the press. It was evident that he had drawn blood at last, for a more violent outcry arose, and many other knives and swords were discernible in the faint light. Barker, after having wounded more than one man, seemed on the point of being flung back again, when Buck suddenly stepped out into the street. He had no weapon, for he affected rather the peaceful magnificence of the great burgher, than the pugnacious dandyism which had replaced the old sombre dandyism in Barker. But with a blow of his clenched fist he broke the pane of the next shop, which was the old curiosity shop, and, plunging in his hand, snatched a kind of Japanese scimitar, and calling out, "Kensington! Kensington!" rushed to Barker's assistance.

===Chinese===
众人的声音正要扬起来唱第二节，却被一阵骚动和一声呐喊截断了。巴克先生已一跃跳到街上，高喊一声"南肯辛顿！"，手中已拔出匕首。比人眨一下眼还快的工夫，整条挤得满满当当的街便满是咒骂与扭打了。巴克先生被甩回了铺面，可他只用了这一瞬，便把剑也一道拔了出来，喊道："这可不是我头一回从你们这帮人里杀出一条血路了，"便又扑进了人群。显然他终于见了血，因为更剧烈的叫喊声轰然响起，在昏暗的光线里，许多别的刀剑也影影绰绰可见。巴克先生在刺伤了一人之后，眼看又要被击退，这时巴克忽然一步跨到了街上。他手无寸铁，因为他所偏爱的，是大市民那种和平的华贵气派，而不是巴克先生那种取代了旧日阴沉花腔的好斗的花花公子做派。但他握紧拳头一拳砸碎了隔壁那间铺子——那间古玩铺——的玻璃，把手伸了进去，抓出了一种日本式的弯刀，高喊"肯辛顿！肯辛顿！"便冲上去援助巴克先生。

===Original===
Barker's sword was broken, but he was laying about him with his dagger. Just as Buck ran up, a man of Notting Hill struck Barker down, but Buck struck the man down on top of him, and Barker sprang up again, the blood running down his face.

Suddenly all these cries were cloven by a great voice, that seemed to fall out of heaven. It was terrible to Buck and Barker and the King, from its seeming to come out the empty skies; but it was more terrible because it was a familiar voice, and one which at the same time they had not heard for so long.

"Turn up the lights," said the voice from above them, and for a moment there was no reply, but only a tumult.

"In the name of Notting Hill and of the great Council of the City, turn up the lights."

There was again a tumult and a vagueness for a moment, then the whole street and every object in it sprang suddenly out of the darkness, as every lamp sprang into life. And looking up they saw, standing upon a balcony near the roof of one of the highest houses, the figure and the face of Adam Wayne, his red hair blowing behind him, a little streaked with grey.

===Chinese===
巴克先生的剑断了，可他还在以匕首四下挥砍。就在巴克奔到跟前的当口，一个诺丁山人把巴克先生打翻在地，可巴克又一拳把那人打翻在他身上，巴克先生便纵身跃起，鲜血顺着脸往下淌。

忽然，所有的喊叫声都被一个巨大的声音劈开了，那声音仿佛从天而降。它使巴克、巴克先生和国王都感到了一阵恐惧，因为它似乎来自空旷的天穹；可更可怖的是——这是一个他们熟悉的声音，同时又是一个他们已经太久没有听到的声音。

"把灯点亮，"那声音从他们头顶落下，一时间无人应答，只有一片骚然。

"以诺丁山之名，以本城伟大的议会之名，把灯点亮。"

又是一阵骚然、一阵迷蒙，紧接着，整条街和街上的一切，便随着每一盏灯同时复活，霍然从黑暗中跃了出来。他们抬头望去，只见在一栋最高的房子、靠近屋顶的一处阳台上，立着亚当·韦恩的身影与面孔，他的红发在身后飘扬，其间已夹杂了少许灰白。

===Original===
"What is this, my people?" he said. "Is it altogether impossible to make a thing good without it immediately insisting on being wicked? The glory of Notting Hill in having achieved its independence, has been enough for me to dream of for many years, as I sat beside the fire. Is it really not enough for you, who have had so many other affairs to excite and distract you? Notting Hill is a nation. Why should it condescend to be a mere Empire? You wish to pull down the statue of General Wilson, which the men of Bayswater have so rightly erected in Westbourne Grove. Fools! Who erected that statue? Did Bayswater erect it? No. Notting Hill erected it. Do you not see that it is the glory of our achievement that we have infected the other cities with the idealism of Notting Hill? It is we who have created not only our own side, but both sides of this controversy. O too humble fools, why should you wish to destroy your enemies? You have done something more to them. You have created your enemies. You wish to pull down that gigantic silver hammer, which stands, like an obelisk, in the centre of the Broadway of Hammersmith. Fools! Before Notting Hill arose, did any person passing through Hammersmith Broadway expect to see there a gigantic silver hammer? You wish to abolish the great bronze figure of a knight standing upon the artificial bridge at Knightsbridge. Fools! Who would have thought of it before Notting Hill arose? I have even heard, and with deep pain I have heard it, that the evil eye of our imperial envy has been cast towards the remote horizon of the west, and that we have objected to the great black monument of a crowned raven, which commemorates the skirmish of Ravenscourt Park. Who created all these things? Were they there before we came? Cannot you be content with that destiny which was enough for Athens, which was enough for Nazareth? the destiny, the humble purpose, of creating a new world. Is Athens angry because Romans and Florentines have adopted her phraseology for expressing their own patriotism? Is Nazareth angry because as a little village it has become the type of all little villages out of which, as the Snobs say, no good can come? Has Athens asked everyone to wear the chlamys? Are all followers of the Nazarene compelled to wear turbans? No! but the soul of Athens went forth and made men drink hemlock, and the soul of Nazareth went forth and made men consent to be crucified. So has the soul of Notting Hill gone forth and made men realise what it is to live in a city. Just as we inaugurated our symbols and ceremonies, so they have inaugurated theirs; and are you so mad as to contend against them? Notting Hill is right; it has always been right. It has moulded itself on its own necessities, its own sine qua non; it has accepted its own ultimatum. Because it is a nation it has created itself; and because it is a nation it can destroy itself. Notting Hill shall always be the judge. If it is your will because of this matter of General Wilson's statue to make war upon Bayswater—"

===Chinese===
"这是怎么一回事，我的人民？"他说，"难道一件事情，就非得在变好之后，立即又闹着要变坏不可吗？诺丁山既已赢得了独立，那一份荣耀，已够我在炉火旁梦想了许多年了。这难道当真还不够你们受用的吗——你们还有那么多别的事务来激发你们、分你们的心？诺丁山是一个国度。它何苦降低身份，去当一个区区的帝国呢？你们想要推倒威尔逊将军的铜像——那座贝斯沃特人理直气壮地竖在韦斯特伯恩格罗夫（Westbourne Grove）的铜像。蠢人！那座铜像是谁竖的？是贝斯沃特竖的吗？不。是诺丁山竖的。你们难道看不出，我们这一番事业的荣耀之处，正是在于我们已用诺丁山的理想主义感染了其他城邦吗？这场争端的双方，不仅是我们这一方，连对方都是我们一手创造出来的。哦，谦卑过了头的蠢人啊，你们何苦要毁灭你们的敌人呢？你们对他们做了更进一步的事。你们创造了你们的敌人。你们想要推倒那柄巨硕无朋的银锤——它像方尖碑一样，矗立在哈默史密斯大马路（the Broadway of Hammersmith）的正中央。蠢人！在诺丁山崛起之前，可有谁穿过哈默史密斯大马路时，指望着在那里看见一柄巨硕的银锤？你们想要废除骑士桥（Knightsbridge）那座人造桥上、傲然伫立的青铜骑士巨像。蠢人！在诺丁山崛起之前，谁会想到这个？我甚至听人说——我怀着深痛听人这么说——我们那帝国式的妒眼，已投向了西方遥远的天际线，我们对那座加冕乌鸦的黑色巨碑起了异议——那座碑是为纪念雷文斯考特公园（Ravenscourt Park）那场小冲突而立的。这一切，是谁创造的？在我们到来之前，它们就在那里吗？难道你们就不能满足于那一份足以令雅典满足、足以令拿撒勒（Nazareth）满足的天命吗？那一份创造一个新世界的天命，那一个谦卑的旨意。雅典会因罗马人和佛罗伦萨人借用了她的辞令来表达他们自己的爱国之心而恼怒吗？拿撒勒会因自己作为一个小村庄，竟成了所有小村庄的典型——势利鬼们说，从那种小村庄里'出不了什么好人'——而恼怒吗？雅典可曾要求人人都披上希腊披风（chlamys）？拿撒勒人（the Nazarene）的信徒可曾被强令缠头巾？不！然而雅典的灵魂出去了，叫人饮鸩（hemlock）；拿撒勒的灵魂出去了，叫人情愿被钉上十字架。诺丁老的灵魂也已出去了，叫人明白住在一座城里究竟意味着什么。正如我们创立了我们的象征与仪典，他们也创立了他们的；你们难道疯得要去与他们相争吗？诺丁山是对的；它一向是对的。它是按照它自身的必需、它自身那不可或缺的根本（sine qua non）来塑造自己的；它已接受了它自己的最后通牒。因为它是一个国度，它创造了自己；又因为它是一个国度，它也能毁灭自己。诺丁山将永远是裁判者。倘若你们因威尔逊将军铜像一事，便执意要向贝斯沃特开战——"

===Original===
A roar of cheers broke in upon his words, and further speech was impossible. Pale to the lips, the great patriot tried again and again to speak; but even his authority could not keep down the dark and roaring masses in the street below him. He said something further, but it was not audible. He descended at last sadly from the garret in which he lived, and mingled with the crowd at the foot of the houses. Finding General Turnbull, he put his hand on his shoulder with a queer affection and gravity, and said—

"Tomorrow, old man, we shall have a new experience, as fresh as the flowers of spring. We shall be defeated. You and I have been through three battles together, and have somehow or other missed this peculiar delight. It is unfortunate that we shall not probably be able to exchange our experiences, because, as it most annoyingly happens, we shall probably both be dead."

===Chinese===
一阵如雷的欢呼打断了他的话，再想说什么已无可能。这位伟大的爱国者面白如纸，嘴唇全无血色，一次又一次地试着开口；可即便以他的威望，也压不住楼下街头那一片阴沉而咆哮的人海。他又说了些什么，却已听不分明。末了，他黯然从他所住的那间阁楼走下来，融入了屋脚的人群之中。他寻见了特恩布尔将军，便带着一种古怪的深情与郑重，把手搭在他肩上，说道——

"明日啊，老伙计，我们将尝到一种全新的滋味，新鲜得如同春天的花朵。我们将被打败。你和我一起经历过三场战役，却莫名其妙地都与这一种奇特的快活无缘。遗憾的是，我们大概没法再彼此交换这番体验了，因为，偏偏这么恼人地，我们俩多半都得死。"

===Original===
Turnbull looked dimly surprised.

"I don't mind so much about being dead," he said, "but why should you say that we shall be defeated?"

"The answer is very simple," replied Wayne, calmly. "It is because we ought to be defeated. We have been in the most horrible holes before now; but in all those I was perfectly certain that the stars were on our side, and that we ought to get out. Now I know that we ought not to get out; and that takes away from me everything with which I won."

===Chinese===
特恩布尔一脸隐约的诧异。

"死不死我倒不太在乎，"他说，"可你为什么说我们会被打败呢？"

"答案很简单，"韦恩平静地答道，"因为我们理应被打败。我们从前也曾陷于最可怕的绝境；但在那一切之中，我都十分确信星辰是站在我们这边的，我们理应脱身。如今我知道我们理应脱不了身；仅此一点，便夺走了我曾赖以取胜的一切。"

===Original===
As Wayne spoke he started a little, for both men became aware that a third figure was listening to them—a small figure with wondering eyes.

"Is it really true, my dear Wayne," said the King, interrupting, "that you think you will be beaten tomorrow?"

"There can be no doubt about it whatever," replied Adam Wayne; "the real reason is the one of which I have just spoken. But as a concession to your materialism, I will add that they have an organised army of a hundred allied cities against our one. That in itself, however, would be unimportant."

Quin, with his round eyes, seemed strangely insistent.

"You are quite sure," he said, "that you must be beaten?"

"I am afraid," said Turnbull, gloomily, "that there can be no doubt about it."

"Then," cried the King, flinging out his arms, "give me a halberd! Give me a halberd, somebody! I desire all men to witness that I, Auberon, King of England, do here and now abdicate, and implore the Provost of Notting Hill to permit me to enlist in his army. Give me a halberd!"

He seized one from some passing guard, and, shouldering it, stamped solemnly after the shouting columns of halberdiers which were, by this time, parading the streets. He had, however, nothing to do with the wrecking of the statue of General Wilson, which took place before morning.

===Chinese===
韦恩正说着，忽地一怔，因为两人都察觉第三个身影正在听他们说话——一个小个子，一双睁得圆圆的、惊奇的眼睛。

"亲爱的韦恩，这是当真的吗？"国王插话道，"你当真认为你明日会被打败？"

"这是毫无疑问的，"亚当·韦恩答道，"真正的理由，就是我方才所说的那一条。不过，姑且向你们的唯物论让个步，我还可以补充一句：他们有一支由一百个结盟城邦组成的有组织的大军，来对付我们这一个。不过，这一点本身并不重要。"

奎因瞪着他那双圆眼睛，执拗得有些异样。

"你完全确信，"他说，"你非被打败不可？"

"恐怕，"特恩布尔阴郁地说，"这是毫无疑问的。"

"那么，"国王张开双臂高喊，"给我一柄戟！谁给我一柄戟！我要让天下人都来作证：我，奥伯伦，英格兰国王，就在此时此地退位，并恳请诺丁山区督准许我入伍从军。给我一柄戟！"

他从某个路过的卫士手里夺过一柄，扛上肩，一本正经地踩着步子，跟在那些此时正在街上游行的、呐喊着的戟兵方阵后头。不过，第二天清晨之前便发生的捣毁威尔逊将军铜像一事，他却并未参与。

## II. The Last Battle / 第二章 最后一战

===Original===
The day was cloudy when Wayne went down to die with all his army in Kensington Gardens; it was cloudy again when that army had been swallowed up by the vast armies of a new world. There had been an almost uncanny interval of sunshine, in which the Provost of Notting Hill, with all the placidity of an onlooker, had gazed across to the hostile armies on the great spaces of verdure opposite; the long strips of green and blue and gold lay across the park in squares and oblongs like a proposition in Euclid wrought in a rich embroidery. But the sunlight was a weak and, as it were, a wet sunlight, and was soon swallowed up. Wayne spoke to the King, with a queer sort of coldness and languor, as to the military operations. It was as he had said the night before—that being deprived of his sense of an impracticable rectitude, he was, in effect, being deprived of everything. He was out of date, and at sea in a mere world of compromise and competition, of Empire against Empire, of the tolerably right and the tolerably wrong. When his eye fell on the King, however, who was marching very gravely with a top hat and a halberd, it brightened slightly.

===Chinese===
韦恩率领他全部的军队，开赴肯辛顿花园（Kensington Gardens）去赴死的那一天，天是阴的；当这支军队被一个新世界的浩大联军所吞没时，天仍旧是阴的。当中有过一段近乎诡异的阳光，在那段阳光里，诺丁山区督以一种旁观者才有的全然恬静，凝望着对面大片绿地上对峙的敌军；一条条绿、蓝、金的长带横陈于公园之中，呈方块与长方，恰如一道以华美绣工做成的欧几里得几何题。可那阳光是一种软弱的、可以说湿润的阳光，很快便被吞没了。韦恩以一股古怪的冷淡与倦怠，向国王谈及军事。正如果他前一夜所说的那样——他既被剥夺了那种不讲道理的正直之感，实际上便等于被剥夺了一切。他已经过时了，在一个纯属妥协与竞争、帝国对帝国、差强人意的对与差强人意的错的世界里茫然失措。然而，当他的目光落到国王身上——那国王正戴着高筒帽、提着一柄戟，极其庄重地行进着——他的眼神微微亮了一下。

===Original===
"Well, your Majesty," he said, "you at least ought to be proud today. If your children are fighting each other, at least those who win are your children. Other kings have distributed justice, you have distributed life. Other kings have ruled a nation, you have created nations. Others have made kingdoms, you have begotten them. Look at your children, father!" and he stretched his hand out towards the enemy.

Auberon did not raise his eyes.

===Chinese===
"好吧，陛下，"他说，"至少今日您是该骄傲的。纵然您的孩子们彼此相斗，至少获胜的一方也是您的孩子。别的国王分赐的是公正，您分赐的是生命。别的国王统治一个国度，您创造了国度。别人制造王国，您却是生养了它们。看看您的孩子们吧，父亲！"说着他把手伸向敌方。

奥伯伦没有抬眼。

===Original===
"See how splendidly," cried Wayne, "the new cities come on—the new cities from across the river. See where Battersea advances over there—under the flag of the Lost Dog; and Putney—don't you see the Man on the White Boar shining on their standard as the sun catches it? It is the coming of a new age, your Majesty. Notting Hill is not a common empire; it is a thing like Athens, the mother of a mode of life, of a manner of living, which shall renew the youth of the world—a thing like Nazareth. When I was young I remember, in the old dreary days, wiseacres used to write books about how trains would get faster, and all the world be one empire, and tramcars go to the moon. And even as a child I used to say to myself, 'Far more likely that we shall go on the crusades again, or worship the gods of the city.' And so it has been. And I am glad, though this is my last battle."

===Chinese===
"看哪，多么壮丽，"韦恩喊道，"那些新城邦涌上来了——从河对岸来的新城邦。看巴特西（Battersea）从那边推进——在'迷途之犬'旗下；还有帕特尼（Putney）——你可看见他们旗帜上'白公猪上的汉子'在阳光下闪闪发亮？一个新时代正在到来啊，陛下。诺丁山不是寻常的帝国；它是像雅典一样的事物，是一种生活方式、一种生存仪态的母亲，它将使世界重获青春——它是像拿撒勒一样的事物。我还记得我年轻时，在那些沉闷的旧日子里，自作聪明的人惯于写书，预言火车会怎样越来越快，全世界将合为一个帝国，电车将开到月亮上去。而即便是个孩子，我也常对自己说：'倒不如说，我们将再度踏上十字军的征途，或再度供奉城邦的众神，那样还更靠谱些。'果不其然，就成了这样。我为此欣慰，纵然这是我最后一战。"

===Original===
Even as he spoke there came a crash of steel from the left, and he turned his head.

"Wilson!" he cried, with a kind of joy. "Red Wilson has charged our left. No one can hold him in; he eats swords. He is as keen a soldier as Turnbull, but less patient—less really great. Ha! and Barker is moving. How Barker has improved; how handsome he looks! It is not all having plumes; it is also having a soul in one's daily life. Ha!"

And another crash of steel on the right showed that Barker had closed with Notting Hill on the other side.

"Turnbull is there!" cried Wayne. "See him hurl them back! Barker is checked! Turnbull charges—wins! But our left is broken. Wilson has smashed Bowles and Mead, and may turn our flank. Forward, the Provost's Guard!"

And the whole centre moved forward, Wayne's face and hair and sword flaming in the van.

===Chinese===
他正说着，左边传来一声钢铁的撞击，他转过头去。

"威尔逊！"他带着一种欣喜喊道，"红发威尔逊（Red Wilson）冲击了我们的左翼。谁也按不住他；他是吃剑长大的。他是个和特恩布尔一样凌厉的战士，却不如他有耐性——不如他真正伟大。哈！巴克先生也动了。巴克先生大有长进啊；他看起来多么英武！这不仅仅是因为有羽饰；这更是因为日常生活里有了灵魂。哈！"

右边又是一声钢铁的撞击，表明巴克先生已从另一侧与诺丁山接了战。

"特恩布尔在那儿！"韦恩喊道，"看他把他们击退！巴克先生受阻！特恩布尔冲锋——得手！可我们左翼已破。威尔逊击溃了鲍尔斯和米德，可能包抄我军侧翼。区督近卫，前进！"

整个中军向前推进，韦恩的面孔、头发与剑锋在前锋中如火焰般闪耀。

===Original===
The King ran suddenly forward.

The next instant a great jar that went through it told that it had met the enemy. And right over against them through the wood of their own weapons Auberon saw the Purple Eagle of Buck of North Kensington.

On the left Red Wilson was storming the broken ranks, his little green figure conspicuous even in the tangle of men and weapons, with the flaming red moustaches and the crown of laurel. Bowles slashed at his head and tore away some of the wreath, leaving the rest bloody, and, with a roar like a bull's, Wilson sprang at him, and, after a rattle of fencing, plunged his point into the chemist, who fell, crying, "Notting Hill!" Then the Notting Hillers wavered, and Bayswater swept them back in confusion. Wilson had carried everything before him.

===Chinese===
国王忽然奔上前去。

紧接着，一阵贯穿全军的大震，表明它已与敌军相遇。透过己方兵器的丛林，奥伯伦在他们正对面，看见了北肯辛顿的巴克那面紫鹰（the Purple Eagle）旗。

左翼上，红发威尔逊正在猛攻溃散的阵线，他那矮小的绿色身影即便在人丛与兵器的纠缠之中也分外醒目——火红的髭须，头顶桂冠。鲍尔斯朝他头上劈了一刀，撕去了桂冠的一部分，剩下的尽是血污，威尔逊一声如公牛般的怒吼，朝他扑去，叮叮当当一通交锋之后，一剑刺进了这位药剂师的身体。药剂师倒下，高呼"诺丁山！"诺丁山人随之动摇，贝斯沃特将他们乱哄哄地赶了回去。威尔逊所向披靡。

===Original===
On the right, however, Turnbull had carried the Red Lion banner with a rush against Barker's men, and the banner of the Golden Birds bore up with difficulty against it. Barker's men fell fast. In the centre Wayne and Buck were engaged, stubborn and confused. So far as the fighting went, it was precisely equal. But the fighting was a farce. For behind the three small armies with which Wayne's small army was engaged lay the great sea of the allied armies, which looked on as yet as scornful spectators, but could have broken all four armies by moving a finger.

Suddenly they did move. Some of the front contingents, the pastoral chiefs from Shepherd's Bush, with their spears and fleeces, were seen advancing, and the rude clans from Paddington Green. They were advancing for a very good reason. Buck, of North Kensington, was signalling wildly; he was surrounded, and totally cut off. His regiments were a struggling mass of people, islanded in a red sea of Notting Hill.

===Chinese===
然而右翼上，特恩布尔已擎着红狮旗（the Red Lion）一阵猛冲，杀向巴克先生的人马，金鸟旗（the Golden Birds）苦苦支撑，摇摇欲坠。巴克先生的人马纷纷倒下。中军处，韦恩与巴克正胶着厮杀，固执而混乱。单就战况而言，双方势均力敌。可这一场厮杀，却是一场闹剧。因为与韦恩这支小军队交战的那三支小军队的背后，还铺展着结盟诸城的浩瀚人海——他们暂时还只是冷眼旁观的看客，可只消动一根手指，便能将这四支军队统统碾碎。

忽然，他们动了。只见一些前线的分遣队——来自牧羊人丛（Shepherd's Bush）、手执长矛与羊皮的牧业首领们——正在推进，还有来自帕丁顿格林（Paddington Green）的粗野氏族。他们之所以推进，理由十分充分：北肯辛顿的巴克正疯狂地打信号求援；他已被包围，被彻底切断。他的几个团成了一团挣扎的人众，像孤岛一样困在诺丁山红色的海洋里。

===Original===
The allies had been too careless and confident. They had allowed Barker's force to be broken to pieces by Turnbull, and the moment that was done, the astute old leader of Notting Hill swung his men round and attacked Buck behind and on both sides. At the same moment Wayne cried, "Charge!" and struck him in front like a thunderbolt.

Two-thirds of Buck's men were cut to pieces before their allies could reach them. Then the sea of cities came on with their banners like breakers, and swallowed Notting Hill forever. The battle was not over, for not one of Wayne's men would surrender, and it lasted till sundown, and long after. But it was decided; the story of Notting Hill was ended.

===Chinese===
盟军太过大意、太过自信了。他们竟任由巴克先生的部队被特恩布尔打了个七零八落，而此事一成，诺丁山那位老辣的统帅便把人马一转，从背后和两侧夹击巴克。同一瞬间，韦恩高喊一声"冲！"便如雷霆一般从他正面劈下。

巴克的部下，三分之二在盟军赶到之前便已被砍杀殆尽。随即，城邦的海洋擎着旗帜如怒涛般涌来，将诺丁山永远吞没。战役并未结束，因为韦恩的人没有一个肯投降，它一直持续到日落，又持续到日落之后很久。但胜败已决；诺丁山的故事，到此终结。

===Original===
When Turnbull saw it, he ceased a moment from fighting, and looked round him. The evening sunlight struck his face; it looked like a child's.

"I have had my youth," he said. Then, snatching an axe from a man, he dashed into the thick of the spears of Shepherd's Bush, and died somewhere far in the depths of their reeling ranks. Then the battle roared on; every man of Notting Hill was slain before night.

===Chinese===
特恩布尔看见时，他停了一刻厮杀，环顾四周。傍晚的阳光照在他脸上；那张脸看起来像个孩子。

"我有过我的青春了，"他说。随即他从一个人手里夺过一柄斧，冲进了牧羊人丛那密密麻麻的长矛之中，阵亡在他们摇摇晃晃的阵列深处某个远远的地方。随后战斗继续轰鸣；入夜之前，诺丁山的人已尽数战死。

===Original===
