Wayne was standing by a tree alone after the battle. Several men approached him with axes. One struck at him. His foot seemed partly to slip; but he flung his hand out, and steadied himself against the tree.

Barker sprang after him, sword in hand, and shaking with excitement.

"How large now, my lord," he cried, "is the Empire of Notting Hill?"

Wayne smiled in the gathering dark.

"Always as large as this," he said, and swept his sword round in a semicircle of silver.

===Chinese===
战后，韦恩独自站在一棵树旁。几个人提着斧子朝他逼近。其中一人朝他劈来。他的脚似乎微微一滑；但他一伸手，稳稳地撑住了那棵树。

巴克先生提着剑紧随其后扑来，兴奋得浑身发抖。

"大人，"他喊道，"诺丁山的帝国，如今还有多大？"

韦恩在渐浓的暮色中微微一笑。

"一向就这么大，"他说，手腕一挥，将剑划出一道银色的半圆。

===Original===
Barker dropped, wounded in the neck; and Wilson sprang over his body like a tiger-cat, rushing at Wayne. At the same moment there came behind the Lord of the Red Lion a cry and a flare of yellow, and a mass of the West Kensington halberdiers ploughed up the slope, knee-deep in grass, bearing the yellow banner of the city before them, and shouting aloud.

At the same second Wilson went down under Wayne's sword, seemingly smashed like a fly. The great sword rose again like a bird, but Wilson seemed to rise with it, and, his sword being broken, sprang at Wayne's throat like a dog. The foremost of the yellow halberdiers had reached the tree and swung his axe above the struggling Wayne. With a curse the King whirled up his own halberd, and dashed the blade in the man's face. He reeled and rolled down the slope, just as the furious Wilson was flung on his back again. And again he was on his feet, and again at Wayne's throat. Then he was flung again, but this time laughing triumphantly. Grasped in his hand was the red and yellow favour that Wayne wore as Provost of Notting Hill. He had torn it from the place where it had been carried for twenty-five years.

===Chinese===
巴克先生倒了下去，颈上受了伤；威尔逊如一只虎猫从他身上跃过，直扑韦恩。同一时刻，红狮旗之主身后传来一声呐喊、一抹黄色火光，一大群西肯辛顿的戟兵蹚过齐膝深的草丛犁上坡来，擎着该城的黄色旗帜，高声呐喊。

同一瞬间，威尔逊在韦恩剑下倒地，仿佛一只苍蝇被拍碎。那柄巨剑如鸟般再度扬起，可威尔逊竟似随之一同升起，他的剑既断，便如恶犬一般扑向韦恩的咽喉。最前头的黄衣戟兵已冲到树前，举起斧头朝正苦苦招架的韦恩头顶劈下。国王咒骂一声，抡起自己那柄戟，将戟刃砸在那人脸上。那人踉跄着滚下了坡，恰如狂怒的威尔逊再度被仰面摔倒。可他又站了起来，又扑向韦恩的咽喉。接着他再一次被摔开，可这一回他发出了胜利的大笑。他攥在手里的，是韦恩身为诺丁山区督所佩的红黄两色徽记——他从那佩了二十五年的地方，把它硬生生扯了下来。

===Original===
With a shout the West Kensington men closed round Wayne, the great yellow banner flapping over his head.

"Where is your favour now, Provost?" cried the West Kensington leader.

And a laugh went up.

Adam struck at the standard-bearer and brought him reeling forward. As the banner stooped, he grasped the yellow folds and tore off a shred. A halberdier struck him on the shoulder, wounding bloodily.

"Here is one colour!" he cried, pushing the yellow into his belt; "and here!" he cried, pointing to his own blood—"here is the other."

===Chinese===
西肯辛顿人一声呐喊，把韦恩团团围住，巨大的黄旗在他头顶猎猎作响。

"你的徽记这会儿在哪儿呢，区督？"西肯辛顿的带头人喊道。

随即哄笑声四起。

亚当一剑劈向旗手，打得他踉跄前倾。旗帜一低，他便一把抓住那黄色的褶皱，撕下一绺。一名戟兵在他肩上劈了一刀，鲜血淋漓。

"这是一种颜色！"他高喊，把那片黄色塞进腰带；"这儿呢！"他指着自家身上流出的血高喊——"这是另一种。"

===Original===
At the same instant the shock of a sudden and heavy halberd laid the King stunned or dead. In the wild visions of vanishing consciousness, he saw again something that belonged to an utterly forgotten time, something that he had seen somewhere long ago in a restaurant. He saw, with his swimming eyes, red and yellow, the colours of Nicaragua.

Quin did not see the end. Wilson, wild with joy, sprang again at Adam Wayne, and the great sword of Notting Hill was whirled above once more. Then men ducked instinctively at the rushing noise of the sword coming down out of the sky, and Wilson of Bayswater was smashed and wiped down upon the floor like a fly. Nothing was left of him but a wreck; but the blade that had broken him was broken. In dying he had snapped the great sword and the spell of it; the sword of Wayne was broken at the hilt. One rush of the enemy carried Wayne by force against the tree. They were too close to use halberd or even sword; they were breast to breast, even nostrils to nostrils. But Buck got his dagger free.

===Chinese===
就在同一瞬间，一柄突如其来的沉重战戟当头砸下，把国王打得人事不省——也不知是昏是死。在渐渐消逝的意识那狂乱的幻象里，他又看见了某种属于一段全然被遗忘的时光的东西，某种他很久很久以前在某间餐厅里见过的东西。他用那双模糊的眼睛，看见了红与黄——尼加拉瓜的颜色。

奎因没有看到结局。狂喜的威尔逊再度扑向亚当·韦恩，诺丁山那柄巨剑再度在头顶抡圆。众人本能地一缩脖——只听得那剑从天而降时呼啸的风声，贝斯沃特的威尔逊便如一只苍蝇，被砸得糊在了地上。剩下的不过是一具残骸；但击碎他的那柄剑也断了。在垂死之际，他崩断了那柄巨剑、崩断了它的魔力；韦恩的剑在剑柄处断裂。敌军一涌，将韦恩硬生生推到了树前。双方贴得太近，戟也罢、剑也罢都使不开；他们胸贴着胸，甚至鼻尖贴着鼻尖。可巴克把匕首抽了出来。

===Original===
"Kill him!" he cried, in a strange stifled voice. "Kill him! Good or bad, he is none of us! Do not be blinded by the face!…  God! have we not been blinded all along!" and he drew his arm back for a stab, and seemed to close his eyes.

Wayne did not drop the hand that hung on to the tree-branch. But a mighty heave went over his breast and his whole huge figure, like an earthquake over great hills. And with that convulsion of effort he rent the branch out of the tree, with tongues of torn wood; and, swaying it once only, he let the splintered club fall on Buck, breaking his neck. The planner of the Great Road fell face foremost dead, with his dagger in a grip of steel.

===Chinese===
"杀了他！"他用一种古怪而憋闷的声音喊道，"杀了他！无论善恶，他都不是我们的人！别被那张脸蒙蔽了！……上帝啊！我们难道不从头到尾一直被蒙在鼓里吗！"他缩回手臂，准备一刺，似乎闭上了眼睛。

韦恩没有松开紧抓树枝的那只手。但他的胸膛、他整个巨大的身躯上，涌过了一阵惊天动地的起伏，宛如大地震掠过群山。凭着那一阵痉挛般的发力，他把那根树枝连根从树上扯了下来，带出一缕缕撕裂的木舌；他只抡了那么一下，便让那根裂开的木棒砸在巴克身上，折断了他的颈椎。这位大马路（the Great Road）的谋划者，脸朝下扑倒在地，一命呜呼，手里仍以钢铁般的握力攥着匕首。

===Original===
"For you and me, and for all brave men, my brother," said Wayne, in his strange chant, "there is good wine poured in the inn at the end of the world."

The packed men made another lurch or heave towards him; it was almost too dark to fight clearly. He caught hold of the oak again, this time getting his hand into a wide crevice and grasping, as it were, the bowels of the tree. The whole crowd, numbering some thirty men, made a rush to tear him away from it; they hung on with all their weight and numbers, and nothing stirred. A solitude could not have been stiller than that group of straining men. Then there was a faint sound.

"His hand is slipping," cried two men in exultation.

"You don't know much of him," said another, grimly (a man of the old war). "More likely his bone cracks."

"It is neither—by God, it is neither!" said one of the first two.

"What is it, then?" asked the second.

"The tree is falling," he replied.

===Chinese===
"为你，为我，为一切勇敢的人，我的兄弟，"韦恩以他那古怪的吟诵腔调说道，"在世界尽头的那间客栈里，已有好酒斟上。"

挤成一团的人又朝他涌了一阵、推了一阵；天色已暗得几乎看不清厮杀了。他又抓住了那棵橡树，这一回他把手伸进了一道宽阔的裂缝，仿佛攥住了那棵树的脏腑。整个人群——约莫三十来号人——一拥而上，要把他从树上拽开；他们以全部的体重和人数吊在上面，却纹丝不动。即便是旷野里的荒寂，也比不过这一群使尽全力的汉子那般寂静。接着，传来了一阵细微的声响。

"他的手在打滑！"两人欢呼起来。

"你太不了解他了，"另一个人（一个旧战时的老兵）冷冷地说，"多半是他的骨头在响。"

"都不是——老天爷，都不是！"先前两人中的一个说。

"那是什么？"第二个人问。

"树要倒了，"他答道。

===Original===
"As the tree falleth, so shall it lie," said Wayne's voice out of the darkness, and it had the same sweet and yet horrible air that it had had throughout, of coming from a great distance, from before or after the event. Even when he was struggling like an eel or battering like a madman, he spoke like a spectator. "As the tree falleth, so shall it lie," he said. "Men have called that a gloomy text. It is the essence of all exultation. I am doing now what I have done all my life, what is the only happiness, what is the only universality. I am clinging to something. Let it fall, and there let it lie. Fools, you go about and see the kingdoms of the Earth, and are liberal and wise and cosmopolitan, which is all that the devil can give you—all that he could offer to Christ, only to be spurned away. I am doing what the truly wise do. When a child goes out into the garden and takes hold of a tree, saying, 'Let this tree be all I have,' that moment its roots take hold on hell and its branches on the stars. The joy I have is what the lover knows when a woman is everything. It is what a savage knows when his idol is everything. It is what I know when Notting Hill is everything. I have a city. Let it stand or fall."

As he spoke, the turf lifted itself like a living thing, and out of it rose slowly, like crested serpents, the roots of the oak. Then the great head of the tree, that seemed a green cloud among grey ones, swept the sky suddenly like a broom, and the whole tree heeled over like a ship, smashing everyone in its fall.

===Chinese===
"树倒何处，便卧何处（As the tree falleth, so shall it lie），"韦恩的声音从黑暗中传来，它带着那自始至终一贯的、甜美而又骇人的气韵——仿佛来自极远的远方，来自事件之前或之后。纵然他像鳗鱼一般挣扎、像疯子一般捶打时，他说话的口气仍像一个旁观者。"树倒何处，便卧何处，"他说，"人们素来把这句话叫做一段阴郁的经文。它却是一切狂喜的精髓。我现在所做的，正是我毕生所做的，是唯一的幸福，是唯一的普世之道。我紧紧攀住一样东西。让它倒下吧，让它就那么卧在那里吧。蠢人们，你们周游四方，遍览地上的列国，开明、睿智、怀世界主义之胸怀——这已是魔鬼所能赐予你们的一切——这也正是他当年献给基督的一切，结果只被一脚踢开。我做的，是真正智者所做的事。当一个孩子走进花园，抱住一棵树，说'让这棵树成为我全部的拥有'，就在那一刻，它的根便抓住了地狱，它的枝梢便抓住了星辰。我所拥有的这份喜悦，是恋人深知一个女人即是一切时的那份喜悦；是野人深知他的偶像即是一切时的那份喜悦；是我深知诺丁山即是一切时的那份喜悦。我有一座城。让它立着也好，让它倒下也罢。"

他正说着，草皮像活物一般自己翻了起来，从底下缓缓升起橡树的根，如一条条长了冠鬃的蛇。随即那棵大树如帚一般猛地扫过天空——它的树冠在灰云间本似一朵碧云——整棵树便如一艘船般倾覆下来，在它倒下之时，把底下所有的人尽数砸烂。

## III. Two Voices / 第三章 两声对话

===Original===
In a place in which there was total darkness for hours, there was also for hours total silence. Then a voice spoke out of the darkness, no one could have told from where, and said aloud—

"So ends the Empire of Notting Hill. As it began in blood, so it ended in blood, and all things are always the same."

And there was silence again, and then again there was a voice, but it had not the same tone; it seemed that it was not the same voice.

"If all things are always the same, it is because they are always heroic. If all things are always the same, it is because they are always new. To each man one soul only is given; to each soul only is given a little power—the power at some moments to outgrow and swallow up the stars. If age after age that power comes upon men, whatever gives it to them is great. Whatever makes men feel old is mean—an empire or a skinflint shop. Whatever makes men feel young is great—a great war or a love-story. And in the darkest of the books of God there is written a truth that is also a riddle. It is of the new things that men tire—of fashions and proposals and improvements and change. It is the old things that startle and intoxicate. It is the old things that are young. There is no sceptic who does not feel that many have doubted before. There is no rich and fickle man who does not feel that all his novelties are ancient. There is no worshipper of change who does not feel upon his neck the vast weight of the weariness of the universe. But we who do the old things are fed by nature with a perpetual infancy. No man who is in love thinks that anyone has been in love before. No woman who has a child thinks that there have been such things as children. No people that fight for their own city are haunted with the burden of the broken empires. Yes, O dark voice, the world is always the same, for it is always unexpected."

===Chinese===
在一个整整数时辰全然漆黑的地方，也整整数时辰全然寂静。而后，一个声音从黑暗中响起——谁也说不清来自何方——高声说道——

"诺丁山的帝国，就这样落幕了。它始于血，亦终于血；万事万物，向来皆是如此。"

复归寂静，而后又有一个声音响起，可那腔调并不相同；那似乎并不是同一个声音。

"倘若万事万物向来皆是如此，那是因为它们向来都是英雄的。倘若万事万物向来皆是如此，那是因为它们向来都是崭新的。每人都只被赋予一个灵魂；每个灵魂都只被赋予一点点力量——那在某些时刻足以生长壮大、吞噬星辰的力量。倘若这一股力量一世又一世地降临于人，那么，无论是什么把它赋予了他们，那东西便是伟大的。凡使人觉得苍老的，便是卑微的——一个帝国也好，一间吝啬的小铺也罢。凡使人觉得年轻的，便是伟大的——一场大战也好，一段爱情也罢。而在上帝诸书最幽暗的那些篇章里，写着一个真理，那同时也是一个谜。人之所以厌倦，厌倦的恰是那些新事物——那些时尚、方案、改良与变革。能使人惊骇、使人沉醉的，是那些旧事物。年轻的，恰恰是那些旧事物。没有一个怀疑论者不觉得，在他之前早已有许多人怀疑过。没有一个富贵而善变的人不觉得，他一切的新奇玩意儿都古已有之。没有一个变革的崇拜者不觉得，宇宙那浩瀚的倦怠之重正压在他颈上。可我们这些做旧事物的人，天生便被哺以永恒的童稚。恋爱中的男人，从不以为在他之前曾有人恋爱过。生了孩子的女人，从不以为世上曾有过'孩子'这种东西。为自己城邦而战的人民，从不会被那些破碎帝国的重负所纠缠。是的，哦，黑暗中的声音啊，世界向来皆是如此，因为它向来都出人意料。"

===Original===
A little gust of wind blew through the night, and then the first voice answered—

"But in this world there are some, be they wise or foolish, whom nothing intoxicates. There are some who see all your disturbances like a cloud of flies. They know that while men will laugh at your Notting Hill, and will study and rehearse and sing of Athens and Jerusalem, Athens and Jerusalem were silly suburbs like your Notting Hill. They know that the Earth itself is a suburb, and can feel only drearily and respectably amused as they move upon it."

===Chinese===
一阵夜风吹过，随即第一个声音答道——

"可在这世上，有那么一些人——无论明智也罢、愚蠢也罢——没有什么能使他们沉醉。有那么一些人，看着你们这一切纷扰，如同看着一团苍蝇。他们知道，当人们嘲笑你的诺丁山，转而去研究、去排演、去歌颂雅典和耶路撒冷之时，雅典和耶路撒冷也曾是与你的诺丁山一样的可笑市郊。他们知道，连这大地本身也不过是市郊，当他们行于其上之时，也只能感到一种沉闷的、体面的、适可而止的趣味。"

===Original===
"They are philosophers or they are fools," said the other voice. "They are not men. Men live, as I say, rejoicing from age to age in something fresher than progress—in the fact that with every baby a new sun and a new moon are made. If our ancient humanity were a single man, it might perhaps be that he would break down under the memory of so many loyalties, under the burden of so many diverse heroisms, under the load and terror of all the goodness of men. But it has pleased God so to isolate the individual soul that it can only learn of all other souls by hearsay, and to each one goodness and happiness come with the youth and violence of lightning, as momentary and as pure. And the doom of failure that lies on all human systems does not in real fact affect them any more than the worms of the inevitable grave affect a children's game in a meadow. Notting Hill has fallen; Notting Hill has died. But that is not the tremendous issue. Notting Hill has lived."

===Chinese===
"他们是哲人，要么就是蠢人，"另一个声音说，"他们不是人。人，如我所说，是活着的——一世又一世地为某种比进步更新鲜的东西而欢欣——为这样一桩事实：每有一个婴孩降生，便有一轮新的太阳、一轮新的月亮被造出来。倘若我们古老的人类是一个单独的人，也许他会在如此众多忠诚的记忆之下崩溃，会在如此众多形形色色英雄业绩的重负之下崩溃，会在人类一切善性的重压与可怖之下崩溃。然而上帝乐意把单个的灵魂如此隔绝开来，使它只能凭道听途说来了解其余一切灵魂；而每一份善、每一份幸福，降临到每一个人身上时，都带着闪电般的青春与暴烈，同样短暂，同样纯粹。而那横亘于一切人类制度之上的失败之命，其实并不真正影响他们——正如那不可避免的坟墓里的蛆虫，并不真正影响孩子们在草地上的一场游戏。诺丁山倒了；诺丁山死了。可这并非那惊天动地的要害。诺丁山曾经活过。"

===Original===
"But if," answered the other voice, "if what is achieved by all these efforts be only the common contentment of humanity, why do men so extravagantly toil and die in them? Has nothing been done by Notting Hill than any chance clump of farmers or clan of savages would not have done without it? What might have been done to Notting Hill if the world had been different may be a deep question; but there is a deeper. What could have happened to the world if Notting Hill had never been?"

The other voice replied—

"The same that would have happened to the world and all the starry systems if an apple-tree grew six apples instead of seven; something would have been eternally lost. There has never been anything in the world absolutely like Notting Hill. There will never be anything quite like it to the crack of doom. I cannot believe anything but that God loved it as He must surely love anything that is itself and unreplaceable. But even for that I do not care. If God, with all His thunders, hated it, I loved it."

And with the voice a tall, strange figure lifted itself out of the debris in the half-darkness.

===Chinese===
"可假如，"另一个声音答道，"假如这一切努力所成就的，不过是人类寻常的满足，那人们又何必在其中如此竭力地劳作、如此竭力地赴死呢？诺丁山可曾做出过什么，是一簇偶然凑在一起的农夫、或一个蛮人的部族所做不出来的呢？倘若世界另换一副模样，本可以对诺丁山做些什么——这或许是个深奥的问题；可还有更深的一个。倘若诺丁山从不曾存在过，世界会怎样？"

另一个声音答道——

"一如一棵苹果树结了六个果子而非七个，世界与一切星河将会怎样；有某样东西，将永远地失去了。这世上从不曾有过任何一样东西，绝对地像诺丁山。直到末日（the crack of doom），也不会再有任何东西完全像它。我无法不信，上帝爱它——正如他必爱一切自成其是、无可替代之物。可即便为了这一点，我也不在乎。倘若上帝带着他全部的雷霆憎恨它，我仍爱它。"

伴随这声音，一个高大的、奇异的身影，从半明半暗的废墟之中立了起来。

===Original===
The other voice came after a long pause, and as it were hoarsely.

"But suppose the whole matter were really a hocus-pocus. Suppose that whatever meaning you may choose in your fancy to give to it, the real meaning of the whole was mockery. Suppose it was all folly. Suppose—"

"I have been in it," answered the voice from the tall and strange figure, "and I know it was not."

A smaller figure seemed half to rise in the dark.

"Suppose I am God," said the voice, "and suppose I made the world in idleness. Suppose the stars, that you think eternal, are only the idiot fireworks of an everlasting schoolboy. Suppose the sun and the moon, to which you sing alternately, are only the two eyes of one vast and sneering giant, opened alternately in a never-ending wink. Suppose the trees, in my eyes, are as foolish as enormous toadstools. Suppose Socrates and Charlemagne are to me only beasts, made funnier by walking on their hind legs. Suppose I am God, and having made things, laugh at them."

===Chinese===
另一个声音隔了良久才响起，似乎带着几分嘶哑。

"可假如整件事，说到底，只不过是一场戏法呢。假如无论你在幻想中随你高兴赋予它什么意义，整件事真正的意味，原是嘲弄呢。假如这一切全是一场荒唐呢。假如——"

"我曾身在其中，"那个高大而奇异的身影发出的声音答道，"我知道它不是。"

一个较矮小的身影似乎在黑暗中半直起身来。

"假如我是上帝呢，"那声音说，"又假如我是出于无所事事而造了这个世界呢。假如你所认为永恒的星辰，不过是一个永生不老的学童所放的愚蠢烟火呢。假如你轮流歌颂的太阳与月亮，不过是一个巨大而冷嘲的巨人的两只眼睛，在永不休止的一眨一眨中轮流睁开呢。假如在我眼中，树木愚蠢得如同硕大无朋的毒蕈呢。假如苏格拉底（Socrates）和查理曼（Charlemagne）于我不过是野兽，只因用后腿走路而显得更滑稽罢了呢。假如我是上帝，造了万物，又对它们发笑呢。"

===Original===
"And suppose I am man," answered the other. "And suppose that I give the answer that shatters even a laugh. Suppose I do not laugh back at you, do not blaspheme you, do not curse you. But suppose, standing up straight under the sky, with every power of my being, I thank you for the fools' paradise you have made. Suppose I praise you, with a literal pain of ecstasy, for the jest that has brought me so terrible a joy. If we have taken the child's games, and given them the seriousness of a Crusade, if we have drenched your grotesque Dutch garden with the blood of martyrs, we have turned a nursery into a temple. I ask you, in the name of Heaven, who wins?"

The sky close about the crests of the hills and trees was beginning to turn from black to grey, with a random suggestion of the morning. The slight figure seemed to crawl towards the larger one, and the voice was more human.

===Chinese===
"又假如我是人呢，"另一个答道，"假如我给出的回答，足以粉碎连一笑都不可粉碎之物呢。假如我并不回敬你以笑，并不亵渎你，并不咒骂你。而是假如我挺直地站在天穹之下，以我全部生命的一切力量，为你所造的这座愚人的乐园而向你道谢呢。假如我以一种字面意义上的、因狂喜而生的剧痛，赞美你这场给我带来了如此可怖的喜悦的玩笑呢。倘若我们竟把孩童的游戏，赋予了十字军东征一般的庄严，倘若我们竟用殉道者的血，浸透了你那座怪诞的荷兰式花园，那我们便是把一间育婴室变成了一座神庙。我以天堂之名问你——谁赢了？"

山峦与树冠四周那贴近的天穹，正开始由黑转灰，带出一缕杂乱的晨意。那瘦小的身影似乎朝着那高大的身影爬近了些，声音也更带了几分人情。

===Original===
"But suppose, friend," it said, "suppose that, in a bitterer and more real sense, it was all a mockery. Suppose that there had been, from the beginning of these great wars, one who watched them with a sense that is beyond expression, a sense of detachment, of responsibility, of irony, of agony. Suppose that there were one who knew it was all a joke."

The tall figure answered—

"He could not know it. For it was not all a joke."

And a gust of wind blew away some clouds that sealed the skyline, and showed a strip of silver behind his great dark legs. Then the other voice came, having crept nearer still.

"Adam Wayne," it said, "there are men who confess only in articulo mortis; there are people who blame themselves only when they can no longer help others. I am one of them. Here, upon the field of the bloody end of it all, I come to tell you plainly what you would never understand before. Do you know who I am?"

===Chinese===
"可假如，朋友，"它说，"假如在一种更苦涩、更真实的意义上，这一切当真是一场嘲弄呢。假如，自这些伟大的战争伊始，便有一个人，以一种无法言传的感觉旁观着它们——一种超然之感、责任之感、讥诮之感、苦痛之感。假如有一个人，明知这一切不过是一个玩笑呢。"

那高大的身影答道——

"他不可能知道。因为这一切并不全然是玩笑。"

一阵风吹走了封住天际线的几片云，在他那两条巨大的、漆黑的腿后，露出了一道银色。随后另一个声音又响起，又爬得近了些。

"亚当·韦恩，"它说，"世有些人，只在临终之际（in articulo mortis，拉丁语，意为'在临死的时刻'）才肯忏悔；有些人，只在再也帮不了别人的时候才肯自责。我便是其中之一。在这儿，在这鲜血淋漓的结局的战场上，我来向你坦白讲明一件你从前怎么也弄不懂的事。你知道我是谁吗？"

===Original===
"I know you, Auberon Quin," answered the tall figure, "and I shall be glad to unburden your spirit of anything that lies upon it."

"Adam Wayne," said the other voice, "of what I have to say you cannot in common reason be glad to unburden me. Wayne, it was all a joke. When I made these cities, I cared no more for them than I care for a centaur, or a merman, or a fish with legs, or a pig with feathers, or any other absurdity. When I spoke to you solemnly and encouragingly about the flag of your freedom and the peace of your city, I was playing a vulgar practical joke on an honest gentleman, a vulgar practical joke that has lasted for twenty years. Though no one could believe it of me, perhaps, it is the truth that I am a man both timid and tenderhearted. I never dared in the early days of your hope, or the central days of your supremacy, to tell you this; I never dared to break the colossal calm of your face. God knows why I should do it now, when my farce has ended in tragedy and the ruin of all your people! But I say it now. Wayne, it was done as a joke."

===Chinese===
"我认得你，奥伯伦·奎因，"那高大的身影答道，"你心里若有任何重担，我很乐意替你卸下。"

"亚当·韦恩，"另一个声音说，"我说的话，按常理来讲，你是不可能乐意替我卸下的。韦恩，这一切都是一个玩笑。当初我造出这些城邦之时，我对它们毫不在意，正如我毫不在意一头半人马、一条美人鱼、一条长腿的鱼、一只有羽毛的猪，或任何别的荒诞之物。当初我庄严而恳切地对你谈起你那自由的旗帜、你那城邦的安宁，我不过是在戏弄一位诚实的绅士——一个庸俗的恶作剧，一个持续了二十年的恶作剧。尽管也许没有人会相信，但事实是，我这个人既胆小又心软。在你怀抱希望之初、在你如日中天之时，我从不敢把这件事告诉你；我从不敢打破你脸上那副巨大的镇定。天知道为什么我现在要这么做——在我的闹剧已以悲剧、以你全体子民的覆灭而告终的此刻！可我现在还是要说。韦恩，这一切，当初都是一个玩笑。"

===Original===
There was silence, and the freshening breeze blew the sky clearer and clearer, leaving great spaces of the white dawn.

At last Wayne said, very slowly—

"You did it all only as a joke?"

"Yes," said Quin, briefly.

"When you conceived the idea," went on Wayne, dreamily, "of an army for Bayswater and a flag for Notting Hill, there was no gleam, no suggestion in your mind that such things might be real and passionate?"

"No," answered Auberon, turning his round white face to the morning with a dull and splendid sincerity; "I had none at all."

===Chinese===
一阵沉默，渐强的晨风把天空吹得愈来愈清，留出大块大块的、白色的曙光。

末了，韦恩极其缓慢地说——

"这一切，你当初全然只是当作一个玩笑？"

"是的，"奎因简短地说。

"当初你构想出——"韦恩如梦似幻地接着说，"给贝斯沃特一支军队、给诺丁山一面旗帜——这个念头之时，你脑海中便没有一丝一毫的闪光、一丝一毫的暗示，觉得这些事情可能成为真实的、可能成为热血沸腾的吗？"

"没有，"奥伯伦答道，把他那张又圆又白的脸转向晨光，带着一种沉滞而辉煌的真诚；"完全没有。"

===Original===
Wayne sprang down from the height above him and held out his hand.

"I will not stop to thank you," he said, with a curious joy in his voice, "for the great good for the world you have actually wrought. All that I think of that I have said to you a moment ago, even when I thought that your voice was the voice of a derisive omnipotence, its laughter older than the winds of heaven. But let me say what is immediate and true. You and I, Auberon Quin, have both of us throughout our lives been again and again called mad. And we are mad. We are mad, because we are not two men, but one man. We are mad, because we are two lobes of the same brain, and that brain has been cloven in two. And if you ask for the proof of it, it is not hard to find. It is not merely that you, the humorist, have been in these dark days stripped of the joy of gravity. It is not merely that I, the fanatic, have had to grope without humour. It is that, though we seem to be opposite in everything, we have been opposite like man and woman, aiming at the same moment at the same practical thing. We are the father and the mother of the Charter of the Cities."

===Chinese===
韦恩从他上方的高处一跃而下，伸出手来。

"我不停下来感谢你，"他说，声音里带着一种古怪的喜悦，"为你确乎为这世界所成就的那一桩大善。那一切，我在方才——即便在我以为你的声音乃是一个讥诮的全能者之声、其笑声比诸天之风更古老之时——便已对你说过了。但让我说一点眼前而真切的。你和我，奥伯伦·奎因，我们两个，一生之中都曾被人一再地称作疯子。而我们确是疯子。我们是疯子，因为我们不是两个人，而是同一个人。我们是疯子，因为我们是同一个大脑的两叶，而那个大脑被劈成了两半。你若要这事的证据，并不难找。不仅仅在于你，这个幽默家，在这些黑暗的日子里被剥夺了庄重之乐。也不仅仅在于我，这个狂热者，不得不在没有幽默的黑暗中摸索。而在于——纵然我们似乎在一切方面都彼此对立，我们却像男人与女人那样地对立着，在同一时刻瞄准着同一件实际的事物。我们两个，是城市宪章（the Charter of the Cities）的父亲和母亲。"

===Original===
Quin looked down at the debris of leaves and timber, the relics of the battle and stampede, now glistening in the growing daylight, and finally said—

"Yet nothing can alter the antagonism—the fact that I laughed at these things and you adored them."

Wayne's wild face flamed with something godlike, as he turned it to be struck by the sunrise.

"I know of something that will alter that antagonism, something that is outside us, something that you and I have all our lives perhaps taken too little account of. The equal and eternal human being will alter that antagonism, for the human being sees no real antagonism between laughter and respect, the human being, the common man, whom mere geniuses like you and me can only worship like a god. When dark and dreary days come, you and I are necessary, the pure fanatic, the pure satirist. We have between us remedied a great wrong. We have lifted the modern cities into that poetry which everyone who knows mankind knows to be immeasurably more common than the commonplace. But in healthy people there is no war between us. We are but the two lobes of the brain of a ploughman. Laughter and love are everywhere. The cathedrals, built in the ages that loved God, are full of blasphemous grotesques. The mother laughs continually at the child, the lover laughs continually at the lover, the wife at the husband, the friend at the friend. Auberon Quin, we have been too long separated; let us go out together. You have a halberd and I a sword, let us start our wanderings over the world. For we are its two essentials. Come, it is already day."

In the blank white light Auberon hesitated a moment. Then he made the formal salute with his halberd, and they went away together into the unknown world.

===Chinese===
奎因低头望着脚下那一堆落叶与断木的残骸——那场战役与奔逃所留下的遗物，此刻在渐明的天光里闪闪发亮——末了他说——

"然而，那一种对立是无可更改的——我嘲笑这些东西，而你想这些东西，这一事实无可更改。"

韦恩那张狂野的脸，在他转过脸去迎接日出之时，因某种近乎神祇的光彩而燃烧起来。

"我知道有一样东西能更改那一种对立，一样在我们之外的东西，一样你我毕生或许都太看轻了的东西。那平等的、永恒的人，会更改那一种对立；因为人并不觉得笑与敬畏之间有什么真正的对立——人，那个寻常人，那个你我这种不过天才一等的人物只能像崇拜神明一样崇拜的寻常人。当黑暗沉闷的日子来临，你和我都是不可或缺的——纯粹的狂热者，纯粹的讽刺者。我们二人合力，补救了一桩大错。我们把近代的诸城，提升到了那一种诗意之中——但凡懂得人性的人都知道，那诗意比平庸之物要普遍到不可估量。可在健康的人身上，我们之间并无战争。我们不过是一个庄稼汉大脑的两个脑叶。笑声与爱，无处不在。那些在敬爱上帝的岁月里建造起来的大教堂，里头满是亵渎神明的滑稽造像。母亲对着孩子不住地笑，恋人对着恋人不住地笑，妻子对着丈夫，朋友对着朋友。奥伯伦·奎因，我们分离得太久了；让我们一同走出去吧。你有一柄戟，我有一柄剑，让我们动身去漫游这世界吧。因为我们正是它的两大要素。来吧，天已经亮了。"

在一片空白的白光里，奥伯伦迟疑了片刻。随即他以戟行了一个正式的军礼，两人便一同离去，走进了那未知的世界。
